# Roadmap: foundations of adic spaces, Part II — analytic and formal geometry

This roadmap continues the Tau Ceti roadmap **Foundations of adic spaces** (`AdicSpaces`, called *the
anchor* below). The anchor builds Huber rings and pairs, the valuation and adic spectra, rational
localisation, the structure presheaf, sheafiness for strongly noetherian and stably uniform Tate rings,
and the category of adic spaces with open and closed immersions, morphisms locally of finite type and
gluing. It stops before fibre products and says that separatedness, properness and the diagonal
belong "together with completed tensor products and fibre products in a separate roadmap". This is
that roadmap. It develops the analytic and formal geometry that p-adic Hodge theory, the étale
cohomology of rigid spaces, overconvergent automorphic forms and p-adic Shimura varieties use:

- **completed tensor products** of Huber pairs (topology, plus ring, universal property,
  uniformisation) and **fibre products** of adic spaces under Huber's noetherian hypotheses;
- the **morphism theory**: adic morphisms, the finite-type classes, separated, proper and partially
  proper morphisms with their valuative criteria, finite and quasi-finite morphisms, continuous
  differentials, unramified, smooth and étale morphisms, the Jacobian criterion and standard étale
  presentations;
- the **classical affinoid algebras** and **analytification** of schemes locally of finite type over
  a nonarchimedean field, Tate's rigid-analytic spaces and Huber's rigid–adic comparison, analytic
  groups and invariant differentials;
- **noetherian formal geometry**: Spf, formal schemes, completion, coherent formal modules, the
  theorem on formal functions, Grothendieck's existence and algebraisation theorems;
- **formal models**: admissible formal schemes over an arbitrary complete rank-one valuation ring,
  admissible blow-ups, flattening, Raynaud's theorem, generic fibres, the good-reduction locus,
  residue discs and Hasse domains with fractional valuation bounds;
- **coherent sheaves**: the sheaf of a finite module, Tate acyclicity for finite modules and for
  every sheafy Tate pair, Kiehl's theorems on affinoid and quasi-Stein spaces, vector bundles on
  sheafy affinoids, the proper mapping theorem, base change, proper GAGA, and **traces** along finite
  locally free morphisms with pull–identify–trace on correspondences;
- the **étale and pro-étale sites** of AdicEtaleGeometry A1 on these carriers, smooth pairs and
  boundary complements;
- **sousperfectoid** rings and spaces, products of perfectoid spaces with smooth rigid spaces, and
  the coefficient algebras and sheaves of p-adic families;
- **dagger geometry**: overconvergent Tate algebras, dagger algebras, Monsky–Washnitzer weak
  completions, dagger spaces, strict neighbourhoods, the comparison with rigid spaces, and the
  overconvergent, logarithmic and compactly supported de Rham complexes.

The morphism theory is built once, here, for the whole area; AdicEtaleGeometry, PerfectoidSpaces,
DiamondsAndVStacks and the Shimura-variety roadmaps import it.

| Layer | Title | What it builds |
|---|---|---|
| R0 | Morphisms and admissible affinoid products | affinoid algebras, completed tensor products, fibre products, the morphism classes, differentials, smooth and étale morphisms |
| R1 | Analytification and algebraic correspondences | X ↦ X^ad, rigid spaces and affinoid subdomains, the rigid–adic comparison, analytic groups |
| F0 | Formal geometry | Spf and formal schemes, completion, coherent formal modules, formal functions, existence and algebraisation |
| R2 | Formal schemes, generic fibres and Hasse domains | admissible formal schemes, blow-ups, Raynaud's theorem, generic fibres, good reduction, Hasse domains |
| R3 | Coherent sheaves and finite traces | M̃, Tate and Kiehl, vector bundles, the proper mapping theorem, GAGA, traces |
| R4 | Étale and pro-étale sites | A1's sites on these carriers, functoriality, smooth pairs, boundary complements |
| R5 | Families and sousperfectoid spaces | sousperfectoid rings and spaces, perfectoid × smooth, coefficient algebras and sheaves |
| F1 | Dagger geometry and overconvergent de Rham complexes | dagger algebras and spaces, strict neighbourhoods, de Rham complexes |

```text
anchor Layers 0–5 → R0 → R1 → R2 → R3 → R5
F0 → R0,  F0 → R2,  F0 → R3
R0, R1, AdicEtaleGeometry A1 → R4
R0–R4 → F1
AdicEtaleGeometry A1, PerfectoidSpaces P1–P3 → R5
```

F0 depends on nothing in this roadmap and precedes R0 (Huber's sheafiness theorem for rings with a
noetherian ring of definition uses F0's Artin–Rees input) and R2.

## Prerequisites and boundaries

- **The anchor** (Tau Ceti roadmap `AdicSpaces`), Layers 0–5. Of it, Tau Ceti already contains: Huber
  rings and pairs, pairs of definition, completion, weighted restricted power series with their
  universal property, strong noetherianness, topologically-finite-type maps (`Huber.IsTopologicallyFiniteType`),
  rational localisation, `Spa` and rational subsets, the analytic locus `spaAnalytic`, the quotient pair
  `Huber.Pair.quotient` with the closed embedding `Spa(A/J) → Spa(A, A⁺)` onto `V(J)`
  (`isClosedEmbedding_spaComap_quotientMk`), uniform and stably uniform rings (`Huber.IsUniform`,
  `Huber.IsStablyUniform`), ring-level and pair-level sheafiness (`Huber.IsSheafyRing`,
  `Huber.IsStablySheafyRing`, `Huber.IsSheafyForEveryPresentation` — the anchor's `IsSheafyPair`),
  sheafiness of strongly noetherian Tate rings (`isSheafyRing_of_isStronglyNoetherian`, Wedhorn 8.28(b)),
  exactness of the two-piece Laurent cover of a strongly noetherian Tate ring (`laurentCover_exact`),
  strictness of linear maps of finite modules over complete noetherian Tate rings
  (`isStrictMap_of_module_finite`), `M ⊗_A A⟨T⟩ ≅ M⟨T⟩` (`restrictedMvPowerSeriesBaseChange`), the
  Gauss norm on `K⟨X⟩` and Gauss points of polydiscs, the gluing of valuations along directed unions
  (`ValuationSpectrum.ofDirected`), pre-adic spaces with their morphisms, open immersions
  (`PreAdicSpace.IsOpenImmersion`) and the category `TauCeti.AdicSpace` of adic spaces, and the
  augmented Čech complex criterion `quasiIso_cechAugmentation_iff`. None of this is re-planned: the
  targets below cite these declarations, and the R0 and R3 targets on rings with a noetherian ring of
  definition are the non-Tate case that the library's Tate-ring theorems do not cover.
  Still owned by the anchor and cited, not planned here: strong noetherianness of complete rank-one
  fields and of `K⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩` (anchor §0.5), closed immersions defined affinoid-locally by closed ideals
  (anchor §5.1), morphisms locally of finite type (anchor §5.2), gluing (§5.3), the closed and open
  discs (§5.4), Tate acyclicity in all degrees and Corollary 8.35 for strongly noetherian Tate rings
  (§4.1) and the Buzzard–Verberkmoes theorem (§4.2).
- **AdicEtaleGeometry A1** owns the étale and pro-étale sites with the corrected covering convention,
  geometric points, and the local description of étale and finite étale morphisms. R4 builds no
  second site. **PerfectoidSpaces P1–P3** own perfectoid Tate rings, their rational localisations and
  fibre products, tilting and almost purity; **DiamondsAndVStacks D0** owns Cartan's criterion and the
  Čech-to-derived comparison on a basis. These three roadmaps form one bundle with this one.
- **SchemeAndStackFoundations SF.2** (Godement resolutions and flasque sheaves) and the Tau Ceti
  roadmaps **StableReduction** Layers 0 and 2 (Fitting ideals; coherence of higher direct images along
  proper morphisms of locally noetherian schemes), **JacobianChallenge** Layers B and C (acyclicity of
  affines, Čech-to-derived on separated schemes, flat base change of higher direct images) and
  **ModularCurves** 0e (effective faithfully flat descent of modules) are cited for F0 and R3.
- Notions that this roadmap needs from roadmaps it may not cite (projective bundles, ample sheaves,
  Serre finiteness and vanishing, Chow's lemma, the Deligne–Rapoport moduli of generalised elliptic
  curves, the Gel'fand spectrum of a Banach ring, the affinoid criterion for tilde-limits, de Jong's
  strictly semistable alteration) are stated as targets of this roadmap, at the end of the layer that
  uses them, under the heading "Notions this roadmap states for its own use".
- Not in scope: derived étale cohomology of the sites (ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology H0), the
  specialisation morphism and nearby cycles (ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology H1), rigid cohomology, frames,
  tubes of frames, Robba rings and Frobenius structures (PadicDifferentialEquationsAndRigidCohomology
  RD.0–RD.6), the ordinary-locus boundary cohomology formed from F1's complexes
  (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentationsPartII AG2.4), bounded-height Grassmannians over deformation rings
  (LocalGaloisDeformationRings L7), continuous torsor descent with coefficients (PerfectoidSpaces P9),
  and the adic Fargues–Fontaine curve (anchor Layer 6).
- **Consumers.** PerfectoidSpaces P2–P9, AdicEtaleGeometry A0–A4, DiamondsAndVStacks D2–D6, the
  étale-cohomology, Hodge–Tate, overconvergent-forms, Shimura-variety, Néron-model, deformation-ring
  and formal-moduli roadmaps, and the dagger and rigid-cohomology roadmaps, each importing the layers
  named in its own document.

## Conventions

1. **Huber pairs** are Tau Ceti's `Huber.Pair A`: the plus ring is explicit data. Morphisms are
   `Huber.Pair.Hom` (continuous, carrying `A⁺` into `B⁺`). Pairs of definition are `Huber.PairOfDefinition`.
2. **Complete includes Hausdorff**, as in the anchor; every statement about complete rings, modules
   or pairs carries both.
3. **Adic spaces** are the objects of the anchor's category (`TauCeti.AdicSpace`): locally `Spa(A, A⁺)`
   for sheafy complete Huber pairs. A point is *analytic* if it has an open neighbourhood whose ring of
   sections has a topologically nilpotent unit. A Huber ring is *of noetherian type* if it has a
   noetherian ring of definition or is a strongly noetherian Tate ring; an adic space is *locally
   noetherian* if it is locally `Spa` of such a ring.
4. **The base field.** `K` is complete for a nontrivial rank-one nonarchimedean absolute value,
   `O_K = K°`, `ϖ` a pseudouniformiser, `Spa K = Spa(K, K°)`. `O_K` is noetherian exactly when `K` is
   discretely valued; no statement assumes this unless it says so.
5. **Completed tensor products** `B ⊗̂_A C` are formed only along adic structure maps, with the
   topology from rings and ideals of definition, plus ring the integral closure of the image of
   `B⁺ ⊗_{A⁺} C⁺`, then completion. They are neither uniform nor sheafy in general; every fibre product
   carries its own existence and sheafiness hypotheses.
6. **Fibre products** are taken in the anchor's category and exist under Huber's hypotheses (R0); the
   map from `|X ×_Z Y|` to the fibre product of the underlying sets is surjective, not injective.
7. **Formal schemes** (F0) are locally `Spf A` for `A` complete and separated for an adic topology with
   a finitely generated ideal of definition. Admissible formal `O_K`-schemes (R2) are topologically
   finitely presented and `ϖ`-torsion-free, and are not noetherian unless `K` is discretely valued.
8. **Rigid spaces** are Tate's (R1). `X^an` is the rigid and `X^ad` the adic analytification; they are
   identified only through Huber's functor `r_K`.
9. **Valuation bounds.** `|f| ≤ |g|^{a/b}` means `v(f^b) ≤ v(g^a)`; no root of `g` in the base field is
   assumed. Tubes and strict Hasse loci are interiors of preimages under specialisation (R2).
10. **Coherent and locally free.** Coherent sheaves are defined on locally noetherian adic spaces only;
    vector bundles on every adic space, corresponding to finite projective modules on sheafy Tate
    affinoids.
11. **Traces.** The trace of an endomorphism of a finite projective module is `LinearMap.projectiveTrace`
    (R3), through `dualTensorHomEquiv`; Mathlib's `LinearMap.trace` and `Algebra.trace` are used only for
    free modules. A trace is never defined as a sum over geometric points.
12. **Names.** Ring-level declarations live in `Huber`, adic-space geometry in `AdicSpace`, formal
    schemes in `FormalScheme`, dagger algebras in `Dagger` and dagger spaces in `DaggerSpace`.

**How the targets are written.** Each layer lists its targets in dependency order, numbered `R0.1`,
`R0.2`, …; each is a definition, construction, theorem, comparison or lemma. A definition or construction
states the object, then its *API* (the lemmas it needs, named relative to the declaration) and its
*Tests* (examples a wrong definition would fail); a theorem states its hypotheses; every target names its
*Source* (short keys are resolved in the Sources section at the end, with the locator as "§, result,
page") and what it *Needs*: earlier targets of this roadmap, layers of other roadmaps, or the library
(Mathlib and Tau Ceti declarations named in the target text). Lemmas are intermediate steps; their
statements are in `Suggested.lean` where they can be typed.


<a id="r0"></a>

## R0. Morphisms and admissible affinoid products

**R0. Morphisms and admissible affinoid products.** Depends on the anchor and F0. Three parts: (a) ring-level inputs — rings of noetherian type, adic homomorphisms, Huber's sheafiness theorem for rings with a noetherian ring of definition (Case I of Huber 1994 Theorem 2.2, whose Tate-ring case is the library's), the completed tensor product with its universal property, uniformisation and the uniform completed tensor product, completed tensor products of Banach modules, then adic morphisms, the finite-type hierarchy, locally noetherian spaces, fibre products, closed subspaces and the diagonal; (b) separated, universally closed, proper and partially proper morphisms with the valuative criteria, finite and quasi-finite morphisms; (c) continuous differentials, flat, unramified, smooth and étale morphisms, the local structure theorems and étale affinoid morphisms over a field. Classical `K`-affinoid algebras (Noether normalisation, residue fields, the supremum seminorm, integrality of `A°`, reductions) open the layer. Where a target below specialises to Tate rings, the library's theorem is cited in its text and the target is the non-Tate case.


### Ring-level inputs

**R0.1 Huber rings of noetherian type (Huber's noetherian hypothesis)** (definition `Huber.IsNoetherianType`). Let A be a commutative topological ring which is a Huber ring (Tau Ceti `IsHuberRing A`). A is *of noetherian type* if (i) A admits a pair of definition (A₀, I) (Tau Ceti `PairOfDefinition A`) whose ring of definition A₀ is a noetherian ring, or (ii) A is a Tate ring (`IsTateRing A`) and strongly noetherian in Tau Ceti's sense (`IsStronglyNoetherian A`: every completed restricted power-series ring A⟨X₁,…,X_k⟩, k ≥ 0, is a noetherian ring). This is the hypothesis Huber places on affinoid rings in Theorem 2.2 and Propositions 3.6–3.8 of the 1994 paper ('has a noetherian ring of definition or is a strongly noetherian Tate ring'). Conventions: the predicate concerns the topological ring only (no plus ring); completeness is not part of it, and every result that needs a complete ring assumes completeness and the Hausdorff property separately. Clause (i) does not say that A is a noetherian ring: every discrete ring is of noetherian type because its prime subring (the image of ℤ) is an open noetherian subring with ideal of definition 0. For a Tate ring clause (i) implies clause (ii) (a Tate ring with a noetherian ring of definition is strongly noetherian), so for Tate rings the predicate is exactly strong noetherianness; the converse implication fails: ℂ_p is strongly noetherian but has no noetherian ring of definition (a ring of definition A₀ ⊆ O_{ℂ_p} contains some p^N·O_{ℂ_p}, and the A₀-ideal p^N·𝔪_{ℂ_p} is not finitely generated). Lean name: `Huber.IsNoetherianType A` (a Prop-valued class). *API* (on `Huber`): `IsNoetherianType, isNoetherianType_iff, IsNoetherianType.of_pairOfDefinition, IsNoetherianType.of_isStronglyNoetherian, IsNoetherianType.of_discreteTopology, IsNoetherianType.isStronglyNoetherian, IsNoetherianType.isNoetherianRing_of_isTateRing, IsNoetherianType.completion, IsNoetherianType.of_isTopologicallyFiniteType, IsNoetherianType.isClosed_ideal, IsNoetherianType.isStablySheafyRing, IsNoetherianType.of_ringEquiv`. *Tests:* `isNoetherianType_discrete_polynomial` (non-example): The discrete ring ℤ[T₁, T₂, …] (countably many variables) is of noetherian type (via its prime subring ℤ) but is not a noetherian ring; a definition 'A is a noetherian ring' fails this test. `isNoetherianType_padicPowerSeries` (computation): ℤ_p⟦T⟧ with the (p, T)-adic topology is of noetherian type through the pair of definition (ℤ_p⟦T⟧, (p, T)), and has no topologically nilpotent unit, so clause (ii) does not apply. `not_isNoetherianType_perfectoidDisc` (non-example): For a perfectoid field K, the Tate ring K⟨T^{1/p^∞}⟩ is not of noetherian type: it is not a noetherian ring (the ideal generated by all T^{1/p^n} is not finitely generated), so it is not strongly noetherian, and a noetherian ring of definition A₀ would make A = A₀[1/ϖ] noetherian. `isNoetherianType_iff_isStronglyNoetherian_of_isTateRing` (compatibility): For a Tate ring A, `IsNoetherianType A ↔ IsStronglyNoetherian A` (Tau Ceti's predicate). `isNoetherianType_of_subsingleton` (degenerate): The zero ring is of noetherian type. `isNoetherianType_Cp_without_noetherian_ringOfDefinition` (non-example): ℂ_p is of noetherian type by clause (ii) (strongly noetherian, BGR 5.2.6), but no ring of definition of ℂ_p is noetherian: every ring of definition A₀ satisfies p^N·O_{ℂ_p} ⊆ A₀ ⊆ O_{ℂ_p}, and the A₀-ideal p^N·𝔪_{ℂ_p} is not finitely generated. A definition by clause (i) alone ('has a noetherian ring of definition') fails this test; no other listed test separates it from the disjunction. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.7, p. 535. *Needs:* R0.2.

- **R0.2** A noetherian ring of definition stays noetherian under completion. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, §7.3, (7.3.1).

**R0.3 Adic homomorphisms of Huber rings** (definition `Huber.IsAdicHom`). Let A and B be Huber rings. A ring homomorphism φ : A → B is *adic* if there exist a pair of definition (A₀, I) of A and a ring of definition B₀ of B such that φ(A₀) ⊆ B₀ and the ideal φ(I)·B₀ of B₀ is an ideal of definition of B₀ (it is finitely generated, and the subspace topology of B₀ is the φ(I)B₀-adic topology); equivalently, there is a Tau Ceti pair of definition Q of B with φ(P.ringOfDefinition) ⊆ Q.ringOfDefinition and Q.idealOfDefinition equal to the ideal of Q.ringOfDefinition generated by φ(P.idealOfDefinition). Every adic homomorphism is continuous. Lean name: `Huber.IsAdicHom φ` (a Prop). Huber 1994 recalls this definition from his 1993 paper; Wedhorn records it as Definition 6.23. *API* (on `Huber`): `IsAdicHom, IsAdicHom.continuous, IsAdicHom.comp, IsAdicHom.of_comp, IsAdicHom.id, IsAdicHom.of_isTateRing, IsAdicHom.of_radical_eq, IsAdicHom.of_discrete_iff, isAdicHom_completion_coe, isAdicHom_algebraMap_weightedRestrictedSubring, IsAdicHom.comp_isOpenQuotientMap, IsTopologicallyFiniteType.isAdicHom`. *Tests:* `isAdicHom_padicInt_padic` (computation): The inclusion ℤ_p → ℚ_p is adic: A₀ = B₀ = ℤ_p, I = pℤ_p, and pℤ_p is an ideal of definition of ℤ_p. `not_isAdicHom_padicInt_powerSeries` (non-example): The inclusion ℤ_p → ℤ_p⟦T⟧ with the p-adic topology on ℤ_p and the (p, T)-adic topology on ℤ_p⟦T⟧ is continuous but not adic: p·ℤ_p⟦T⟧ contains no power of (p, T). A definition 'adic = continuous' fails this test. `isAdicHom_of_discrete_iff` (characterisation): For a discrete ring A and a Huber ring B, a ring homomorphism A → B is adic iff B is discrete (Wedhorn Example 6.24); in particular ℤ → ℤ_p is continuous and not adic. `isAdicHom_completion_coe` (compatibility): For a Huber ring A the completion map A → Â is adic, with the pair of definition of Â given by Tau Ceti `PairOfDefinition.completion` (Â₀ the closure of the image of A₀, Î generated by the image of I). `isAdicHom_id` (degenerate): The identity of a Huber ring is adic. *Source:* Huber94 §3, par. before the Def. of adic morphisms, p. 532.

- **R0.4** Topologically-finite-type homomorphisms are adic. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Lem. 3.3(ii), condition (b).

- **R0.5** Rings topologically of finite type over a ring of noetherian type are of noetherian type. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Cor. 3.4(iii).

- **R0.6** Ideals of complete rings of noetherian type are closed. *Source:* Huber94 §2, Lem. 2.3(ii).

### Sheafiness of rings of noetherian type (Huber 1994 Theorem 2.2)

- **R0.7** Exactness of the extended Čech complex of localisations. *Source:* Stacks Tag 01X9, Lem. 30.2.1.

- **R0.8** Completion of a strict exact complex is strict exact. *Source:* Huber94 §2, proof of (1.3).

- **R0.9** Linear maps of finite modules over a Huber ring with a noetherian ring of definition are strict (Huber Lemma 2.3(i)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, Lem. 2.3.

- **R0.10** Finite modules over a complete Huber ring with a noetherian ring of definition are complete, with closed submodules (Huber Lemma 2.3(ii)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, Lem. 2.3.

- **R0.11** Completion of a finite module over a Huber ring with a noetherian ring of definition is base change to Â (Huber Lemma 2.3(iii)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, Lem. 2.3.

- **R0.12** Spec of a Huber ring and of an open subring agree away from the open primes (Huber 1993 Lemma 3.7). *Source:* Huber93 §3, Lem. 3.7.

**R0.13 The blow-up scheme Proj(⊕ Jⁿ) of a finitely generated submodule (Huber 1994 (1.1)–(1.2))** (construction `AlgebraicGeometry.submoduleBlowup`). Let φ : C → B be a ring homomorphism, F = {f₀, …, fₙ} ⊆ B a finite subset and J ⊆ B the C-submodule generated by F. Let R(J) := ⊕_{n≥0} Jⁿ·Tⁿ ⊆ B[T] (J⁰ := φ(C); for n ≥ 1, Jⁿ is the additive subgroup generated by the products j₁⋯jₙ, j_i ∈ J), an ℕ-graded ring (Mathlib `GradedAlgebra`) generated over R(J)₀ = φ(C) by the degree-one elements f·T, f ∈ F. Put Y := Proj R(J) with g : Y → Spec C the composite of Mathlib's `Proj.toSpecZero` and Spec(C → φ(C)). R(J), Y, g, the covering in (a) and the chart rings in (b) need no hypothesis on F (Huber's (1.1) allows any C-submodule J). Assume now that F generates the unit ideal of B (F·B = B). The evaluation R(J) → B, T ↦ 1, maps the irrelevant ideal onto an ideal containing F, hence onto B, and so defines s : X := Spec B → Y (Mathlib `Proj.fromOfGlobalSections`) with g ∘ s = Spec φ; this is Huber's diagram (1.1) in the case I := J·B = B, where X = Proj(⊕ Iⁿ) = Spec B. Properties (Huber (1.2)(i),(ii)): (a) the opens D₊(f·T), f ∈ F, cover Y and s⁻¹(D₊(f·T)) = D(f), so s is affine; (b) for a nonempty S ⊆ F with product t = ∏_{f∈S} f, the open U = D₊(t·T^{|S|}) is affine with O_Y(U) = C[f/f' : f ∈ F, f' ∈ S] ⊆ B_t (the φ(C)-subalgebra of the localisation B_t generated by these fractions) and O_X(s⁻¹U) = B_t; (c) g is projective, hence proper and separated, and Y is noetherian when C is. Lean name: `AlgebraicGeometry.submoduleBlowup φ F`. Tau Ceti's `reesAlgebra.grade`, `affineBlowupι` and `opensRange_affineBlowupι` are the case C = B with J an ideal; this target is the submodule case over a ring map and specialises to them. *API* (on `AlgebraicGeometry`): `submoduleBlowup, submoduleBlowup.gradedRing, submoduleBlowup.toSpec, submoduleBlowup.fromSpec, submoduleBlowup.fromSpec_toSpec, submoduleBlowup.fromSpec_preimage_basicOpen, submoduleBlowup.iSup_basicOpen_eq_top, submoduleBlowup.isAffineHom_fromSpec, submoduleBlowup.basicOpenIsoSpec, submoduleBlowup.isProper_toSpec, submoduleBlowup.gradedRing_eq_reesAlgebra, submoduleBlowup.isIso_toSpec_restrict`. *Tests:* `submoduleBlowup_test_unit` (degenerate): For F = {1} (so J = φ(C)), R(J) = φ(C)[T], Y ≅ Spec φ(C) and s = Spec(φ : C → B) corestricted; g is the closed immersion Spec φ(C) → Spec C. `submoduleBlowup_test_padicLaurent` (computation): For C = ℤ_p⟨T⟩ ⊆ B = ℚ_p⟨T⟩ and F = {p, T}, O_Y(D₊(pT')) = ℤ_p⟨T⟩[T/p] and O_Y(D₊(TT')) = ℤ_p⟨T⟩[p/T] inside ℚ_p⟨T⟩ and ℚ_p⟨T⟩_T respectively (T' the grading variable). `submoduleBlowup_reesAlgebra_compat` (compatibility): If B = C, φ = id and F generates an ideal 𝔞 of C (not necessarily the unit ideal), R(J) is Mathlib's `reesAlgebra 𝔞` as a graded subalgebra of C[T]. `submoduleBlowup_fromSpec_preimage` (characterisation): For every f ∈ F, s⁻¹(D₊(f·T)) = D(f) ⊆ Spec B; in particular s⁻¹ of the covering {D₊(f_i·T)} is the covering {D(f_i)} of Spec B. `submoduleBlowup_test_not_unit_ideal` (non-example): For C = B = ℤ_p⟨T⟩ and F = {p, T}, F·B ≠ B and there is no morphism s : Spec B → Y with s⁻¹(D₊(f·T)) = D(f) for f ∈ F: the prime (p, T) of B lies in no D(f), f ∈ F. *Source:* Huber94 §2, proof of Thm 2.5, Case I, (1.1), p. 526.

- **R0.14** The submodule blow-up is an isomorphism away from the centre (Huber 1994 (1.2)(iii)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, (1.2)(iii).

- **R0.15** Deep Čech cocycles are coboundaries from a fixed level (Huber 1994 (1.3.1)(i)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, (1.3.1)(i).

- **R0.16** The Čech complexes of Iⁱ𝒢 form a neighbourhood basis in the natural topology (Huber 1994 (1.3.1)(ii)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, (1.3.1)(ii).

- **R0.17** Strict Čech exactness for standard rational coverings when B has a noetherian ring of definition (Huber 1994 (1.3)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, (1.3), p. 526.

**R0.18 Huber pairs with a noetherian ring of definition are sheafy (Huber 1994 Theorem 2.2, Case I)** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) be a Huber pair with A complete and Hausdorff and having a noetherian ring of definition, and X = Spa(A, A⁺). Then: (a) for every rational subset U ⊆ X the complete ring 𝒪_X(U) has a noetherian ring of definition; (b) for every finitely generated A-module M, every rational subset U and every g₀, …, g_m ∈ 𝒪_X(U) generating the unit ideal, the augmented Čech complex of the standard rational covering {V_j = U ∩ R(g/g_j)} of U with terms M ⊗_A 𝒪_X(V_{j₀} ∩ ⋯ ∩ V_{j_p}) (natural topologies) is exact with strict differentials; (c) every open covering of a rational subset U has a refinement by such a standard rational covering; (d) the structure presheaf 𝒪_X is a sheaf of complete topological rings, i.e. (A, A⁺) is sheafy (the anchor's `Huber.IsSheafyForEveryPresentation`). Sheaf-cohomology vanishing is R3.3. Tau Ceti's `isSheafyRing_of_isStronglyNoetherian` is Case II (strongly noetherian Tate rings); this target is Case I. *Hypotheses:* A complete Hausdorff Huber ring with a noetherian ring of definition (A is not assumed Tate). A⁺ a ring of integral elements; M a finitely generated A-module (for (b)). *Source:* Huber94 §2, Thm 2.2, p. 524. *Needs:* R0.17, R0.10, R0.2.

- **R0.19** Discrete Huber pairs are sheafy with Čech-acyclic rational coverings (Wedhorn Theorem 8.28(c)). *Source:* Wedhorn §8.2, Thm 8.28.

- **R0.20** Complete rings of noetherian type are stably sheafy. *Source:* Huber94 §2, Thm 2.2.

### The completed tensor product

**R0.21 The completed tensor product of Huber pairs along adic homomorphisms** (construction `Huber.Pair.completedTensor`). Let (A, A⁺), (B, B⁺), (C, C⁺) be Huber pairs (Tau Ceti `Pair`) and f : (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺), g : (A, A⁺) → (C, C⁺) morphisms of Huber pairs (Tau Ceti `Pair.Hom`) whose underlying ring homomorphisms are adic (R0.3). Choose a pair of definition (A₀, I) of A and rings of definition B₀ ⊆ B⁺, C₀ ⊆ C⁺ with f(A₀) ⊆ B₀, g(A₀) ⊆ C₀ such that f(I)B₀ and g(I)C₀ are ideals of definition. Let E := B ⊗_A C (Mathlib `Algebra.TensorProduct`, B and C being A-algebras through f and g), let F ⊆ E be the image of B₀ ⊗_{A₀} C₀ (a subring), and give E the group topology for which {Iⁿ·F : n ≥ 0} is a fundamental system of neighbourhoods of 0, where Iⁿ·F is the additive subgroup generated by the products of images of elements of Iⁿ with elements of F. Then E is a Huber ring with pair of definition (F, I·F), the maps inl : b ↦ b ⊗ 1 and inr : c ↦ 1 ⊗ c are adic, the topology does not depend on the choice of (A₀, I, B₀, C₀), and E⁺ := the integral closure in E of the image of B⁺ ⊗_{A⁺} C⁺ is a ring of integral elements of E. The *completed tensor product* is the complete Hausdorff Huber pair B ⊗̂_A C := (Ê, Ê⁺), where Ê is the Hausdorff completion of E (Tau Ceti `IsHuberRing.completion`) and Ê⁺ is the closure of the image of E⁺; it carries the morphisms of Huber pairs inl : (B, B⁺) → B ⊗̂_A C, inr : (C, C⁺) → B ⊗̂_A C with inl ∘ f = inr ∘ g. It is a Tate ring whenever B or C is. Pinned conventions: B ⊗̂_A C always means this completed pair; the plus ring is the integral closure of the image of B⁺ ⊗_{A⁺} C⁺ (not the image itself), then closed up in the completion; no uniformisation is performed; the construction is not defined unless both f and g are adic. Lean name: `Huber.Pair.completedTensor f g hf hg`. *API* (on `Huber.Pair`): `completedTensor, completedTensor.inl, completedTensor.inr, completedTensor.inl_comp, completedTensor.tmul, completedTensor.denseRange_tmul, completedTensor.pairOfDefinition, completedTensor.isAdicHom_inl, completedTensor.plus_eq, completedTensor.lift, completedTensor.lift_comp_inl, completedTensor.hom_ext, completedTensor.map, completedTensor.comm, completedTensor.cancelBaseChange, completedTensor.isTateRing, completedTensor_adicRing`. *Tests:* `completedTensor_test_polydisc` (computation): For a complete nonarchimedean field K: (K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) ⊗̂_{(K,K°)} (K⟨S⟩, K°⟨S⟩) is isomorphic, compatibly with inl and inr, to (K⟨T, S⟩, K°⟨T, S⟩) with T ↦ T ⊗ 1 and S ↦ 1 ⊗ S. `completedTensor_test_plus_not_image` (non-example): For K = ℚ_p and L = ℚ_p(√p): (L, O_L) ⊗̂_K (L, O_L) ≅ (L × L, O_L × O_L), and the idempotent (1, 0) lies in the plus ring but not in the image of O_L ⊗_{ℤ_p} O_L (that image is O_L[Y]/(Y² − p) embedded by Y ↦ (√p, −√p)). A definition taking the plus ring to be the image of B⁺ ⊗ C⁺ fails. `completedTensor_test_not_adic` (non-example): For A = ℤ_p (p-adic), B = ℤ_p⟦X⟧ ((p, X)-adic), C = ℚ_p: A → B is not adic, and there is no Huber-ring topology on B ⊗_A C = ℤ_p⟦X⟧[1/p] making B → B ⊗_A C and C → B ⊗_A C continuous with ℤ_p⟦X⟧ open (Morel Example II.3.2.2); the construction requires adicness of both maps. `completedTensor_test_unit` (degenerate): For g = id: (B, B⁺) ⊗̂_A (A, A⁺) ≅ (B̂, closure of B⁺) via inl, and for complete B this is (B, B⁺). `completedTensor_test_banach` (compatibility): If A, B, C are Banach rings with a topologically nilpotent unit and f, g bounded, the topology of B ⊗̂_A C equals the topology of the completion of B ⊗_A C for the product seminorm (Kedlaya–Liu Definition 2.1.10), and the plus ring equals Kedlaya–Liu's coproduct plus ring (completion of the integral closure of the image of B⁺ ⊗_{A⁺} C⁺). `completedTensor_test_algebraic` (compatibility): The map B ⊗[A] C → B ⊗̂_A C from Mathlib's algebraic tensor product has dense image and kernel the closure of 0 for the {Iⁿ·F} topology. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Lem. 3.9(i), p. 537. *Needs:* R0.3, R0.125.

**R0.22 Universal property of the completed tensor product** (theorem). Let f : (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) and g : (A, A⁺) → (C, C⁺) be morphisms of Huber pairs with adic underlying ring homomorphisms, and let D = B ⊗̂_A C with inl, inr (R0.21). Let R be a complete Hausdorff nonarchimedean topological commutative ring and R⁺ ⊆ R a closed subring integrally closed in R (for instance a complete Huber pair, or (O_X(X), O_X⁺(X)) for an adic space X). For all continuous ring homomorphisms φ : B → R, ψ : C → R with φ(B⁺) ⊆ R⁺, ψ(C⁺) ⊆ R⁺ and φ ∘ f = ψ ∘ g, there is exactly one continuous ring homomorphism χ : D → R with χ(D⁺) ⊆ R⁺, χ ∘ inl = φ and χ ∘ inr = ψ. In particular the square of f, g, inl, inr is cocartesian in the category of complete Hausdorff Huber pairs. If moreover R is Huber and φ is adic, then χ is adic. No adicness of φ or ψ is needed. *Hypotheses:* f, g morphisms of Huber pairs whose underlying ring homomorphisms are adic. R complete Hausdorff, nonarchimedean (open additive subgroups form a basis of neighbourhoods of 0). R⁺ ⊆ R a closed subring, integrally closed in R. φ, ψ continuous, φ(B⁺) ⊆ R⁺, ψ(C⁺) ⊆ R⁺, φ ∘ f = ψ ∘ g. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Prop. 3.7, p. 536. *Needs:* R0.21, R0.3, R0.125.

- **R0.23** Base change of weighted restricted power series and of their quotients. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Prop. 3.7.

- **R0.24** Completed base change along a topologically-finite-type map preserves noetherian type and sheafiness. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Prop. 3.7.

- **R0.25** Rational localisation commutes with completed tensor products. *Source:* KL15 §2.4, after Def. 2.4.12.

### The spectral seminorm, uniformization and the uniform completed tensor product

**R0.26 The norm of a Tate ring attached to a ring of definition, a pseudouniformiser and a radius** (definition `Huber.TateNormDatum`). Let A be a Tate ring (Tau Ceti `IsTateRing A`). A *norm datum* d = (A₀, ϖ, r) on A consists of a ring of definition A₀ (the `ringOfDefinition` of a Tau Ceti `PairOfDefinition A`), a pseudouniformiser ϖ of A lying in A₀ (Tau Ceti `IsPseudoUniformizer ϖ`: a topologically nilpotent unit; A₀ contains one by Wedhorn Proposition 6.14) and a real number r with 0 < r < 1. The *norm of A attached to d* is α_d : A → ℝ≥0, α_d(x) := inf{ rⁿ : n ∈ ℤ, ϖ⁻ⁿx ∈ A₀ } (Kedlaya–Liu Remark 2.4.4). The exponent set is nonempty and downward closed, so α_d(x) = r^{N(x)} with N(x) = sup{ n ∈ ℤ : x ∈ ϖⁿA₀ } ∈ ℤ ∪ {+∞} (r^{+∞} := 0). Then α_d is a nonarchimedean ring seminorm (Mathlib `RingSeminorm A` with `IsNonarchimedean`), α_d(1) ≤ 1 and α_d(ϖx) = r·α_d(x); its closed balls are {α_d ≤ rⁿ} = ϖⁿA₀ (n ∈ ℤ), so it defines the topology of A; it vanishes exactly on the closure of {0} = ⋂ₙ ϖⁿA₀, so it is a norm iff A is Hausdorff, and (A, α_d) is a Banach ring in Kedlaya–Liu's sense iff A is moreover complete. Pinned conventions: ϖ must lie in A₀; the function depends on d, and only its equivalence class (for fixed ϖ and r) and the topology it defines are canonical. Lean names: `Huber.TateNormDatum A` for d and `Huber.tateNorm d` for α_d. *API* (on `Huber`): `TateNormDatum, tateNorm, tateNorm_apply, isNonarchimedean_tateNorm, tateNorm_one_le, tateNorm_pseudoUniformizer_mul, tateNorm_le_zpow_iff, tateNorm_hasBasis_nhds_zero, tateNorm_eq_zero_iff, tateNorm_equiv_of_ringOfDefinition, tateNorm_rpow, tateNorm_map_le`. *Tests:* `tateNorm_padic` (computation): For A = ℚ_p with A₀ = ℤ_p, ϖ = p and r = 1/p, α_d(x) = |x|_p for every x. `tateNorm_not_multiplicative` (non-example): For A = ℚ_p with A₀ = ℤ_p, ϖ = p² and r = 1/p: α_d(p) = 1 and α_d(p²) = 1/p ≠ α_d(p)², so α_d is neither multiplicative nor power-multiplicative although A is a field; a definition expecting α_d to be the absolute value of the field fails this test. `tateNorm_zero_ring` (degenerate): On the zero ring (a Tate ring with A₀ = 0 and ϖ = 0 = 1) α_d is 0. `tateNorm_banachAlgebra` (compatibility): For a Banach algebra (A, ‖·‖) over a complete nonarchimedean field K with ‖cy‖ = |c|‖y‖ and ‖1‖ = 1, A₀ the unit ball, ϖ ∈ K with 0 < |ϖ| < 1 and r = |ϖ|: ‖x‖ ≤ α_d(x) ≤ r⁻¹‖x‖ for every x, and α_d(x) < r⁻¹‖x‖ for x ≠ 0. `tateNorm_le_pow_iff_tateAlgebra` (characterisation): For A = K⟨T⟩, A₀ = K°⟨T⟩, ϖ ∈ K° with 0 < |ϖ| < 1 and n ≥ 0: α_d(f) ≤ rⁿ iff every coefficient of f lies in ϖⁿK°. *Source:* KL15 §2.4, Rem. 2.4.4, p. 39.

**R0.27 The spectral seminorm of a Tate ring** (definition `Huber.spectralSeminorm`). Let A be a Tate ring with a norm datum d = (A₀, ϖ, r) and norm α_d (R0.26). The *spectral seminorm* of (A, d) is |x|_sp := lim_{n→∞} α_d(xⁿ)^{1/n} = inf_{n≥1} α_d(xⁿ)^{1/n} (Kedlaya–Liu Definition 2.1.9; the limit exists by Fekete's lemma), which is Mathlib's `smoothingSeminorm α_d` (defined because α_d(1) ≤ 1 and α_d is nonarchimedean). It is a power-multiplicative nonarchimedean ring seminorm (Mathlib `IsPowMul`: |xⁿ|_sp = |x|_spⁿ for n ≥ 1) with |x|_sp ≤ α_d(x). It does not change when A₀ is replaced by another ring of definition containing ϖ (equivalent norms have the same spectral seminorm), and replacing r by r' raises it to the power log r'/log r. Intrinsic properties: |x|_sp < 1 iff x is topologically nilpotent; |x|_sp ≤ 1 for every power-bounded x; the kernel {x : |x|_sp = 0} is the ideal ⋂ₙ ϖⁿ·A°° (A°° the topologically nilpotent elements), it contains the nilradical and the closure of {0}; |1|_sp = 1 if A is Hausdorff and nonzero. Functoriality: if φ : A → B is a continuous ring homomorphism of Tate rings and B carries a datum (B₀, φ(ϖ), r) with φ(A₀) ⊆ B₀ (such B₀ exists because φ is adic), then |φ(x)|_sp ≤ |x|_sp. Pinned convention: the Gel'fand-spectrum formula |x|_sp = max_{β ∈ M(A)} β(x) (Berkovich) is not part of this target; it is the input of R3.35. Lean name: `Huber.spectralSeminorm d`. *API* (on `Huber`): `spectralSeminorm, spectralSeminorm_apply, spectralSeminorm_eq_smoothingSeminorm, isPowMul_spectralSeminorm, spectralSeminorm_le_tateNorm, spectralSeminorm_eq_of_ringOfDefinition, spectralSeminorm_rpow, spectralSeminorm_lt_one_iff, spectralSeminorm_le_one_of_isPowerBounded, spectralSeminorm_eq_zero_iff, spectralSeminorm_one, spectralSeminorm_map_le, spectralSeminorm_eq_spectralNorm`. *Tests:* `spectralSeminorm_tateAlgebra` (computation): For A = K⟨T⟩ with A₀ = K°⟨T⟩, ϖ ∈ K° (0 < |ϖ| < 1) and r = |ϖ|: |Σ aₙTⁿ|_sp = sup |aₙ| (the Gauss norm). `spectralSeminorm_dualNumbers` (non-example): For A = K[ε]/(ε²) with A₀ = K° ⊕ K°ε: |ε|_sp = 0 while α_d(ε) = 1; a definition taking |·|_sp to be the norm α_d, or claiming it is always a norm, fails. `spectralSeminorm_eq_spectralNorm` (compatibility): For a finite extension L of a complete nontrivially normed nonarchimedean field K, with A₀ the unit ball of L, ϖ ∈ K and r = |ϖ|: |·|_sp equals Mathlib's `spectralNorm K L` (both are power-multiplicative K-algebra norms on L; Mathlib `spectralNorm_unique`). `spectralSeminorm_lt_one_iff_test` (characterisation): In ℚ_p⟨T⟩: |pT|_sp < 1 and pT is topologically nilpotent, while |T|_sp = 1 and T is power-bounded but not topologically nilpotent. `spectralSeminorm_zero_ring` (degenerate): On the zero ring |·|_sp = 0; on a Hausdorff nonzero A, |1|_sp = 1. *Source:* KL15 §2.1, Def. 2.1.9, p. 27. *Needs:* R0.26, R0.3.

**R0.28 The spectral topology of a Tate ring** (definition `Huber.SpectralTop`). Let A be a Tate ring. The *spectral topology* on A is the topology defined by a spectral seminorm |·|_sp (R0.27): the ring topology whose neighbourhoods of 0 are generated by the balls {x : |x|_sp < ε}, ε > 0. For every pseudouniformiser ϖ of A the sets ϖⁿ·A°° (n ≥ 0; A°° the ideal of topologically nilpotent elements) form a basis of neighbourhoods of 0 for it; in particular it does not depend on the norm datum. Write A_sp for A with this topology (Lean: the type synonym `Huber.SpectralTop A`, with the ring equivalence `toSpectralTop : A ≃+* SpectralTop A`). Then: (i) the identity A → A_sp is continuous; (ii) A_sp is a Tate ring with pseudouniformiser ϖ and pair of definition (A_sp,≤1, ϖ·A_sp,≤1), where A_sp,≤1 = {x : |x|_sp ≤ 1}; (iii) the topologically nilpotent elements of A_sp are those of A, and A_sp° = {x : |x|_sp ≤ 1} is bounded, so A_sp is uniform (Hansen–Kedlaya Definition 2.3, the anchor's Layer 4.2 predicate); (iv) every ring of integral elements A⁺ of A is a ring of integral elements of A_sp, giving a Tau Ceti `Pair` on A_sp and a morphism of Huber pairs (A, A⁺) → (A_sp, A⁺); (v) every continuous ring homomorphism φ : A → B of Tate rings is continuous as a map A_sp → B_sp; (vi) the identity A → A_sp is a homeomorphism iff A° is bounded, i.e. iff A is uniform. Lean name: `Huber.SpectralTop A`. *API* (on `Huber`): `SpectralTop, SpectralTop.hasBasis_nhds_zero, SpectralTop.nhds_zero_eq_seminorm, SpectralTop.continuous_toSpectralTop, SpectralTop.isTateRing, SpectralTop.pairOfDefinition, SpectralTop.isTopologicallyNilpotent_iff, SpectralTop.powerBoundedSubring_eq, SpectralTop.isBounded_powerBoundedSubring, SpectralTop.pair, SpectralTop.map, SpectralTop.homeomorph_of_isBounded`. *Tests:* `spectralTop_uniform_tateAlgebra` (degenerate): For A = K⟨T⟩ the identity K⟨T⟩ → SpectralTop K⟨T⟩ is a homeomorphism (K°⟨T⟩ = A° is bounded). `spectralTop_dualNumbers` (computation): For A = K[ε]/(ε²) with the topology of a finite free K-module, the closure of {0} in SpectralTop A is K·ε, so SpectralTop A is not Hausdorff while A is. `spectralTop_weightedSeries` (non-example): Let A = {Σ aₙTⁿ ∈ K⟦T⟧ : |aₙ|(n+1) → 0} with the complete submultiplicative norm ‖f‖ = sup |aₙ|(n+1). Its spectral seminorm is the Gauss norm sup |aₙ| (a norm), T ∈ (SpectralTop A)° while T ∉ A° (‖Tⁿ‖ = n + 1), and SpectralTop A is not complete; so the identity A → SpectralTop A is not a homeomorphism although both are Hausdorff, and A is not uniform. `spectralTop_isUniform` (characterisation): For every Tate ring A, (SpectralTop A)° is bounded, i.e. SpectralTop A is uniform in the sense of Hansen–Kedlaya Definition 2.3. *Source:* KL15 §2.3, Rem. 2.3.11(c), p. 36. *Needs:* R0.27, R0.26, R0.3.

- **R0.29** The spectral topology has the same continuous valuations, adic spectrum and rational subsets. *Source:* KL15 §2.8, Def. 2.8.13.

**R0.30 The uniformization of a Tate Huber pair** (construction `Huber.Pair.uniformization`). Let (A, A⁺) be a Huber pair (Tau Ceti `Pair`) with A a Tate ring. Its *uniformization* is the Huber pair (A^u, A^{u+}) where A^u is the Hausdorff completion of A_sp = `Huber.SpectralTop A` (R0.28; Tau Ceti `IsHuberRing.completion`, `IsTateRing.completion`), i.e. the separated completion of A for its spectral seminorm (Kedlaya–Liu Definition 2.8.13), and A^{u+} is the closure of the image of A⁺ in A^u. It comes with the morphism of Huber pairs ι : (A, A⁺) → (A^u, A^{u+}), the composite of the continuous identity A → A_sp and the completion map. Properties: (a) (A^u, A^{u+}) is a complete Hausdorff Huber pair, A^u is a Tate ring, and A^u is uniform (A^{u°} is bounded; the continuous extension of |·|_sp to A^u is a power-multiplicative norm defining its topology); (b) ι has dense image and kernel {x : |x|_sp = 0}; (c) for every complete Hausdorff Huber pair (B, B⁺) with B° bounded and every morphism of Huber pairs φ : (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) there is exactly one morphism φ^u : (A^u, A^{u+}) → (B, B⁺) with φ^u ∘ ι = φ, so uniformization is left adjoint to the inclusion of complete Hausdorff uniform Tate Huber pairs into Tate Huber pairs; (d) Spa(ι) : Spa(A^u, A^{u+}) → Spa(A, A⁺) is a homeomorphism under which rational subsets correspond to rational subsets; (e) if A is complete, Hausdorff and uniform, ι is an isomorphism. Pinned conventions: morphisms are Tau Ceti `Pair.Hom` (continuous, plus rings respected), which for Banach rings are the bounded morphisms (Hansen–Kedlaya Remark 2.11); the plus ring is the closure of the image of A⁺, not the image. Lean name: `Huber.Pair.uniformization`. *API* (on `Huber.Pair`): `uniformization, toUniformization, uniformization.isTateRing, uniformization.isBounded_powerBoundedSubring, uniformization.lift, uniformization.lift_comp_toUniformization, uniformization.hom_ext, uniformization.map, uniformization.denseRange_toUniformization, uniformization.plus_eq_closure, uniformization.extendSpectralSeminorm, uniformization.spaComap_homeomorph, uniformization.spaComap_preimage_rationalSubset, uniformization.isIso_of_isUniform`. *Tests:* `uniformization_dualNumbers` (computation): For K complete nonarchimedean and (A, A⁺) = (K[ε]/(ε²), K° + Kε): (A, A⁺)^u ≅ (K, K°) with ι(a + bε) = a. `uniformization_weightedSeries` (non-example): For A = {Σ aₙTⁿ ∈ K⟦T⟧ : |aₙ|(n+1) → 0} with ‖f‖ = sup |aₙ|(n+1) and any ring of integral elements A⁺: the spectral seminorm is the Gauss norm, a norm, yet A^u ≅ K⟨T⟩ ≠ A; a definition taking only the Hausdorff quotient of A for |·|_sp (no completion) gives a ring that is not complete. `uniformization_tateAlgebra` (degenerate): (K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩)^u = (K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) and ι is the identity; the uniformization of the zero pair is the zero pair. `uniformization_lift_dualNumbers` (characterisation): For (A, A⁺) = (K[ε]/(ε²), K° + Kε) and the uniform pair (K, K°), the morphism a + bε ↦ a factors uniquely through ι, and every morphism from (A, A⁺) to a complete Hausdorff pair with bounded power-bounded subring kills ε. `uniformization_banach` (compatibility): For a Banach ring A containing a topologically nilpotent unit, the ring A^u is Kedlaya–Liu's uniformization (separated completion for the spectral seminorm) and A^{u+} is their 'completion of the image of A⁺'. *Source:* KL15 §2.8, Def. 2.8.13, p. 63. *Needs:* R0.28, R0.29, R0.27, R0.3.

**R0.31 The uniform completed tensor product of Tate Huber pairs** (construction `Huber.Pair.uniformCompletedTensor`). Let (A, A⁺), (B, B⁺), (C, C⁺) be complete Hausdorff Huber pairs with A a Tate ring, and f : (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺), g : (A, A⁺) → (C, C⁺) morphisms of Huber pairs (Tau Ceti `Pair.Hom`; f and g are adic and B, C are Tate by Wedhorn Proposition 6.25). The *uniform completed tensor product* is B ⊗̂ᵘ_A C := (B ⊗̂_A C)^u, the uniformization (R0.30) of the completed tensor product (R0.21), with inlᵘ := ι ∘ inl and inrᵘ := ι ∘ inr. Properties: (a) it is a complete Hausdorff uniform Tate Huber pair and inlᵘ ∘ f = inrᵘ ∘ g; (b) it is a pushout of (f, g) in the category of complete Hausdorff uniform Tate Huber pairs: for every such (R, R⁺) and morphisms φ : (B, B⁺) → (R, R⁺), ψ : (C, C⁺) → (R, R⁺) with φ ∘ f = ψ ∘ g there is exactly one morphism χ : B ⊗̂ᵘ_A C → (R, R⁺) with χ ∘ inlᵘ = φ and χ ∘ inrᵘ = ψ; (c) the canonical morphism ι : B ⊗̂_A C → B ⊗̂ᵘ_A C induces a homeomorphism of adic spectra identifying rational subsets; (d) ι is an isomorphism iff B ⊗̂_A C is uniform (for instance K⟨T⟩ ⊗̂_K K⟨S⟩ = K⟨T, S⟩), and it is not an isomorphism in general: ℂ_p ⊗̂_{ℚ_p} ℂ_p is not uniform (Kedlaya–Liu Remark 2.8.5). No sheafiness of B ⊗̂ᵘ_A C and no fibre-product property of its Spa is claimed. Lean name: `Huber.Pair.uniformCompletedTensor f g`. *API* (on `Huber.Pair`): `uniformCompletedTensor, uniformCompletedTensor.inl, uniformCompletedTensor.inr, uniformCompletedTensor.inl_comp, uniformCompletedTensor.lift, uniformCompletedTensor.lift_comp_inl, uniformCompletedTensor.hom_ext, uniformCompletedTensor.isBounded_powerBoundedSubring, uniformCompletedTensor.fromCompletedTensor, uniformCompletedTensor.spaHomeomorph, uniformCompletedTensor.map, uniformCompletedTensor.comm, uniformCompletedTensor.unitIso`. *Tests:* `uniformCompletedTensor_polydisc` (computation): For a complete nonarchimedean field K: (K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) ⊗̂ᵘ_{(K,K°)} (K⟨S⟩, K°⟨S⟩) ≅ (K⟨T, S⟩, K°⟨T, S⟩), and ι from the completed tensor product is an isomorphism. `uniformCompletedTensor_Cp` (non-example): For A = ℚ_p and B = C = ℂ_p the canonical morphism ℂ_p ⊗̂_{ℚ_p} ℂ_p → ℂ_p ⊗̂ᵘ_{ℚ_p} ℂ_p is not an isomorphism, because ℂ_p ⊗̂_{ℚ_p} ℂ_p is not uniform; a definition that omits the uniformization is not a pushout among uniform pairs. `uniformCompletedTensor_unit` (degenerate): For g = id: B ⊗̂ᵘ_A A ≅ B^u, which is (B, B⁺) when B is uniform. `uniformCompletedTensor_galois` (characterisation): For a finite Galois extension L/K of complete nonarchimedean fields: L ⊗̂ᵘ_K L ≅ ∏_{σ ∈ Gal(L/K)} L via x ⊗ y ↦ (x·σ(y))_σ, the σ-component being lift (id_L, σ). `uniformCompletedTensor_of_isUniform` (compatibility): If B ⊗̂_A C is uniform (e.g. B, C perfectoid over a perfectoid A), the canonical morphism B ⊗̂_A C → B ⊗̂ᵘ_A C is an isomorphism of Huber pairs compatible with inl, inr. *Source:* KL15 §2.8, Rem. 2.8.5, p. 60. *Needs:* R0.21, R0.22, R0.30, R0.3.

### Completed tensor products of Banach modules

**R0.32 Completed tensor products of Banach modules over complete Tate rings** (construction `Huber.IsBanachModule`). Let A be a complete Tate ring (Tau Ceti IsTateRing, complete and Hausdorff) with a norm datum d = (A₀, ϖ, r) and norm α_d (R0.26); for instance A = L a complete nonarchimedean field with its absolute value. A Banach A-module is a complete Hausdorff topological A-module V whose topology is defined by an additive subgroup V₀ stable under A₀ with V = ⋃ₙ ϖ⁻ⁿV₀, the ϖⁿV₀ forming a basis of neighbourhoods of 0; equivalently, an A-module complete for a norm |·| with |av| ≤ c·α_d(a)·|v| (Kedlaya–Liu Definition 2.2.7), a lattice being the A₀-submodule generated by {|·| ≤ 1} (which is {|·| ≤ 1} itself when c ≤ 1). For Banach A-modules V, W the completed tensor product V ⊗̂_A W is the Hausdorff completion of V ⊗_A W for the tensor topology, in which the images of ϖⁿ·(V₀ ⊗_{A₀} W₀), n ≥ 0, form a basis of neighbourhoods of 0. Equivalently it is the completion for the product seminorm |x| = inf max_i |v_i|·|w_i| over presentations x = Σ v_i ⊗ w_i (Kedlaya–Liu Definition 2.1.10, stated there for rings). It is a Banach A-module whose lattice is the closure of the image of V₀ ⊗ W₀; up to equivalence of norms it does not depend on the lattices. (a) Universal property: (v, w) ↦ v ⊗ w is a continuous A-bilinear map whose image spans a dense submodule. Every continuous A-bilinear map β: V × W → M into a complete Hausdorff topological A-module M whose topology has a basis of neighbourhoods of 0 consisting of A₀-submodules (for instance a Banach A-module with lattice M₀, for which continuity of β is equivalent to boundedness: β(V₀ × W₀) ⊆ ϖ⁻ᵏM₀ for some k) factors uniquely as β = β̂ ∘ ⊗ with β̂: V ⊗̂_A W → M continuous and A-linear. (b) Functoriality: continuous A-linear f: V → V' and g: W → W' induce f ⊗̂ g, compatibly with identities and composition, and A ⊗̂_A V ≅ V. (c) Compatibility: if V = B and W = C are complete Huber A-algebras (adic structure maps; B₀, C₀ rings of definition containing the images of A₀), then V ⊗̂_A W is B ⊗̂_A C of R0.21 as a topological A-module, and its multiplication is the continuous extension of that of B ⊗_A C. (d) Exactness: − ⊗̂_A W carries a topologically split short exact sequence of Banach A-modules (continuous maps with a continuous retraction and a continuous section) to a topologically split short exact sequence. The field statements of Kedlaya–Liu Lemma 2.2.9 (injectivity of V ⊗_L W → V ⊗̂_L W, the cross-norm property, kernels and strictness of f ⊗̂ X) are not part of this target: they are R0.128, which is proved from (f) and the existence of t-orthogonal bases (R0.127). (e) Finite projective modules: for a finite projective A-module P with its natural topology (a finite Banach module, Kedlaya–Liu Lemma 2.2.12), P ⊗_A W → P ⊗̂_A W is an isomorphism. (f) Orthonormal bases: let A = L be a complete nonarchimedean field and let V have an orthonormal basis (e_i)_{i∈I}: every v ∈ V is uniquely Σ c_i e_i with c_i → 0 along the cofinite filter, and |v| = max |c_i|. Then for every Banach L-module E, E ⊗̂_L V ≅ c₀(I, E), the Banach module of I-indexed families in E tending to 0 with the supremum norm, via x ⊗ Σ c_i e_i ↦ (c_i x)_i, isometrically. If (e_i) is only t-orthogonal for some 0 < t ≤ 1 (t·max|c_i| ≤ |Σ c_i e_i| ≤ max|c_i|), the same map is a topological isomorphism with t·|·|_sup ≤ |·| ≤ |·|_sup. (g) Base change: for a continuous homomorphism A → A' of complete Tate rings, A' ⊗̂_A V is a Banach A'-module, and A'' ⊗̂_{A'} (A' ⊗̂_A V) ≅ A'' ⊗̂_A V for A → A' → A''. In particular M ⊗̂_A A⟨T/s⟩ is defined for a Banach A-module M and a rational localisation A → A⟨T/s⟩; it equals M ⊗_A A⟨T/s⟩ when M is finite projective (e) or finite over a strongly noetherian A (Huber 1994 Lemma 2.4(ii)). Conventions: the orthonormal description uses null families (c₀), not bounded families: E ⊗̂_L c₀(I, L) = c₀(I, E), whereas the mixed completed tensor product of R5.22 with the profinite flat module ∏_I O gives bounded families. The existence of orthonormal or t-orthogonal bases, for instance for Banach spaces of countable type (Bosch–Güntzer–Remmert 2.7.2/3, cited by Hansen–Kedlaya Remark 7.8), is a hypothesis of (f); it is proved in R0.127. *API* (on `Huber`): `IsBanachModule, BanachModule.CompletedTensor, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.tmul, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.lift, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.lift_tmul, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.ext, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.map, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.lid, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.equivCompletedTensor, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.exact_of_split, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.injective_tensorProduct_of_field, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.ker_map_of_field, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.finiteProjectiveEquiv, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.orthonormalEquiv, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.orthogonalEquiv, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.baseChange, BanachModule.CompletedTensor.cancelBaseChange`. *Tests:* `banachCompletedTensor_test_tateAlgebra` (computation): ℚ_p⟨T⟩ ⊗̂_{ℚ_p} ℚ_p⟨S⟩ ≅ ℚ_p⟨T, S⟩ as Banach ℚ_p-modules, Tᵐ ⊗ Sⁿ ↦ TᵐSⁿ. `banachCompletedTensor_test_notAlgebraic` (non-example): The map ℚ_p⟨T⟩ ⊗_{ℚ_p} ℚ_p⟨S⟩ → ℚ_p⟨T⟩ ⊗̂_{ℚ_p} ℚ_p⟨S⟩ is injective but not surjective: Σ pⁿTⁿSⁿ is not in its image. The uncompleted tensor product is the wrong object. `banachCompletedTensor_test_unit` (degenerate): A ⊗̂_A V ≅ V for every Banach A-module V, a ⊗ v ↦ av. `banachCompletedTensor_test_nullSequences` (characterisation): With the standard orthonormal basis of c₀(ℕ, ℚ_p), ℂ_p ⊗̂_{ℚ_p} c₀(ℕ, ℚ_p) ≅ c₀(ℕ, ℂ_p). The bounded sequence (1, 1, …) is not in it, whereas the mixed completed tensor product of R5 with ∏_ℕ ℤ_p contains it: a definition by bounded families fails. `banachCompletedTensor_test_finiteExtension` (compatibility): For a finite extension L/K of complete nonarchimedean fields and a Banach K-space V, L ⊗_K V → L ⊗̂_K V is an isomorphism (L is finite free over K, part (e)). *Source:* KL15 §2.1, Def. 2.1.10, p. 27. *Needs:* R0.26, R0.21.

### Adic morphisms, finiteness classes, locally noetherian spaces

**R0.33 Adic morphisms of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.Hom.IsAdic`). A morphism f : X → Y of adic spaces (the anchor's category, Layer 5) is *adic* if every x ∈ X has an open affinoid neighbourhood U and there is an open affinoid V ⊆ Y with f(U) ⊆ V such that the ring homomorphism O_Y(V) → O_X(U) induced by f is adic (R0.3). Equivalently (R0.34) f maps analytic points to analytic points, and then the ring maps O_Y(V) → O_X(U) are adic for all open affinoids with f(U) ⊆ V. Here a point is analytic if it has an open neighbourhood U whose ring O_X(U) contains a topologically nilpotent unit; on Spa(A, A⁺) this means that its support is not open (Tau Ceti `ValuationSpectrum.IsAnalyticPoint`). Every morphism into an analytic adic space is adic. Lean name: `AdicSpace.IsAdic f` (a `MorphismProperty` of the anchor's category). The predicate is on morphisms and is named `Hom.IsAdic` because Tau Ceti's `PreAdicSpace.isAdic` is the object property. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsAdic, isAdic_iff_image_analytic, IsAdic.isAdicHom_app, image_nonanalytic_subset, IsAdic.of_isAnalytic, IsAdic.comp, IsAdic.of_comp, IsAdic.of_isOpenImmersion, IsAdic.spa_iff, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.isAdic, IsAdic.isStableUnderBaseChange`. *Tests:* `isAdic_test_disc` (computation): The structure morphism of the closed unit disc Spa(ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℤ_p⟨T⟩) → Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p) is adic (ℚ_p is Tate). `not_isAdic_test_powerSeries` (non-example): Spa(ℤ_p⟦T⟧, ℤ_p⟦T⟧) → Spa(ℤ_p, ℤ_p) ((p, T)-adic over p-adic) is not adic: the point given by the T-adic valuation of 𝔽_p((T)) is analytic (support (p) not open) and maps to the non-analytic point of Spa ℤ_p with support (p). A definition 'adic = continuous on rings' fails. `isAdic_of_isAnalytic_test` (characterisation): Every morphism X → Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p) is adic, because Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p) is analytic. `isAdic_spa_iff` (compatibility): For a morphism of complete Huber pairs φ : (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) (Tau Ceti `Pair.Hom`) with sheafy pairs, Spa(φ) is adic iff the ring homomorphism of φ is adic (`Huber.IsAdicHom`). `isAdic_id_test` (degenerate): Identities and open immersions are adic. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Def. of adic morphisms, p. 532. *Needs:* R0.3.

- **R0.34** A morphism is adic exactly when it preserves analytic points. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.2(ii).

**R0.35 Morphisms locally of weakly finite type and the finite-type hierarchy** (definition `AdicSpace.LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType`). A morphism f : X → Y of adic spaces is *locally of weakly finite type* if every x ∈ X has an open affinoid neighbourhood U and there is an open affinoid V ⊆ Y with f(U) ⊆ V such that the ring homomorphism O_Y(V) → O_X(U) is topologically of finite type (Tau Ceti `IsTopologicallyFiniteType`, which is Huber 1994's and Wedhorn 6.29(i)'s notion; no condition on the plus rings); f is *of weakly finite type* if moreover f is quasi-compact. Hierarchy (Huber 1996 after 1.2.1): locally of finite presentation (R0.37) ⇒ locally of finite type ⇒ locally of +weakly finite type (R0.36) ⇒ locally of weakly finite type ⇒ adic (R0.33). Here 'locally of finite type' is the anchor's Layer 5.2 notion — the ring map (O_Y(V), O_Y⁺(V)) → (O_X(U), O_X⁺(U)) is topologically of finite type as a map of pairs, i.e. presented by a quotient mapping out of a weighted restricted power-series pair A⟨X₁,…,X_n⟩_M — which is exactly Huber 1994's Definition following Lemma 3.5; this target does not redefine it. Comparison with Tau Ceti: the ring-level condition is Tau Ceti's `IsTopologicallyFiniteType`, whose weight families satisfy `IsWeightFamily`, equivalent for Huber rings to Huber's 'M_i·A open' by Huber 1994 Lemma 1.1 = Wedhorn Lemma 6.20 (the anchor's Layer 0.4 equivalence), and whose completeness hypothesis on the target is automatic here since O_X(U) is complete. Lean names: `AdicSpace.LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType f`, `AdicSpace.IsWeaklyFiniteType f`. Morphisms locally of finite type are the anchor's §5.2; this target adds the weakly and +weakly finite type classes and their relations. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType, IsWeaklyFiniteType, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.isAdic, LocallyOfFiniteType.locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType, LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType.locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.comp, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.of_comp, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.restrict, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.of_isOpenImmersion, locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType_spa_iff, LocallyOfWeaklyFiniteType.isStableUnderBaseChange`. *Tests:* `locallyOfFiniteType_test_disc` (computation): Spa(ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℤ_p⟨T⟩) → Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p) is locally of finite type, hence locally of weakly finite type. `plusWeaklyFiniteType_not_finiteType_test` (non-example): For K = ℚ_p and A⁺ = {∑ a_n Tⁿ ∈ K⟨T⟩ : a₀ ∈ O_K, |a_n| < 1 for n ≥ 1}, the morphism Spa(K⟨T⟩, A⁺) → Spa(K, O_K) is locally of +weakly finite type (A⁺ = O_K + K⟨T⟩°°, E = ∅) but not locally of finite type (over (K, K°) the only plus ring making K → K⟨T⟩ topologically of finite type as pairs is K⟨T⟩°, Huber 1994 Lemma 4.4). A definition of lft ignoring plus rings fails. `locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType_discrete_iff` (compatibility): For discrete rings A, B, Spa(B, B) → Spa(A, A) is locally of weakly finite type iff A → B is of finite type (Mathlib `RingHom.FiniteType`; Wedhorn Example 6.32(1)), matching Mathlib's `AlgebraicGeometry.LocallyOfFiniteType` for Spec B → Spec A. `locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType_id` (degenerate): Identities and open immersions are locally of weakly finite type. `not_locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType_test` (non-example): Spa(ℤ_p⟦T⟧) → Spa(ℤ_p) is not locally of weakly finite type: it is not adic. *Source:* Huber96 §1.2, Def. 1.2.1 and the following remarks, pp. 45-47. *Needs:* R0.4, R0.33.

**R0.36 Morphisms locally of +weakly finite type** (definition `AdicSpace.LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType`). A morphism f : X → Y of adic spaces is *locally of +weakly finite type* if every x ∈ X has an open affinoid neighbourhood U and there is an open affinoid V ⊆ Y with f(U) ⊆ V such that O_Y(V) → O_X(U) is topologically of finite type (Tau Ceti `IsTopologicallyFiniteType`) and there is a finite set E ⊆ O_X(U) (necessarily contained in O_X⁺(U)) such that O_X⁺(U) is the smallest ring of integral elements of O_X(U) containing E and the image of O_Y⁺(V), i.e. the integral closure in O_X(U) of the subring generated by the image of O_Y⁺(V), E and the topologically nilpotent elements O_X(U)°° (Huber 1996 Definition 1.2.1(ii)). It is *of +weakly finite type* if moreover quasi-compact. This is the finiteness condition in Huber's definitions of proper morphisms (proper = of +weakly finite type, separated, universally closed; Huber 1996 Definition 1.3.2(ii)) and of partially proper morphisms (locally of +weakly finite type, separated, universally specializing; Definition 1.3.3(ii)). Lean names: `AdicSpace.LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType f`, `AdicSpace.IsPlusWeaklyFiniteType f`. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType, IsPlusWeaklyFiniteType, LocallyOfFiniteType.locallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType, LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType.locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType, LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType.comp, LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType.restrict, LocallyOfPlusWeaklyFiniteType.isStableUnderBaseChange, IsPlusWeaklyFiniteType.isWeaklyFiniteType`. *Tests:* `plusWeaklyFiniteType_test_disc` (computation): Spa(ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℤ_p⟨T⟩) → Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p) is of +weakly finite type with E = {T}: ℤ_p⟨T⟩ is integral over ℤ_p[T] + ℚ_p⟨T⟩°°. `plusWeaklyFiniteType_not_finiteType` (non-example): Spa(K⟨T⟩, O_K + K⟨T⟩°°) → Spa(K, O_K) is of +weakly finite type with E = ∅ but not locally of finite type (Huber 1994 Lemma 4.4). `plusWeaklyFiniteType_iff_finiteType_over_field` (characterisation): Over Spa(k, k°) for a complete nonarchimedean field k: a +weakly-finite-type affinoid Spa(A, A⁺) with A⁺ = A° is locally of finite type (Huber 1994 Lemma 4.4). `plusWeaklyFiniteType_id` (degenerate): Identities are of +weakly finite type (E = ∅). *Source:* Huber96 §1.2, Def. 1.2.1(ii), p. 46 (page image of a full copy of the book). *Needs:* R0.35.

**R0.37 Morphisms locally of finite presentation** (definition `AdicSpace.LocallyOfFinitePresentation`). A morphism f : X → Y of adic spaces is *locally of finite presentation* if every x ∈ X has an open affinoid neighbourhood U and there is an open affinoid V ⊆ Y with f(U) ⊆ V such that (O_Y(V), O_Y⁺(V)) → (O_X(U), O_X⁺(U)) is topologically of finite type as a morphism of pairs (anchor Layer 5.2) and, whenever O_Y(V) carries the discrete topology, O_X(U) is a finitely presented O_Y(V)-algebra (Mathlib `RingHom.FinitePresentation`). This is Huber 1996 Definition 1.2.1(v) verbatim; the discrete case is the only one in which the condition adds anything. Consequences: over an analytic Y, locally of finite presentation = locally of finite type; over Y whose discrete affinoid rings are noetherian rings (for instance t(𝔜) for a locally noetherian formal scheme 𝔜) the two notions agree as well; they differ over discrete non-noetherian affinoids. Warning: unlike local finite type, local finite presentation is not stable under arbitrary base change (a non-discrete base chart imposes nothing, a discrete chart after base change does); R0.44 (iv) gives the correct hypotheses. (Huber 1996 Corollary 1.2.3(i) states unconditional stability under the book's standing hypothesis (1.1.1), which excludes such examples.) Lean name: `AdicSpace.LocallyOfFinitePresentation f`. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `LocallyOfFinitePresentation, LocallyOfFinitePresentation.locallyOfFiniteType, locallyOfFinitePresentation_iff_of_isAnalytic, locallyOfFinitePresentation_iff_of_discrete_noetherian, LocallyOfFinitePresentation.comp, LocallyOfFinitePresentation.restrict, LocallyOfFinitePresentation.of_isOpenImmersion, locallyOfFinitePresentation_spa_iff, LocallyOfFinitePresentation.baseChange`. *Tests:* `locallyOfFinitePresentation_test_hypersurface` (computation): Spa(ℚ_p⟨T, S⟩/(TS − p)) → Spa(ℚ_p) is locally of finite presentation. `locallyOfFinitePresentation_discrete_iff` (compatibility): For discrete rings A → B, Spa(B, B) → Spa(A, A) is locally of finite presentation iff A → B is finitely presented (Mathlib `RingHom.FinitePresentation`), matching `AlgebraicGeometry.LocallyOfFinitePresentation` for Spec B → Spec A. `locallyOfFiniteType_not_finitePresentation_discrete` (non-example): For the discrete ring A = ℤ[T₁, T₂, …] and the ideal 𝔞 = (T₁, T₂, …), Spa(A/𝔞) → Spa(A) is locally of finite type (a quotient mapping) but not locally of finite presentation; so 'lfp = lft on locally noetherian spaces' fails for Huber's noetherian-type condition, which every discrete ring satisfies. `locallyOfFinitePresentation_iff_of_isAnalytic` (characterisation): If Y is analytic, f is locally of finite presentation iff it is locally of finite type. `locallyOfFinitePresentation_id` (degenerate): Identities and open immersions are locally of finite presentation. `locallyOfFinitePresentation_not_stable_baseChange` (non-example): For A = ℤ_p ⊕ M (M = ⊕_{i∈ℕ} 𝔽_p·e_i, M² = 0, ring of definition ℤ_p ⊕ 0 with its p-adic topology; A is outside Huber 1996's standing hypothesis (1.1.1)), Spa(ℤ_p, ℤ_p) → Spa(A, A) (from (a, m) ↦ a) is locally of finite presentation, but its base change along the adic morphism Spa(A/pA, A/pA) → Spa(A, A) is Spa(𝔽_p, 𝔽_p) → Spa(𝔽_p ⊕ M, 𝔽_p ⊕ M), which is not (the kernel M of 𝔽_p ⊕ M → 𝔽_p is not finitely generated). A definition requiring finite presentation only 'up to topology' at non-discrete charts cannot be base-change stable; an implementation claiming `IsStableUnderBaseChange` fails this test. *Source:* Huber96 §1.2, Def. 1.2.1(v), p. 46 (page image of a full copy of the book). *Needs:* R0.35, R0.34.

**R0.38 Locally noetherian adic spaces (Huber's noetherian hypothesis)** (definition `AdicSpace.IsLocallyNoetherian`). An adic space X (the anchor's category, Layer 5) is *locally noetherian* if every x ∈ X has an open affinoid neighbourhood U whose complete Hausdorff ring O_X(U) is of noetherian type (R0.1): it has a noetherian ring of definition, or it is a strongly noetherian Tate ring. This is the hypothesis of Huber 1994 Propositions 3.6–3.8 ('every point has an open affinoid neighbourhood U such that O(U) has a noetherian ring of definition or is a strongly noetherian Tate ring'). The condition places no restriction on plus rings. Conventions: a locally noetherian analytic adic space is one locally of the form Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (the setting of Zavyalov's foundational results); because every discrete ring is of noetherian type, every discretely ringed adic space is locally noetherian in this sense, and results that need honest noetherian rings (for instance on coherent sheaves) must add that hypothesis. Lean name: `AdicSpace.IsLocallyNoetherian X` (a class). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsLocallyNoetherian, IsLocallyNoetherian.exists_affinoid, IsLocallyNoetherian.spa, IsLocallyNoetherian.affinoid_basis, IsLocallyNoetherian.of_isOpenImmersion, IsLocallyNoetherian.of_locallyOfWeaklyFiniteType, IsLocallyNoetherian.of_isClosedImmersion, IsLocallyNoetherian.isStablySheafy, isLocallyNoetherian_iff_of_isAnalytic, IsLocallyNoetherian.pullback`. *Tests:* `isLocallyNoetherian_test_disc` (computation): The closed unit disc Spa(ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℤ_p⟨T⟩) is locally noetherian (ℚ_p⟨T⟩ is a strongly noetherian Tate ring). `isLocallyNoetherian_test_formal` (computation): Spa(ℤ_p⟦T⟧, ℤ_p⟦T⟧) with the (p, T)-adic topology is locally noetherian and not analytic. `not_isLocallyNoetherian_test_perfectoid` (non-example): For a perfectoid field K with pseudouniformiser ϖ = (ϖ^♭)^♯, X = Spa(K⟨T^{1/p^∞}⟩, K°⟨T^{1/p^∞}⟩) (sheafy, perfectoid) is not locally noetherian: let x₀ be the point f ↦ |f(0)|. For every open affinoid U ∋ x₀, the points y_c (T^{1/pⁿ} ↦ c^{1/pⁿ}, c = ((ϖ^♭)^m)^♯) lie in U for m ≫ 0, and in the Tate ring O_X(U) the ideals (T^{1/pⁿ}) form a strictly increasing chain: T^{1/p^{n+1}} = g·T^{1/pⁿ} would give |c|^{1/p^{n+1} − 1/pⁿ} ≤ sup_U |g| < ∞ for all such c. So O_X(U) is not noetherian, hence not of noetherian type, because a Tate ring of noetherian type is strongly noetherian, in particular noetherian. A definition 'X is covered by sheafy affinoids' would accept X. `isLocallyNoetherian_test_discrete` (degenerate): Spa(A, A) for any discrete ring A (for instance ℤ[T₁, T₂, …]) is locally noetherian, since its prime subring is a noetherian ring of definition; a definition 'locally Spa of noetherian rings' fails this test. `isLocallyNoetherian_iff_of_isAnalytic` (characterisation): An analytic adic space is locally noetherian iff it is covered by Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (analytic points have Tate rational neighbourhoods, anchor Layer 2.3; Tate + noetherian ring of definition ⇒ strongly noetherian). *Source:* Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.7, p. 535. *Needs:* R0.1, R0.5.

### Fibre products

- **R0.39** Valuations agreeing on a common subring extend jointly to the tensor product. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Lem. 3.9(i).

**R0.40 Spa of a sheafy completed tensor product is the fibre product** (theorem). Let f : (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) and g : (A, A⁺) → (C, C⁺) be morphisms of complete Hausdorff Huber pairs with adic ring homomorphisms, such that Spa A, Spa B, Spa C are adic spaces (their pairs are sheafy), and let D = B ⊗̂_A C (R0.21). If D is sheafy (the anchor's `Huber.IsSheafyForEveryPresentation`), then Spa D with the morphisms Spa(inl) : Spa D → Spa B and Spa(inr) : Spa D → Spa C is a fibre product of Spa B → Spa A ← Spa C in the category of adic spaces: for every adic space X and morphisms a : X → Spa B, b : X → Spa C with Spa(f) ∘ a = Spa(g) ∘ b there is a unique morphism X → Spa D composing to a and b. Sheafiness of D is a hypothesis, not a conclusion: it is supplied by R0.24 in the noetherian case and by other stages in other cases (perfectoid: PerfectoidSpaces P2; sousperfectoid: R5). *Hypotheses:* f, g morphisms of complete Hausdorff Huber pairs with adic ring homomorphisms. (A, A⁺), (B, B⁺), (C, C⁺) sheafy. D = B ⊗̂_A C sheafy. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Prop. 3.7, p. 536. *Needs:* R0.21, R0.22.

- **R0.41** Fibre products along a finite-type morphism over an arbitrary affinoid base change. *Source:* Huber96 §1.2, proof of Prop. 1.2.2, part 2).

**R0.42 Existence of fibre products of adic spaces** (theorem). Let f : X → S and g : Y → S be morphisms of adic spaces (the anchor's category, Layer 5). Assume that Y is covered by open affinoids Spa(B, B⁺) with B complete and stably sheafy (anchor `Huber.IsStablySheafyRing`) — for instance Y locally noetherian (R0.38, by R0.20) — and that one of the following holds: (a) f is locally of finite type (anchor Layer 5.2, which is Huber's notion); (b) f is locally of weakly finite type (R0.35) and g is adic (R0.33). Then the fibre product X ×_S Y exists in the category of adic spaces (Mathlib `CategoryTheory.Limits.HasPullback f g` for that category). Its projections p : X ×_S Y → X and q : X ×_S Y → Y satisfy: q is locally of finite type in case (a), and locally of weakly finite type (locally of finite type if f is) in case (b); p is adic whenever g is adic. X ×_S Y is covered by stably sheafy affinoids, and is locally noetherian if Y is. Local form: over affinoid S = Spa A, X = Spa C, Y = Spa B with g adic, X ×_S Y = Spa(C ⊗̂_A B) (R0.40); with g not adic, X ×_S Y is the ascending union of affinoids of R0.41 and need not be affinoid. Nothing is asserted outside these hypotheses: fibre products of adic spaces do not exist in general. *Hypotheses:* f : X → S, g : Y → S morphisms of adic spaces. Y covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (e.g. Y locally noetherian). (a) f locally of finite type, or (b) f locally of weakly finite type and g adic. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.7, p. 535. *Needs:* R0.40, R0.41, R0.23, R0.4, R0.34, R0.35, R0.33, R0.5, R0.20, R0.38, R0.21, R0.25.

- **R0.43** Points of a fibre product surject onto compatible pairs, not injectively. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Lem. 3.9(i).

- **R0.44** Stability of adicness and of the finite-type classes under base change. *Source:* Wedhorn §8.6, Rem. 8.57 (the adic, weakly-finite-type and quasi-compact clauses); Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.7, p. 535 (the locally-finite-type clause).

### Closed subspaces and the diagonal

**R0.45 Closed adic subspaces cut out by ideal sheaves; closed, open and locally closed embeddings** (definition `AdicSpace.closedSubspace`). Let X be an adic space (the anchor's category). (1) Affinoid model: for a complete Hausdorff sheafy Huber pair (A, A⁺) and a closed ideal J ⊆ A with A/J sheafy, the quotient pair A/J := (A/J, integral closure of the image of A⁺) (Tau Ceti `Pair.quotient`; Huber's (A^▷/J, (A⁺/(J ∩ A⁺))^c)) gives Spa(A/J) → Spa(A), a homeomorphism onto the closed set {v : J ⊆ supp v} (Tau Ceti `Pair.Hom.isClosedEmbedding_spaComap_quotientHom`, `range_spaComap_quotientHom`). (2) An ideal sheaf 𝓘 ⊆ O_X (a sheaf of ideals of the underlying sheaf of rings) is *admissible* if X has a cover by open affinoids U = Spa(A, A⁺) such that J_U := 𝓘(U) is a closed ideal of A, A/J_U is sheafy, and for every rational W ⊆ U the ideal 𝓘(W) is the kernel of O_X(W) → O_{Spa(A/J_U)}(W ∩ V(J_U)). The *closed adic subspace* V(𝓘) is (Z, O_Z, (v_z)) with Z = {x ∈ X : 𝓘_x ≠ O_{X,x}}, O_Z = (O_X/𝓘)|_Z topologised on each such U as the structure sheaf of Spa(A/J_U) (so O_X(U) → O_Z(U ∩ Z) is continuous and open), and v_z induced through O_{X,z} → O_{X,z}/𝓘_z; it is an adic space with V(𝓘) ∩ U ≅ Spa(A/J_U) over U, and the inclusion ι : V(𝓘) → X is a morphism of adic spaces. V(𝓘) is retained as an adic space (a ringed space with valuations), never replaced by the closed subset Z. (3) A morphism h : T → X is a *closed embedding* if it is isomorphic over X to ι : V(𝓘) → X for an admissible 𝓘; an *open embedding* if it is an isomorphism onto an open subspace; a *locally closed embedding* if it factors as a closed embedding into an open subspace U ⊆ X followed by the inclusion of U. Comparison with the anchor (Layer 5.1): closed embeddings are exactly the anchor's closed immersions (affinoid-locally Spa(A/J) → Spa(A) with J closed and A/J sheafy), with 𝓘 = ker(O_X → h_*O_T); this target adds the ideal-sheaf description, the universal property and locally closed embeddings, and does not redefine closed immersions. On a locally noetherian X every ideal of an affinoid ring of noetherian type is closed and A/J is of noetherian type, hence sheafy (R0.6, R0.20); the identification of admissible ideals with coherent ideals is R3.19. Lean names: `AdicSpace.closedSubspace 𝓘`, `AdicSpace.IsLocallyClosedImmersion`. The affinoid model is the anchor's §5.1 and Tau Ceti's `Huber.Pair.quotient` with `isClosedEmbedding_spaComap_quotientMk`; this target adds the ideal-sheaf form on an arbitrary adic space, open and locally closed embeddings. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `closedSubspace, closedSubspace.ι, closedSubspace.range_ι, closedSubspace.affinoidIso, closedSubspace.lift, closedSubspace.isClosedImmersion_ι, IsClosedImmersion.isoClosedSubspace, closedSubspace.pullback, IsLocallyClosedImmersion, IsLocallyClosedImmersion.of_isOpenImmersion, IsLocallyClosedImmersion.comp, closedSubspace.isLocallyNoetherian, closedSubspace.locallyOfFiniteType_ι`. *Tests:* `closedSubspace_test_origin` (computation): For X = Spa(ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℤ_p⟨T⟩) and 𝓘 = T·O_X, V(𝓘) ≅ Spa(ℚ_p, ℤ_p), the inclusion of the origin, with O(V(𝓘)) = ℚ_p⟨T⟩/(T) = ℚ_p. `closedSubspace_test_nonreduced` (non-example): For X as above, V(T²·O_X) and V(T·O_X) have the same underlying closed set {origin} but different rings (ℚ_p[T]/(T²) has a nonzero nilpotent); a definition by closed subsets identifies them and fails. `closedSubspace_test_not_closed_ideal` (non-example): For the Tate ring A = C(ℤ_p, ℚ_p) (supremum norm) and the non-closed ideal J of functions vanishing near 0, Spa(A/J) and Spa(A/J̄) have the same underlying space but A/J is not Hausdorff; J·O is not admissible, and only J̄ defines a closed subspace. `closedSubspace_affinoid_compat` (compatibility): For X = Spa(A, A⁺) and a closed ideal J with A/J sheafy, V(𝓘_J) ≅ Spa of Tau Ceti's `Pair.quotient A⁺ J`, and ι on points is `Pair.Hom.spaComap (Pair.quotientHom _ J)`, with image {v : J ⊆ supp v}. `closedSubspace_test_zero` (degenerate): V(0) = X and V(O_X) = ∅. *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, (1.4.1), p. 60. *Needs:* R0.6, R0.20, R0.23.

- **R0.46** The diagonal of a morphism locally of weakly finite type is a locally closed embedding. *Source:* Zavyalov-Q Appendix B.7, Lem. B.7.3 and its proof.

### Étale affinoid morphisms over a field

- **R0.49** Noether normalisation for K-affinoid algebras. *Source:* KL15 §2.5, Lem. 2.5.4.

- **R0.50** Residue fields of K-affinoid algebras at maximal ideals are finite over K. *Source:* Wedhorn §5.7 (Tate algebras), Prop. 5.61.

- **R0.51** K-affinoid algebras are Jacobson rings. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §1.1, Thm 1.1.5(2).

- **R0.52** Maximal ideals of Tate algebras are generated by polynomials. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, proof of Lem. A.1.2(2).

**R0.53 The supremum seminorm of a K-affinoid algebra** (definition `Huber.Affinoid.supNorm`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one and A a K-affinoid algebra. Max A is the set of maximal ideals of A (Mathlib MaximalSpectrum A); for x ∈ Max A with ideal m_x and f ∈ A, |f(x)| is the absolute value of f mod m_x in the finite extension A/m_x of K (R0.50), for the unique extension of |·|. The supremum seminorm of f ∈ A is |f|_sup = sup_{x ∈ Max A} |f(x)| (and |f|_sup = 0 when A = 0). It is finite: |f|_sup ≤ |f|_π for the residue norm |f|_π = inf{‖g‖ : π(g) = f} of every presentation π : K⟨X⟩ → A (‖·‖ the Gauss norm). It is a power-multiplicative K-algebra seminorm (|f + g|_sup ≤ max(|f|_sup, |g|_sup), |fg|_sup ≤ |f|_sup|g|_sup, |λf|_sup = |λ||f|_sup for λ ∈ K, |fⁿ|_sup = |f|_supⁿ), it is contracted by every K-algebra homomorphism φ : A → B of K-affinoid algebras (|φ(f)|_sup ≤ |f|_sup), it is a norm iff A is reduced, and on a Tate algebra K⟨X⟩ it is the Gauss norm. Lean name: Huber.Affinoid.supNorm : A → ℝ≥0. *API* (on `Huber.Affinoid`): `supNorm, supNorm_le_residueNorm, supNorm_add_le, supNorm_mul_le, supNorm_pow, supNorm_algebraMap, supNorm_map_le, supNorm_eq_zero_iff, supNorm_tateAlgebra, supNorm_eq_spectralSeminorm, exists_supNorm_eq, mem_powerBoundedSubring_iff_supNorm_le_one`. *Tests:* `supNorm_test_dualNumbers` (non-example): For A = K⟨T⟩/(T²), |T|_sup = 0 although T ≠ 0 and every residue norm of T is positive; a definition of |·|_sup as a residue norm fails, and |·|_sup is not a norm on a non-reduced algebra. `supNorm_test_gauss` (compatibility): On A = K⟨T⟩, |Σ a_n Tⁿ|_sup = max_n |a_n| (the Gauss norm, Mathlib MvPowerSeries.gaussNorm with all weights 1). `supNorm_test_zero` (degenerate): For A = 0, Max A = ∅ and |·|_sup = 0; for A = K, |λ|_sup = |λ| for λ ∈ K. `supNorm_test_sqrt` (computation): For A = ℚ_p⟨T⟩/(T² − p), |T|_sup = |p|^{1/2}, a value outside |ℚ_p^×|: the supremum seminorm takes values in √|K^×| ∪ {0}, not in |K|. `supNorm_test_nonrational_points` (non-example): For K = ℚ_p and f = T^p − T ∈ ℚ_p⟨T⟩: |f|_sup = ‖f‖ = 1 (attained at a point with residue field ℚ_{p²}, the unramified quadratic extension, where the reduction of T lies in 𝔽_{p²} ∖ 𝔽_p), but |f(c)| ≤ |p| for every c ∈ ℤ_p. A definition taking the supremum over K-rational points only (or over Hom_K(A, K)) fails (Conrad, Several approaches, Exercise 1.1.3(4)). *Source:* Kedlaya06 §2.1, Def. 2.1.7, p. 9. *Needs:* R0.50, R0.51, R0.124.

- **R0.54** Maximum modulus principle for K-affinoid algebras. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §1.2, Thm 1.2.6(5).

- **R0.55** Power-bounded elements of K-affinoid algebras are those of supremum seminorm at most one. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §1.2, discussion after Thm 1.2.6.

- **R0.56** On a reduced K-affinoid algebra the supremum norm defines the topology. *Source:* KL15 §2.5, Cor. 2.5.6.

- **R0.57** Homomorphisms of K-affinoid algebras are continuous. *Source:* Wedhorn §5.7 (Tate algebras), Prop. 5.60.

- **R0.58** Power-bounded elements are integral over the image of the unit ball of a Tate algebra. *Source:* Huber94 §4, proof of Lem. 4.4, p. 543 (page image read).

- **R0.59** Finite homomorphisms of K-affinoid algebras induce finite maps of reductions. *Source:* KL15 §2.5, Cor. 2.5.7.
