- **R0.60** A ring of definition with reduced reduction is the ring of power-bounded elements. *Source:* FK Ch. 0, Appendix B.1(b), Prop. B.1.3(1), p. 236 (PDF p. 254 of arXiv v5).

**R0.61 Separated morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsSeparated`). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of adic spaces (the category of the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5) that is locally of weakly finite type (R0.35), with X covered by open affinoids Spa(A, A⁺) with A complete and stably sheafy (anchor `Huber.IsStablySheafyRing`; automatic if Y is so covered, Wedhorn Remark 8.55(2), and for X locally noetherian). Such an f is adic, so the fibre product X ×_Y X exists in the category of adic spaces (R0.42, case (b) with g = f, the second factor X being covered by stably sheafy affinoids) and the diagonal Δ_f: X → X ×_Y X is the morphism with both projections equal to the identity. The morphism f is separated if the image Δ_f(|X|) is a closed subset of the underlying space |X ×_Y X| of the adic fibre product (Huber 1996, 1.3.1). Conventions: (1) X ×_Y X is the fibre product of adic spaces, never the topological fibre product |X| ×_{|Y|} |X|; the comparison map |X ×_Y X| → |X| ×_{|Y|} |X| is surjective and in general not injective (R0.43). (2) Separatedness is only defined for morphisms locally of weakly finite type whose source is covered by stably sheafy affinoids; no separatedness predicate is attached to a morphism outside that class. Huber states Definition 1.3.1 without the second condition because his standing hypothesis (1.1.1) makes every adic space locally of noetherian type, hence stable (Wedhorn Corollary 8.35); without it X ×_Y X need not exist. (3) By R0.46 the diagonal is a locally closed embedding, so a separated f has Δ_f a closed embedding (closed adic subspace in the sense of R0.45) whenever X, Y are locally strongly noetherian analytic adic spaces and f is locally of finite type (Zavyalov, Quotients, Corollary B.7.4). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsSeparated, IsSeparated.isClosed_range_diagonal, IsSeparated.iff_isClosedImmersion_diagonal, IsSeparated.of_isAffinoid, IsSeparated.comp, IsSeparated.of_comp, IsSeparated.baseChange, IsSeparated.of_isOpenImmersion, IsSeparated.isAffinoid_inter, IsSeparated.iff_valuativeCriterion`. *Tests:* `IsSeparated_test_closedDisc` (characterisation): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. The structure morphism D → Spa(K, K°) is separated: D ×_K D is the closed bidisc Spa(K⟨T₁,T₂⟩, K°⟨T₁,T₂⟩) and Δ(D) = V(T₁ − T₂) is closed. At the same time the diagonal of |D| is not closed in the product of topological spaces |D| × |D|, because |D| is not Hausdorff (the Gauss point has proper specialisations); a definition testing closedness in |X| ×_{|Y|} |X| would wrongly declare D non-separated. `IsSeparated_test_doubledOrigin` (non-example): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. Let 0 ∈ D be the point V(T) (a closed point) and let X be obtained by gluing two copies D₁, D₂ of D along the open subspace D ∖ {0} (AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.3). Then X → Spa(K, K°) is locally of finite type but not separated: the point (0₁, 0₂) of the open subspace D₁ ×_K D₂ of X ×_K X lies in the closure of Δ(D ∖ {0}) but not in Δ(X). `IsSeparated_test_id` (degenerate): For every adic space X the identity of X is separated, and every open embedding U → X is separated (its diagonal is an isomorphism). `IsSeparated_test_diagonal_closedImmersion` (compatibility): For f locally of topologically finite type between locally strongly noetherian analytic adic spaces, AdicSpace.IsSeparated f holds if and only if Δ_f is a closed embedding in the sense of R0.45; this is the shape of Mathlib's AlgebraicGeometry.IsSeparated (diagonal a closed immersion) for schemes. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Def. 1.3.1, p. 51. *Needs:* R0.35, R0.33, R0.42, R0.43, R0.46, R0.45.

**R0.62 Universally closed morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.UniversallyClosed`). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of adic spaces that is locally of weakly finite type. The morphism f is universally closed if, for every adic morphism g: Y' → Y and every pullback square of f along g in the category of adic spaces, with base change f': X' → Y', the continuous map |f'|: |X'| → |Y'| is a closed map (Huber 1996, 1.3.2(i)). Such pullbacks exist whenever Y' is covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (R0.42, case (b)), in particular always under Huber's standing hypothesis (1.1.1); the pullback along g = id_Y always exists (X' = X), so a universally closed f has |f| closed. The condition is independent of the choice of pullback (pullbacks are unique up to unique isomorphism), so quantifying over pullback squares (the shape of Mathlib's MorphismProperty.universally) is the same as quantifying over the base changes X ×_Y Y' that exist. Conventions: base change is only taken along adic morphisms, which is the class along which Huber's fibre product (b) exists; over an analytic Y every morphism is adic (R0.35), so there the condition is 'closed after every base change'. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `UniversallyClosed, UniversallyClosed.isClosedMap, UniversallyClosed.baseChange, UniversallyClosed.comp, UniversallyClosed.universallySpecializing, UniversallyClosed.of_universallySpecializing_of_quasiCompact, UniversallyClosed.of_isClosedImmersion`. *Tests:* `UniversallyClosed_test_id` (degenerate): The identity of any adic space, and every closed embedding, is universally closed. `UniversallyClosed_test_closedDisc` (non-example): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. The morphism D → Spa(K, K°) is quasi-compact, separated and of +weakly finite type but not universally closed: it is not universally specializing, since for the Gauss point x ∈ D and the valuation ring A ⊊ k(x)⁺ of k(x) that is the preimage of the valuation ring of the residue field k(T) at T = ∞, the pair (x, A) has a centre on Spa(K, K°) but none on D (T ∈ K°⟨T⟩ would have to lie in A). A definition requiring only that |f| itself be closed would accept D, since |D| → |Spa(K, K°)| (a point) is trivially closed. `UniversallyClosed_test_specializing` (compatibility): If f is universally closed then every base change of |f| along an adic morphism is a specializing map in the sense of Mathlib's SpecializingMap; this mirrors Mathlib's universallyClosed_eq_universallySpecializing for schemes, whose other half (quasi-compact plus universally specializing implies universally closed) is R0.70 here. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Def. 1.3.2(i), p. 51. *Needs:* R0.35, R0.33, R0.42.

**R0.63 Proper morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsProper`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is proper if it is of +weakly finite type (quasi-compact and locally of +weakly finite type, R0.35), separated (R0.61) and universally closed (R0.62) (Huber 1996, 1.3.2(ii)). Conventions: (1) the finiteness condition is '+weakly finite type', which includes quasi-compactness and the plus-ring condition O_X⁺(U) = integral closure of O_Y⁺(V)[E ∪ O_X(U)°°] for a finite E; it is neither 'locally of finite type' (Mathlib's IsProper for schemes) nor 'weakly finite type'. (2) Properness of rigid analytic varieties in Kiehl's sense (Conrad AWS, Definition 3.2.3) is a different definition; the comparison is R1.35. (3) Because separatedness (R0.61) needs X ×_Y X to exist, the predicate is stated for X covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (automatic under Huber's standing hypothesis (1.1.1), where every adic space is locally of noetherian type). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsProper, IsProper.toIsSeparated, IsProper.toUniversallyClosed, IsProper.quasiCompact, IsProper.comp, IsProper.baseChange, IsProper.iff_isPartiallyProper_and_quasiCompact, IsProper.iff_valuativeCriterion, IsFinite.isProper, IsProper.isFinite_of_locallyQuasiFinite, IsProper.isClosedMap`. *Tests:* `IsProper_test_closedDisc` (non-example): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. D → Spa(K, K°) is quasi-compact, separated, of +weakly finite type, and |D| is quasi-compact; nevertheless it is not proper (R0.62, test UniversallyClosed_test_closedDisc). A definition 'quasi-compact, separated, with closed underlying map' would wrongly make it proper. `IsProper_test_projectiveLine` (computation): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. Let P¹ be glued from D₀ = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) and D_∞ = Spa(K⟨S⟩, K°⟨S⟩) along {|T| = 1} ≅ {|S| = 1}, S = T⁻¹ (anchor gluing, Layer 5.3). Then P¹ → Spa(K, K°) is proper: every pair (x, A) of R0.66 has exactly one centre, lying in D₀ if T ∈ A and in D_∞ otherwise. `IsProper_test_openDisc` (non-example): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. The open unit disc B → Spa(K, K°) is partially proper but not quasi-compact, hence not proper. `IsProper_test_finite` (degenerate): The identity, every closed embedding, and Spa(L, L°) → Spa(K, K°) for a finite extension L/K are proper. `IsProper_test_mathlib_shape` (compatibility): IsProper f ↔ IsPartiallyProper f ∧ QuasiCompact f (R0.70), mirroring Mathlib's AlgebraicGeometry.IsProper.eq_valuativeCriterion and universallyClosed_eq_universallySpecializing for schemes. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Def. 1.3.2(ii), p. 51. *Needs:* R0.35, R0.61, R0.62.

**R0.64 Universally specializing morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.SpecializingAt`). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of adic spaces. For x ∈ X, f is specializing at x if for every specialization y' of f(x) (that is, y' ∈ closure{f(x)} in |Y|) there is a specialization x' of x with f(x') = y'. If f is locally of weakly finite type, f is universally specializing at x₀ ∈ X if for every adic morphism g: Y' → Y, every pullback square of f along g in the category of adic spaces with base change f': X' → Y' and projection p: X' → X, and every point x₀' ∈ X' with p(x₀') = x₀, the base change f' is specializing at x₀'; f is universally specializing if it is so at every point (Huber 1996, Definition 1.3.3(i)). Such pullbacks exist whenever Y' is covered by complete stably sheafy affinoids (R0.42 (b)), in particular always under Huber's standing hypothesis (1.1.1). Convention: 'f specializing at every point' is exactly Mathlib's SpecializingMap for the continuous map |f|. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `SpecializingAt, UniversallySpecializingAt, UniversallySpecializing, specializingAt_forall_iff, UniversallySpecializing.baseChange, UniversallySpecializing.comp, UniversallyClosed.universallySpecializing, UniversallySpecializingAt.iff_valuativeCriterion`. *Tests:* `UniversallySpecializing_test_closedEmbedding` (degenerate): Identities and closed embeddings are universally specializing (their base changes are closed maps, hence specializing). `UniversallySpecializing_test_discInP1` (non-example): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. The open embedding D₀ → P¹ (notation of IsProper_test_projectiveLine) is not specializing at the Gauss point x: the type-5 point of D_∞ at the Gauss point in the direction |T| > 1 is a specialization of x in P¹ with no preimage specializing x in D₀. Being an open embedding therefore does not imply being universally specializing. `UniversallySpecializing_test_specializingMap` (compatibility): f is specializing at every point iff Mathlib's SpecializingMap holds for the continuous map |f| : |X| → |Y|. `UniversallySpecializing_test_openDisc` (computation): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. The open unit disc B → Spa(K, K°) is universally specializing (R0.69: every (x, A) with x ∈ B has its centre in B, since |T(x)| < 1). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Def. 1.3.3(i), p. 51. *Needs:* R0.35, R0.42.

**R0.65 Partially proper morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsPartiallyProper`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is partially proper if it is locally of +weakly finite type (R0.35), separated (R0.61) and universally specializing (R0.64) (Huber 1996, 1.3.3). Conventions: partial properness is a relative notion for morphisms; an open subspace U ⊆ X is partially proper over X only in special cases (the inclusion of the open unit disc into the closed disc is, the inclusion of the closed disc into P¹ is not). The Kedlaya-Liu notion of a partially proper open subset (inverse image of an open subset of the real quotient) is a different definition, compared only through its uses. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsPartiallyProper, IsPartiallyProper.toIsSeparated, IsPartiallyProper.universallySpecializing, IsPartiallyProper.comp, IsPartiallyProper.baseChange, IsProper.isPartiallyProper, IsPartiallyProper.isProper_of_quasiCompact, IsPartiallyProper.iff_valuativeCriterion, IsPartiallyProper.isCompact_closure`. *Tests:* `IsPartiallyProper_test_openDisc` (computation): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. B → Spa(K, K°) is partially proper and not quasi-compact. `IsPartiallyProper_test_closedDisc` (non-example): Throughout the tests K is a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc and B = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ D the open unit disc (ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser), as in the AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5.4. D → Spa(K, K°) is not partially proper (the pair (x, A) at the Gauss point in the direction T = ∞ has no centre on D). `IsPartiallyProper_test_id` (degenerate): Identities and closed embeddings are partially proper (they are proper, R0.47); the open embedding of the closed unit disc D into the projective line P¹ over Spa(K, K°) is not partially proper (the type-5 point of D's Gauss point in the direction T = ∞ lies in P¹ \ D). `IsPartiallyProper_test_proper` (compatibility): IsProper f ↔ IsPartiallyProper f ∧ QuasiCompact f; in particular every proper morphism of R0.63 is partially proper. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Def. 1.3.3(ii), p. 51. *Needs:* R0.35, R0.61, R0.64.

**R0.66 Valuation rings of an analytic adic space and their centres** (definition `AdicSpace.ValuationRing`). Let X be an analytic adic space. For x ∈ X let k(x) be the residue field of the local ring O_{X,x}, k(x)⁺ ⊆ k(x) the valuation ring of the valuation v_x, and κ(x) = (k(x)^∧, k(x)⁺^∧) the completed residue affinoid field with its canonical morphism ι_x: Spa κ(x) → X (AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 5; see requests). A valuation ring of X is a pair (x, A) with x ∈ X and A a valuation ring of the field k(x) such that A ⊆ k(x)⁺; X_v denotes the set of valuation rings of X (Huber 1996, 1.3.5). Since A ⊆ k(x)⁺ are valuation rings of the same field, A contains the maximal ideal of k(x)⁺, so A is open in k(x) and its closure Â in k(x)^∧ is a valuation ring making (k(x)^∧, Â) an affinoid field (Hübner, §2) with a canonical morphism j: Spa κ(x) → Spa(k(x)^∧, Â). For a morphism g: Spa κ(x) → Z to an adic space Z, a point z ∈ Z is a centre of (x, A) on Z with respect to g if there is a morphism c: Spa(k(x)^∧, Â) → Z with c ∘ j = g and c(s) = z, s the closed point of Spa(k(x)^∧, Â). A centre of (x, A) on X means a centre with respect to ι_x; for a morphism f: X → Y, a centre of (x, A) on Y means a centre with respect to f ∘ ι_x. Topology: X_v carries the topology generated by the sets D(U, h) = {(x, A) ∈ X_v : x ∈ U, h(x) ∈ A} for U ⊆ X open and h ∈ O_X(U), h(x) the image of h in k(x). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `ValuationRing, ValuationRing.affinoidField, ValuationRing.IsCentre, ValuationRing.IsCentre.specializes, ValuationRing.isCentre_iff_of_isAffinoid, ValuationRing.map, ValuationRing.self_isCentre, ValuationRing.instTopologicalSpace, ValuationRing.continuous_fst, ValuationRing.isCentre_iff_specializes, ValuationRing.existsUnique_of_specializes`. *Tests:* `ValuationRing_test_trivial` (degenerate): For every x ∈ X, (x, k(x)⁺) ∈ X_v and x is its unique centre on X (take c = ι_x, since then Â = k(x)⁺^∧ and j is the identity). `ValuationRing_test_gaussPoint` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, k its residue field, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the closed unit disc, x_G ∈ D the Gauss point (|Σ aₙTⁿ| = max |aₙ|), whose residue field k(x_G)⁺/𝔪 is k(T). For a ∈ k let A_a ⊆ k(x_G)⁺ be the preimage of the valuation ring of k(T) at T = a, and A_∞ the preimage of the valuation ring at T = ∞. Then (x_G, A_a) has exactly one centre on D, the type-5 point of direction a, and (x_G, A_∞) has no centre on D because T ∈ K°⟨T⟩ = O_D⁺(D) has image outside A_∞; both have a unique centre on Spa(K, K°). `ValuationRing_test_notGeneralisation` (non-example): If x has rank 2, with k(x)⁺ ⊊ k(x)°, then (x, k(x)°) ∉ X_v: valuation rings larger than k(x)⁺ describe generalisations, not specialisations, and are excluded; a definition allowing all valuation rings of k(x) would make every x a centre of pairs lying over its generalisation. `ValuationRing_test_affinoidCentre` (characterisation): For X = Spa(B, B⁺) affinoid and (x, A) ∈ X_v, (x, A) has a centre on X if and only if the image of B⁺ in k(x) lies in A, and the centre is then unique (Huber 1.3.6). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Def. 1.3.5, p. 52.

- **R0.67** Valuative criterion for separatedness (Huber 1.3.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Prop. 1.3.7.

- **R0.68** Valuative criterion for universal specialization (Huber 1.3.8). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Prop. 1.3.8.

**R0.69 Valuative criterion for partial properness and properness of analytic adic spaces (Huber 1.3.9)** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of analytic adic spaces which is quasi-separated and locally of +weakly finite type, with X covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (for instance X locally noetherian; needed for X ×_Y X, as in R0.67). Then f is partially proper (R0.65) if and only if for every (x, A) ∈ X_v and every centre y of (x, A) on Y there is exactly one centre z of (x, A) on X with f(z) = y. If moreover f is of +weakly finite type (in particular quasi-compact), the same condition is equivalent to f being proper (R0.63). Huber's Lemma 1.3.10 rephrases the condition as the unique extension of morphisms along Spa(K, K⁺) → Spa(K, K'⁺) for affinoid fields sharing the field K; the X_v form stated here is the declaration. *Hypotheses:* X, Y analytic adic spaces (the source proves the criterion only in this case). f quasi-separated and locally of +weakly finite type; for the proper version f of +weakly finite type. X covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (e.g. locally noetherian), the hypothesis under which this roadmap constructs X ×_Y X. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Cor. 1.3.9, pp. 54-55. *Needs:* R0.67, R0.68, R0.65, R0.63, R0.70, R0.66.

- **R0.70** Proper equals partially proper and quasi-compact (Huber 1.3.4). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Lem. 1.3.4 and proof.

- **R0.71** The space of valuation rings of a qcqs analytic adic space is spectral (Huber 1.3.12). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, (1.3.11) and Lem. 1.3.12.

- **R0.72** Closures of quasi-compact sets under partially proper morphisms (Huber 1.3.13). *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Lem. 1.3.13 and proof.

- **R0.73** The diagonal of a separated morphism is a closed embedding. *Source:* Zavyalov-Q Appendix B.6, Lem. B.6.14.

- **R0.74** Universally closed, separated and proper morphisms are stable under adic base change. *Source:* Stacks Tag 01W4 (Morphisms, Lem. 29.42.5).

- **R0.75** Composites of universally closed, separated and proper morphisms. *Source:* Stacks Tag 01W3 (Morphisms, Lem. 29.42.4).

- **R0.76** Cancellation: g ∘ h proper and g separated imply h proper. *Source:* Stacks Tag 01W6 (Morphisms, Lem. 29.42.7).

- **R0.77** Universal closedness descends along surjections. *Source:* Stacks Tag 03GN (Morphisms, Lem. 29.42.9).

**R0.78 Finite morphism of complete Huber pairs** (definition `Huber.Pair.Hom.IsFinite`). Let (A, A⁺) and (B, B⁺) be complete Huber pairs (Tau Ceti Huber.Pair on complete Hausdorff Huber rings A, B) and φ: (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) a morphism of Huber pairs (tauceti Huber.Pair.Hom: continuous, φ(A⁺) ⊆ B⁺). The morphism φ is finite if it is of topologically finite type as a morphism of affinoid rings (Huber 1994, §3: φ factors through a quotient mapping A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩_M → B, i.e. a surjective continuous open ring map with B⁺ the integral closure of the image of the plus ring) and the ring maps A → B and A⁺ → B⁺ are integral (Huber 1996, 1.4.2). Consequences: B⁺ is the integral closure of φ(A⁺) in B (always); B is a finite A-module carrying the natural A-module topology when A is Tate (Zavyalov, Quotients, B.2.9 and B.3.4). Huber (1.4.2) states these consequences under his standing noetherian assumption (1.1.1), referring to [Hu4, 3.12.10] (unread); outside the Tate case they are not claimed here. For complete Tate pairs, φ is finite if and only if A → B is module-finite (Mathlib RingHom.Finite) and A⁺ → B⁺ is integral. *API* (on `Huber.Pair.Hom`): `IsFinite, IsFinite.finite, IsFinite.isIntegral_plus, IsFinite.plus_eq_integralClosure, IsFinite.isModuleTopology, isFinite_iff_finite_and_isIntegral, IsFinite.isTopologicallyFiniteType, IsFinite.id, IsFinite.comp, IsFinite.quotientHom, IsFinite.isIso_of_bijective`. *Tests:* `Pair.Hom.IsFinite_test_ramified` (computation): For p prime, (Q_p, Z_p) → (Q_p(√p), Z_p[√p]) is finite: Q_p(√p) = Q_p ⊕ Q_p√p and √p is integral over Z_p. `Pair.Hom.IsFinite_test_plusNotIntegral` (non-example): Let K be a complete rank-one nonarchimedean field whose residue field k carries a nontrivial valuation ring R ⊊ k, and K⁺ ⊊ K° the preimage of R. Then id: (K, K⁺) → (K, K°) is not finite: K → K is finite but K° is not integral over the integrally closed K⁺ ≠ K°. A definition that tests only the underlying ring map would accept it; the corresponding Spa(K, K°) → Spa(K, K⁺) is an open, non-closed embedding. `Pair.Hom.IsFinite_test_tateAlgebra` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra with plus ring K°⟨T⟩. The structure map (K, K°) → (K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) is of topologically finite type but not finite (T is not integral over K). `Pair.Hom.IsFinite_test_quotient` (degenerate): For a closed ideal J of a complete Huber ring A, the quotient morphism S → S.quotient J (tauceti Huber.Pair.quotientHom) is finite; the identity of every complete Huber pair is finite. `Pair.Hom.IsFinite_test_mathlib` (compatibility): For complete Tate pairs: IsFinite φ ↔ φ.toRingHom.Finite ∧ Algebra.IsIntegral A⁺ B⁺ (Zavyalov B.2.8-B.2.9). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, (1.4.2), p. 61. *Needs:* R0.35.

**R0.79 Finite morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsFinite`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is finite if every y ∈ Y has an open affinoid neighbourhood V ⊆ Y such that f⁻¹(V) is affinoid and the induced morphism of complete Huber pairs (O_Y(V), O_Y⁺(V)) → (O_X(f⁻¹V), O_X⁺(f⁻¹V)) is finite (R0.78) (Huber 1996, 1.4.4). Huber states that then f⁻¹(V) is affinoid and finite over V for every open affinoid V ⊆ Y ([Hu4, 3.6.20, 3.12.12]); in this library that global form is proved for V = Spa(A, A⁺) with A a strongly noetherian complete Tate ring (Zavyalov, Quotients, Theorem B.2.18) and is recorded as a gap in general. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsFinite, IsFinite.of_spa, IsFinite.isAffinoid_preimage, IsFinite.quasiCompact, IsFinite.comp, IsFinite.baseChange, IsFinite.of_isClosedImmersion, IsFinite.isProper, IsFinite.iff_isProper_and_quasiFinite, IsFinite.locallyQuasiFinite, IsFinite.of_faithfullyFlat_baseChange`. *Tests:* `IsFinite_test_squaring` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra with plus ring K°⟨T⟩. Assume 2 ∈ K^×. Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) → Spa(K⟨S⟩, K°⟨S⟩), S ↦ T², is finite: K⟨T⟩ = K⟨S⟩ ⊕ K⟨S⟩·T is free of rank 2 and K°⟨T⟩ is integral over K°⟨S⟩. `IsFinite_test_rationalEmbedding` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra with plus ring K°⟨T⟩. The rational embedding {|T| ≤ |ϖ|} ⊆ Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) is an open embedding, quasi-finite, with affinoid source, but not finite (its image is not closed; K⟨T, T/ϖ⟩ is not a finite K⟨T⟩-module). `IsFinite_test_genericPoint` (non-example): For K⁺ ⊊ K° as in Pair.Hom.IsFinite_test_plusNotIntegral, Spa(K, K°) → Spa(K, K⁺) is not finite: it is an open embedding onto the generic point (not closed), although both sides have the same ring K. `IsFinite_test_closedImmersion` (degenerate): Identities and closed embeddings (R0.83) are finite. `IsFinite_test_affinoid` (compatibility): For complete strongly noetherian Tate pairs, Spa φ: Spa(B, B⁺) → Spa(A, A⁺) is finite iff φ is finite in the sense of R0.78; and a morphism of rigid spaces is finite in Conrad's sense (AWS Definition 3.1.6) iff its adic counterpart is (Huber 1.4.5(iv), via R1.31). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, (1.4.4), p. 61. *Needs:* R0.78.

**R0.80 The Huber pair of a finite algebra over a noetherian affinoid ring** (construction `Huber.Pair.finiteAlgebra`). Let (A, A⁺) be a complete Huber pair such that A is a complete noetherian Tate ring or has a noetherian ring of definition (for instance A strongly noetherian Tate, or an affinoid algebra over a complete nonarchimedean field). Let ψ: A → C be a finite A-algebra (C commutative and finitely generated as an A-module). Equip C with the natural A-module topology (the finest topology making C a topological A-module; Mathlib moduleTopology A C; Huber 1994, p. 524) and let C⁺ be the integral closure of ψ(A⁺) in C. Then: (i) C is a complete Hausdorff Huber ring, Tate if A is, every A-submodule of C is closed, and ψ is continuous; (ii) C⁺ is a ring of integral elements, so Huber.Pair.finiteAlgebra (C, C⁺) is a complete Huber pair; (iii) ψ is a finite morphism of Huber pairs (R0.78); (iv) universal property: for every complete Huber pair (D, D⁺) with a morphism χ: (A, A⁺) → (D, D⁺), morphisms of Huber pairs (C, C⁺) → (D, D⁺) under (A, A⁺) are exactly the A-algebra maps C → D; (v) if A is strongly noetherian Tate, so is C; (vi) Spa(C, C⁺) → Spa(A, A⁺) is a finite morphism of adic spaces, and for A strongly noetherian Tate every finite morphism X → Spa(A, A⁺) is isomorphic over Spa(A, A⁺) to one of this form, with C = O_X(X). Convention: the noetherian hypothesis is part of the construction; for a non-noetherian complete Tate ring A a finite A-algebra C = A/J with J not closed has a non-Hausdorff natural topology, so no complete Huber pair structure exists on C itself. In the Tate case the module topology, completeness and closedness of submodules of clause (i) are Tau Ceti's `IsTateRing.isModuleTopology`, `completeSpace_moduleTopology` and `isClosed_of_isNoetherian`, which this target consumes; the new content is the pair (C, C⁺) and the noetherian-ring-of-definition case. *API* (on `Huber.Pair`): `finiteAlgebra, finiteAlgebra_plus, finiteAlgebra.isModuleTopology, finiteAlgebra.completeSpace, finiteAlgebra.isClosed_submodule, finiteAlgebra.isTateRing, finiteAlgebra.isStronglyNoetherian, finiteAlgebra.algebraMap_isFinite, finiteAlgebra.homEquiv, finiteAlgebra.map, finiteAlgebra_quotient, AdicSpace.IsFinite.exists_finiteAlgebra`. *Tests:* `finiteAlgebra_test_self` (degenerate): For C = A (ψ = id), Huber.Pair.finiteAlgebra returns (A, A⁺): the natural topology of A over itself is its own topology and A⁺ is integrally closed in A. `finiteAlgebra_test_squareRoot` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra with plus ring K°⟨T⟩. Assume 2 ∈ K^×. For A = K⟨T⟩ and C = A[U]/(U² − T), the pair (C, C⁺) is isomorphic to (K⟨U⟩, K°⟨U⟩) with T ↦ U². `finiteAlgebra_test_nonClosedIdeal` (non-example): Let A = K⟨X₁, X₂, …⟩ be the complete Tate ring of restricted power series in countably many variables (coefficients tending to zero along the cofinite filter) and J the algebraic ideal generated by the X_i. Then Σₙ ϖⁿXₙ lies in the closure of J but not in J, so the natural topology of the finite A-algebra A/J is not Hausdorff: the construction needs the noetherian hypothesis. `finiteAlgebra_test_quotient` (compatibility): For A noetherian and J ⊆ A an ideal (automatically closed), Huber.Pair.finiteAlgebra (A ⧸ J) agrees with tauceti Huber.Pair.quotient: same topology (quotient = natural topology) and same plus ring (integral closure of the image of A⁺). `finiteAlgebra_test_homEquiv` (characterisation): For (D, D⁺) a complete Huber pair over (A, A⁺), Hom_{(A,A⁺)}((C, C⁺), (D, D⁺)) ≃ (C →ₐ[A] D). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, (1.4.2), p. 61. *Needs:* R0.78, R0.79, R0.10, R0.5.

- **R0.81** Finiteness from monic relations with topologically nilpotent values (Huber 1.4.3). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, Lem. 1.4.3.

**R0.82 Finite morphisms are proper (Huber 1.4.5(ii))** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a finite morphism of adic spaces (R0.79) with Y analytic and covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (for instance Y locally noetherian; then so is X, its affinoid rings being topologically of finite type over those of Y). Then f is proper (R0.63). (Huber states 1.4.5(ii) for arbitrary adic spaces, deducing the specializing property from Huber–Knebusch [HK, 2.1.2, 2.1.6]; that general case is recorded as a gap.) *Hypotheses:* Y analytic (the proof via the valuative criterion 1.3.9 needs analytic spaces; X is then analytic). Y covered by open affinoids with complete stably sheafy rings (needed for X ×_Y X and the valuative criterion as stated in R0.69). f finite. *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, (1.4.4) and Lem. 1.4.5(ii) with proof, pp. 61-62. *Needs:* R0.79, R0.78, R0.69, R0.66, R0.63, R0.35.

- **R0.83** Closed embeddings are finite (Huber 1.4.5(iii)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, (1.4.1) and Lem. 1.4.5(iii).

- **R0.84** Topological nilpotence in a fibre spreads to a rational neighbourhood (Huber 1.4.8). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, Lem. 1.4.8.

- **R0.85** Finiteness near a point with proper fibre (Huber 1.4.6-1.4.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, Prop. 1.4.6 and Lem. 1.4.7.

- **R0.86** Finiteness descends along finite faithfully flat affinoid covers (Huber 1.4.9). *Source:* Huber96 §1.4, Lem. 1.4.9.

- **R0.87** Base change of a finite algebra over a complete Tate ring needs no completion. *Source:* Zavyalov-Q Appendix B.3, Lem. B.3.5.

**R0.88 Locally quasi-finite and quasi-finite morphisms of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.LocallyQuasiFinite`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is locally quasi-finite if it is locally of weakly finite type (R0.35) and for every y ∈ Y the fibre f⁻¹(y) is a discrete subspace of |X|; it is quasi-finite if moreover it is quasi-compact (Huber 1996, 1.5.1). Conventions: discreteness is of the fibre with the subspace topology of |X| (not of the fibre adic space X ×_Y Spa κ(y)); for f of weakly finite type between analytic adic spaces quasi-finite is equivalent to finiteness of all fibres (R0.89). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `LocallyQuasiFinite, QuasiFinite, LocallyQuasiFinite.isDiscrete_preimage_singleton, QuasiFinite.finite_preimage_singleton, LocallyQuasiFinite.comp, LocallyQuasiFinite.baseChange, IsFinite.quasiFinite, LocallyQuasiFinite.of_isOpenImmersion, LocallyQuasiFinite.iff_finite_stalk, IsFinite.iff_isProper_and_quasiFinite, Unramified.locallyQuasiFinite`. *Tests:* `LocallyQuasiFinite_test_squaring` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, 2 ∈ K^×. Spa(K⟨T⟩) → Spa(K⟨S⟩), S ↦ T², is quasi-finite: the fibre over the Gauss point is the Gauss point, and over the classical point S = a it consists of the points T = ±√a (one point if a = 0, and a single point with residue field K(√a) if a is not a square in K). `LocallyQuasiFinite_test_projection` (non-example): The projection of the closed bidisc Spa(K⟨T₁,T₂⟩) → Spa(K⟨T₁⟩) is not locally quasi-finite: its fibres are discs, which are not discrete. `LocallyQuasiFinite_test_infiniteDisjointUnion` (non-example): The disjoint union of countably many copies of Spa(K, K°), mapped to Spa(K, K°), is locally quasi-finite (fibres discrete) but not quasi-finite (not quasi-compact; the fibre is infinite): 'finite fibres' is the wrong definition of local quasi-finiteness. `LocallyQuasiFinite_test_openImmersion` (degenerate): Open embeddings and identities are locally quasi-finite; affinoid open embeddings are quasi-finite. `LocallyQuasiFinite_test_finiteAlgebra` (compatibility): For a finite morphism of complete Tate pairs φ: (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺), Spa φ is quasi-finite and φ is quasi-finite in Mathlib's sense (Algebra.QuasiFinite, which a module-finite algebra satisfies); for schemes Mathlib's AlgebraicGeometry.LocallyQuasiFinite is likewise equivalent to discrete fibres for morphisms locally of finite type. *Source:* Huber96 §1.5, Def. 1.5.1, p. 66. *Needs:* R0.35.

- **R0.89** Fibre criteria for quasi-finiteness (Huber 1.5.2). *Source:* Huber96 §1.5, Lem. 1.5.2.

- **R0.90** Stalk criterion for local quasi-finiteness (Huber 1.5.4). *Source:* Huber96 §1.5, Prop. 1.5.4.

**R0.91 Quasi-finite and proper equals finite (Huber 1.5.5)** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism locally of finite type between analytic adic spaces, with Y locally noetherian (R0.38; for analytic Y: locally Spa of strongly noetherian Tate rings, which is Huber's standing assumption (1.1.1) for analytic spaces). Then f is finite (R0.79) if and only if f is quasi-finite (R0.88) and proper (R0.63) (Huber 1996, Proposition 1.5.5). *Hypotheses:* X, Y analytic adic spaces; Y locally noetherian (needed by R0.86 and the valuative criterion). f locally of finite type. properness in the sense of Huber 1.3.2 (+weakly finite type, separated, universally closed). *Source:* Huber96 §1.5, Prop. 1.5.5 and proof, pp. 68-70. *Needs:* R0.88, R0.79, R0.82, R0.63, R0.89, R0.85, R0.86, R0.66, R0.67, R0.43, R0.44, R0.38.

- **R0.92** Partially proper locally quasi-finite morphisms are finite near closures of points (Huber 1.5.6). *Source:* Huber96 §1.5, Prop. 1.5.6.

**R0.93 Module of continuous differentials of a topologically finite type map** (construction `Huber.ContinuousKaehlerDifferential`). Let φ: A → B be a continuous homomorphism of complete Huber rings (complete Hausdorff f-adic rings) which is topologically of finite type (tauceti TauCeti.Huber.IsTopologicallyFiniteType), with A of noetherian type (R0.1: a noetherian ring of definition, or strongly noetherian Tate — Huber's standing assumption (1.1.1)); then B and B ⊗̂_A B are of noetherian type (R0.5, R0.24), their ideals are closed and finite modules with the natural topology are complete and Hausdorff. Let m: B ⊗̂_A B → B be the continuous extension of b ⊗ b' ↦ bb' to the completed tensor product (R0.21) and I = ker m. The module of continuous differentials is Ωᶜ_{B/A} := I/I², a B-module via (B ⊗̂_A B)/I ≅ B, with the natural B-module topology, and d = d_{B/A}: B → Ωᶜ_{B/A}, d(b) = class of 1 ⊗ b − b ⊗ 1 (Huber 1996, (1.6.2)). Properties: (a) for every finite N ⊆ B with A[N] dense in B, I is generated by the 1 ⊗ n − n ⊗ 1 (n ∈ N); hence Ωᶜ_{B/A} is a finitely generated B-module generated by d(N). (b) d is a continuous A-derivation and is universal: for every complete Hausdorff topological B-module M and every continuous A-derivation D: B → M there is a unique continuous B-linear u: Ωᶜ_{B/A} → M with u ∘ d = D. (c) If A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩_M → B is a quotient mapping with kernel 𝔞, then Ωᶜ_{B/A} ≅ (⊕ᵢ B dXᵢ)/(B-span of Σᵢ (∂P/∂Xᵢ) dXᵢ, P ∈ 𝔞), where ∂/∂Xᵢ are the termwise partial derivatives (which preserve A⟨X⟩_M); in particular Ωᶜ_{A⟨X⟩_M/A} is free with basis dX₁,…,dXₙ. (d) If B is a finite A-module, the canonical B-linear map Ω[B⁄A] → Ωᶜ_{B/A} from Mathlib's KaehlerDifferential (induced by d) is an isomorphism. Under this hypothesis Ωᶜ_{B/A} is complete and Hausdorff; without it (non-noetherian A) the ideal generated by the 1 ⊗ n − n ⊗ 1 need not be closed and none of (a)-(d) is asserted. The coherent sheaf Ω¹_{X/Y} on a locally noetherian analytic adic space is glued from these modules by the localisation compatibility (API localizationEquiv); it is R3.36. *API* (on `Huber`): `ContinuousKaehlerDifferential, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.D, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.continuous_D, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.lift, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.lift_comp_D, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.hom_ext, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.fg, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.restrictedMvPowerSeriesBasis, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.presentationEquiv, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.fromKaehler, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.fromKaehler_bijective_of_finite, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.t2Space_of_isNoetherianRing, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.map, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.baseChangeEquiv, ContinuousKaehlerDifferential.localizationEquiv`. *Tests:* `ContinuousKaehlerDifferential_test_tateAlgebra` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring; K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra. Ωᶜ_{K⟨T⟩/K} is free of rank 1 with basis dT, and d(Σ aₙTⁿ) = (Σ n aₙTⁿ⁻¹) dT. `ContinuousKaehlerDifferential_test_notAlgebraic` (non-example): For K = Q_p, Mathlib's Ω[Q_p⟨T⟩⁄Q_p] is not a finitely generated Q_p⟨T⟩-module (the fraction field of Q_p⟨T⟩ has uncountable transcendence degree over Q_p, e.g. the exp(p c T), c ∈ Z_p, p odd, are algebraically independent for Q-linearly independent c), while Ωᶜ_{Q_p⟨T⟩/Q_p} ≅ Q_p⟨T⟩: defining Ωᶜ as algebraic Kähler differentials is wrong. `ContinuousKaehlerDifferential_test_finite` (compatibility): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring; K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra. Assume 2 ∈ K^×. For A = K⟨S⟩ → B = K⟨T⟩, S ↦ T² (finite), the map Ω[B⁄A] → Ωᶜ_{B/A} is an isomorphism and both are B/(2T)·dT. `ContinuousKaehlerDifferential_test_surjective` (degenerate): If A → B is surjective (a quotient mapping), Ωᶜ_{B/A} = 0; in particular Ωᶜ_{A/A} = 0. `ContinuousKaehlerDifferential_test_derivations` (characterisation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring; K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra. Continuous K-derivations K⟨T⟩ → K⟨T⟩ are exactly g·d/dT for g ∈ K⟨T⟩, i.e. Hom_{K⟨T⟩}(Ωᶜ_{K⟨T⟩/K}, K⟨T⟩) ≅ K⟨T⟩ via u ↦ u(dT). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Def. 1.6.1 and the par. after it, p. 76. *Needs:* R0.21, R0.22, R0.1, R0.5, R0.24, R0.6, R0.10.

- **R0.94** First fundamental exact sequence of continuous differentials (Huber 1.6.3). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.3(i).

- **R0.95** Conormal exact sequence for quotient mappings (Huber 1.6.3). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.3(ii).

- **R0.96** Stalks of continuous differentials of locally quasi-finite morphisms (Huber 1.6.4(i)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Lem. 1.6.4(i) and the definition of Ω_{X/Y} before it.

**R0.97 Flat morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.Flat`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is flat if for every x ∈ X the local homomorphism of stalks O_{Y,f(x)} → O_{X,x} is flat (Mathlib Module.Flat) (Huber 1996, 1.7.5(b); Zavyalov, Quotients, Definition B.4.1). Stalks are colimits of rings, with no topology (AdicSpaces anchor, Layer 3.3). Conventions: flatness is stalkwise; for morphisms of strongly noetherian Tate affinoids it implies flatness of O_Y(Y) → O_X(X) (Zavyalov B.4.3), and for affinoids of topologically finite type over a nonarchimedean field the two are equivalent (Zavyalov B.4.3, B.4.6); in general the converse is not known (Zavyalov B.4.9) and is not asserted. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `Flat, Flat.flat_stalkMap, Flat.comp, Flat.of_isOpenImmersion, Flat.flat_appTop_of_isStronglyNoetherian, Flat.of_flat_of_tateAlgebra, Flat.baseChange_of_locallyQuasiFinite, Etale.flat, Smooth.flat`. *Tests:* `Flat_test_openImmersion` (degenerate): Open embeddings are flat (stalk maps are isomorphisms), and so are identities. `Flat_test_origin` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring; K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra. The closed embedding Spa(K, K°) → Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) of the origin is not flat: T is a nonzerodivisor of O_{D,0} that becomes 0 in K. `Flat_test_squaring` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring; K⟨T⟩ the Tate algebra. Assume 2 ∈ K^×. Spa(K⟨T⟩) → Spa(K⟨S⟩), S ↦ T², is flat: K⟨T⟩ is free over K⟨S⟩, hence the morphism is flat by Zavyalov B.4.6. `Flat_test_ringLevel` (compatibility): For f: Spa(B, B°) → Spa(A, A°) between affinoids topologically of finite type over K: AdicSpace.Flat f ↔ Module.Flat A B (Zavyalov B.4.3 and B.4.6); this matches Mathlib's AlgebraicGeometry.Flat, which is also stalkwise-flatness for schemes. *Source:* Zavyalov-Q Appendix B.4, Def. B.4.1, p. 49.

**R0.98 Unramified morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.Unramified`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is unramified if it is locally of finite type (R0.35) and for every complete Huber pair A, every closed ideal I ⊆ A with I² = 0 and every morphism Spa A → Y, the restriction map Hom_Y(Spa A, X) → Hom_Y(Spa(A/I), X) is injective (Huber 1996, 1.6.5). Test objects: A ranges over complete Huber pairs (A, A⁺) (Tau Ceti Huber.Pair on complete Hausdorff rings), Spa A is the affinoid pre-adic space of the AdicSpaces anchor (Layer 3.4; A need not be sheafy), and morphisms Spa A → Y are morphisms of pre-adic spaces; I ⊆ A is a closed ideal with I² = 0 and A/I is the quotient Huber pair (tauceti Huber.Pair.quotient), whose plus ring is A⁺/I because I ⊆ A°° ⊆ A⁺. Huber allows non-complete affinoid rings and arbitrary square-zero I; since Spa A = Spa Â and the closure of a square-zero ideal is square-zero, restricting to complete A and closed I changes nothing. The morphism Spa(A/I) → Spa A is a homeomorphism. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `Unramified, Unramified.hom_ext, Unramified.comp, Unramified.of_comp, Unramified.baseChange, Unramified.of_isImmersion, Unramified.iff_differentials_eq_zero, Unramified.isOpenImmersion_diagonal, Unramified.locallyQuasiFinite, Unramified.iff_formallyUnramified_of_finite`. *Tests:* `Unramified_test_origin` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). The closed embedding of the origin Spa(K, K°) → D is unramified but not étale (not flat), and the squaring map T ↦ T² restricted to the rational subset {|T| = 1} (2 ∈ K^×) is unramified, indeed étale, but not injective on points: unramifiedness is neither étaleness nor injectivity. `Unramified_test_squaring` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). Assume 2 ∈ K^×. For f: D → D, S ↦ T², Ωᶜ = K⟨T⟩/(2T)·dT, so f is unramified exactly on the complement of the origin: its restriction to the rational subset {|T| = 1} is unramified and f itself is not. `Unramified_test_id` (degenerate): Identities and open embeddings are unramified. `Unramified_test_finiteAlgebra` (compatibility): For a finite morphism (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) of complete noetherian Tate pairs, Spa(B, B⁺) → Spa(A, A⁺) is unramified iff Algebra.FormallyUnramified A B (Mathlib), since Ωᶜ_{B/A} = Ω[B⁄A] (R0.93 (d)) and R0.108. *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Def. 1.6.5, p. 78. *Needs:* R0.35.

**R0.99 Smooth morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.Smooth`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is smooth if it is locally of finite presentation (R0.37) and for every complete Huber pair A, every closed ideal I ⊆ A with I² = 0 and every morphism Spa A → Y, the restriction map Hom_Y(Spa A, X) → Hom_Y(Spa(A/I), X) is surjective (Huber 1996, 1.6.5). Test objects: A ranges over complete Huber pairs (A, A⁺) (Tau Ceti Huber.Pair on complete Hausdorff rings), Spa A is the affinoid pre-adic space of the AdicSpaces anchor (Layer 3.4; A need not be sheafy), and morphisms Spa A → Y are morphisms of pre-adic spaces; I ⊆ A is a closed ideal with I² = 0 and A/I is the quotient Huber pair (tauceti Huber.Pair.quotient), whose plus ring is A⁺/I because I ⊆ A°° ⊆ A⁺. Huber allows non-complete affinoid rings and arbitrary square-zero I; since Spa A = Spa Â and the closure of a square-zero ideal is square-zero, restricting to complete A and closed I changes nothing. The morphism Spa(A/I) → Spa A is a homeomorphism. The relative dimension of f at x is the rank of the (locally free, R0.109) module of continuous differentials near x. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `Smooth, Smooth.exists_lift, Smooth.comp, Smooth.baseChange, Smooth.restrictedPowerSeries, Smooth.differentials_locallyFree, Smooth.relativeDimension, Smooth.exists_etale_polydisc, Smooth.isOpenMap, Smooth.flat`. *Tests:* `Smooth_test_polydisc` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). Spa(K⟨T₁,…,Tₙ⟩, K°⟨T₁,…,Tₙ⟩) → Spa(K, K°) is smooth of relative dimension n (Ωᶜ free on dT₁,…,dTₙ). `Smooth_test_node` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). Spa(K⟨T₁,T₂⟩/(T₁T₂)) → Spa(K, K°) is not smooth: Ωᶜ = (C dT₁ ⊕ C dT₂)/(T₂dT₁ + T₁dT₂) is not locally free at the origin. `Smooth_test_notAlgebraSmooth` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). D → Spa(K, K°) is smooth, but Mathlib's Algebra.Smooth K K⟨T⟩ is false (K⟨T⟩ is not even of finite type as a K-algebra); defining smoothness as algebraic smoothness of coordinate rings is wrong. `Smooth_test_openImmersion` (degenerate): Identities and open embeddings are smooth (they are étale, R0.101). `Smooth_test_discrete` (compatibility): Over a discrete base (Huber 1.6.6(i)): for A, B discrete noetherian rings with B of finite presentation over A and plus rings as in Huber's discrete affinoid rings, Spa(B, B⁺) → Spa(A, A⁺) is smooth iff Mathlib's Algebra.Smooth A B. *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Def. 1.6.5, p. 78. *Needs:* R0.37, R0.35.

**R0.100 Étale morphism of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.Etale`). A morphism f: X → Y of adic spaces is étale if it is locally of finite presentation (R0.37) and for every complete Huber pair A, every closed ideal I ⊆ A with I² = 0 and every morphism Spa A → Y, the restriction map Hom_Y(Spa A, X) → Hom_Y(Spa(A/I), X) is bijective (Huber 1996, 1.6.5). Equivalently, f is smooth (R0.99) and unramified (R0.98). Test objects: A ranges over complete Huber pairs (A, A⁺) (Tau Ceti Huber.Pair on complete Hausdorff rings), Spa A is the affinoid pre-adic space of the AdicSpaces anchor (Layer 3.4; A need not be sheafy), and morphisms Spa A → Y are morphisms of pre-adic spaces; I ⊆ A is a closed ideal with I² = 0 and A/I is the quotient Huber pair (tauceti Huber.Pair.quotient), whose plus ring is A⁺/I because I ⊆ A°° ⊆ A⁺. Huber allows non-complete affinoid rings and arbitrary square-zero I; since Spa A = Spa Â and the closure of a square-zero ideal is square-zero, restricting to complete A and closed I changes nothing. The morphism Spa(A/I) → Spa A is a homeomorphism. Conventions: this is Huber's infinitesimal definition; the Kedlaya–Liu and Scholze–Weinstein definition ('locally an open immersion followed by a finite étale map') agrees with it for analytic adic spaces by R0.119, and the algebraic notion Algebra.Etale of Mathlib applied to coordinate rings does not (a rational embedding is étale but its ring map is not of finite type). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `Etale, Etale.iff_smooth_and_unramified, Etale.lift, Etale.comp, Etale.baseChange, Etale.of_comp, Etale.of_isOpenImmersion, Etale.exists_standardPresentation, Etale.iff_flat_and_unramified, Etale.locallyQuasiFinite, Etale.isOpenMap, Etale.exists_openImmersion_finiteEtale, Etale.finiteEtaleEquiv`. *Tests:* `Etale_test_kummer` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). Let n ∈ K^× (as an integer) and T^± denote the annulus Spa(K⟨T, T⁻¹⟩, K°⟨T, T⁻¹⟩). The map T^± → T^±, S ↦ Tⁿ, is finite and étale: it is the standard presentation K⟨S^±⟩⟨T⟩/(Tⁿ − S) with Jacobian nTⁿ⁻¹ a unit (R0.114). `Etale_test_rationalEmbedding` (non-example): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudo-uniformiser, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). For K = Q_p the rational embedding {|T| ≤ |p|} → D is étale (an open embedding), but Q_p⟨T⟩ → Q_p⟨T, T/p⟩ is not of finite type as an algebra (the image of Spec is an infinite, co-infinite set of closed points, contradicting Chevalley's theorem), so it is not Algebra.Etale: 'étale' cannot be defined as Mathlib's Algebra.Etale on coordinate rings. `Etale_test_frobenius` (non-example): Let K have characteristic p. Then Spa(K⟨T⟩) → Spa(K⟨S⟩), S ↦ T^p, is finite, flat (K⟨T⟩ is free on 1, T, …, T^{p−1} over K⟨T^p⟩) and bijective on points, but not étale: Ωᶜ = K⟨T⟩·dT ≠ 0 (the derivative pT^{p−1} vanishes), so it is not unramified. `Etale_test_openImmersion` (degenerate): Identities and open embeddings are étale (R0.101). `Etale_test_finiteEtaleAlgebra` (compatibility): For (A, A⁺) a complete strongly noetherian Tate pair and B a finite A-algebra with the pair structure of R0.80: Spa(B, B⁺) → Spa(A, A⁺) is étale iff Algebra.Etale A B (R0.112, Huber 1.6.6(ii)); for affinoid Y the standard presentations of R0.114 are the adic counterpart of Mathlib's Algebra.Etale.iff_isStandardSmoothOfRelativeDimension_zero. *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Def. 1.6.5, p. 78. *Needs:* R0.37, R0.35, R0.99, R0.98.

- **R0.101** Open embeddings are étale (Huber 1.6.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.7(i).

- **R0.102** Locally closed embeddings are unramified (Huber 1.6.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.7(i).

- **R0.103** Relative polydiscs are smooth (Huber 1.6.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.7(i).

- **R0.104** Composites of unramified, smooth and étale morphisms (Huber 1.6.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.7(ii).

- **R0.105** Cancellation for unramified, smooth and étale morphisms (Huber 1.6.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.7(iii).

- **R0.106** Unramified, smooth and étale are local on the source (Huber 1.6.7). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.7(v) and Def. 1.6.5(ii).

- **R0.107** Unramified morphisms are locally quasi-finite (Huber 1.7.4). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Cor. 1.7.4.

- **R0.108** Characterisations of unramified morphisms (Huber 1.6.8). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.8.

- **R0.109** Continuous differentials of a smooth morphism are locally free (Huber 1.6.9(i)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.9(i).

- **R0.110** Jacobian criterion for closed subspaces of smooth spaces (Huber 1.6.9(ii)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.9(ii).

- **R0.111** Differential criterion for étale and smooth morphisms between smooth spaces (Huber 1.6.9(iii)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Prop. 1.6.9(iii).

**R0.112 Finite étale adic spaces over a noetherian affinoid are finite étale algebras (Huber 1.6.6(ii))** (comparison). Let (A, A⁺) be a complete strongly noetherian Tate Huber pair. The functor C ↦ Spa(Huber.Pair.finiteAlgebra C) (R0.80), from finite étale A-algebras (Mathlib Algebra.Etale A C with C a finite A-module) and A-algebra maps, to adic spaces X with a finite (R0.79) and étale (R0.100) morphism X → Spa(A, A⁺) and Spa(A, A⁺)-morphisms, is an equivalence of categories, with quasi-inverse X ↦ O_X(X). The plus ring of the adic space attached to C is the integral closure of A⁺ in C. (Huber states 1.6.6(ii) for noetherian complete f-adic A; the essential surjectivity beyond the strongly noetherian Tate case rests on [Hu4] and is recorded as a gap.) *Hypotheses:* A complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (the construction of R0.80 and Zavyalov B.2.18 are available here). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Ex. 1.6.6(ii), pp. 78-79. *Needs:* R0.80, R0.79, R0.100, R0.108, R0.93, R0.121, R0.122.

**R0.113 Morphisms of Huber pairs of algebraically finite type** (definition `Huber.Pair.Hom.IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType`). Let (A, A⁺) be a Huber pair (not necessarily complete). For finite subsets M₁,…,Mₙ ⊆ A with each MᵢA open, let A[X₁,…,Xₙ]_M be the polynomial ring A[X] with the subspace topology of A⟨X⟩_M (Huber 1994, §3; for Tate A and Mᵢ = {1} the Gauss-norm topology) and plus ring the integral closure of A⁺[m·Xᵢ : m ∈ Mᵢ, 1 ≤ i ≤ n]. A morphism of Huber pairs φ: (A, A⁺) → (B, B⁺) is of algebraically finite type if it factors through a quotient mapping A[X₁,…,Xₙ]_M → B, i.e. a surjective, continuous, open ring homomorphism with B⁺ the integral closure of the image of the plus ring. This is the polynomial analogue of Huber's topologically finite type and is equivalent to Huber 1996, Definition 1.2.5(ii) (A^▷ → B^▷ adic with rings of definition A₀ → B₀ of finite type, and B⁺ the integral closure of an open subring D with A⁺ → D of algebraically finite type), by Remark 1.2.6(ii)-(iii) (choose the weights so that the images of the m·Xᵢ generate a ring of definition over A₀); it is the notion used in Corollary 1.7.3(iii); the completion of such a φ is topologically of finite type, and Spa B = Spa B̂. *API* (on `Huber.Pair.Hom`): `IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType, Huber.Pair.polynomialWeighted, IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType.finiteType, IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType.completion, IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType.exists_fg_plus, IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType.comp, IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType.id, IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType.localizationAway, isAlgebraicallyFiniteType_iff_huber`. *Tests:* `IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType_test_torus` (computation): For A = (W(κ)[1/p], W(κ)) with the p-adic topology and B = (W(κ)[1/p][T^{±1}], integral closure of W(κ)[T^{±1}]), A → B is of algebraically finite type (quotient of A[X, Y] by (XY − 1), Mᵢ = {1}), and its completion is the torus (W(κ)[1/p]⟨T^{±1}⟩, W(κ)⟨T^{±1}⟩). `IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType_test_tateAlgebra` (non-example): (Q_p, Z_p) → (Q_p⟨T⟩, Z_p⟨T⟩) is topologically of finite type but not of algebraically finite type (Q_p⟨T⟩ is not a finitely generated Q_p-algebra). `IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType_test_id` (degenerate): The identity of a Huber pair and the quotient maps (A, A⁺) → (A/J, integral closure) are of algebraically finite type (n = 0). `IsAlgebraicallyFiniteType_test_finiteType` (compatibility): If φ is of algebraically finite type then φ.toRingHom is of finite type in Mathlib's sense (Algebra.FiniteType A B), and the completion φ̂ is topologically of finite type (TauCeti.Huber.IsTopologicallyFiniteType). *Source:* Huber96 §1.2, Def. 1.2.5 and Rem. 1.2.6, p. 50. *Needs:* R0.35.

**R0.114 Standard étale presentation of étale affinoid maps (Huber 1.7.1)** (theorem). Let A = (A, A⁺) be a Huber pair (not necessarily complete) whose completion is of noetherian type or discrete (R0.1; Huber's standing assumption (1.1.1), used in point (3) of the proof) and s: X → Y = Spa A a morphism of adic spaces with X affinoid. The following are equivalent: (i) s is étale (R0.100); (ii) there are n ≥ 0, finite subsets T₁,…,Tₙ ⊆ A with each TᵢA open, and f₁,…,fₙ ∈ B := A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩_{T₁,…,Tₙ} such that, with I = (f₁,…,fₙ) ⊆ B, the image of det(∂fᵢ/∂Xⱼ) in B/I is a unit and X is Y-isomorphic to Spa(B/I); (iii) the same with f₁,…,fₙ ∈ A[X₁,…,Xₙ]. Here B/I is the quotient Huber pair (plus ring the integral closure of the image), and Spa(B/I) is Spa of its completion. The number of equations equals the number of variables. If A is Tate, the presentation in (iii) can be chosen with T₁ = … = Tₙ = {1} (Huber 1996, Corollary 1.7.2(ii)). *Hypotheses:* X affinoid, Y = Spa A affinoid (A not necessarily complete). Â of noetherian type or discrete (so every affinoid ring topologically of finite type over A is noetherian or discrete). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Prop. 1.7.1, p. 80. *Needs:* R0.100, R0.95, R0.108, R0.110, R0.93, R0.103, R0.101, R0.115, R0.37, R0.35, R0.1.

- **R0.115** Stability of standard étale presentations under perturbation (Huber 1.7.2). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Cor. 1.7.2(i) and proof.

- **R0.116** Algebraic models of affinoid étale maps (Huber 1.7.3(iii)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Cor. 1.7.3(iii) and its proof.

**R0.117 Smooth morphisms are locally étale over relative polydiscs (Huber 1.6.10)** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) be a Huber pair whose completion is of noetherian type or discrete, and f: X → Spa A a morphism of adic spaces (Huber 1996, Corollary 1.6.10). Then f is smooth if and only if every x ∈ X has an open neighbourhood U for which there are n ≥ 0, finite subsets M₁,…,Mₙ ⊆ A with each MᵢA open, and an étale morphism g: U → Spa A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩_M with f|_U equal to the composite of g and the structure morphism (Huber's diagram (*)). If A is Tate and f is smooth, one can take M₁ = … = Mₙ = {1}, i.e. g: U → Spa A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩ étale. *Hypotheses:* (A, A⁺) Huber pair with Â of noetherian type or discrete (continuous differentials, R0.109); Tate for the simplified form. *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Cor. 1.6.10 and proof, p. 80. *Needs:* R0.99, R0.109, R0.111, R0.103, R0.93, R0.104, R0.106, R0.100, R0.38.

- **R0.118** Étale morphisms of affinoids over a field embed locally into finite étale covers (de Jong–van der Put 3.1.4). *Source:* dJvdP §3.1, Prop. 3.1.4.

- **R0.119** Étale maps are locally open embeddings into finite étale covers (Huber 2.2.8). *Source:* Huber96 §2.2, Lem. 2.2.8 and proof.

- **R0.120** Étale toric charts on smooth affinoids (Scholze 2013, Lemma 5.2). *Source:* Scholze13 §5, Lem. 5.2.

**R0.121 Étale equals flat and unramified for analytic adic spaces (Huber 1.7.5)** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism locally of finite type between analytic adic spaces, with Y locally noetherian (Huber's standing assumption (1.1.1); needed by Lemma 1.7.6, R0.122). The following are equivalent: (a) f is étale; (b) f is flat (R0.97) and unramified (R0.98); (c) for every maximal point x ∈ X, O_{X,x} is flat over O_{Y,f(x)} and O_{X,x}/𝔪_{f(x)}O_{X,x} is a finite separable field extension of the residue field of O_{Y,f(x)}. *Hypotheses:* X, Y analytic; Y locally noetherian. f locally of finite type. *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Prop. 1.7.5 and proof, p. 87. *Needs:* R0.100, R0.97, R0.98, R0.107, R0.90, R0.96, R0.89, R0.114, R0.108, R0.101, R0.104, R0.122, R0.106, R0.130, R0.38.

- **R0.122** Flatness of the ring map of an étale affinoid morphism (Huber 1.7.6). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Lem. 1.7.6 and proof.

- **R0.123** Smooth and étale morphisms are open (Huber 1.7.7-1.7.9). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, (1.7.7), Prop. 1.7.8 and Lem. 1.7.9.

- **R0.124** On a Tate algebra the Gauss norm is the maximum of |f(x)| over Max K⟨X⟩. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §1.1, Exercise 1.1.3(3).

- **R0.125** Adic homomorphisms are bounded and adic for every choice of rings of definition. *Source:* Huber93 §1, Lem. 1.8 and proof, p. 459 (GDZ scan, page image read).

- **R0.126** Spa of a continuous homomorphism and the analytic locus (affinoid adicness criterion). *Source:* Wedhorn §7.5, Lem. 7.46(1),(2).

- **R0.127** Banach spaces of countable type have t-orthogonal Schauder bases. *Source:* HK §7, Rem. 7.8.

- **R0.128** Completed tensor products over a nonarchimedean field: cross norm, injectivity, kernels and strictness (Kedlaya–Liu Lemma 2.2.9). *Source:* KL15 §2.2, Lem. 2.2.9(b).

- **R0.129** The fibre of a morphism over a point (Huber (1.2.4)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.2, (1.2.4).

- **R0.130** Flat locally quasi-finite morphisms stay flat after base change (Huber 1.6.4(ii)). *Source:* Huber96 §1.6, Lem. 1.6.4(ii) and proof.

- **R0.131** The closure of a maximal point is the intersection of the rational subsets containing it (Huber 1.5.10). *Source:* Huber96 §1.5, Lem. 1.5.10 and proof.

- **R0.132** Closure of a pro-constructible set is its set of specializations (Stacks 0903). *Source:* Stacks Tag 0903, Lem. 5.23.6.

- **R0.133** Images of pro-constructible sets under quasi-compact spectral maps are pro-constructible. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0A2S, Lem. 5.23.3.


<a id="r1"></a>

## R1. Analytification and algebraic correspondences

**R1. Analytification and algebraic correspondences.** Depends on R0. Affinoid subdomains of `Max A`; Huber's fibre product of a scheme locally of finite type with an adic space; the analytification functor `X ↦ X^ad` over `K` with its universal property and its compatibilities with fibre products, immersions, finite, smooth and étale morphisms; local rings, constructible sets and connectedness; separatedness and properness comparisons; Tate's rigid spaces and Huber's rigid–adic comparison; analytification of group schemes, abelian schemes and isogenies with their invariant differentials; dimension of affinoid domains and the identity principle.

- **R1.1** For a Tate ring of topologically finite type over K, the power-bounded subring is the only ring of integral elements making it of topologically finite type over (K, K°). *Source:* Huber94 §4, Lem. 4.4.

### Affinoid subdomains

**R1.2 Affinoid subdomains of the maximal spectrum of a K-affinoid algebra** (definition `RigidSpace.IsAffinoidSubdomain`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one, A a K-affinoid algebra and X = Max A. A subset U ⊆ X is an affinoid subdomain if there is a homomorphism of K-affinoid algebras i : A → A_U such that Max(i) : Max A_U → X (R0.50) has image in U and is universal for this property: for every homomorphism φ : A → B into a K-affinoid algebra with Max(φ)(Max B) ⊆ U there is a unique K-algebra homomorphism φ_U : A_U → B with φ_U ∘ i = φ. The A-algebra A_U (the coordinate ring of U) is unique up to unique A-isomorphism; Max(i) is a bijection onto U inducing isomorphisms of residue fields; for affinoid subdomains V ⊆ U there is a unique A-algebra map ρ^U_V : A_U → A_V, with ρ^V_W ∘ ρ^U_V = ρ^U_W. Rational subdomains X(f₁,…,f_n/f₀) = {x : |f_j(x)| ≤ |f₀(x)| for all j}, for f₀,…,f_n ∈ A without common zero, are affinoid subdomains with coordinate ring A⟨f/f₀⟩ = A⟨T₁,…,T_n⟩/(f₀T_j − f_j) (Weierstrass domains f₀ = 1, Laurent domains as intersections). Lean name: RigidSpace.IsAffinoidSubdomain A U, with coordinate ring RigidSpace.IsAffinoidSubdomain.coordRing. *API* (on `RigidSpace`): `IsAffinoidSubdomain, IsAffinoidSubdomain.coordRing, IsAffinoidSubdomain.lift, IsAffinoidSubdomain.maxSpectrumEquiv, IsAffinoidSubdomain.restrict, IsAffinoidSubdomain.rational, IsAffinoidSubdomain.inter, IsAffinoidSubdomain.preimage, IsAffinoidSubdomain.trans, IsAffinoidSubdomain.quotient_pow_equiv, IsAffinoidSubdomain.flat, IsAffinoidSubdomain.spa_compat`. *Tests:* `isAffinoidSubdomain_test_disc` (computation): In Max K⟨T⟩, U = {|T| ≤ |c|} (0 < |c| ≤ 1) is a Weierstrass domain with A_U = K⟨T, S⟩/(T − cS) ≅ K⟨S⟩ and restriction T ↦ cS. `isAffinoidSubdomain_test_openDisc` (non-example): The open disc {|T| < 1} ⊆ Max K⟨T⟩ is open in the canonical topology but is not an affinoid subdomain: by the maximum modulus principle an affinoid subdomain inside it lies in some {|T| ≤ |c|^{1/n}}, which misses points with |T| = |c|^{1/(n+1)}; a definition by 'open subset' fails. `isAffinoidSubdomain_test_univ` (degenerate): U = Max A is an affinoid subdomain with A_U = A (i = id), and U = ∅ with A_U = 0. `isAffinoidSubdomain_test_rational_compat` (compatibility): For f₀,…,f_n ∈ A without common zero, the coordinate ring of X(f/f₀) is, as a topological ring, Tau Ceti's completed rational localisation of (A, A°) at (f₁,…,f_n; f₀) (PairOfDefinition.completionLocalization), and Max of it is the set of classical points of the anchor's rational subset R(f/f₀). *Source:* Conrad-AWS §2.2, Def. 2.2.1, p. 12. *Needs:* R0.50, R0.51, R0.55, R0.21.

- **R1.3** Coordinate rings of affinoid subdomains are flat. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §2.2, discussion after Def. 2.2.1.

**R1.4 The Gerritzen–Grauert theorem** (theorem). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one, A a K-affinoid algebra and U ⊆ Max A an affinoid subdomain. Then there are finitely many rational subdomains V₁,…,V_n covering Max A — induced by a rational covering of Spa(A, A°) — such that U ∩ V_i is a rational subdomain of Max A_{V_i} for every i. In particular U is a finite union of rational subdomains of Max A, and affinoid subdomains are open in the canonical topology. Lean name: RigidSpace.IsAffinoidSubdomain.exists_rationalCover. *Hypotheses:* K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one. A is a K-affinoid algebra: a commutative K-algebra with a complete Hausdorff first-countable ring topology for which K → A is strictly topologically of finite type (Tau Ceti IsStrictlyTopologicallyFiniteType (algebraMap K A): a continuous open surjection of K-algebras K⟨X₁,…,X_n⟩ = restrictedMvPowerSeriesCompletion n K → A; Wedhorn Definition 5.57). U an affinoid subdomain (R1.2). *Source:* Conrad-AWS §2.2, Thm 2.2.5, p. 14. *Needs:* R1.2, R1.45.

**R1.5 Tate's acyclicity theorem for finite coverings by affinoid subdomains** (theorem). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one, A a K-affinoid algebra, M an A-module and U₁,…,U_n affinoid subdomains covering Max A (R1.2). Then the augmented Čech complex 0 → M → ∏_i M ⊗_A A_{U_i} → ∏_{i<j} M ⊗_A A_{U_i ∩ U_j} → ⋯ is exact. In particular U ↦ A_U is a sheaf for finite coverings of affinoid subdomains by affinoid subdomains, with vanishing higher Čech cohomology, and it extends uniquely to a sheaf O_A for the Tate topology on Max A. Lean name: RigidSpace.cech_exact_of_affinoidCover. *Hypotheses:* K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one. A is a K-affinoid algebra: a commutative K-algebra with a complete Hausdorff first-countable ring topology for which K → A is strictly topologically of finite type (Tau Ceti IsStrictlyTopologicallyFiniteType (algebraMap K A): a continuous open surjection of K-algebras K⟨X₁,…,X_n⟩ = restrictedMvPowerSeriesCompletion n K → A; Wedhorn Definition 5.57). M an arbitrary A-module. {U_i} a finite covering of Max A by affinoid subdomains. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §2.3, Thm 2.3.3, p. 17. *Needs:* R1.2, R1.3, R1.4, R1.45.

- **R1.6** Local rings of affinoid spaces at points are noetherian with the algebraic completion. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §2.3, after Exercise 2.3.6.

**R1.7 Rigid-analytic spaces over K in the sense of Tate (BGR 9.3.1)** (definition `RigidSpace`). Let K be a complete rank-one nonarchimedean field. For a K-affinoid algebra A (a quotient of some Tate algebra K⟨T₁,…,Tₙ⟩, with its Banach topology), the affinoid space Sp(A) is the set Max A of maximal ideals with the Tate (strong) G-topology: a subset U ⊆ Max A is admissible open if it has a set-theoretic covering {U_i} by affinoid subdomains such that for every homomorphism of K-affinoid algebras φ : A → B with Max(φ)(Max B) ⊆ U finitely many U_i cover Max(φ)(Max B), and a family {V_j} of admissible opens is an admissible covering of its union V if for every φ : A → B with Max(φ)(Max B) ⊆ V the covering {Max(φ)⁻¹(V_j)} of Max B has a refinement by finitely many affinoid subdomains (Conrad AWS Definition 2.2.6; BGR 9.1.4); together with Tate's structure sheaf O_A, U ↦ A_U on affinoid subdomains. A rigid-analytic space over K is a pair (X, O_X) of a G-topologized set X and a sheaf of K-algebras O_X with local stalks, admitting an admissible covering by open subspaces isomorphic to affinoid spaces Sp(A_i). Morphisms are morphisms of locally G-ringed K-spaces. Rig_K denotes this category. A morphism f is a closed immersion if there is an admissible affinoid covering {U_i} of the target with each f⁻¹(U_i) affinoid and O(U_i) → O(f⁻¹U_i) surjective (Conrad AWS Example 3.1.5); f is separated if its diagonal is a closed immersion, quasi-separated if its diagonal is quasi-compact, and proper (Kiehl) if it is separated, quasi-compact and relatively compact over an admissible affinoid covering of the target (Conrad AWS Definition 3.2.3). *API*: `RigidSpace, RigidSpace.Sp, RigidSpace.Sp.map, RigidSpace.homSpEquiv, RigidSpace.IsAdmissibleOpen, RigidSpace.IsAdmissibleCover, RigidSpace.restrict, RigidSpace.glue, RigidSpace.IsSeparated, RigidSpace.IsQuasiSeparated, RigidSpace.IsProper, RigidSpace.pullback, RigidSpace.IsClosedImmersion, RigidSpace.closedSubspace`. *Tests:* `RigidSpace.test_sp_point` (degenerate): Sp(K) is a one-point rigid space with O(Sp K) = K, and Sp(0) is empty. `RigidSpace.test_sp_homEquiv` (characterisation): For K-affinoid algebras A, B, morphisms Sp(B) → Sp(A) of rigid spaces correspond to K-algebra maps A → B. `RigidSpace.test_nonadmissible_cover` (non-example): The set-theoretic cover of the closed unit disc by {|T| < 1} and {|T| = 1} is not admissible; a definition allowing all set-theoretic covers by admissible opens would make the disc disconnected, contradicting O(Sp K⟨T⟩) = K⟨T⟩ having no nontrivial idempotents. `RigidSpace.test_global_sections` (computation): O(Sp(K⟨T⟩/(T² − T))) = K × K and Sp of it is two points. `RigidSpace.test_openDisc_admissible` (characterisation): The open unit disc {|T| < 1} ⊆ Sp K⟨T⟩ is an admissible open, admissibly covered by the Weierstrass domains {|T| ≤ |c|^{1/n}} (0 < |c| < 1); a definition using only finite unions of affinoid subdomains (the weak G-topology) fails this. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §2.4, Def. 2.4.1, p. 19. *Needs:* R1.2, R1.4, R1.5.

- **R1.8** At a classical point, the local ring of X^ad has the same completion as the local ring of X. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, Lem. A.1.2(1)-(2).

**R1.9 Rigid analytification X ↦ X^an of schemes locally of finite type over K** (construction `RigidSpace.analytification`). For a K-scheme X locally of finite type there is a rigid-analytic space X^an with a morphism of locally G-ringed K-spaces i_X: X^an → X that is final among rigid spaces over K with such a morphism: for every rigid space Y over K, composition with i_X is a bijection Hom_{Rig_K}(Y, X^an) → Hom_K(Y, X). X ↦ X^an is a functor; A^{n,an} is the union of the closed polydiscs of radius |c|^{-j} (0 < |c| < 1); for affine X = V(I) ⊆ A^n, X^an is the closed analytic subspace of A^{n,an} cut out by I; and i_X is a bijection onto the closed points of X; for a Zariski-open U ⊆ X the analytification of the open immersion U → X is an isomorphism of U^an onto the admissible open i_X⁻¹(U) of X^an; and for morphisms X → Z, Y → Z of K-schemes locally of finite type the canonical map (X ×_Z Y)^an → X^an ×_{Z^an} Y^an is an isomorphism. *API* (on `RigidSpace`): `analytification, analytification.toScheme, analytification.homEquiv, analytificationFunctor, analytification.bijective_closedPoints, analytification.pullbackIso`. *Tests:* `rigidAnalytification_test_point` (degenerate): (Spec K)^an = Sp(K). `rigidAnalytification_test_affineLine_hom` (characterisation): For an affinoid Sp(A), Hom(Sp A, (A¹_K)^an) = A (not the unit ball of A). `rigidAnalytification_test_closedPoints` (computation): The points of (A¹_K)^an are the closed points of A¹_K, i.e. monic irreducible polynomials over K; for K algebraically closed they are K. `rigidAnalytification_test_not_qc` (non-example): (A¹_K)^an is not quasi-compact, so it is not the affinoid Sp(K⟨T⟩). *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, p. 32. *Needs:* R1.7, R0.53, R0.54, R1.46, R0.55.

- **R1.10** Closures of constructible sets are compatible with analytification; closedness is detected on X^ad. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, Thm A.1.3(2)-(3).

- **R1.11** A finite locally free surjection of adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa K is an effective epimorphism. *Source:* Conrad-RA §4.2, Cor. 4.2.5.

### Huber's fibre product of a scheme and an adic space

- **R1.12** The affine chart of X ×_Y S: the increasing union of the affinoids Spa(A_k, A_k⁺) and its universal property on affinoid test objects. *Source:* Huber94 §3, proof of Prop. 3.8.

**R1.13 Huber's fibre product X ×_Y S of a scheme locally of finite type with an adic space over a scheme** (construction `AdicSpace.schemePullback`). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of schemes locally of finite type, S an adic space satisfying Huber's condition (every point has an open affinoid neighbourhood Spa(A, A⁺) with A having a noetherian ring of definition or A a strongly noetherian Tate ring; R0.38), and g: S_ → Y a morphism of locally ringed spaces, where S_ = (|S|, O_S) is the underlying locally ringed space of S (forgetting the topology of O_S and the valuations). Then there are an adic space R = X ×_Y S, a morphism of adic spaces p: R → S and a morphism of locally ringed spaces q: R_ → X with g ∘ p_ = f ∘ q, such that for every adic space U, every morphism of adic spaces u: U → S and every morphism of locally ringed spaces v: U_ → X with g ∘ u_ = f ∘ v there is a unique morphism of adic spaces w: U → R with p ∘ w = u and q ∘ w_ = v. The morphism p is locally of finite type (R0.35) and R satisfies Huber's condition. Over an affine open Spec B ⊆ Y, an affine open Spec C ⊆ f⁻¹(Spec B) with C = B[T₁,…,Tₙ]/I and an open affinoid Spa(A, A⁺) ⊆ g⁻¹(Spec B), the open subspace p⁻¹(Spa(A,A⁺)) ∩ q⁻¹(Spec C) of R is the increasing union ⋃_k Spa(A_k, A_k⁺) of R1.12. A Y-morphism h: X₁ → X₂ of Y-schemes locally of finite type induces h_S: X₁ ×_Y S → X₂ ×_Y S; this is functorial, and the triple (R, p, q) is unique up to unique isomorphism, so the construction is independent of the charts, presentations and sets E chosen. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `schemePullback, schemePullback.fst, schemePullback.snd, schemePullback.condition, schemePullback.lift, schemePullback.lift_fst, schemePullback.lift_snd, schemePullback.hom_ext, schemePullback.map, schemePullback.locallyOfFiniteType_fst, schemePullback.isLocallyNoetherian, schemePullback.isoOpenSubscheme, schemePullback.trans, schemePullback.baseChangeIso, schemePullback.affineChart`. *Tests:* `schemePullback_test_affineLine` (computation): For Y = Spec K, X = Spec K[T] and S = Spa(K, K°), morphisms Spa(D, D⁺) → X ×_Y S over S correspond to elements of D (not of D⁺): the element ϖ^{-1} ∈ K gives a K-point lying in the chart R_1 but not in R_0. `schemePullback_test_id` (degenerate): For X = Y and f = id, p: X ×_Y S → S is an isomorphism and q = g ∘ p_. `schemePullback_test_empty` (degenerate): For X = ∅ the adic space X ×_Y S is empty. `schemePullback_test_not_unit_disc` (non-example): For X = Spec K[T] over Y = Spec K, the single affinoid Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) with its evident maps does not satisfy the universal property: the morphism Spa(K,K°)_ → Spec K[T] given by T ↦ ϖ^{-1} does not factor through it. Hence a definition taking only the unit-polydisc chart is wrong. `schemePullback_test_finite` (compatibility): For C finite over B and S = Spa(A, A⁺), Spec C ×_{Spec B} S is the affinoid Spa(C ⊗_B A, integral closure of the image of A⁺) with the finite-module topology, as constructed in R0.80. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.8, p. 536. *Needs:* R1.12, R0.38, R0.35, R0.24, R0.42.

- **R1.14** Points of X ×_Y S and of X^ad: surjectivity onto compatible pairs; classical points correspond to closed points; q_X is not injective. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Lem. 3.9(ii).

### The analytification functor over K

**R1.15 The adic analytification X ↦ X^ad = X ×_{Spec K} Spa(K, K°) of schemes locally of finite type over K** (construction `AdicSpace.analytification`). Fix a field K complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |·| of rank one, its valuation ring K° = {|x| ≤ 1}, a pseudo-uniformizer ϖ (0 < |ϖ| < 1), and Spa K := Spa(K, K°), a one-point adic space. The identity of K defines a morphism of locally ringed spaces c_K: (Spa K)_ → Spec K. For a scheme X locally of finite type over K, its analytification is X^ad := X ×_{Spec K} Spa K (R1.13), an adic space locally of finite type over Spa K, with the morphism of locally ringed spaces q_X: X^ad_ → X lying over c_K; a K-morphism h: X₁ → X₂ gives h^ad := h_{Spa K}. This defines a functor (·)^ad: Sch^{lft}_K → Adic^{lft}_K from K-schemes locally of finite type to adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa K, and q is a natural transformation (·)^ad_ → (·) of functors to locally ringed spaces over Spec K. For X = Spec K[T₁,…,Tₙ]/I, X^ad = ⋃_{k≥0} Spa(A_k, A_k°) with A_k = K⟨ϖ^k T₁,…,ϖ^k Tₙ⟩/I·K⟨ϖ^k T⟩ (functions on the closed polydisc of radius |ϖ|^{-k} modulo I), glued along the rational embeddings Spa(A_k, A_k°) = {|ϖ^k Tᵢ| ≤ 1 for all i} ⊆ Spa(A_h, A_h°) for k ≤ h; the plus ring of each chart is its power-bounded subring (R1.1). The result does not depend on ϖ or on the presentation, up to unique isomorphism over Spa K compatible with q. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `analytification, analytification.toScheme, analytification.structureMorphism, analytification.locallyOfFiniteType, analytificationFunctor, analytification.toScheme_naturality, analytification.homEquiv, analytification.affineChart, analytification.isoOfPresentation, analytificationSpecIso, analytification.isoOpenSubscheme, analytification.baseFieldIso, analytification.isLocallyNoetherian`. *Tests:* `analytification_spec_field` (degenerate): (Spec K)^ad ≅ Spa(K, K°), a single point, and q is c_K; the empty scheme analytifies to the empty adic space. `analytification_test_finiteExtension` (computation): For a finite field extension L/K, (Spec L)^ad ≅ Spa(L, L°), a single point whose residue field is L. `analytification_test_not_unitDisc` (non-example): (A¹_K)^ad is not affinoid; in particular it is not Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩), which is the open subspace {|T| ≤ 1}, and it is not Spa of the completion of K[T] for any adic topology. `analytification_test_chart_polydisc` (compatibility): For X = A^n_K the chart k = 0 of X^ad is the anchor's closed polydisc Spa(K⟨T₁,…,Tₙ⟩, K°⟨T₁,…,Tₙ⟩) (Tau Ceti `ValuationSpectrum.closedPolydisc` after completion), with plus ring Tau Ceti `Huber.Pair.powerBounded`. `analytification_test_points_finiteAlgebra` (characterisation): For a finite K-algebra L that is a field, morphisms Spa(L, L°) → X^ad over Spa K correspond to X(L) = Hom_K(Spec L, X). *Source:* Wedhorn §8.7, Def. 8.64, p. 92. *Needs:* R1.13, R1.12, R1.1.

**R1.16 Universal property of X^ad: morphisms from adic spaces over Spa K into X^ad are morphisms of locally ringed spaces into X** (theorem).
