Let X be a scheme locally of finite type over K and Y an adic space over Spa K with structure morphism π: Y → Spa K. Composition with q_X gives a bijection, natural in Y and in X, Hom_{Spa K}(Y, X^ad) → Hom_{LRS/Spec K}(Y_, X), w ↦ q_X ∘ w_, onto the set of morphisms of locally ringed spaces v: Y_ → X whose composite with X → Spec K is c_K ∘ π_. In particular, for an affinoid Y = Spa(D, D⁺) over Spa K with D complete and an affine X = Spec C, Hom_{Spa K}(Y, X^ad) ≅ Hom_{K-alg}(C, D) = X(D); for X = A^n_K this is D^n; and for a finite field extension L/K, Hom_{Spa K}(Spa(L, L°), X^ad) = X(L). *Hypotheses:* X locally of finite type over K (K complete, rank one). Y an arbitrary adic space over Spa K; no finiteness hypothesis on Y. *Source:* Zavyalov24 §6, Def. 6.1, p. 12. *Needs:* R1.13, R1.15.

- **R1.17** The adic affine space A^{n,ad}: an increasing union of closed polydiscs, not quasi-compact, with entire functions as global sections. *Source:* Hübner §4, Ex. 4.3.

- **R1.18** Analytification preserves and reflects surjectivity. *Source:* Huber94 §3, Lem. 3.9(ii).

### Compatibilities

- **R1.19** Analytification of open and closed immersions: open subspaces and closed adic subspaces cut out by the analytified ideal. *Source:* Conrad-RA §2.2, Ex. 2.2.11.

- **R1.20** Analytification commutes with fibre products and preserves finite products. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, Lem. A.1.2(3).

- **R1.21** Analytification of finite and finite locally free morphisms. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.2, proof of Thm A.2.1.

- **R1.22** The analytification morphism q_X: X^ad → X is flat. *Source:* Zavyalov24 §6, Lem. 6.7.

**R1.23 Analytification of O_X-modules F ↦ F^ad = q_X^*F, its exactness, and analytified ideal sheaves** (construction `AdicSpace.analytificationModule`). For a K-scheme X locally of finite type and an O_X-module F, put F^ad := q_X^*F = O_{X^ad} ⊗_{q_X⁻¹O_X} q_X⁻¹F, the pullback of sheaves of modules along the morphism of ringed spaces q_X: X^ad_ → X (Mathlib `SheafOfModules.pullback` for the continuous functor of opens V ↦ q_X⁻¹(V) and the ring map q_X^♯; O_{X^ad} regarded as a sheaf of rings). Then F ↦ F^ad is an exact functor O_X-Mod → O_{X^ad}-Mod with stalks (F^ad)_x ≅ O_{X^ad,x} ⊗_{O_{X,q(x)}} F_{q(x)}; it commutes with tensor products (the sheafified tensor product of sheaves of modules — Mathlib has the monoidal structure only on presheaves of modules, so this is built here) and with pullback along analytified morphisms ((h^*G)^ad ≅ (h^ad)^*G^ad), (O_X)^ad = O_{X^ad}, and for a quasi-coherent ideal I ⊆ O_X the map I^ad → O_{X^ad} is injective with image the ideal sheaf I·O_{X^ad} of the closed adic subspace V(I)^ad (R1.19). The same holds relatively for the analytification X ×_{Spec A} S → X of a scheme X locally of finite type over Spec A, with S = Spa(A, A⁺) a strongly noetherian Tate affinoid (Zavyalov §6). It does not hold for X ×_Y S → X with an arbitrary scheme Y and morphism S → Y, whose projection need not be flat (Y = X = A¹_K and S = Spa K mapping to the origin: the projection is the inclusion of a point and q^* is not exact). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `analytificationModule, analytificationModule.map, analytificationModule.exact, analytificationModule.unitIso, analytificationModule.tensorIso, analytificationModule.pullbackIso, analytificationModule.ideal_injective, analytificationModule.idealSheaf_closedImmersion, analytificationModule.stalkIso, analytificationModule.chartSections`. *Tests:* `analytification_modules_test_structureSheaf` (degenerate): (O_X)^ad ≅ O_{X^ad} and 0^ad = 0. `analytification_modules_test_skyscraper` (computation): For X = A¹_K, (O_X/(T))^ad is the skyscraper sheaf K at the classical point 0 of A^{1,ad}_K. `analytification_modules_test_ideal` (characterisation): For a quasi-coherent ideal I ⊆ O_X, I^ad → O_{X^ad} is injective with image I·O_{X^ad}; a definition by the naive presheaf pullback q_X⁻¹F without tensoring with O_{X^ad} fails, since q_X⁻¹O_X ≠ O_{X^ad}. `analytification_modules_test_exact` (compatibility): For the exact sequence 0 → O_{A¹} → O_{A¹} → O_{A¹}/(T) → 0 given by multiplication by T, the analytified sequence 0 → O_{A^{1,ad}} → O_{A^{1,ad}} → K_0 → 0 is exact (T is a nonzerodivisor on every chart K⟨ϖ^kT⟩). `analytification_modules_test_affine_tilde` (compatibility): For X = Spec C and M a C-module, the sections of (M̃)^ad over a chart Spa(A_k, A_k°) are M ⊗_C A_k (compare Mathlib `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Modules` and the associated-module sheaf of R3.1). *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, Lem. A.1.2(4), p. 32. *Needs:* R1.22, R1.15, R1.19.

- **R1.24** Continuous differentials of the analytification charts: Ωᶜ_{A_k/A'} ≅ A_k ⊗_C Ω_{C/B'} (chart form of Ω_{X^ad/Y^ad} ≅ (Ω_{X/Y})^ad). *Source:* Zavyalov24 §5, proof of Lem. 5.9.

- **R1.25** Analytification preserves étale, smooth and unramified morphisms. *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Prop. 1.7.1.

### Local rings, constructible sets, connectedness

- **R1.26** X is connected iff X^ad is connected. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, Thm A.1.3(1).

### Separatedness and properness

- **R1.27** The analytified projective space P^{n,ad} → Spa K is proper; relatively (P^n_Y)^ad → Y^ad is proper. *Source:* Conrad-AWS §3.2, Ex. 3.2.5.

**R1.28 Separatedness and properness comparison: f is separated (proper) iff f^ad is** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of K-schemes locally of finite type (K complete of rank one). (i) f is separated iff f^ad is separated (R0.61). (ii) If f is proper then f^ad is proper (R0.63). (iii) If f is of finite type (quasi-compact) and f^ad is proper, then f is proper. In particular a K-scheme X of finite type is separated (resp. proper) iff X^ad is separated (resp. proper) over Spa(K, K°); for instance P^{n,ad} is proper and A^{1,ad} is separated but not proper. (ii′) Relatively: for a proper morphism f: X → Y of schemes and an analytic adic space S satisfying Huber's condition with S_ → Y, the projection X ×_Y S → S is proper; the forward direction of (i) also holds relatively. *Hypotheses:* K complete of rank one; in (iii) f quasi-compact (A^{1,ad} → Spa K is not quasi-compact, and neither is A¹ → Spec K proper). *Source:* Conrad-RA Appendix A.1, p. 38. *Needs:* R1.13, R1.20, R1.19, R1.10, R1.18, R1.27, R0.61, R0.63, R0.35, R0.43, R0.69, R0.47, R0.75, R0.76, R0.77, R0.74.

**R1.29 For f of finite type: f is finite (resp. a closed immersion) iff f^ad is** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of finite type between K-schemes locally of finite type (K complete of rank one). Then f is finite iff f^ad is finite (R0.79), and f is a closed immersion iff f^ad is a closed embedding (R0.45). *Hypotheses:* f of finite type (quasi-compact); K complete of rank one. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.2, Thm A.2.1(2), p. 33. *Needs:* R1.21, R1.14, R1.19, R1.28, R1.20, R1.23, R1.8, R0.82, R0.79, R0.83, R0.45.

**R1.30 f is étale (resp. smooth) iff f^ad is** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of K-schemes locally of finite type (K complete of rank one). Then f is étale (resp. smooth, resp. unramified) iff f^ad is étale (resp. smooth, resp. unramified) in the sense of Huber 1996 Definition 1.6.5. *Hypotheses:* K complete of rank one. *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.2, Thm A.2.1(1), p. 33. *Needs:* R1.25, R1.14, R1.24, R1.8, R0.121, R1.20, R0.97, R0.117, R0.109, R0.48, R0.108.

### Rigid–adic comparison

**R1.31 Huber's functor r_K from rigid-analytic spaces to adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°)** (construction `AdicSpace.rigidToAdic`). Let K be a complete rank-one nonarchimedean field. There is a functor r_K: Rig_K → Adic^{lft}_K together with, for each rigid space X, a morphism of ringed sites ρ_X: (|r_K(X)|, O_{r_K(X)}) → (X, O_X) over Sp K = Spa K (Huber 1994 Proposition 4.3) such that r_K(X) is locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), ρ_X is locally coherent, and (r_K(X), ρ_X) is final: for every adic space Z locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°) and every locally coherent morphism of ringed sites λ: (|Z|, O_Z) → (X, O_X) over Sp K there is a unique morphism f: Z → r_K(X) over Spa K with ρ_X ∘ f = λ. Moreover (Huber 1996 (1.1.11) and (1.4.1)): (a) r_K(Sp A) = Spa(A, A°) and r_K of a morphism of affinoids is induced by the K-algebra map; (b) r_K carries open immersions to open embeddings and r_K(U ∩ V) = r_K(U) ∩ r_K(V); (c) a family of admissible opens is an admissible covering iff the r_K(U_i) cover r_K(X); (d) the points of X correspond bijectively to the classical points of r_K(X) (points x with k(x) finite over K), with r_K(U) ∩ X = U for admissible U; if X is quasi-separated, U ↦ r_K(U) is a bijection from the quasi-compact admissible opens of X onto the quasi-compact opens of r_K(X), under which affinoid opens correspond to affinoid opens; (e) r_K commutes with fibre products; (f) a morphism f of rigid spaces is a closed immersion iff r_K(f) is a closed embedding (R0.45). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `rigidToAdic, rigidToAdic.rho, rigidToAdic.lift, rigidToAdic_Sp, rigidToAdic.isOpenImmersion_map, rigidToAdic_isAdmissibleCover_iff, rigidToAdic.classicalPointsEquiv, rigidToAdic.preservesPullbacks, rigidToAdic.locallyOfFiniteType, rigidToAdic.quasiCompactOpensEquiv, rigidToAdic.isClosedImmersion_iff`. *Tests:* `rigidToAdic_test_point` (degenerate): r_K(Sp K) ≅ Spa(K, K°), a single point, and ρ_{Sp K} is the identity of the one-point ringed site. `rigidToAdic_test_gaussPoint` (non-example): r_K(Sp K⟨T⟩) contains the Gauss point f ↦ |f|_sup, which is not classical; a definition of r_K(Sp A) as Max A with the rigid topology fails, since |r_K(Sp A)| ≠ Max A. `rigidToAdic_test_nonadmissible_cover` (characterisation): For the closed disc X = Sp K⟨T⟩, the images of the admissible opens {|T| < 1} = ⋃_n {|T|^n ≤ |ϖ|} and {|T| = 1} do not cover r_K(X): they miss the rank-two point specialising the Gauss point with |T| < 1 infinitesimally; accordingly the covering is not admissible. `rigidToAdic_test_Sp_compat` (compatibility): For A = K⟨T⟩ the underlying space of r_K(Sp A) is Tau Ceti's ValuationSpectrum.spa of the power-bounded subring of the complete ring K⟨T⟩ (Huber.Pair.powerBounded), which Spa-of-the-completion identifies with TauCeti.ValuationSpectrum.closedPolydisc 1 K (defined on the uncompleted restricted series ring). A definition using the smaller plus ring A⁺ = integral closure of K° + A°° fails: Spa(A, A⁺) contains the extra point x_{>1} with |T(x_{>1})| > 1 (Wedhorn Example 7.57). `rigidToAdic_test_bidisc` (computation): r_K(Sp K⟨T₁⟩ ×_{Sp K} Sp K⟨T₂⟩) ≅ Spa(K⟨T₁, T₂⟩, K°⟨T₁, T₂⟩) ≅ r_K(Sp K⟨T₁⟩) ×_{Spa K} r_K(Sp K⟨T₂⟩). *Source:* Huber94 §4, Prop. 4.3, p. 541. *Needs:* R1.7, R1.1, R0.40, R1.47, R0.57, R1.4, R0.45.

**R1.32 r_K is fully faithful, generates the lft adic spaces, restricts to an equivalence on quasi-separated objects, and detects affinoids** (theorem). Let K be complete of rank one. (i) r_K: Rig_K → Adic_K is fully faithful (Huber 1994 4.5(ii)). (ii) The smallest saturated full subcategory of adic spaces over Spa(K, K°) containing the image of r_K (closed under open subspaces, open covers and isomorphism) consists of the adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°) (4.5(iii)). (iii) r_K restricts to an equivalence between quasi-separated rigid-analytic spaces over K and quasi-separated adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°) (4.5(iv); Huber 1996 (1.1.11), also over a rigid base X and r_K(X)). (iv) For a rigid space X, r_K(X) is affinoid iff X is affinoid (4.5(v)). *Hypotheses:* K complete of rank one; in (iii) both sides quasi-separated. *Source:* Huber94 §4, Prop. 4.5(ii)–(v), p. 543. *Needs:* R1.31, R1.1, R0.35, R1.49, R1.7.

**R1.33 The rigid and adic ringed topoi agree: ρ_X induces an equivalence Shv(r_K(X)) ≃ Shv(X)** (theorem). Let K be complete of rank one and X a rigid-analytic space over K. The morphism of ringed sites ρ_X: (|r_K(X)|, O_{r_K(X)}) → (X, O_X) induces an equivalence of ringed topoi (Shv(r_K(X)), O_{r_K(X)}) ≃ (Shv(X), O_X) (Huber 1994 Proposition 4.5(i)). For X = Sp(A) this is Huber 1993 Corollary 4.4: sheaves for the Grothendieck topology of rational subdomains of Max A are the same as sheaves on the topological space Spa(A, A°). *Hypotheses:* K complete of rank one. *Source:* Huber94 §4, Prop. 4.5(i), p. 543. *Needs:* R1.31, R1.47, R1.4, R1.7.

**R1.34 r_K(X^an) ≅ X^ad: the adic space of the rigid analytification is the adic analytification** (comparison). Let K be complete of rank one and X a K-scheme locally of finite type. There is a unique isomorphism r_K(X^an) ≅ X^ad over Spa(K, K°) compatible with the maps to X (i_X ∘ ρ_{X^an} and q_X); it is natural in X and commutes with fibre products. *Hypotheses:* K complete of rank one; X locally of finite type over K (no separatedness). *Source:* Huber94 §4, Rem. 4.6(i), p. 543. *Needs:* R1.31, R1.9, R1.15, R1.16, R1.19, R1.20.

**R1.35 Separated, partially proper and proper morphisms of rigid spaces versus their adic counterparts (Huber 1996 1.3.19)** (comparison). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of rigid-analytic spaces over K (K complete of rank one), with Kiehl's notions: separated (diagonal a closed immersion), partially proper (relative compactness of affinoid pieces, BGR/Kiehl) and proper (= quasi-compact and partially proper, R1.7). (i) f is separated iff r_K(f) is separated (R0.61). (ii) If f is partially proper then r_K(f) is partially proper (R0.65); the converse holds when K° is discretely valued. (iii) If f is proper then r_K(f) is proper (R0.63); the converse holds when K° is discretely valued. (iv) If f is locally quasi-finite, the converses in (ii)–(iii) hold for arbitrary K (Huber 1996 Lemma 1.5.9, R0.91). *Hypotheses:* K complete of rank one; the converses of (ii)–(iii) need K° discrete in the source. the proofs of (i) and of the converses are in Huber's habilitation [Hu4, 3.11.21] and Lütkebohmert [L1], neither read. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Rem. 1.3.19, pp. 59-60. *Needs:* R1.31, R1.7, R0.61, R0.65, R0.63, R0.69, R0.92, R0.88.

**R1.36 Rigid and adic étale, unramified and smooth morphisms agree under Huber's functor** (comparison). Let K be a complete rank-one nonarchimedean field and f: Y → X a morphism of rigid analytic spaces over K (R1.7), with r_K(f): r_K(Y) → r_K(X) the morphism of adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa K given by Huber's functor (R1.31). Call f étale (resp. unramified) in the rigid sense if for every point y of Y the local homomorphism O_{X,f(y)} → O_{Y,y} is flat and unramified (resp. unramified); for affinoids Y = Sp B → X = Sp A this means that A → B is flat and the universal finite differential module Ω^f_{B/A} vanishes (resp. that Ω^f_{B/A} = 0). Call f smooth in the rigid sense if Y has an admissible covering by affinoid opens V, each mapping into an affinoid open W of X and admitting an étale morphism in the rigid sense V → 𝔹^n_W over W to a relative closed polydisc; this is equivalent to Huber's pointwise Definition 1.7.10(ii) (for every point y, affinoid neighbourhoods V of y and W of f(y) with such a factorisation), see the last proof step. Then: (a) r_K(f) is étale in Huber's sense (R0.100) iff f is étale in the rigid sense; (b) r_K(f) is unramified (R0.98) iff f is unramified in the rigid sense; (c) r_K(f) is smooth (R0.99) iff f is smooth in the rigid sense. In particular, for K-affinoid algebras A → B the morphism Spa(B, B°) → Spa(A, A°) is étale iff B ≅ A⟨T₁,…,Tₙ⟩/(G₁,…,Gₙ) with det(∂Gᵢ/∂Tⱼ) invertible in B (a special étale presentation). (a) and (c) are Huber 1996, Definition 1.7.10 and Proposition 1.7.11 (printed p. 89); (b) is not in Huber's proposition (Diao–Lan–Liu–Zhu Remark 3.2.15 cite 1.7.11 for it) and is proved here from Huber 1.6.8 and de Jong–van der Put §3.1. *Hypotheses:* K complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value of rank one. f: Y → X a morphism of rigid analytic spaces over K; r_K is Huber's functor to adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa K. Rigid étale and unramified are defined through the local rings of the rigid spaces (de Jong–van der Put §3.1); rigid smooth is 'locally étale over a relative closed polydisc'. *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Def. 1.7.10 and Prop. 1.7.11, p. 89. *Needs:* R1.7, R1.31, R0.100, R0.98, R0.99, R0.114, R0.108, R0.93, R0.117, R1.6, R0.51, R0.122, R0.103, R0.104, R0.106, R0.109, R0.111, R1.1.

### Analytic groups and invariant differentials

**R1.37 Analytic groups: analytification of group schemes, finite locally free group schemes, abelian varieties and isogenies** (construction `AdicSpace.analytificationFunctor.monoidal`). Let Sch^{lft}_K be the category of K-schemes locally of finite type (a full subcategory of Over(Spec K) closed under finite products, with its cartesian monoidal structure) and Adic^{lft}_K the category of adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa K = Spa(K, K°), which has finite products X ×_{Spa K} Y (R0.42) and terminal object Spa K. Because (·)^ad preserves finite products (R1.20), it is a monoidal functor and carries group objects to group objects: a group scheme (G, m, e, i) locally of finite type over K gives the analytic group G^ad with multiplication m^ad, unit e^ad: Spa K → G^ad and inverse i^ad, and a homomorphism φ gives the homomorphism φ^ad; commutative group schemes give commutative group objects. Relatively, for a group scheme G → Y locally of finite type and S → Y as in R1.13, G ×_Y S is a group object over S. Properties: (a) for an affinoid Spa(D, D⁺) over Spa K, the group of its points in G^ad is G(D); (b) if G is finite locally free of rank d over Y then G^ad → Y^ad is finite, with O over each affinoid finite projective of rank d; (c) if A is an abelian variety over K then A^ad is a commutative analytic group that is proper, smooth of dimension dim A and connected over Spa K; (d) if φ: A → B is an isogeny (a finite surjective homomorphism) then φ^ad is a finite surjective homomorphism whose kernel A^ad ×_{B^ad, e^ad} Spa K is (ker φ)^ad, a finite analytic group. *API* (on `AdicSpace.analytificationFunctor`): `monoidal, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.mul_def, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.one_def, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.inv_def, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.map, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.pointsMulEquiv, AdicSpace.analytificationCommGrp, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.relative, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.isFinite_of_finiteLocallyFree, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.abelianVariety_isProper, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.abelianVariety_smooth, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.isogeny_isFinite, AdicSpace.analytificationGrp.kernelIso`. *Tests:* `analytificationGrp_test_trivial` (degenerate): The trivial group scheme Spec K analytifies to the trivial analytic group Spa K. `analytificationGrp_test_Ga_points` (computation): For G = G_a (Tau Ceti `AdditiveGroup.groupScheme`) and an affinoid Spa(D, D⁺) over Spa K, G^ad(Spa(D, D⁺)) = (D, +) as a group. `analytificationGrp_test_unitCircle` (non-example): The unit circle {|T| = 1} = Spa(K⟨T, T⁻¹⟩, K°⟨T, T⁻¹⟩) is an open analytic subgroup of G_m^ad that is not the analytification of any algebraic subgroup of G_m; analytic groups have more subgroups than algebraic ones, so the analytification functor on group objects is not essentially surjective onto open subgroups. `analytificationGrp_test_alphaP` (compatibility): In characteristic p, α_p = Spec K[T]/(T^p) with additive law analytifies to the one-point, non-reduced finite analytic group Spa(K[T]/(T^p), K°+ (T)) whose coordinate ring is the Hopf algebra K[T]/(T^p) (an object of Tau Ceti `FiniteLocallyFreeBicommutativeHopfAlgCat`): finite flat analytic groups need not be étale. `analytificationGrp_test_abelianVariety` (characterisation): For an abelian variety A over K (Tau Ceti `AlgebraicGeometry.AbelianVariety`), A^ad is a commutative analytic group, proper and smooth of dimension dim A over Spa K. *Source:* Conrad-MR §2, p. 11 (multiplication by n on the smooth locus over an artin base). *Needs:* R1.15, R1.13, R1.20, R1.16, R1.21, R1.28, R1.25, R1.26, R1.18, R0.42.

**R1.38 Analytification of quotient maps by finite locally free subgroups: (A/G)^ad ≅ A^ad/G^ad** (theorem). Let π: A → B be a homomorphism of group schemes locally of finite type over K that is finite locally free and surjective, with kernel G = A ×_{B, e} Spec K (a finite locally free subgroup scheme) — for instance the quotient A → A/G of an abelian variety by a finite locally free subgroup scheme (AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A3) or the isogeny E → E/C of an elliptic curve by a finite locally free subgroup (the Tau Ceti roadmap `ModularCurves`). Then π^ad: A^ad → B^ad is a finite locally free surjective homomorphism of analytic groups with kernel G^ad; the morphism A^ad ×_{Spa K} G^ad → A^ad ×_{B^ad} A^ad, (a, g) ↦ (a, a·g), is an isomorphism; and B^ad is the quotient of A^ad by the right action of G^ad in Adic^{lft}_K: for every adic space Z locally of finite type over Spa K, composition with π^ad identifies Hom(B^ad, Z) with the morphisms h: A^ad → Z satisfying h(a·g) = h(a). In this sense (A/G)^ad ≅ A^ad/G^ad. *Hypotheses:* π finite locally free and surjective with kernel G; the algebraic quotient is an input (constructed by AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli A3 for abelian schemes and by the Tau Ceti roadmap ModularCurves, Layer 2B for elliptic curves). the quotient property is proved against test objects locally of finite type over Spa K; the existence of quotients of analytic groups by finite flat analytic subgroups not arising from algebra is not claimed. *Source:* Conrad-RA §4.2, Cor. 4.2.5. *Needs:* R1.37, R1.21, R1.18, R1.20, R1.11.

- **R1.39** Invariant differentials of an analytified group: ω_{G^ad/Y^ad} ≅ (ω_{G/Y})^ad. *Source:* Stacks Tag 047I (Groupoids, Lem. 39.6.3).

### Dimension of affinoid domains and the rigid identity principle

- **R1.40** Every maximal ideal of a K-affinoid domain has height equal to its dimension. *Source:* Conrad-IC §2.1, Lem. 2.1.5.

- **R1.41** Identity principle on connected normal rigid spaces. *Source:* Conrad-IC §2.1, Lem. 2.1.4.

- **R1.42** If T generates an open ideal then every T^n·U is a neighbourhood of 0: Huber's 'M·A open' gives a Tau Ceti weight family. *Source:* Huber94 §1, Lem. 1.1.

- **R1.43** Extending a valuation along a finitely generated algebra with value group in the divisible hull. *Source:* Wedhorn §8.7, proof of Prop. 8.63.

- **R1.44** Fibre products of schemes are fibre products of locally ringed spaces. *Source:* Stacks Tag 01I4 (Schemes, Lem. 26.6.7).

- **R1.45** Finite coverings of Max A by rational subdomains are refined by standard rational coverings (Huber's product trick on classical points). *Source:* KL15 §2.5, Lem. 2.5.10.

- **R1.46** Morphisms from a rigid space to a finite-type affine K-scheme are K-algebra maps on global sections (Conrad, Lemma A.1.1). *Source:* Conrad-IC Appendix A.1, Lem. A.1.1.

**R1.47 Classical points of Spa(A, A°) for a K-affinoid algebra: constructible density, and rational subsets ↔ rational subdomains of Max A (Huber 1993 §4)** (theorem). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value of rank one and A a K-affinoid algebra. For m ∈ Max A the field A/m is finite over K, so |·| extends uniquely to A/m; the composite |·|_m lies in Spa(A, A°), and m ↦ |·|_m is an injection Max A → Spa(A, A°) whose image is the set of classical points (points x with k(x) finite over K). Let L_A = {v ∈ Spv A : v(a) ≤ 1 for a ∈ A°, v(a) < 1 for a ∈ A°°}. Then: (i) L_A is the closure of Max A in the constructible topology of Spv A (Huber 1993 Theorem 4.1); (ii) Max A is dense in the constructible topology of Spa(A, A°) (Corollary 4.2); (iii) two constructible subsets of Spa(A, A°) with the same classical points are equal (Corollary 4.3); (iv) for every rational subset U of Spa(A, A°), U ∩ Max A is a rational subdomain of Max A, and U ↦ U ∩ Max A is a bijection from the rational subsets of Spa(A, A°) onto the rational subdomains of Max A, preserving inclusions and finite intersections, with a finite family of rational subsets covering U iff their traces cover U ∩ Max A. *Hypotheses:* K complete, nontrivially valued, rank one; A a K-affinoid algebra with plus ring A° (the statement is not claimed for other rings of integral elements). 'rational subdomain' in the sense of BGR 7.2.3, as in R1.2. *Source:* Huber93 §4, before Thm 4.1, p. 471. *Needs:* R0.50, R1.2, R1.4.

- **R1.48** Restricted power series are flat over polynomials for strongly noetherian Tate rings. *Source:* Zavyalov24 §6, proof of Lem. 6.7.

**R1.49 Affinoid opens locally of finite type over a noetherian-type affinoid are of topologically finite type (Huber 1994 Proposition 3.6)** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of adic spaces locally of finite type (R0.35) and U ⊆ X, V ⊆ Y open affinoid subspaces with f(U) ⊆ V, and assume that O_Y(V) has a noetherian ring of definition or is a strongly noetherian Tate ring. Then the morphism of affinoid rings (O_Y(V), O⁺_Y(V)) → (O_X(U), O⁺_X(U)) is of topologically finite type. In particular, for an affinoid adic space Spa(B, B⁺) locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°) (K complete of rank one), B is a K-affinoid algebra and B⁺ = B° (R1.1). *Hypotheses:* O_Y(V) of Huber's noetherian type; U and V arbitrary open affinoids (not just members of a chosen cover). *Source:* Huber94 §3, Prop. 3.6, p. 535. *Needs:* R0.35, R1.1.

**R1.50 A K-scheme locally of finite type is connected iff its rigid analytification is connected** (theorem). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value of rank one and X a K-scheme locally of finite type, with rigid analytification i_X: X^an → X (R1.9). Then X is connected if and only if X^an is connected (X^an has no admissible covering by two disjoint nonempty admissible opens). The core case: if X is normal and connected, then X^an is connected. More precisely, the irreducible components of X^an are the analytifications Z^an of the irreducible components Z of X (Conrad 1999, Theorem 2.3.1). *Hypotheses:* K complete, nontrivially valued, rank one. X locally of finite type over K; no separatedness or quasi-compactness assumed. *Source:* Conrad-IC §2.3, Thm 2.3.1, p. 16. *Needs:* R1.9, R1.7, R1.41, R1.31.


<a id="f0"></a>

## F0. Formal geometry

**F0. Noetherian formal geometry.** Depends on Mathlib's schemes and adic completion and on the Tau Ceti coherent-cohomology roadmaps named above; nothing in this roadmap precedes it. Adic rings of finite ideal type, `Spf`, formal schemes and their thickenings, adic, finite-type and proper morphisms, closed formal subschemes, completion of schemes and coherent sheaves along a closed subset, coherent formal modules, the theorem on formal functions, Grothendieck's existence theorem and the algebraisation theorem (formal GAGA), following EGA I §10 and EGA III §§4–5.


### Adic rings

**F0.1 Coherent modules on a locally noetherian formal scheme as compatible systems** (theorem). Let X be a locally noetherian formal scheme, 𝒥 an ideal of definition and X_n = (X, O_X/𝒥^{n+1}), u_{mn} : X_m → X_n (m ≤ n). (i) An O_X-module F is coherent iff it is isomorphic to lim_n F_n for an inverse system (F_n) of coherent O_{X_n}-modules with u_{mn}*(F_n) ≅ F_m for m ≤ n; the system is then isomorphic to (F ⊗ O_{X_n})_n (EGA I, 10.11.3). Thus F ↦ (F ⊗ O_{X_n})_n is an equivalence from Coh(X) to the category of such compatible systems, with quasi-inverse lim. (ii) Hom_{O_X}(F, G) ≅ lim_n Hom_{O_{X_n}}(F_n, G_n), and likewise for the Hom sheaves and for ⊗ (EGA I, 10.11.4, 10.11.7). (iii) A morphism θ : F → G of coherent modules is surjective iff θ_0 : F_0 → G_0 is (EGA I, 10.11.5). (iv) O_X and every ideal of definition are coherent (EGA I, 10.11.1). In particular, for a noetherian scheme X with a coherent ideal 𝓘, Coh(X̂_{/V(𝓘)}) is equivalent to Stacks' category Coh(X, 𝓘) of inverse systems (F_n) of coherent O_X-modules with F_n annihilated by 𝓘^n and F_{n+1}/𝓘^n F_{n+1} ≅ F_n (Stacks Tag 0EHN; the Stacks index n corresponds to X_{n−1} here). *Hypotheses:* X locally noetherian; 𝒥 an ideal of definition. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.11.3) Théorème, p. 204. *Needs:* F0.38, F0.9, F0.18, F0.20, F0.36, F0.2, F0.30, F0.60.

**F0.2 Adic rings of finite ideal type and their ideals of definition** (definition `IsAdicRing`). Let A be a commutative topological ring. An ideal J of A is an ideal of definition when J is open and every neighbourhood of 0 contains J^n for some n ≥ 1 (EGA 0, 7.1.2). The ring A is an adic ring of finite ideal type, written IsAdicRing A, when there is a finitely generated ideal I of A such that the topology of A is the I-adic topology (Mathlib IsAdic I: the powers I^n are open and form a basis of neighbourhoods of 0) and A is I-adically separated and complete (Mathlib IsAdicComplete I A). An adic noetherian ring is an adic ring of finite ideal type whose underlying ring is noetherian; then every ideal of definition is finitely generated. Conventions: completeness always includes separatedness; the topology is part of the data, so k⟦t⟧ with the discrete topology and k⟦t⟧ with the t-adic topology are different adic rings; morphisms of adic rings are continuous ring homomorphisms. For I as above, an ideal J is an ideal of definition if and only if IsAdic J holds, if and only if J is open and J^n ⊆ I for some n (EGA 0, 7.1.4(i)); so every statement below may use any ideal of definition. Two of the lemmas are Mathlib's `IsAdic.isTopologicallyNilpotent_iff_mem_radical` and `IsAdic.isLinearTopology`; the class itself bundles Mathlib's `IsAdic` and `IsAdicComplete`. *API* (on `IsAdicRing`): `exists_isAdic, isAdic_iff_of_isAdic, isAdicComplete_of_isAdic, le_jacobson_of_isAdic, isTopologicallyNilpotent_iff, radical_isAdic, continuous_iff_map_le_radical, quotient, isHuberRing, of_isLocalRing, adicCompletion`. *Tests:* `IsAdicRing.padicInt` (computation): ℤ_[p] with its p-adic topology satisfies IsAdicRing and IsNoetherianRing, and the ideal span {p} is an ideal of definition: IsAdic (Ideal.span {(p : ℤ_[p])}). `IsAdicRing.of_discreteTopology` (degenerate): Every commutative ring with the discrete topology satisfies IsAdicRing with the zero ideal as ideal of definition (⊥ is finitely generated, IsAdic ⊥ is the discrete topology, IsAdicComplete ⊥ A holds by Mathlib IsAdicComplete.bot). `not_isAdicRing_padic` (non-example): ℚ_[p] with its valuation topology does not satisfy IsAdicRing. A tempting wrong definition (“complete topological ring with an open ideal”) accepts ℚ_[p], because ℚ_[p] itself is an open ideal. `not_isAdicRing_polynomial_adic` (non-example): k[X] with the (X)-adic topology is not an adic ring (not complete), while its completion k⟦X⟧ with the (X)-adic topology is; completeness is not implied by IsAdic. `IsAdicRing.isHuberRing_test` (compatibility): For IsAdicRing A with finitely generated ideal of definition I, the Tau Ceti PairOfDefinition with ring of definition ⊤ and ideal of definition I (transported to ⊤) exists, so TauCeti.Huber.IsHuberRing A holds. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.1.2) Définition, p. 60.

- **F0.3** Noetherianity of adically complete rings and of adic completions. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.2.6) Corollaire.

**F0.4 The completed localisation A_{f} of an adic ring** (construction `AdicRing.CompletedAway`). Let A be an adic ring of finite ideal type with ideal of definition I and let f ∈ A. The completed localisation is A_{f} := AdicCompletion (I·A_f) A_f, where A_f = Localization.Away f, with the I·A_{f}-adic topology; equivalently A_{f} = lim_n (A ⧸ I^{n+1})_f (EGA 0, 7.6.1–7.6.2, 7.6.15). It does not depend on the choice of I. The canonical map A → A_{f} is continuous and sends f to a unit; it has the universal property: every continuous ring homomorphism u : A → B into a complete separated linearly topologised ring B with u(f) a unit factors uniquely through a continuous homomorphism A_{f} → B (EGA 0, 7.6.6). A_{f} is an adic ring of finite ideal type with ideal of definition I·A_{f}, and A_{f} ⧸ I^{n+1}A_{f} ≅ (A ⧸ I^{n+1})_f; if A is noetherian then A_{f} is noetherian and flat over A (EGA 0, 7.6.11, 7.6.13). For g a multiple of f in A (g ∈ √(fA) mod I suffices) there is a unique continuous A-algebra map A_{f} → A_{g}. Tau Ceti's `completionLocalization` and `existsUnique_continuous_ringHom_completion_locTopology` give A⟨T/s⟩ and its universal property for every Huber ring; this target is the case T = {1}, s = f with the carrier `AdicCompletion`, and its content is the adic-ring structure and the comparison with that construction. *API* (on `AdicRing`): `CompletedAway, CompletedAway.algebraMap_continuous, CompletedAway.lift, CompletedAway.lift_comp_algebraMap, CompletedAway.hom_ext, CompletedAway.quotientEquiv, CompletedAway.map, CompletedAway.flat, CompletedAway.isLocalRing_colim`. *Tests:* `completedAway_one` (degenerate): AdicRing.CompletedAway A 1 ≃ A as topological A-algebras. `completedAway_eq_zero_iff` (characterisation): AdicRing.CompletedAway A f is the zero ring iff Ideal.Quotient.mk I f is nilpotent (every open prime contains f). `completedAway_quotient` (compatibility): For every n, AdicRing.CompletedAway A f ⧸ I^(n+1)·A_{f} ≃ Localization.Away (Ideal.Quotient.mk (I^(n+1)) f) as A-algebras. `completedAway_powerSeries_X` (computation): For A = k[X]⟦T⟧ (T-adic; the completion of k[X, T] along (T)) and f = X: CompletedAway A X ≃ (Localization.Away X : k[X]-algebra)⟦T⟧ with the T-adic topology. `not_completedAway_eq_localization` (non-example): For A = ℤ_p⟦T⟧ with I = (p, T) and f = T, CompletedAway A T = 0 whereas Localization.Away T ≠ 0; the completed localisation is not the ordinary localisation. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.6.6), p. 73. *Needs:* F0.2, F0.3.

- **F0.5** Restricted power series over an adic noetherian ring. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.5.1).

- **F0.6** Adic algebras topologically of finite type over an adic noetherian ring. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.5.5) Prop..

**F0.7 Completed tensor product of adic rings of finite ideal type** (construction `AdicRing.CompletedTensorProduct`). Let φ : A → B and ψ : A → C be continuous homomorphisms of adic rings of finite ideal type, with ideals of definition I_B ⊆ B and I_C ⊆ C. Let J ⊆ B ⊗_A C be the ideal generated by I_B ⊗ 1 and 1 ⊗ I_C (finitely generated). The completed tensor product is B ⊗̂_A C := AdicCompletion J (B ⊗_A C) with its J-adic topology. It is an adic ring of finite ideal type (ideal of definition J·(B ⊗̂_A C)), independent of the choice of I_B, I_C, and B ⊗̂_A C ⧸ J^{n+1} ≅ (B ⧸ I_B^{n+1}) ⊗_{A} (C ⧸ I_C^{n+1}) modulo J^{n+1}. Universal property (EGA 0, 7.7.6, used in EGA I, 10.7.2): the canonical continuous maps B → B ⊗̂_A C ← C induce a bijection between continuous A-algebra maps B ⊗̂_A C → D and pairs of continuous A-algebra maps (B → D, C → D), for every adic ring D of finite ideal type (more generally every complete separated linearly topologised A-algebra D). Warning: B ⊗̂_A C need not be noetherian when B and C are (EGA I, 10.7.4); it is noetherian when B is topologically of finite type over A and C is noetherian (EGA I, 10.13.5). *API* (on `AdicRing`): `CompletedTensorProduct, CompletedTensorProduct.inl, CompletedTensorProduct.inr, CompletedTensorProduct.lift, CompletedTensorProduct.lift_comp_inl, CompletedTensorProduct.hom_ext, CompletedTensorProduct.quotientEquiv, CompletedTensorProduct.map, CompletedTensorProduct.isNoetherianRing, CompletedTensorProduct.restrictedEquiv`. *Tests:* `completedTensor_powerSeries` (computation): For A = ℤ_[p], B = ℤ_[p]⟦S⟧ and C = ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ with their (p,S)- and (p,T)-adic topologies, B ⊗̂_A C ≃ ℤ_[p]⟦S, T⟧ (MvPowerSeries (Fin 2) ℤ_[p] with the (p, S, T)-adic topology). `completedTensor_self_left` (degenerate): A ⊗̂_A C ≃ C as topological A-algebras. `completedTensor_discrete` (compatibility): If A, B, C are discrete (all ideals of definition 0) then B ⊗̂_A C = B ⊗_A C (Mathlib TensorProduct) with the discrete topology. `completedTensor_lift_unique` (characterisation): For D = ℤ_[p] and the two continuous maps S ↦ 0, T ↦ p, the induced map ℤ_[p]⟦S,T⟧ → ℤ_[p] is the unique continuous one with these values. `completedTensor_not_tensor` (non-example): ℤ_[p]⟦S⟧ ⊗_{ℤ_[p]} ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ (uncompleted) is not complete for J; the canonical map to ℤ_[p]⟦S,T⟧ is not surjective (Σ S^n T^n is not in its image). *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.7.1), p. 75. *Needs:* F0.2, F0.6, F0.5.

- **F0.8** Inverse limits of Mittag-Leffler systems of abelian groups. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. 0, (13.2.2) Prop., p. 66 (Publ. IHES 11).

- **F0.9** Finite modules over an adic noetherian ring as compatible systems. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (7.2.9) Prop..

### The formal spectrum

**F0.10 The formal spectrum Spf A of an adic ring** (construction `FormalScheme.Spf`). Let A be an adic ring of finite ideal type (F0.2) with ideal of definition I. The formal spectrum Spf A is the locally ringed space defined as follows. Its underlying space is the closed subspace of Spec A consisting of the open prime ideals, which is V(I) = PrimeSpectrum.zeroLocus I and is homeomorphic to Spec(A ⧸ I^{n+1}) for every n via the quotient maps (EGA I, 10.1.1–10.1.2). For each n let O_n be the structure sheaf of Spec(A ⧸ I^{n+1}) transported to V(I); the structure sheaf is O_{Spf A} := lim_n O_n, the limit in sheaves of commutative rings along the reduction maps. Then: Γ(Spf A, O) ≅ A (EGA I, 10.1.3); for f ∈ A and D(f) := D(f) ∩ V(I), Γ(D(f), O) ≅ A_{f} (the completed localisation, F0.4) and (D(f), O|_{D(f)}) ≅ Spf A_{f} (EGA I, 10.1.4); the stalk at an open prime p is colim_{f ∉ p} A_{f}, a local ring with residue field Frac(A ⧸ p), noetherian when A is noetherian (EGA I, 10.1.6). A continuous homomorphism φ : B → A induces a morphism of locally ringed spaces Spf φ : Spf A → Spf B, q ↦ φ⁻¹(q) (EGA I, 10.2.1). Convention: Spf A is a locally ringed space (Mathlib LocallyRingedSpace); the topology of the rings of sections is not part of the data (see F0.16), and for quasi-compact open U the ring Γ(U, O) carries the limit topology of the discrete rings Γ(U, O_n), recorded as an API item. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `Spf, Spf.mem_iff, Spf.homeomorphSpecQuotient, Spf.globalSectionsIso, Spf.basicOpen, Spf.basicOpenSectionsIso, Spf.restrictBasicOpenIso, Spf.stalk_isLocalRing, Spf.stalk_isNoetherianRing, Spf.map, Spf.fromSpecQuotient, Spf.sectionsTopology`. *Tests:* `Spf_padicInt_points` (computation): The underlying space of Spf ℤ_[p] (p-adic topology) is a single point, the prime (p), and the global sections are ℤ_[p]. `Spf_discrete_eq_Spec` (compatibility): For a ring R with the discrete topology, Spf R ≅ Spec.locallyRingedSpaceObj R as locally ringed spaces (Mathlib Spec). `Spf_globalSections` (characterisation): For every adic ring of finite ideal type A, Γ(Spf A, ⊤) ≅ A as rings, compatibly with Spf.map. `Spf_padicInt_ne_Spec` (non-example): Spf ℤ_[p] (p-adic) is not isomorphic to Spec ℤ_[p]: the latter has two points. A wrong definition taking all primes of A (not only open ones) fails this test. `Spf_basicOpen_eq_empty` (degenerate): For A = ℤ_[p] and f = p, the basic open D(p) of Spf A is empty and CompletedAway A p = 0. `Spf_powerSeries_polynomial_sections` (computation): For A = k[X]⟦T⟧ with the T-adic topology, Γ(D(X), O_{Spf A}) ≅ (Localization.Away X)⟦T⟧ and the underlying space is homeomorphic to Spec k[X]. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.1.2) Définition, p. 181. *Needs:* F0.2, F0.4.

- **F0.11** Spf A depends only on the topology of A. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.1.1).

**F0.12 Morphisms into Spf A and automatic continuity** (theorem). Let X be a formal scheme (F0.16) and A an adic ring of finite ideal type with ideal of definition I. Taking global sections gives a bijection between morphisms of locally ringed spaces X → Spf A and ring homomorphisms φ : A → Γ(X, O_X) that are continuous on affine formal opens, i.e. such that for every affine formal open U ≅ Spf B of X the composite A → Γ(U, O_X) ≅ B is continuous; equivalently φ(a) vanishes in the residue field κ(x) for all a ∈ I and x ∈ X (EGA I, 10.2.2, 10.4.6). In particular, for adic rings A, B of finite ideal type, Hom_LRS(Spf B, Spf A) ≃ {continuous ring homomorphisms A → B}, so Spf is a fully faithful contravariant functor from adic rings of finite ideal type (with continuous homomorphisms) to locally ringed spaces, and every morphism of locally ringed spaces between formal schemes is continuous on rings of sections (it is a morphism of topologically ringed spaces in the sense of EGA I, 10.4.5). *Hypotheses:* X a formal scheme; A adic of finite ideal type (the ideal of definition I is finitely generated, which is used for automatic continuity). *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, after (10.2.2), p. 183. *Needs:* F0.10, F0.4, F0.2, F0.16, F0.58.

### Formal schemes, ideals of definition and thickenings

- **F0.13** A formal scheme of finite ideal type is the colimit of its thickenings along an ideal of definition of finite type. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.6.2) Prop..

**F0.14 Morphisms of formal schemes of finite ideal type as compatible systems of morphisms of thickenings** (theorem). Let X, Y be formal schemes of finite ideal type (no noetherian hypothesis) with ideals of definition of finite type 𝒥 ⊆ O_X and 𝒦 ⊆ O_Y, and X_n = (X, O_X/𝒥^{n+1}), Y_n = (Y, O_Y/𝒦^{n+1}) with ι^X_n, ι^Y_n as in F0.13. (i) If f : X → Y satisfies f*(𝒦)·O_X ⊆ 𝒥, then f*(𝒦^{n+1})·O_X ⊆ 𝒥^{n+1} for all n, and there are unique morphisms of schemes f_n : X_n → Y_n with ι^Y_n ∘ f_n = f ∘ ι^X_n; they satisfy f_n ∘ (X_m → X_n) = (Y_m → Y_n) ∘ f_m for m ≤ n (EGA I, (10.5.6), (10.6.7)). (ii) The map f ↦ (f_n)_n is a bijection from the set of morphisms f : X → Y with f*(𝒦)·O_X ⊆ 𝒥 onto the set of sequences of morphisms of schemes f_n : X_n → Y_n with f_n ∘ (X_m → X_n) = (Y_m → Y_n) ∘ f_m for all m ≤ n. (iii) Relative form: let S be a formal scheme of finite ideal type with an ideal of definition ℒ of finite type, S_n = (S, O_S/ℒ^{n+1}), and p : X → S, q : Y → S morphisms with p*(ℒ)·O_X ⊆ 𝒥 and q*(ℒ)·O_Y = 𝒦 (Y adic over S, with its ideal of definition ℒO_Y). Then every S-morphism u : X → Y satisfies u*(𝒦)·O_X ⊆ 𝒥, and u ↦ (u_n) is a bijection Hom_S(X, Y) ≃ lim_n Hom_{S_n}(X_n, Y_n), the limit along the maps Hom_{S_n}(X_n, Y_n) → Hom_{S_m}(X_m, Y_m), u_n ↦ u_n ×_{S_n} S_m (EGA I, (10.6.11)). In particular, for X and Y adic over S and 𝒥 = ℒO_X, 𝒦 = ℒO_Y, Hom_S(X, Y) ≃ lim_n Hom_{S_n}(X_n, Y_n) (Fujiwara–Kato I, Exercise I.1.7). For locally noetherian X, Y, S these are parts (ii)–(iii) of F0.21; a formal scheme of finite ideal type need not have a largest ideal of definition, so the ideal condition in (ii) is part of the statement. *Hypotheses:* X, Y (and S) formal schemes of finite ideal type; no noetherian hypothesis. 𝒥, 𝒦 (and ℒ) ideals of definition of finite type. (iii): p*(ℒ)·O_X ⊆ 𝒥 and q*(ℒ)·O_Y = 𝒦. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.6.7), p. 191. *Needs:* F0.13, F0.18, F0.12, F0.2, F0.16.

**F0.15 Formal schemes of finite ideal type adic over S are adic inductive systems of S_n-schemes** (theorem). Let S be a formal scheme of finite ideal type with an ideal of definition ℒ of finite type and S_n = (S, O_S/ℒ^{n+1}). A formal scheme X of finite ideal type with a morphism f : X → S is adic over S if ℒ·O_X is an ideal of definition of X (it is then of finite type; Fujiwara–Kato I, Definition 1.3.1; for locally noetherian X, S this is F0.23). An (S_n)-adic inductive system is a sequence of S_n-schemes X_n (no finiteness hypothesis) with S_n-morphisms X_m → X_n (m ≤ n) over S_m → S_n identifying X_m with X_n ×_{S_n} S_m; its morphisms are sequences of S_n-morphisms compatible with the transition maps. Then X ↦ (X_n)_n := ((X, O_X/(ℒO_X)^{n+1}))_n = (X ×_S S_n)_n is an equivalence from the category of formal schemes of finite ideal type adic over S (with S-morphisms) to the category of (S_n)-adic inductive systems. A quasi-inverse sends (X_n) to the formal scheme X attached to the adic thickening system (X_n) (F0.19) with the limit f : X → S of the structure maps; f is adic over S, with ℒ·O_X = ker(O_X → O_{X_0}) and (ℒO_X)^{n+1} = ker(O_X → O_{X_n}) (Fujiwara–Kato I, Exercise I.1.8). X is locally noetherian iff X_0 is (EGA I, (10.6.4)), and for locally noetherian S this is F0.24. In particular Hom_S(X, Y) ≃ lim_n Hom_{S_n}(X_n, Y_n) for X, Y adic over S. *Hypotheses:* S a formal scheme of finite ideal type with an ideal of definition ℒ of finite type; no noetherian hypothesis. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.12.3) Théorème, p. 206. *Needs:* F0.19, F0.13, F0.14, F0.2, F0.18, F0.16.

**F0.16 Formal schemes and locally noetherian formal schemes** (definition `FormalScheme`). A formal scheme (of finite ideal type) is a locally ringed space X such that every point has an open neighbourhood U with (U, O_X|_U) isomorphic, as a locally ringed space, to Spf A for some adic ring A of finite ideal type (F0.10); such U are the affine formal opens. X is locally noetherian if every point has an affine formal open neighbourhood Spf A with A noetherian, and noetherian if moreover its underlying space is quasi-compact (EGA I, 10.4.2). Morphisms of formal schemes are morphisms of locally ringed spaces; by F0.12 they are automatically continuous on rings of sections, so this category agrees with EGA's category of formal preschemes of this type (EGA I, 10.4.5). Pinned conventions: (1) 'scheme' means Mathlib's Scheme (EGA's préschéma; no separatedness); (2) the topology on Γ(U, O_X) for an affine formal open U is recovered, not stored: it is the J-adic topology for any finitely generated ideal J ⊆ Γ(U, O_X) whose radical is the ideal of sections vanishing at every point of U; (3) every scheme is a formal scheme (take A discrete) and Scheme → FormalScheme is fully faithful (EGA I, 10.4.8). *API*: `FormalScheme, FormalScheme.instCategory, FormalScheme.IsLocallyNoetherian, FormalScheme.IsNoetherian, FormalScheme.isLocallyNoetherian_iff_forall_affineOpens, FormalScheme.Spf_isNoetherian_iff, FormalScheme.isBasis_affineOpens, FormalScheme.restrict, FormalScheme.IsAffineOpen.isoSpf, FormalScheme.sectionsTopology_eq_adic, FormalScheme.ofScheme, FormalScheme.stalk_isNoetherianRing`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.ofScheme_Spec` (compatibility): For every commutative ring R, FormalScheme.ofScheme (Spec R) ≅ Spf R with R discrete, and FormalScheme.ofScheme is fully faithful. `FormalScheme.Spf_padicInt_isNoetherian` (computation): Spf ℤ_[p] is a noetherian formal scheme and is not in the essential image of FormalScheme.ofScheme (its global sections ℤ_[p] with a single point contradict Spec ℤ_[p] having two points). `FormalScheme.not_isLocallyNoetherian_Spf_OC` (non-example): For O_C the p-adic completion of the integral closure of ℤ_p in an algebraic closure of ℚ_p (p-adic topology), Spf O_C is a formal scheme that is not locally noetherian. A wrong definition of local noetherianity via the underlying space (here a point) accepts it. `FormalScheme.Spf_restrict_basicOpen` (characterisation): For IsAdicRing A and f ∈ A, the open D(f) of Spf A is an affine formal open with Γ ≅ AdicRing.CompletedAway A f. `FormalScheme.Hom_Spf_powerSeries` (computation): Morphisms Spf ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p]⟦S⟧ correspond to elements of the maximal ideal (p, T) of ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ (image of S). `FormalScheme.formalTorus_baseChange` (compatibility): The formal torus Spf O_C⟨T, T⁻¹⟩ over O_C (p-adic topology) is a formal scheme of finite ideal type that is not locally noetherian, and it is the fibre product of the noetherian formal torus Spf ℤ_[p]⟨T, T⁻¹⟩ with Spf O_C over Spf ℤ_[p] (F0.22); both lie in the same category FormalScheme. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.4.2) Définition, p. 185. *Needs:* F0.10, F0.4, F0.2.

**F0.17 Gluing formal schemes along open subschemes** (construction `FormalScheme.GlueData`). A glue datum of formal schemes is a glue datum of locally ringed spaces (Mathlib LocallyRingedSpace.GlueData: pieces U_i, open immersions V_ij → U_i, isomorphisms t_ij : V_ij ≅ V_ji with the cocycle condition) in which every U_i is a formal scheme. The glued locally ringed space X is a formal scheme; each ι_i : U_i → X is an open immersion with image covering X; X is locally noetherian if every U_i is, and noetherian if moreover the index set is finite and every U_i is noetherian. Universal property: morphisms X → Y of formal schemes correspond to families of morphisms f_i : U_i → Y agreeing on the V_ij (EGA 0, 4.1.7; EGA I, 10.4.2 is local, and EGA I, 10.7.3 constructs products by gluing affine pieces as in the scheme case). Gluing commutes with FormalScheme.ofScheme (Mathlib Scheme.GlueData). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `GlueData, GlueData.glued, GlueData.ι, GlueData.glue_condition, GlueData.desc, GlueData.ι_desc, GlueData.isLocallyNoetherian, GlueData.ofScheme`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.GlueData.projectiveLine_globalSections` (computation): For the glue datum of Spf ℤ_[p]⟨x⟩ and Spf ℤ_[p]⟨y⟩ along x ↦ y⁻¹, Γ(glued, ⊤) ≅ ℤ_[p]. `FormalScheme.GlueData.single` (degenerate): For a glue datum with one piece U (V_11 = U, t = id), glued ≅ U. `FormalScheme.GlueData.ofScheme_glued` (compatibility): For a Mathlib Scheme.GlueData D, FormalScheme.ofScheme D.glued ≅ glued of the image glue datum. `FormalScheme.GlueData.projectiveLine_not_affine` (non-example): The glued P¹ datum above is not an affine formal scheme (it is not isomorphic to Spf of its global sections ℤ_[p], which is a point). *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (4.1.7), p. 39. *Needs:* F0.16.

**F0.18 Ideals of definition of a formal scheme and the thickenings X_n** (definition `FormalScheme.IdealSheafData`). Let X be a formal scheme. An ideal-sheaf datum on X is, as in Mathlib Scheme.IdealSheafData, an ideal 𝒥(U) ⊆ Γ(U, O_X) for each affine formal open U, with 𝒥(D(f)) = 𝒥(U)·A_{f} for basic opens (it is the sheaf 𝒥(U)^Δ on U). It is an ideal of definition when 𝒥(U) is an ideal of definition of the adic ring Γ(U, O_X) for every affine formal open U; it suffices to check this on an affine formal open cover (EGA I, 10.3.3, 10.3.5, 10.5.1). For an ideal of definition 𝒥: (X, O_X/𝒥) is a scheme X_𝒥 (affine if X is, locally noetherian if X is), with a canonical morphism X_𝒥 → X which is a homeomorphism on spaces (EGA I, 10.5.2); O_X = lim_n O_X/𝒥^{n+1} (EGA I, 10.5.3). Notation: X_n := (X, O_X/𝒥^{n+1}), n ≥ 0. If X is locally noetherian: the powers 𝒥^{n+1} are ideals of definition and form a fundamental system; there is a largest ideal of definition 𝒯, the unique one with X_𝒯 reduced, 𝒯(U) = √(𝒥(U)) = sections vanishing at every point, and X_red := X_𝒯 (EGA I, 10.5.1, 10.5.4). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `IdealSheafData, IdealSheafData.IsIdealOfDefinition, IdealSheafData.isIdealOfDefinition_of_cover, thickening, thickeningι, thickeningMap, structureSheafIsoLimit, IdealSheafData.isIdealOfDefinition_pow, largestIdealOfDefinition, reduction`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.IdealSheafData.isIdealOfDefinition_Spf` (characterisation): On Spf A, J^Δ is an ideal of definition iff IsAdic J; and every ideal of definition of Spf A is of this form for a unique J. `FormalScheme.thickening_Spf` (computation): For X = Spf A and 𝒥 = J^Δ: X.thickening 𝒥 n ≅ Spec (A ⧸ J^(n+1)) as schemes. `FormalScheme.largestIdealOfDefinition_powerSeries` (computation): For X = Spf ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧, the largest ideal of definition is (p, T)^Δ and X_red ≅ Spec (ZMod p). `FormalScheme.ofScheme_idealOfDefinition` (degenerate): For a scheme Y viewed as a formal scheme, the zero ideal is an ideal of definition and Y.thickening 0 n = Y; the largest ideal of definition of a locally noetherian scheme is its nilradical. `FormalScheme.not_idealOfDefinition_maximal_OC` (non-example): On Spf O_C, the ideal of sections vanishing at the point (𝔪_C) is not an ideal of definition: 𝔪_C^n = 𝔪_C for all n, so its powers do not tend to 0. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.5.1), p. 186. *Needs:* F0.16, F0.10, F0.2, F0.4.

**F0.19 The formal scheme attached to an adic system of thickenings** (construction `FormalScheme.ThickeningSystem`). An adic thickening system is a sequence of schemes X_0 → X_1 → X_2 → ⋯ with closed immersions u_n : X_n → X_{n+1} that are homeomorphisms on underlying spaces, such that, writing O_n for the structure sheaves on the common space T and 𝒥_n = ker(O_n → O_0): (a) 𝒥_n is locally nilpotent; (b) ker(O_n → O_m) = 𝒥_n^{m+1} for m ≤ n; (c) 𝒥_1 = 𝒥_1/𝒥_1² is an O_0-module of finite type. Then the locally ringed space X := (T, lim_n O_n) is a formal scheme; the maps lim O_k → O_n are surjective; 𝒥 := ker(lim O_k → O_0) is an ideal of definition with ker(lim O_k → O_n) = 𝒥^{n+1} and 𝒥/𝒥² ≅ 𝒥_1; the canonical morphisms ι_n : X_n → X form a cocone; X is locally noetherian (resp. noetherian) when X_0 is (EGA I, 10.6.3–10.6.4). On affine opens: if X_0 = Spec A_0 then every X_n = Spec A_n and X = Spf(lim A_n), with lim A_n adic of ideal of definition ker(lim A_n → A_0) (EGA 0, 7.2.7). EGA 10.6.3 without (b)–(c) produces formal preschemes that need not be of finite ideal type; those are outside this carrier. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `ThickeningSystem, ofThickenings, ofThickenings.ι, ofThickenings.idealOfDefinition, ofThickenings.thickeningIso, ofThickenings.isLocallyNoetherian, ofThickenings.desc, ofThickenings.map`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.ofThickenings_padic` (computation): For X_n = Spec (ZMod (p^(n+1))) with the reduction maps, ofThickenings ≅ Spf ℤ_[p]. `FormalScheme.ofThickenings_const` (degenerate): For the constant system X_n = Y (identity maps, 𝒥 = 0) of a scheme Y, ofThickenings ≅ FormalScheme.ofScheme Y. `FormalScheme.ofThickenings_thickening` (compatibility): For a locally noetherian formal scheme X with ideal of definition 𝒥, ofThickenings (n ↦ X.thickening 𝒥 n) ≅ X (the converse construction, proved in F0.20). `FormalScheme.ofThickenings_affine` (characterisation): If X_0 = Spec A_0 then ofThickenings ≅ Spf (lim_n Γ(X_n)) with the ker-adic topology. `FormalScheme.ofThickenings_nonadic_excluded` (non-example): The system A_n = k[x_1,x_2,…]/((x_1,…,x_{n+1})^(n+1) + (x_{n+2},…)), n ≥ 0, does not satisfy the adic condition ker(A_n → A_m) = J_n^(m+1) (J_n = ker(A_n → A_0)); a construction that only asks locally nilpotent kernels would produce a non-adic ring outside FormalScheme. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.6.3) Prop. b), c), p. 189. *Needs:* F0.10, F0.3, F0.16, F0.18, F0.2, F0.59.

**F0.20 A locally noetherian formal scheme is the colimit of its thickenings** (theorem). Let X be a locally noetherian formal scheme and 𝒥 an ideal of definition; let X_n = (X, O_X/𝒥^{n+1}) with the canonical closed immersions X_m → X_n and morphisms ι_n : X_n → X (F0.18). Then (X_n) is an adic thickening system with X_0 locally noetherian, and (X, (ι_n)) is a colimit of the diagram (X_n) in the category of formal schemes (EGA I, 10.6.2): for every formal scheme Y and compatible morphisms g_n : X_n → Y there is a unique g : X → Y with g ∘ ι_n = g_n. Consequently the canonical morphism ofThickenings(X_n) → X is an isomorphism, and ofThickenings is an equivalence from adic thickening systems with locally noetherian X_0 to pairs (X, 𝒥) of a locally noetherian formal scheme with an ideal of definition (EGA I, 10.6.4 and the remark after it). *Hypotheses:* X locally noetherian; 𝒥 an ideal of definition (exists: take the largest one 𝒯). *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.6.2) Prop., p. 189. *Needs:* F0.18, F0.19, F0.12, F0.16.

**F0.21 Morphisms of locally noetherian formal schemes as compatible systems** (theorem). Let X, Y be locally noetherian formal schemes with ideals of definition 𝒥 ⊆ O_X and 𝒦 ⊆ O_Y, and X_n = (X, O_X/𝒥^{n+1}), Y_n = (Y, O_Y/𝒦^{n+1}). (i) For every morphism f : X → Y, f*(𝒦)·O_X ⊆ 𝒯_X, the largest ideal of definition of X (EGA I, 10.6.10(i)). (ii) If f*(𝒦)·O_X ⊆ 𝒥 then f*(𝒦^{n+1})·O_X ⊆ 𝒥^{n+1} and f induces morphisms of schemes f_n : X_n → Y_n with commuting squares (EGA I, 10.6.7); the map f ↦ (f_n) is a bijection from {f : f*(𝒦)O_X ⊆ 𝒥} onto compatible sequences (f_n) (EGA I, 10.6.9). In particular, with 𝒥 = 𝒯_X, Hom(X, Y) ≃ lim_n Hom(X_n, Y_n) (EGA I, 10.6.10(ii)). (iii) Over a locally noetherian base S with ideal of definition ℒ, f*(ℒ)O_X ⊆ 𝒥, g*(ℒ)O_Y = 𝒦 and S_n = (S, O_S/ℒ^{n+1}): Hom_S(X, Y) ≃ lim_n Hom_{S_n}(X_n, Y_n) (EGA I, 10.6.11). *Hypotheses:* X, Y (and S) locally noetherian formal schemes with chosen ideals of definition. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.6.10) Corollaire (i), p. 192. *Needs:* F0.20, F0.18, F0.12, F0.2.

### A formal scheme of finite ideal type is the colimit of its thickenings

### Morphisms of formal schemes of finite ideal type as compatible systems

**F0.22 Fibre products of formal schemes** (theorem). Let f : X → S and g : Y → S be morphisms of formal schemes (finite ideal type). The fibre product X ×_S Y exists in formal schemes; for affine formal schemes, Spf B ×_{Spf A} Spf C ≅ Spf(B ⊗̂_A C) with the projections induced by the canonical maps into the completed tensor product (EGA I, 10.7.2–10.7.3); every open cover of X, Y, S by affine formal opens gives an affine formal cover of X ×_S Y. If S, X, Y have ideals of definition of finite type ℒ, 𝒥, 𝒦 (automatic when S, X, Y are locally noetherian; needed so that the powers form fundamental systems, F0.13) with f*(ℒ)O_X ⊆ 𝒥 and g*(ℒ)O_Y ⊆ 𝒦, then X ×_S Y is the colimit in formal schemes of the schemes X_n ×_{S_n} Y_n, and the ideal generated by 𝒥 and 𝒦 is an ideal of definition of X ×_S Y whose powers are cofinal with the kernels of the X_n ×_{S_n} Y_n (EGA I, 10.7.4). If S, X, Y are locally noetherian and f is of finite type, then X ×_S Y is locally noetherian and X ×_S Y → Y is of finite type (EGA I, 10.13.5 (ii)); in general X ×_S Y need not be locally noetherian (EGA I, 10.7.4). *Hypotheses:* formal schemes of finite ideal type; local noetherianity of the product only when f is of finite type. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, proof of (10.7.2), p. 193. *Needs:* F0.7, F0.12, F0.17, F0.19, F0.6, F0.25, F0.13, F0.58.

### Adic, finite-type and proper morphisms; closed formal subschemes

**F0.23 Adic morphisms of locally noetherian formal schemes** (definition `FormalScheme.Hom.IsAdic`). A morphism f : X → Y of locally noetherian formal schemes is adic if there is an ideal of definition 𝒦 of Y such that f*(𝒦)·O_X is an ideal of definition of X; then f*(𝒦′)·O_X is an ideal of definition of X for every ideal of definition 𝒦′ of Y (EGA I, 10.12.1). An adic formal scheme over S is a locally noetherian formal S-scheme with adic structure morphism; every S-morphism between adic S-formal schemes is adic. For affine X = Spf A, Y = Spf B, f = Spf φ is adic iff I_B·A is an ideal of definition of A (IsAdic (I_B.map φ)). Notation: for ideals of definition 𝒦 of Y and 𝒥 of X with f*(𝒦)O_X ⊆ 𝒥, f_n : X_n → Y_n denotes the reduction modulo 𝒥^{n+1}, 𝒦^{n+1} (F0.21). Characterisation: f is adic iff for some such pair (𝒦, 𝒥) every square X_m → X_n over Y_m → Y_n (m ≤ n) is cartesian, i.e. X_m = X_n ×_{Y_n} Y_m; in that case 𝒥 = f*(𝒦)O_X (EGA I, 10.12.3 and the proof of 10.12.3.1). (With 𝒥 replaced by f*(𝒦)O_X for a non-adic f the quotients (X, O_X/f*(𝒦^{n+1})O_X) are not schemes — e.g. F_p⟦T⟧ on the one-point space of Spf ℤ_p⟦T⟧ — so the ideal must be an ideal of definition.) *API* (on `FormalScheme.Hom`): `IsAdic, IsAdic.forall, IsAdic.comp, IsAdic.of_comp, isAdic_iff_isPullback, reduction, IsAdic.baseChange, isAdic_ofScheme`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.Hom.isAdic_Spf_restricted` (computation): Spf.map (algebraMap ℤ_[p] ℤ_[p]⟨T⟩) : Spf ℤ_[p]⟨T⟩ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is adic. `FormalScheme.Hom.not_isAdic_Spf_powerSeries` (non-example): Spf ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is not adic; a definition requiring only continuity of the ring map (every morphism) would wrongly call it adic. `FormalScheme.Hom.isAdic_id` (degenerate): The identity of a locally noetherian formal scheme is adic, and every morphism of schemes (discrete formal schemes) is adic with 𝒦 = 0. `FormalScheme.Hom.isAdic_Spf_iff` (characterisation): For continuous φ : B →+* A of adic noetherian rings with ideal of definition K ⊆ B: (Spf.map φ).IsAdic ↔ IsAdic (K.map φ). *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.12.1), p. 206. *Needs:* F0.16, F0.18, F0.2, F0.21.

**F0.24 Adic formal S-schemes are adic inductive systems of S_n-schemes** (theorem). Let S be a locally noetherian formal scheme with ideal of definition ℒ and S_n = (S, O_S/ℒ^{n+1}). An (S_n)-adic inductive system is a sequence of locally noetherian S_n-schemes X_n with morphisms X_m → X_n over S_m → S_n identifying X_m with X_n ×_{S_n} S_m for m ≤ n; morphisms are compatible sequences of S_n-morphisms. The functor X ↦ (X ×_S S_n)_n = (X, O_X/(ℒO_X)^{n+1})_n is an equivalence from adic formal S-schemes (locally noetherian, adic structure morphism) to (S_n)-adic inductive systems with X_0 locally noetherian; its quasi-inverse sends (X_n) to its colimit (F0.19), whose structure morphism to S is adic (EGA I, 10.12.3, 10.12.3.1). In particular Hom_S(X, Y) ≃ lim_n Hom_{S_n}(X_n, Y_n) (EGA I, 10.12.3.2). *Hypotheses:* S locally noetherian formal scheme with ideal of definition ℒ; X_0 locally noetherian. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.12.3) Théorème, p. 206. *Needs:* F0.23, F0.19, F0.20, F0.21, F0.2.

### Formal schemes of finite ideal type adic over S are adic inductive systems

**F0.25 Morphisms of finite type of locally noetherian formal schemes** (definition `FormalScheme.Hom.FiniteType`). Let Y be a locally noetherian formal scheme with ideal of definition 𝒦 and f : X → Y a morphism of formal schemes. f is of finite type if X is locally noetherian, f is adic, and the reduction f_0 : (X, O_X/𝒥) → (Y, O_Y/𝒦) with 𝒥 = f*(𝒦)O_X is a morphism of schemes of finite type (Mathlib LocallyOfFiniteType and QuasiCompact) (EGA I, 10.13.1 (a), 10.13.3). Equivalent conditions: (b) X is locally noetherian and the colimit of an adic system (X_n) over (Y_n) with X_0 → Y_0 of finite type; (c) every point of Y has a noetherian affine formal neighbourhood V = Spf B such that f⁻¹(V) is a finite union of noetherian affine formal opens Spf A_i with each A_i ≅ B{T_1,…,T_r}/𝔞 topologically (EGA I, 10.13.1). The condition does not depend on 𝒦, and then every f_n is of finite type. Affine criterion: Spf A → Spf B (noetherian) is of finite type iff A is topologically a quotient of some B{T} (EGA I, 10.13.4). The local variant f locally of finite type (Huber’s notion used in R2) drops quasi-compactness of f_0. *API* (on `FormalScheme.Hom`): `FiniteType, finiteType_iff_reduction, finiteType_Spf_iff, FiniteType.comp, FiniteType.baseChange, FiniteType.isNoetherian, LocallyOfFiniteType, FiniteType.completion`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.Hom.finiteType_restricted` (computation): Spf ℤ_[p]⟨T₁,…,T_r⟩ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is of finite type, and so is Spf (ℤ_[p]⟨T⟩ ⧸ 𝔞) ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] for every ideal 𝔞. `FormalScheme.Hom.not_finiteType_powerSeries` (non-example): Spf ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is not of finite type, although its reduction Spec F_p → Spec F_p modulo the ideals of definition (p,T) and (p) is of finite type: the adicness condition is essential. `FormalScheme.Hom.finiteType_ofScheme` (compatibility): For a morphism g of locally noetherian schemes, (FormalScheme.ofScheme.map g).FiniteType ↔ LocallyOfFiniteType g ∧ QuasiCompact g. `FormalScheme.Hom.finiteType_id` (degenerate): The identity of a locally noetherian formal scheme is of finite type. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.13.1) a), p. 207. *Needs:* F0.23, F0.24, F0.6, F0.5, F0.59.

- **F0.26** Properness, finiteness and closed immersions are insensitive to thickenings. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0BPG (More on Morphisms, Lem. 37.3.4).

**F0.27 Proper morphisms of locally noetherian formal schemes** (definition `FormalScheme.Hom.IsProper`). A morphism f : X → Y of locally noetherian formal schemes is proper if f is of finite type (F0.25) and, for an ideal of definition 𝒦 of Y and 𝒥 = f*(𝒦)O_X, the reduction f_0 : (X, O_X/𝒥) → (Y, O_Y/𝒦) is a proper morphism of schemes (Mathlib IsProper) (EGA III, 3.4.1). This does not depend on 𝒦, and then every f_n : X_n → Y_n is proper. A closed subset Z of X is proper over Y if, viewed in X_0, it is proper over Y_0 (the reduced closed subscheme on Z is proper over Y_0). For a proper morphism g : X → Y of locally noetherian schemes, a closed subset Y′ ⊆ Y and X′ = g⁻¹(Y′), the completion ĝ : X̂_{/X′} → Ŷ_{/Y′} is proper (EGA III, 3.4.1). *API* (on `FormalScheme.Hom`): `IsProper, isProper_iff_reduction, IsProper.comp, IsProper.baseChange, IsProper.completion, FormalScheme.IsProperSupport, IsProper.isClosedMap`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.Hom.isProper_completion_projectiveLine` (computation): The completion of P¹_{ℤ_[p]} along its special fibre is proper over Spf ℤ_[p]. `FormalScheme.Hom.not_isProper_restricted` (non-example): Spf ℤ_[p]⟨T⟩ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is of finite type but not proper. A definition asking only that the underlying continuous map be closed would accept it (the underlying space A¹_{F_p} → point is closed) since universal closedness is what fails. `FormalScheme.Hom.isProper_ofScheme` (compatibility): For g a morphism of locally noetherian schemes, (FormalScheme.ofScheme.map g).IsProper ↔ AlgebraicGeometry.IsProper g (Mathlib IsProper already implies QuasiCompact, via UniversallyClosed). `FormalScheme.Hom.isProper_id` (degenerate): The identity of a locally noetherian formal scheme is proper. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (3.4.1), p. 119. *Needs:* F0.25, F0.23, F0.18, F0.26.

**F0.28 Closed formal subschemes and closed immersions** (construction `FormalScheme.subscheme`). Let X be a locally noetherian formal scheme and 𝒜 ⊆ O_X a coherent ideal (F0.36). Its support Y = Supp(O_X/𝒜) is closed and (Y, (O_X/𝒜)|_Y) is a locally noetherian formal scheme, noetherian if X is (EGA I, 10.14.1): the closed formal subscheme X.subscheme 𝒜, with canonical morphism j : X.subscheme 𝒜 → X. The assignment 𝒜 ↦ X.subscheme 𝒜 is a bijection from coherent ideals onto closed formal subschemes. A closed immersion is a morphism isomorphic over X to some j (EGA I, 10.14.2). On X = Spf A (A adic noetherian) every coherent ideal is 𝔞^Δ for an ideal 𝔞 of A and Spf A.subscheme 𝔞^Δ ≅ Spf(A/𝔞), with j = Spf of the quotient map. Closed immersions are adic and of finite type (EGA I, 10.14.3); for a locally noetherian scheme X with closed subset X′ and a closed subscheme Z of X, the completion Ẑ_{/Z∩X′} is the closed formal subscheme of X̂ defined by the completed ideal (EGA I, 10.14.7, used in EGA III, 5.1.8). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `subscheme, subschemeι, Hom.IsClosedImmersion, Hom.IsClosedImmersion.ker, Hom.IsClosedImmersion.comp, Hom.IsClosedImmersion.finiteType, subscheme_Spf, completion_subscheme`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.subscheme_Spf_quotient_example` (computation): For IsAdicRing A, A noetherian, and an ideal 𝔞: (Spf A).subscheme (𝔞^Δ) ≅ Spf (A ⧸ 𝔞), and j corresponds to Ideal.Quotient.mk 𝔞. `FormalScheme.subscheme_zero` (degenerate): X.subscheme 0 = X with j the identity; X.subscheme ⊤ is empty. `FormalScheme.subscheme_idealOfDefinition` (compatibility): For an ideal of definition 𝒥, X.subscheme 𝒥 ≅ FormalScheme.ofScheme (X.thickening 𝒥 0). `FormalScheme.IsClosedImmersion.not_of_homeomorph` (non-example): Spf ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is a homeomorphism of underlying spaces (both are points) but not a closed immersion: ℤ_[p] → ℤ_[p]⟦T⟧ is not surjective. A definition by the underlying topological map alone (closed embedding) accepts it; while Spec F_p ⟶ Spf ℤ_[p] is a closed immersion that is a homeomorphism but not an isomorphism. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.14.2) Définition, p. 210. *Needs:* F0.36, F0.1, F0.19, F0.38, F0.25, F0.2, F0.34, F0.30.

- **F0.29** An adic morphism is a closed immersion iff its reduction is. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (4.8.10) Corollaire.

### Completion along a closed subset

**F0.30 The formal completion of a locally noetherian scheme along a closed subset** (construction `FormalScheme.completion`). Let X be a locally noetherian scheme and X′ ⊆ X a closed subset. Let 𝓘 ⊆ O_X be a quasi-coherent ideal of finite type with V(𝓘) = X′ (for example the ideal of the reduced induced closed subscheme). The formal completion X̂ = X̂_{/X′} is the locally noetherian formal scheme ofThickenings(V(𝓘^{n+1}))_n (F0.19): its underlying space is X′ and O_X̂ = lim_n (O_X/𝓘^{n+1})|_{X′}. It does not depend on 𝓘: all such ideals form a filtered set Φ, and on noetherian opens the powers of any one member are cofinal in Φ (EGA I, 10.8.2); 𝓘O_X̂ is an ideal of definition. For X = Spec A (A noetherian) and X′ = V(I): X̂ ≅ Spf Â where Â = AdicCompletion I A with the I-adic topology (EGA I, 10.8.5). Further: X̂_red is the reduced closed subscheme with space X′; X̂ is noetherian iff X′ is quasi-compact (EGA I, 10.8.6); there is a canonical morphism of locally ringed spaces i_X : X̂ → X (the inclusion of X′ with O_X → i_*O_X̂ the limit of the quotient maps) (EGA I, 10.8.7), which is flat (F0.34); for U ⊆ X open, Û_{/U∩X′} is the open formal subscheme of X̂ on U ∩ X′; for X′ = X, X̂ = X. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `completion, completion.ofIdeal, completion.toScheme, completion.idealOfDefinition, completion.specIso, completion.restrict, completion.isNoetherian_iff, completion.reduction, completion.self, completion.flat`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.completion_Spec_padic` (computation): FormalScheme.completion (Spec ℤ) (zeroLocus {p}) ≅ Spf ℤ_[p]. `FormalScheme.completion_affine` (characterisation): For a noetherian ring A and an ideal I, FormalScheme.completion (Spec A) (zeroLocus I) ≅ Spf (AdicCompletion I A) with the I-adic topology. `FormalScheme.completion_self` (degenerate): For X′ = X (closed subset univ), FormalScheme.completion X univ ≅ FormalScheme.ofScheme X; for X′ = ∅ it is empty. `FormalScheme.completion_not_scheme` (non-example): The completion of Spec ℤ along V(p) is not a scheme viewed as a formal scheme: it is Spf ℤ_[p], a one-point space with global sections ℤ_[p], while Spec ℤ_[p] has two points. A construction taking only the closed subscheme V(𝓘) (or the colimit in schemes) loses this. `FormalScheme.completion_ideal_independent` (compatibility): The completions of Spec k[x,y] built from the ideals (y) and (y², x y) (both with zero locus the x-axis) are canonically isomorphic, compatibly with the canonical maps to Spec k[x,y]. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.8.2) Lemme, p. 194. *Needs:* F0.19, F0.3, F0.10, F0.11, F0.16, F0.18.

**F0.31 Extension of a morphism to formal completions** (construction `FormalScheme.completion.map`). Let f : X → Y be a morphism of locally noetherian schemes and X′ ⊆ X, Y′ ⊆ Y closed with f(X′) ⊆ Y′. There is a unique morphism of formal schemes f̂ : X̂_{/X′} → Ŷ_{/Y′} with i_Y ∘ f̂ = f ∘ i_X; it is the colimit of the morphisms V(𝓘^{n+1}) → V(𝓚^{n+1}) induced by f for ideals with f*(𝓚)O_X ⊆ 𝓘, and does not depend on the ideals (EGA I, 10.9.1–10.9.2). On underlying spaces f̂ is the restriction of f to X′. Functoriality: (g ∘ f)^ = ĝ ∘ f̂ and id^ = id (EGA I, 10.9.3). Affine case: for f = Spec φ, φ : B → A, f̂ = Spf φ̂ with φ̂ : B̂ → Â the continuous extension (EGA I, 10.9.1). Base change: if X′ = f⁻¹(Y′) then X̂ ≅ X ×_Y Ŷ as formal schemes, where X is viewed as a formal scheme (EGA I, 10.9.9), and f̂ is adic; completion commutes with fibre products (EGA I, 10.9.7). *API* (on `FormalScheme.completion`): `map, map_toScheme, map_unique, map_id, map_comp, pullbackIso, map_isAdic, prodIso`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.completion.map_id_example` (degenerate): completion.map (𝟙 X) = 𝟙 (completion X X′). `FormalScheme.completion.map_comp_example` (characterisation): completion.map (f ≫ g) = completion.map f ≫ completion.map g whenever f(X′) ⊆ Y′ and g(Y′) ⊆ Z′. `FormalScheme.completion.map_Spec` (computation): For φ : B →+* A noetherian with φ(K) ⊆ I, completion.map (Spec.map φ) corresponds under specIso to Spf.map of AdicCompletion.map-induced ring hom B̂ → Â. `FormalScheme.completion.map_toScheme_example` (compatibility): completion.map f ≫ i_Y = i_X ≫ f. `FormalScheme.completion.map_not_adic` (non-example): For Spec ℤ[x] → Spec ℤ with X′ = V(p, x), Y′ = V(p), completion.map is Spf ℤ_[p]⟦x⟧ → Spf ℤ_[p], which is not adic; adicness needs X′ = f⁻¹(Y′). *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.9.1), p. 198. *Needs:* F0.30, F0.21, F0.7, F0.22.

- **F0.32** Morphisms with equal completions agree near the closed subset. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.9.4) Prop..

**F0.33 Completion of a coherent sheaf along a closed subset** (construction `FormalScheme.completeModule`). Let X be a locally noetherian scheme, X′ ⊆ X closed with completion X̂ = X̂_{/X′}, and F a coherent O_X-module. The completion of F along X′ is F̂ = F_{/X′} := lim_n (F ⊗_{O_X} O_X/𝓘^{n+1})|_{X′} for any 𝓘 of finite type with V(𝓘) = X′; it is independent of 𝓘, a coherent O_X̂-module, and its sections over X̂ are the formal sections of F along X′ (EGA I, 10.8.4). For quasi-compact open U, Γ(U ∩ X′, F̂) = lim_n Γ(U, F/𝓘^{n+1}F). A morphism u : F → G induces û : F̂ → Ĝ, (v ∘ u)^ = v̂ ∘ û, so completion is an additive functor Coh(X) → Coh(X̂). For X = Spec A, F = M~ with M finite: Γ(X̂, F̂) = M̂ = AdicCompletion I M ≅ M ⊗_A Â (EGA I, 10.8.4, 10.10.1; EGA 0, 7.3.3). There is a canonical functorial isomorphism i_X*(F) ≅ F̂, and completion is exact (F0.34). Completion is compatible with ⊗ and Hom: (F ⊗ G)^ ≅ F̂ ⊗ Ĝ, Hom(F, G)^ ≅ Hom(F̂, Ĝ) (EGA I, 10.8.10), and with pullback: for f : X → Y and coherent G on Y, (f*G)^ ≅ f̂*(Ĝ) (EGA I, 10.9.5). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `completeModule, completeModule.sectionsIso, completeModule.specIso, completeModule.pullbackIso, completeModule.tensorIso, completeModule.homIso, completeModule.pullbackCompletionIso, completeModule.support, completeModule.reductionIso`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.completeModule_structureSheaf` (degenerate): (O_X)^ ≅ O_X̂ as O_X̂-modules. `FormalScheme.completeModule_Spec` (computation): For X = Spec A, X′ = zeroLocus I and M a finite A-module, Γ(X̂, (tilde M)^) ≅ AdicCompletion I M as Â-modules. `FormalScheme.completeModule_self` (compatibility): For X′ = X, F̂ ≅ F under completion X univ ≅ ofScheme X. `FormalScheme.completeModule_supportDisjoint` (characterisation): If Supp F ∩ X′ = ∅ then F̂ = 0; more precisely Supp F̂ = Supp F ∩ X′ (EGA I, 10.8.12). `FormalScheme.completeModule_torsion` (non-example): For X = Spec ℤ, X′ = V(p) and F = (ℤ/ℓ)~ with ℓ ≠ p prime, F ≠ 0 but F̂ = 0: completion is not faithful on coherent sheaves; it only detects F near X′. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.8.4) Définition, p. 194. *Needs:* F0.30, F0.36, F0.31, F0.60.

- **F0.34** Completion of coherent sheaves is exact, agrees with pullback along X̂ → X, and commutes with ⊗, Hom and base change. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.8.8) Prop..

- **F0.35** What completion sees: sections and maps near the closed subset. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.8.11) Prop..

### Coherent formal modules

**F0.36 Coherent modules on a locally noetherian formal scheme** (definition `FormalScheme.Coh`). Let X be a locally noetherian formal scheme. An O_X-module is a sheaf of modules over the sheaf of rings O_X (Mathlib SheafOfModules over TauCeti.SheafOfModules.ringCatSheaf of the structure sheaf). It is coherent if it is locally finitely presented (Mathlib SheafOfModules.IsFinitePresentation). Coh(X) denotes the full subcategory of coherent O_X-modules. The topology Γ(U, F) = lim Γ(U, F ⊗ O_X/𝒥^{n+1}) is not part of the data. The following facts are theorems proved downstream, not part of the definition (their proofs use F0.38 and F0.1, which themselves depend on this definition): O_X is a coherent sheaf of rings, so the definition agrees with EGA's coherence (EGA I, 10.10.3, 10.11.1); Coh(X) is abelian and closed under kernels, cokernels, extensions, ⊗ and internal Hom; every ideal of definition is coherent; morphisms of coherent modules are automatically continuous (EGA I, 10.11.6); f* preserves coherence — all in F0.61. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `Coh, isCoherentRing_structureSheaf, Coh.ker_mem, Coh.tensor, Coh.pullback, Coh.reduction, Coh.sectionsTopology, Coh.isIdealOfDefinition_coherent`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.Coh.structureSheaf` (degenerate): O_X is an object of Coh X, and the zero module is coherent. `FormalScheme.Coh.Spf_equiv` (compatibility): For X = Spf A with A adic noetherian, Γ(X, ·) : Coh X ⥤ ModuleCat A lands in finite modules and is an equivalence onto them (F0.38). `FormalScheme.Coh.not_rationals` (non-example): On Spf ℤ_[p], the O-module with sections ℚ_[p] over the point is quasi-coherent in the naive sense (a module over the ring of sections) but not coherent: ℚ_[p] is not a finite ℤ_[p]-module. A definition of coherence as "locally M^Δ for some module M" without finiteness would accept it. `FormalScheme.Coh.ofScheme` (compatibility): For a locally noetherian scheme Y viewed as a formal scheme, Coh (ofScheme Y) is equivalent to the coherent (finitely presented) O_Y-modules of Mathlib Scheme.Modules. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.11.1) Prop., p. 204. *Needs:* F0.16, F0.18.

**F0.37 The coherent module M^Δ on Spf A attached to a finite A-module** (construction `FormalScheme.Spf.completedTilde`). Let A be an adic noetherian ring with ideal of definition I, X = Spec A and 𝔛 = Spf A, identified with the completion X̂_{/V(I)} (F0.30, specIso). For a finite A-module M, M^Δ := (M~)^ is the completion along V(I) of the coherent sheaf M~ on X (EGA I, 10.10.1). Its sections are Γ(D(f) ∩ 𝔛, M^Δ) = M ⊗_A A_{f} = lim_n (M/I^{n+1}M)_f; an A-linear map u : M → N gives u^Δ : M^Δ → N^Δ, so M ↦ M^Δ is an additive functor from finite A-modules to coherent O_𝔛-modules. For A discrete, M^Δ = M~ (Mathlib tilde). *API* (on `FormalScheme.Spf`): `completedTilde, completedTilde.map, completedTilde.basicOpenIso, completedTilde.globalSectionsIso, completedTilde.completionIso, completedTilde.pullbackIso, completedTilde.ideal_map`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.Spf.completedTilde_self` (degenerate): completedTilde A ≅ O_{Spf A} as O-modules. `FormalScheme.Spf.completedTilde_discrete` (compatibility): If A is discrete then completedTilde M ≅ tilde M under Spf A ≅ Spec A (Mathlib AlgebraicGeometry.tilde). `FormalScheme.Spf.completedTilde_basicOpen` (computation): Γ(D(f), completedTilde M) ≅ M ⊗[A] AdicRing.CompletedAway A f. `FormalScheme.Spf.completedTilde_padic_torsion` (computation): For A = ℤ_[p] and M = ZMod (p^k), Γ(Spf A, completedTilde M) = ZMod (p^k). `FormalScheme.Spf.completedTilde_ne_tilde` (non-example): For A = ℤ_[p] (p-adic) the tilde sheaf of M = ℤ_[p] on Spec ℤ_[p] has sections ℚ_[p] over the generic point D(p); the formal module has none there (D(p) ∩ Spf A = ∅): M^Δ is not the restriction of M~ to the open primes, it is its completion. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.10.1), p. 201. *Needs:* F0.33, F0.30, F0.4, F0.10.

**F0.38 Coherent modules on Spf A are finite A-modules** (theorem). Let A be an adic noetherian ring with ideal of definition I and 𝔛 = Spf A. (i) M ↦ M^Δ is exact and Γ(𝔛, M^Δ) ≅ M naturally (EGA I, 10.10.2 (i)). (ii) (M ⊗_A N)^Δ ≅ M^Δ ⊗ N^Δ, Hom_A(M, N)^Δ ≅ Hom(M^Δ, N^Δ), and u ↦ u^Δ is a bijection Hom_A(M, N) ≃ Hom(M^Δ, N^Δ) (EGA I, 10.10.2 (ii)–(iii)). (iii) O_𝔛 is a coherent sheaf of rings (EGA I, 10.10.3). (iv) For an O_𝔛-module F the following are equivalent (EGA I, 10.10.5): (a) F is coherent; (b) F ≅ lim F_n for a system (F_n) of coherent O_{Spec(A/I^{n+1})}-modules with F_{n+1} ⊗ O_{X_n} ≅ F_n; (c) F ≅ M^Δ for a finite A-module M, necessarily M ≅ Γ(𝔛, F). Hence Γ(𝔛, ·) and (·)^Δ are inverse equivalences between Coh(Spf A) and finite A-modules, and the system in (b) is isomorphic to (F ⊗ O_{X_n}) (EGA I, 10.10.6). *Hypotheses:* A adic noetherian; modules finite. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.10.2) Prop., p. 201. *Needs:* F0.37, F0.34, F0.33, F0.9, F0.10, F0.60.

**F0.36 Coherent modules on a locally noetherian formal scheme** (definition `FormalScheme.Coh`). Let X be a locally noetherian formal scheme. An O_X-module is a sheaf of modules over the sheaf of rings O_X (Mathlib SheafOfModules over TauCeti.SheafOfModules.ringCatSheaf of the structure sheaf). It is coherent if it is locally finitely presented (Mathlib SheafOfModules.IsFinitePresentation). Coh(X) denotes the full subcategory of coherent O_X-modules. The topology Γ(U, F) = lim Γ(U, F ⊗ O_X/𝒥^{n+1}) is not part of the data.
