The following facts are theorems proved downstream, not part of the definition (their proofs use F0.38 and F0.1, which themselves depend on this definition): O_X is a coherent sheaf of rings, so the definition agrees with EGA's coherence (EGA I, 10.10.3, 10.11.1); Coh(X) is abelian and closed under kernels, cokernels, extensions, ⊗ and internal Hom; every ideal of definition is coherent; morphisms of coherent modules are automatically continuous (EGA I, 10.11.6); f* preserves coherence — all in F0.61. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `Coh, isCoherentRing_structureSheaf, Coh.ker_mem, Coh.tensor, Coh.pullback, Coh.reduction, Coh.sectionsTopology, Coh.isIdealOfDefinition_coherent`. *Tests:* `FormalScheme.Coh.structureSheaf` (degenerate): O_X is an object of Coh X, and the zero module is coherent. `FormalScheme.Coh.Spf_equiv` (compatibility): For X = Spf A with A adic noetherian, Γ(X, ·) : Coh X ⥤ ModuleCat A lands in finite modules and is an equivalence onto them (F0.38). `FormalScheme.Coh.not_rationals` (non-example): On Spf ℤ_[p], the O-module with sections ℚ_[p] over the point is quasi-coherent in the naive sense (a module over the ring of sections) but not coherent: ℚ_[p] is not a finite ℤ_[p]-module. A definition of coherence as "locally M^Δ for some module M" without finiteness would accept it. `FormalScheme.Coh.ofScheme` (compatibility): For a locally noetherian scheme Y viewed as a formal scheme, Coh (ofScheme Y) is equivalent to the coherent (finitely presented) O_Y-modules of Mathlib Scheme.Modules. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.11.1) Prop., p. 204. *Needs:* F0.16, F0.18.

**F0.39 The theorem on formal functions** (theorem). Let A be a noetherian ring, I ⊆ A an ideal, f : X → Spec A a proper morphism, F a coherent O_X-module and F_n := F/I^{n+1}F. For every p ≥ 0 the maps H^p(X, F)/I^{n+1}H^p(X, F) → H^p(X, F_n) induce an isomorphism of Â-modules H^p(X, F)^∧ ≅ lim_n H^p(X, F_n), where ^∧ is the I-adic completion (Mathlib AdicCompletion I) of the finite A-module H^p(X, F); it is a homeomorphism for the I-adic topology on the left and the limit topology on the right (EGA III, 4.1.7 (4.1.7.1); Stacks Tag 02OC). If A is I-adically complete, then H^p(X, F) ≅ lim_n H^p(X, F_n) (Stacks Tag 087U; EGA III, 5.1.2). The sheaf form — for Y locally noetherian, f : X → Y proper, a coherent ideal 𝓘 ⊆ O_Y and F_n = F/𝓘^{n+1}F, the completion of R^p f_*F along V(𝓘) is lim_n R^p f_*(F_n) — is part of F0.44 (EGA III, 4.1.5). *Hypotheses:* A noetherian (not necessarily complete); f proper; F coherent. the complete-base corollary needs A I-adically complete (Mathlib IsAdicComplete I A). *Source:* Stacks Tag 02OC (Thm 30.20.5). *Needs:* F0.41, F0.8, F0.9, F0.2, Tau Ceti `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Modules.cohomologyMapBaseLinear`.

- **F0.40** Finiteness and uniform Serre vanishing for graded coherent modules over a proper scheme. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (3.3.2) Corollaire.

- **F0.41** Good filtrations and the Mittag-Leffler property of H^p(X, F/I^{n+1}F). *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (4.1.7) Corollaire.

**F0.39 The theorem on formal functions** (theorem). Let A be a noetherian ring, I ⊆ A an ideal, f : X → Spec A a proper morphism, F a coherent O_X-module and F_n := F/I^{n+1}F. For every p ≥ 0 the maps H^p(X, F)/I^{n+1}H^p(X, F) → H^p(X, F_n) induce an isomorphism of Â-modules H^p(X, F)^∧ ≅ lim_n H^p(X, F_n), where ^∧ is the I-adic completion (Mathlib AdicCompletion I) of the finite A-module H^p(X, F); it is a homeomorphism for the I-adic topology on the left and the limit topology on the right (EGA III, 4.1.7 (4.1.7.1); Stacks Tag 02OC). If A is I-adically complete, then H^p(X, F) ≅ lim_n H^p(X, F_n) (Stacks Tag 087U; EGA III, 5.1.2). The sheaf form — for Y locally noetherian, f : X → Y proper, a coherent ideal 𝓘 ⊆ O_Y and F_n = F/𝓘^{n+1}F, the completion of R^p f_*F along V(𝓘) is lim_n R^p f_*(F_n) — is part of F0.44 (EGA III, 4.1.5). *Hypotheses:* A noetherian (not necessarily complete); f proper; F coherent. the complete-base corollary needs A I-adically complete (Mathlib IsAdicComplete I A). *Source:* Stacks Tag 02OC (Thm 30.20.5). *Needs:* F0.41, F0.8, F0.9, F0.2, Tau Ceti `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Modules.cohomologyMapBaseLinear`.

- **F0.42** Formal functions at a point of the base. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (4.2).

- **F0.43** Cohomology of an inverse limit of sheaves. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. 0, proof of (13.3.1) b).

**F0.44 Comparison of algebraic and formal higher direct images** (theorem). Let Y be a locally noetherian scheme, f : X → Y proper, Y′ ⊆ Y closed with coherent ideal 𝓘, X′ = f⁻¹(Y′), f̂ : X̂ → Ŷ the completion (F0.31), F a coherent O_X-module, F̂ its completion and F_k = F/𝒥^{k+1}F with 𝒥 = f*(𝓘)O_X. Let R^n f̂_*(F̂) be the sheaf on Ŷ associated with V ↦ H^n(f̂⁻¹(V), F̂) (cohomology of the underlying abelian sheaves). Then R^n f̂_*(F̂) is a coherent O_Ŷ-module and the canonical maps φ_n : (R^n f_*F)^ → R^n f̂_*(F̂) and ψ_n : R^n f̂_*(F̂) → lim_k R^n f_*(F_k) are isomorphisms of O_Ŷ-modules; ψ_n ∘ φ_n is the canonical map (EGA III, 4.1.5). For Y = Spec A affine noetherian: H^n(X̂, F̂) ≅ H^n(X, F)^∧ ≅ lim_k H^n(X, F_k) (EGA III, 4.1.7), and for every affine open V of Y, H^n(X̂ ∩ f⁻¹(V), F̂) ≅ Γ(V ∩ Y′, R^n f̂_*(F̂)) (EGA III, 4.1.9). *Hypotheses:* Y locally noetherian; f proper; F coherent. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (4.1.5) Théorème, p. 125. *Needs:* F0.43, F0.41, F0.39, F0.31, F0.33, F0.1, Tau Ceti `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Modules.cohomologyMapBaseLinear`.

### Grothendieck's existence theorem

- **F0.45** Over a complete base, every neighbourhood of the closed fibre is everything. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.1.3.1) Lemme.

- **F0.46** Completion of coherent sheaves is fully faithful over a complete base. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.1.3) Corollaire.

- **F0.47** Uniform vanishing and lifting of sections on a proper formal scheme with an ample reduction. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.2.3) Prop..

- **F0.48** Grothendieck existence for projective schemes. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.2.5).

- **F0.49** Comparison along a proper modification up to a power of the exceptional ideal. *Source:* Stacks Tag 088B (Lem. 30.25.3).

- **F0.50** Algebraizing a formal module that maps to a completion with bounded torsion kernel and cokernel. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0889 (Lem. 30.23.6).

- **F0.51** Dévissage: extending algebraizability from thickenings of a closed subscheme. *Source:* Stacks Tag 088A (Lem. 30.25.2).

- **F0.52** Coherent formal modules with proper support live on a proper closed subscheme. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.3.5).

**F0.53 Grothendieck’s existence theorem** (theorem). Let A be an adic noetherian ring with ideal of definition I, Y = Spec A, f : X → Y a separated morphism of finite type, X′ = f⁻¹(V(I)), X̂ = X̂_{/X′} and Ŷ = Spf A. Then F ↦ F̂ is an equivalence from the category of coherent O_X-modules whose support is proper over Spec A to the category of coherent O_X̂-modules whose support is proper over Spf A (EGA III, 5.1.4; Stacks Tag 088E). In particular, if X is proper over Spec A then Coh(X) ≃ Coh(X̂) (EGA III, 5.1.6); equivalently, with X_n = X ×_Y Spec(A/I^{n+1}), coherent O_X-modules correspond to compatible systems (F_n) of coherent O_{X_n}-modules with F_{n+1} ⊗ O_{X_n} ≅ F_n, morphisms to compatible systems of morphisms (EGA III, 5.1.7; Stacks Tag 088F). *Hypotheses:* A adic noetherian (complete and separated for the I-adic topology). f separated of finite type; supports proper over Spec A (automatic when X is proper). *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.1.1), p. 149. *Needs:* F0.46, F0.48, F0.49, F0.51, F0.52, F0.1, F0.30, F0.28, F0.33, F0.65.

### Morphisms and algebraization (formal GAGA)

- **F0.54** Closed formal subschemes proper over Spf A are completions. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.1.8) Corollaire.

- **F0.55** Isomorphisms and closed immersions of proper schemes are detected on completions. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (4.6.8) Prop..

**F0.56 Morphisms from proper schemes are determined by their completions** (theorem). Let A be an adic noetherian ring with ideal of definition I, S = Spec A, S_n = Spec A/I^{n+1}, X → S proper and Y → S separated of finite type, and let X̂, Ŷ be the completions along the preimages of V(I). Then f ↦ f̂ is a bijection Hom_S(X, Y) ≅ Hom_{Spf A}(X̂, Ŷ) (EGA III, 5.4.1). Equivalently, S-morphisms X → Y correspond to compatible systems of S_n-morphisms X_n → Y_n (EGA III, 5.4.2; Stacks Tag 0A42). Consequently X ↦ X̂ is a fully faithful functor from schemes proper over Spec A to formal schemes proper over Spf A; formal schemes in its essential image are called algebraizable. *Hypotheses:* A adic noetherian; X proper over S; Y separated of finite type over S. *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.4.1) Théorème, p. 156. *Needs:* F0.32, F0.45, F0.31, F0.29, F0.54, F0.55, F0.24, F0.23.

**F0.57 Grothendieck’s algebraization theorem (formal GAGA)** (theorem). Let A be an adic noetherian ring with ideal of definition I, S = Spec A, 𝔖 = Spf A, S_n = Spec A/I^{n+1}, f : 𝔛 → 𝔖 a proper morphism of formal schemes (F0.27) with X_n = 𝔛 ×_𝔖 S_n, and L an invertible O_𝔛-module such that L_0 = L ⊗ O_{X_0} is ample on X_0. Then 𝔛 is algebraizable: there are a scheme X proper over S and an isomorphism X̂ ≅ 𝔛 (X is unique up to unique isomorphism by F0.56), and for any such X there is an ample invertible O_X-module M with M̂ ≅ L; in particular X is projective over S (EGA III, 5.4.5). Equivalently (Stacks Tag 089A): a compatible system of schemes X_n over S_n with cartesian squares, X_0 proper over S_0, and invertible L_n with L_{n+1}|_{X_n} ≅ L_n and L_0 ample, comes from a proper X → S with an ample invertible L, X_n ≅ X ×_S S_n and L_n ≅ L|_{X_n}. The ampleness hypothesis cannot be dropped: there are formal schemes proper over Spf A that are not algebraizable (EGA III, 5.4.3). *Hypotheses:* A adic noetherian with ideal of definition I. 𝔛 → Spf A proper. L invertible on 𝔛 with L_0 ample on the reduction X_0 (only the reduction modulo I is assumed ample). *Source:* EGA III₁ Ch. III, (5.4.5) Théorème, p. 157. *Needs:* F0.47, F0.24, F0.29, F0.54, F0.53, F0.35, F0.45, F0.46, F0.56, F0.27, F0.64.

- **F0.58** Morphisms of locally ringed spaces glue along open covers. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, proof of (2.2.4) Prop..

- **F0.59** A nilpotent thickening of an affine scheme is affine. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (5.1.9) Prop..

**F0.60 Internal Hom of sheaves of modules; compatibility of ⊗ and ℋom with pullback and with M ↦ M~** (construction `SheafOfModules.internalHom`). Let (X, O_X) be a ringed space (a scheme, or a locally noetherian formal scheme with its sheaf of rings TauCeti.SheafOfModules.ringCatSheaf) and F, G sheaves of O_X-modules. (a) The internal Hom ℋom_{O_X}(F, G) is the sheaf of O_X-modules U ↦ Hom_{O_U}(F|_U, G|_U) (EGA 0, 4.1.5); Γ(X, ℋom(F, G)) = Hom_{O_X}(F, G), ℋom is left exact in each variable and ℋom(O_X, G) = G. (b) For every morphism of ringed spaces f : Y → X, f*(F ⊗ G) ≅ f*F ⊗ f*G canonically (EGA 0, 4.3.3.1; ⊗ is Tau Ceti SheafOfModules.tensorProduct, f* is Mathlib SheafOfModules.pullback). (c) If F is of finite presentation and f is flat, the canonical map f*ℋom(F, G) → ℋom(f*F, f*G) is an isomorphism (EGA 0, 6.7.6). (d) On Spec A, (M ⊗_A N)~ ≅ M~ ⊗ N~, and Hom_A(M, N)~ ≅ ℋom(M~, N~) when M is finitely presented (EGA I, 1.3.12); hence on a locally noetherian scheme ⊗ and ℋom of coherent modules are coherent. (The formal-scheme analogue is recorded in F0.36 and deduced from the affine case.) Parts (a), (b) and (d) are Tau Ceti's `SheafOfModules.ihom` (via `monoidalClosed`), `ihomObjEquiv`, `Presentation.pullbackTensorIso` and `tildeMonoidal`, which this target cites rather than rebuilds; its content is (c) and the coherence statement. *API* (on `SheafOfModules`): `internalHom, internalHomFunctor, globalSectionsInternalHomEquiv, pullbackTensorIso, AlgebraicGeometry.tildeTensorIso, pullbackInternalHomIso, internalHom_isCoherent`. *Tests:* `SheafOfModules.internalHom_unit` (degenerate): ℋom(O_X, G) ≅ G naturally in G. `SheafOfModules.globalSections_internalHom` (characterisation): Γ(X, ℋom(F, G)) ≃ (F ⟶ G) as Γ(X, O_X)-modules. `SheafOfModules.internalHom_tilde` (computation): For finite modules M, N over a noetherian ring A, ℋom(M~, N~) ≅ (Hom_A(M, N))~ on Spec A; e.g. ℋom((ℤ/p)~, (ℤ/p²)~) ≅ (ℤ/p)~ on Spec ℤ. `SheafOfModules.not_pullback_internalHom_of_not_flat` (non-example): For the closed immersion f : Spec F_p → Spec ℤ (not flat) and F = (ℤ/p)~, G = O: f*ℋom(F, G) = 0 but ℋom(f*F, f*G) = ℋom(O, O) = O ≠ 0; flatness is needed. `SheafOfModules.pullbackTensorIso_unit` (compatibility): For f : Y → X a morphism of ringed spaces, the isomorphism f*(O_X ⊗ G) ≅ f*O_X ⊗ f*G composed with the unitors is the identity of f*G. *Source:* EGA I Ch. 0, (4.1.5), pp. 37–38.

- **F0.61** Closure properties of coherent modules on a locally noetherian formal scheme. *Source:* EGA I Ch. I, (10.11.1) Prop..

- **F0.62** Formal glueing of modules along a flat map that is an isomorphism modulo I. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0ALK (More on Algebra, Lem. 15.91.17).

- **F0.63** Coherent formal modules with proper support form Serre subcategories. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0CYV (Lem. 30.26.11).

### Notions this roadmap states for its own use

**F0.64 Projective bundles, ample sheaves and Serre finiteness and vanishing over a noetherian base** (theorem). For a noetherian ring A and a finite A-module E, the projective bundle P(E) = Proj(Sym E) with its universal property (morphisms S → P(E) over Spec A correspond to invertible quotients of E ⊗ 𝒪_S) and its compatibility with base change along Spec A/I^{n+1} → Spec A. For X proper over A with an ample invertible sheaf L and a coherent 𝒪_X-module F: some power of L is very ample; the A-modules Hⁱ(X, F) are finitely generated; and Hⁱ(X, F ⊗ L^{⊗n}) = 0 for every i > 0 and all n ≫ 0. For a graded algebra S of finite type over A generated in degree 1, X = Proj S and a finitely generated graded S-module M, the maps M_n → Γ(X, M̃(n)) are bijective for n ≫ 0. *Source:* EGA II 4.2.3 (universal property), 3.5.3 (base change of Proj), 4.6.11 (a power of an ample sheaf is very ample); EGA III₁ 2.2.1, 2.2.2 (Serre), 2.3.1–2.3.2 (graded modules), 3.2.1 (finiteness for proper morphisms); Stacks Tags 0B5U, 02O5.

**F0.65 Chow's lemma and dévissage over a noetherian base** (theorem). For a separated morphism of finite type X → S with S noetherian there is a projective (in particular proper, surjective) morphism X′ → X which is an isomorphism over a dense open of X and such that X′ → S is quasi-projective (projective when X → S is proper, EGA II 5.6.2). Dévissage: let X be noetherian and P a property of coherent O_X-modules such that 0 has P and, in every short exact sequence of coherent modules, if two terms have P then so does the third. If for every integral closed subscheme Z ⊆ X with generic point ξ there is a coherent module G with P, supported on Z, whose stalk at ξ is a one-dimensional κ(ξ)-vector space, then every coherent O_X-module has P; if P is moreover stable under direct summands, a test module with nonzero stalk at ξ suffices. *Source:* EGA II 5.6.1, 5.6.2; EGA III₁ 3.1.2, 3.1.3; Stacks Tags 0200, 01YI.


<a id="r2"></a>

## R2. Formal schemes, generic fibres and Hasse domains

**R2. Formal schemes, generic fibres and Hasse domains.** Depends on F0, R0, R1. Algebras topologically of finite type and presentation over `O_K` (arbitrary complete rank-one valuation ring); admissible formal schemes, Huber's type (S) and Conrad's category FS_C; the generic-fibre functor to adic spaces and its invariance under admissible blow-ups; specialisation, tubes and rig-points; Raynaud's theorem; flattening by admissible blow-up after Bosch–Lütkebohmert and Raynaud–Gruson, with its proof broken into its lemmas; formal completions of schemes and the good-reduction locus; smooth formal schemes, Frobenius lifts and residue discs; fractional valuation bounds, section domains and Hasse domains with their transition maps.


### Algebras topologically of finite presentation

**R2.1 Algebras topologically of finite type and of finite presentation over a complete rank-one valuation ring; admissible algebras** (definition `Huber.IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation`). Standing data: K is a field complete for a nontrivial rank-one nonarchimedean absolute value |·|, not assumed discretely valued; O_K = K° = {|x| ≤ 1} is its valuation ring (so O_K is noetherian if and only if K is discretely valued); ϖ ∈ O_K is a pseudouniformiser, 0 < |ϖ| < 1; k = O_K/m_K is the residue field. The ϖ-adic topology on O_K is its valuation topology and does not depend on ϖ. For variables ξ = (ξ_1, …, ξ_n), O_K⟨ξ⟩ is the ring of restricted power series Σ c_ν ξ^ν with c_ν ∈ O_K and c_ν → 0, equivalently the ϖ-adic completion of O_K[ξ]. An O_K-algebra A is topologically of finite type (tft) if A ≅ O_K⟨ξ⟩/a for some n and some ideal a; it is topologically of finite presentation (tfp) if moreover a is finitely generated (this does not depend on the chosen presentation); it is admissible if it is tfp and has no ϖ-torsion, equivalently (O_K being a valuation ring) if it is tfp and flat over O_K. Such A carries the ϖ-adic topology; every O_K-algebra homomorphism between tft algebras is continuous. The word 'admissible' is Bosch–Lütkebohmert's and must not be confused with EGA's admissible topological rings. The same definitions are made verbatim over a noetherian ring R complete for the I-adic topology of an ideal I (Bosch–Lütkebohmert's case (II)), where tft and tfp agree. The topologically-finite-type half is Tau Ceti's `IsTopologicallyFiniteType` with the carrier `restrictedMvPowerSeriesCompletion n O`; this target adds finite presentation and admissibility. *API* (on `Huber`): `IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.of_presentation, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.isTopologicallyFiniteType, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.iff_of_surjective, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.completeLocalization, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.isAdicRing, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.quotient, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.iff_mod, IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation.genericFibre, IsAdmissibleAlgebra, isAdmissibleAlgebra_iff_flat, IsAdmissibleAlgebra.injective_toGenericFibre`. *Tests:* `topologicallyFinitePresentation_test_restricted` (computation): O_K⟨T⟩ satisfies IsAdmissibleAlgebra over O_K, and its generic fibre O_K⟨T⟩ ⊗_{O_K} K is isomorphic to K⟨T⟩ as a topological K-algebra. `topologicallyFinitePresentation_test_torsion` (non-example): A = O_K⟨T⟩/(ϖT) is IsTopologicallyFinitePresentation but not IsAdmissibleAlgebra (the class of T is a nonzero ϖ-torsion element), and its torsion-free quotient is O_K. `topologicallyFinitePresentation_test_madic` (non-example): For K = C_p (where m_K² = m_K), the m_K-adic completion of O_K[T] is k[T]; hence a definition using the m_K-adic topology instead of the ϖ-adic one gives a different, wrong ring. `topologicallyFinitePresentation_test_zeroVars` (degenerate): With no variables, O_K is admissible, and O_K/ϖ^m (m ≥ 1) is tfp but not admissible. `topologicallyFinitePresentation_test_discrete` (compatibility): If K is discretely valued, every tft O_K-algebra is tfp (O_K⟨ξ⟩ is noetherian), and admissible means: noetherian, ϖ-adically complete, flat and tft over O_K. *Source:* BL I §1, definitions before Prop. 1.1, p. 293. *Needs:* F0.2, F0.4.

- **R2.2** Saturated submodules of finite free modules over tft algebras are finitely generated. *Source:* BL I §1, Lem. 1.2(c).

- **R2.3** A flat algebra topologically of finite type over O_K is topologically finitely presented. *Source:* BL I §1, Prop. 1.1(c).

- **R2.4** Algebras topologically of finite presentation are coherent; torsion and annihilators are coherent. *Source:* BL I §1, Prop. 1.3.

- **R2.5** Being topologically finitely presented or admissible is local on Spf; flatness mod ϖ^m and faithful flatness of covers. *Source:* BL I §1, Prop. 1.7 and the globalisation after it.

### Admissible formal schemes, type (S) and FS_C

**R2.6 Admissible formal O_K-schemes (formal schemes locally topologically of finite presentation without ϖ-torsion)** (definition `FormalScheme.IsLocallyTFP`). A formal O_K-scheme locally of tf presentation is a formal scheme X of finite ideal type in the sense of F0 (F0.16: locally Spf A with A an adic ring with finitely generated ideal of definition) with a morphism to Spf O_K, that is covered by open formal subschemes isomorphic to Spf A with A a tfp O_K-algebra carrying the ϖ-adic topology; equivalently ϖO_X is an ideal of definition and each X_m = X ⊗_{O_K} O_K/ϖ^{m+1} is a scheme locally of finite presentation over O_K/ϖ^{m+1}. X is admissible if moreover O_X has no ϖ-torsion; by node R2.5 this holds iff every (equivalently: some covering family of) formal affine open Spf A has A admissible, iff every local ring O_{X,x} is O_K-flat. Morphisms are morphisms of formal O_K-schemes; they are automatically adic. The special fibre is X_s = X ⊗_{O_K} k; the underlying topological spaces of X, X_0 and X_s coincide and are locally noetherian (noetherian when X is quasi-compact) although O_X need not be noetherian. The category of admissible formal O_K-schemes has fibre products: the formal fibre product X ×_Z Y divided by its ϖ-torsion ideal (which is zero if X or Y is flat over Z). A coherent open ideal on X is a coherent ideal sheaf 𝒜 ⊆ O_X which locally on X contains ϖ^m O_X for some m (a single m works when X is quasi-compact). When K is discretely valued, admissible formal O_K-schemes are exactly the locally noetherian formal schemes flat and adic locally of finite type over Spf O_K (F0's notions). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `IsLocallyTFP, IsAdmissible, isAdmissible_spf_iff, isAdmissible_iff_affineOpens, isAdmissible_iff_flat_stalks, IsAdmissible.ofIsOpenImmersion, specialFibre, admissiblePullback, torsionFreeQuotient, CoherentOpenIdeal, IsAdmissible.completion, IsAdmissible.iff_of_discreteValuation, CoherentModule`. *Tests:* `admissible_test_formalAffineLine` (computation): Spf O_K⟨T⟩ is IsAdmissible over Spf O_K and its special fibre is isomorphic to A¹_k = Spec k[T]. `admissible_test_torsion` (non-example): X = Spf(O_K⟨T⟩/(ϖT)) is IsLocallyTFP but not IsAdmissible; its special fibre is A¹_k while its torsion-free quotient is Spf O_K, whose special fibre is a point. `admissible_test_powerSeries` (non-example): Spf O_K[[T]] with the (ϖ,T)-adic topology is not IsLocallyTFP over Spf O_K (ϖ does not generate an ideal of definition), even though for discretely valued K it is a locally noetherian formal O_K-scheme. `admissible_test_completion_projectiveLine` (compatibility): The ϖ-adic completion of P¹_{O_K} is IsAdmissible, is covered by two copies of Spf O_K⟨T⟩ meeting in Spf O_K⟨T,T⁻¹⟩, and its special fibre is P¹_k. `admissible_test_formalTorus` (compatibility): For C = C_p, Spf O_C⟨T^{±1}⟩ is admissible, not locally noetherian, and is the ϖ-adic completion of the base change of the noetherian formal torus Spf Z_p⟨T^{±1}⟩. `admissible_test_base` (degenerate): Spf O_K is admissible and terminal among admissible formal O_K-schemes; its special fibre is Spec k. *Source:* BL I §1, after Prop. 1.7, p. 297. *Needs:* R2.1, R2.5, R2.4, F0.10, F0.16, F0.2, F0.22, F0.7, F0.17, F0.18, F0.23, F0.25, R2.87.

**R2.7 Formal schemes of type (S): the class on which Huber's generic-fibre functor is defined, and its relation to admissible formal schemes and to Conrad's FS_C** (definition `FormalScheme.IsTypeS`). A formal scheme X = (X, O_X) is of type (S) if every point has an open affine neighbourhood U such that the topology of A := O_X(U) is adic and either (a) A is noetherian, or (b) there is s ∈ A such that sA is an ideal of definition and the Tate ring A(1/s) — the localisation A_s topologised by the images of s^m A — is strongly noetherian. For X of type (S) every formal affine open has an adic topology with a finitely generated ideal of definition. Morphisms of type-(S) formal schemes are adic (resp. locally of finite type) when locally the ring maps are adic (resp. topologically of finite type) in Huber's sense. Relations, all proved as part of this target's API: (i) every locally noetherian formal scheme (F0) is of type (S) via (a); (ii) every formal O_K-scheme locally of tf type (covered by Spf A with A tft over O_K with the ϖ-adic topology), in particular every locally tfp and every admissible one, is of type (S) via (b) with s = ϖ, since A[1/ϖ] is a quotient of K⟨ξ⟩ (K-affinoid after separated completion) and hence strongly noetherian; (iii) every object of Conrad's category FS_C (C an adic noetherian ring) is locally noetherian, hence of type (S) via (a); (iv) when K is not discretely valued, admissible formal O_K-schemes such as Spf O_K⟨T⟩ satisfy (b) but not (a) and are not objects of any FS_C, whereas Spf R_0[[T]] for a complete discrete valuation ring R_0 ⊆ O_K lies in FS_{R_0} and satisfies (a) but not (b); (v) for non-discrete K, Spf O_K[[T]] with the (ϖ, T)-adic topology is not of type (S) at all. Formal completions of morphisms locally of finite type along finite-type ideals preserve type (S) and are adic (Huber 1996 (1.9.5)). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `IsTypeS, IsTypeS.of_isLocallyNoetherian, IsTypeS.of_isLocallyTFP, IsTypeS.of_mem_FSC, IsTypeS.ofIsOpenImmersion, IsTypeS.idealOfDefinition_fg, IsTypeS.completion, IsTypeS.spf_microbial, not_isTypeS_powerSeries_of_not_discrete`. *Tests:* `typeS_test_admissible_not_noetherian` (characterisation): For K = C_p, Spf O_K⟨T⟩ is IsTypeS through condition (b) with s = ϖ and does not satisfy condition (a). `typeS_test_powerSeries_nondiscrete` (non-example): For K = C_p, Spf O_K[[T]] with the (ϖ,T)-adic topology is not IsTypeS: O_K[[T]] is not noetherian and no principal ideal is an ideal of definition. `typeS_test_dvr_powerSeries` (computation): For a complete discrete valuation ring R_0, Spf R_0[[T]] is IsTypeS through (a) but not through (b), and it is an object of FS_{R_0}. `typeS_test_microbial` (compatibility): For every complete rank-one valuation ring O_K, Spf O_K is IsTypeS via (b) with s = ϖ, and A(1/s) = K. `typeS_test_discrete` (degenerate): For a noetherian ring A with the discrete topology, Spec A viewed as a formal scheme is of type (S) via (a); its generic fibre d(Spec A) is empty. *Source:* Huber96 §1.9, condition (S), p. 96. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.1, F0.16, F0.2, R0.3.

### Generic fibres

**R2.8 Huber's functor t from locally noetherian formal schemes to adic spaces** (construction `FormalScheme.toAdicSpace`). For a locally noetherian formal scheme X (F0) there are an adic space t(X) and a morphism of locally and topologically ringed spaces π_X: (t(X), O⁺_{t(X)}) → (X, O_X) such that every morphism of locally and topologically ringed spaces (Z, O⁺_Z) → (X, O_X) from an adic space Z factors uniquely through a morphism of adic spaces Z → t(X). On affines t(Spf A) = Spa(A, A), and π_X(x) = {a ∈ A : |a(x)| < 1}, the centre of x. The universal property makes t a functor; t is fully faithful; a morphism f of locally noetherian formal schemes is adic (EGA I 10.12) resp. locally of finite type (EGA I 10.13) if and only if t(f) is adic resp. locally of finite type (Huber 1994 Proposition 4.2(i)). The trivial valuations form a subspace t(X)_t ⊆ t(X) and π_X restricts to an isomorphism of locally ringed spaces (t(X)_t, O⁺|) ≅ (X, O_X); in particular π_X has a section ι_X by trivial valuations. The smallest saturated subcategory of adic spaces containing the image of t consists of the adic spaces locally of the form Spa(A, A⁺) with A finitely generated over a noetherian ring of definition A_0 and A⁺ the integral closure of A_0 in A (4.2(ii)). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `toAdicSpace, toAdicSpace_spf, centerMap, centerMap_spf_apply, toAdicSpace.lift, toAdicSpace.lift_comp_centerMap, toAdicSpace.map_id, toAdicSpace.fullyFaithful, toAdicSpace_isAdic_iff, toAdicSpace_locallyOfFiniteType_iff, trivialValuationSection`. *Tests:* `toAdicSpace_test_spf_Zp` (computation): t(Spf Z_p) = Spa(Z_p, Z_p) has exactly two points; the closed point is non-analytic and maps under π to the unique point of Spf Z_p; the other is Spa(Q_p, Z_p). `toAdicSpace_test_ZpT` (computation): The analytic locus of t(Spf Z_p[[T]]) equals the union over n ≥ 1 of the rational subsets R(Tⁿ/p), is the open unit disc over Q_p, and is not quasi-compact. `toAdicSpace_test_discrete` (degenerate): For a noetherian ring A with the discrete topology, every point of t(Spec A) = Spa(A, A) is non-analytic, and π restricted to the trivial valuations is a homeomorphism onto Spec A. `toAdicSpace_test_notEssentiallySurjective` (non-example): Spa(Q_p, Z_p) is not isomorphic to t(X) for any locally noetherian formal scheme X: a nonempty t(X) contains a non-analytic point (a trivial valuation) over each point of X. `toAdicSpace_test_fullyFaithful` (characterisation): Morphisms Spf Z_p[[S]] → Spf Z_p[[T]] of formal schemes correspond bijectively to morphisms t(Spf Z_p[[S]]) → t(Spf Z_p[[T]]) of adic spaces, i.e. to elements T ↦ g ∈ (p, S)Z_p[[S]]. *Source:* Huber94 §4, Prop. 4.1, p. 539. *Needs:* F0.16, F0.10, F0.23, F0.25, R0.38, R0.33, R0.35, R0.20.

**R2.9 Huber's analytic adic space d(X) of a formal scheme of type (S)** (construction `FormalScheme.genericFibre`). Proposition 1.9.1: for X of type (S) there are an adic space d(X) and a morphism of topologically ringed spaces λ = λ_X: d(X) → X such that (a) d(X) is analytic: for the ideal sheaf I ⊆ O_X of sections vanishing at every point, λ⁻¹(I)·O_{d(X)} = O_{d(X)}; (b) O_X → λ_* O_{d(X)} factors through λ_* O⁺_{d(X)} and λ: (d(X), O⁺_{d(X)}) → (X, O_X) is a morphism of locally ringed spaces; (c) (d(X), λ) is universal among adic spaces with a morphism to X satisfying (a) and (b). Hence d is a functor from type-(S) formal schemes with adic morphisms to analytic adic spaces, and λ is natural. Affine formulas: for X = Spf A with A noetherian, d(X) = t(X)_a with the restriction of π_X (node R2.8); in case (b), with B the integral closure of (the image of) A in A(1/s), d(X) = Spa(A(1/s), B) and λ(x) = {a ∈ A : |a(x)| < 1}. For X locally tfp over O_K (in particular admissible), d(Spf A) = Spa(A[1/ϖ], B); when K is discretely valued (so X is locally noetherian and t(X) is defined) d(X) is the open locus {|ϖ| ≠ 0} of t(X) over Spa(O_K, O_K) (Hübner §8), while for non-discrete K no adic space t(X) is available and only the affine formula is used; d(X) = d(X^{tf}) so ϖ-torsion is invisible. Open formal subschemes U ⊆ X give open subspaces d(U) = λ⁻¹(U), and for every affine U satisfying (S), d(U) is spectral (1.9.3). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `genericFibre, genericFibre.lift, genericFibre.lift_unique, genericFibre.map, genericFibre_spf_of_typeSb, genericFibre_spf_of_isNoetherian, genericFibre_ofIsOpenImmersion, genericFibre_torsionFreeQuotient, genericFibre_isAnalytic, genericFibre_spectralSpace, genericFibre_quasiCompact, genericFibre_spf_microbial`. *Tests:* `genericFibre_test_formalAffineLine` (computation): d(Spf O_K⟨T⟩) is the adic space Spa(K⟨T⟩, O_K⟨T⟩), whose underlying set is closedPolydisc 1 K. `genericFibre_test_powerSeries_dvr` (computation): For K discretely valued with uniformiser ϖ, d(Spf O_K[[T]]) is the union of the rational subsets {|Tⁿ| ≤ |ϖ|}, n ≥ 1, of the closed disc, and is not quasi-compact. `genericFibre_test_torsion` (degenerate): d(Spf O_K/ϖ) = ∅ and d(Spf O_K⟨T⟩/(ϖT)) ≅ d(Spf O_K) = Spa(K, O_K): ϖ-torsion does not contribute. `genericFibre_test_plusRing` (non-example): For p ≠ 2 and A = O_K[X]/(X² − ϖ²), A[1/ϖ] ≅ K × K and the image of A is not integrally closed (X/ϖ ↦ (1, −1) is integral but not in A); the plus ring of d(Spf A) is the integral closure O_K × O_K, while spa of A and of its integral closure agree (spa_integralClosure). `genericFibre_test_noetherian` (compatibility): For A = Z_p⟨T⟩ (both (a) and (b) apply) the two affine formulas agree: Spa(Z_p⟨T⟩, Z_p⟨T⟩)_a = Spa(Q_p⟨T⟩, Z_p⟨T⟩). *Source:* Huber96 §1.9, Prop. 1.9.1, pp. 96-97. *Needs:* R2.7, R2.8, R2.6, R0.33, R0.20.

**R2.10 For formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation, d(X) is the adic space of Raynaud's rigid generic fibre** (comparison). Let X be a formal O_K-scheme locally of tf presentation (in particular admissible), K complete of rank one. (i) For X = Spf A the integral closure B of the image of A in A_K = A ⊗_{O_K} K equals the power-bounded subring A_K°, so d(Spf A) = Spa(A_K, A_K°) = r(Sp A_K), where r is Huber's rigid–adic comparison functor (R1.31). (ii) Raynaud's generic fibre X_rig (Bosch–Lütkebohmert I §4: glue Sp(A_K) over formal affine opens Spf A, basic opens Spf A⟨f⁻¹⟩ going to Laurent domains Sp A_K⟨f⁻¹⟩ = {|f| ≥ 1}) is a quasi-separated rigid K-space, and there is an isomorphism of adic spaces d(X) ≅ r(X_rig), functorial in X and compatible with open formal subschemes (U ↦ λ⁻¹(U)). (iii) In particular d(X) is locally of finite type over Spa(K, O_K), and d(X) is quasi-compact (resp. quasi-separated) when X is quasi-compact (resp. always). *Hypotheses:* K complete, nontrivial rank-one valuation; O_K may be non-noetherian. X locally tfp over Spf O_K. r and its properties are those of R1.31. *Source:* Huber96 §1.9, Ex. 1.9.2.ii, pp. 97-98. *Needs:* R2.9, R2.6, R2.1, R1.31, R1.1, R1.7, R1.32.

- **R2.11** The generic fibre commutes with fibre products and with completed extension of the base field. *Source:* BL I §4, Cor. 4.6.

### Specialisation, tubes and rig-points

**R2.12 The specialisation map λ_X = sp: d(X) → X** (construction `FormalScheme.specialisation`). For X of type (S) the specialisation map is the continuous map λ_X: d(X) → X underlying Proposition 1.9.1. For X = Spf A and x ∈ d(X), λ(x) = {a ∈ A : |a(x)| < 1}, an open prime of A; equivalently λ(x) is the centre of x, the image of the closed point of Spec κ(x)⁺ under the map Spec κ(x)⁺ → Spec A induced by A → κ(x)⁺ (|A(x)| ≤ 1). Properties: (i) λ is continuous: for s ∈ A, λ⁻¹(D(s)) = {|s| = 1} = {|s| ≥ 1} is open and λ⁻¹(V(s)) = {|s| < 1} is closed; (ii) λ is natural: λ_Y ∘ d(f) = f ∘ λ_X; (iii) for X admissible over O_K, λ maps each classical point (residue field finite over K) to a closed point of X_s, and every closed point of X_s is the image of a classical point (Bosch–Lütkebohmert I 3.4, 3.5), λ of the classical point attached to a rig-point being its special point; the restriction sp: X_rig → X to classical points is Berthelot's specialisation map of rigid geometry, which is anticontinuous: preimages of closed subsets are admissible opens (the tubes of node tube); (iv) λ is compatible with admissible blow-ups φ: X' → X, i.e. φ ∘ λ_{X'} = λ_X ∘ d(φ) with d(φ) an isomorphism (node R2.16); (v) for X quasi-compact admissible, the maps λ_{X'} over all admissible blow-ups X' → X induce a homeomorphism d(X) → lim_{X'} |X'| and an isomorphism (d(X), O⁺) ≅ lim (X', O_{X'}) (Zariski–Riemann description; Hübner Theorem 8.9, proof cited from Bhatt's notes Proposition 8.1.3, and Conrad, Several approaches §4.1). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `specialisation, specialisation_spf_apply, specialisation_eq_center, continuous_specialisation, specialisation_preimage_basicOpen, specialisation_naturality, specialisation_classical_isClosed, exists_classical_specialisation_eq, specialisation_blowUp, rigidSpecialisation, specialisationLimitHomeomorph, RigPoint.specialPoint_eq_specialisation`. *Tests:* `specialisation_test_closedDisc` (computation): For X = Spf O_K⟨T⟩, λ sends the Gauss point to the generic point of A¹_k and the classical point T = c (c ∈ O_K) to the closed point T = c̄. `specialisation_test_Spf_OK` (degenerate): For X = Spf O_K, d(X) = Spa(K, O_K) is one point and λ maps it to the unique point of Spf O_K. `specialisation_test_preimage_closed` (non-example): In the closed unit disc, λ⁻¹({0}) = {|T| < 1} is closed and not open: it contains the rank-two point η_{1⁻} (the specialisation of the Gauss point towards 0) but no open neighbourhood of it. `specialisation_test_blowUp` (compatibility): For the admissible blow-up X' of (T, ϖ) on Spf O_K⟨T⟩, λ_X(η_{|ϖ|}) is the closed point 0 of A¹_k, while λ_{X'}(η_{|ϖ|}) is the generic point of the exceptional component, and φ ∘ λ_{X'} = λ_X. `specialisation_test_surjective_closed` (characterisation): For X admissible, the set of λ(x) with x classical is exactly the set of closed points of X_s. *Source:* Huber94 §4, proof of Prop. 4.1, point (1), p. 539. *Needs:* R2.9, R2.10, R2.14, R2.6, R1.31, R1.7.

**R2.13 Tubes ]T[ of locally closed subsets of the special fibre, adic and rigid** (construction `FormalScheme.tube`). Let X be an admissible formal O_K-scheme with specialisation λ: d(X) → X (node R2.12). For T ⊆ X locally closed (X and X_s have the same space): (i) if T = U is open, ]U[ := λ⁻¹(U) = d(X|_U), an open subspace; (ii) if T = Z is closed and X = Spf A with Z = V(f_1, …, f_r) ⊆ Spec A/ϖ (f_i ∈ A), then ]Z[ := ⋃_{m≥1} {x ∈ d(X) : |f_i(x)|^m ≤ |ϖ| for all i}; this is independent of the chosen f_i and of ϖ, equals the interior of the closed set λ⁻¹(Z), and glues over affine covers; (iii) for T = U ∩ Z, ]T[ := λ⁻¹(U) ∩ ]Z[. Each ]T[ is open in d(X). Rigid comparison: the classical points of ]T[ are exactly the classical points x with sp(x) ∈ T, i.e. Berthelot's tube sp⁻¹(T), which is an admissible open of X_rig, and r(sp⁻¹(T)) = ]T[ under R1.31. For closed Z and rational q > 0, the closed tube of radius |ϖ|^q is [Z]_q := {x : |f_i(x)| ≤ |ϖ|^q for all i}, in the convention of node R2.79; it is quasi-compact and ]Z[ = ⋃_{q>0} [Z]_q. The set λ⁻¹(Z) itself is closed, and in general strictly larger than ]Z[ by non-classical boundary points; Huber's pseudo-adic spaces d(X, L) use λ⁻¹(L), not the interior. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `tube, tube_of_isOpen, tube_spf_of_isClosed, tube_eq_interior_preimage, isOpen_tube, tube_inter, tube_preimage, tube_classicalPoints, closedTube, tube_blowUp`. *Tests:* `tube_test_origin_disc` (computation): For X = Spf O_K⟨T⟩ and Z = {0} ⊆ A¹_k, tube X Z = ⋃_{m ≥ 1} {x : |T(x)|^m ≤ |ϖ|}, the open unit disc. `tube_test_preimage_not_open` (non-example): For X = Spf O_K⟨T⟩ and Z = {0}, the point η_{1⁻} lies in λ⁻¹(Z) but not in tube X Z; so defining the adic tube as λ⁻¹(Z) gives a closed, non-open set. `tube_test_classical` (compatibility): The classical points of tube X {0} for X = Spf O_K⟨T⟩ are the Galois orbits of c ∈ K̄ with |c| < 1, i.e. Berthelot's sp⁻¹(0). `tube_test_open_complement` (characterisation): For X = Spf O_K⟨T⟩: tube X (A¹_k ∖ {0}) = {|T| = 1}, and tube X {0} ∪ tube X (A¹_k ∖ {0}) ≠ d(X). `tube_test_whole` (degenerate): tube X X_s = d(X) and tube X ∅ = ∅. *Source:* GK02 §2.3, p. 18. *Needs:* R2.12, R2.9, R2.6, R2.79, R1.31, R1.7, R0.54.

**R2.14 Rig-points of admissible formal schemes** (definition `FormalScheme.RigPoint`). Let X be an admissible formal O_K-scheme. A rig-point of X is a morphism u: T → X of admissible formal O_K-schemes that is a closed immersion, with T = Spf B affine and B a local integral domain of dimension 1; its residue field is Frac(B). (Bosch–Lütkebohmert allow locally closed immersions; over a complete rank-one valuation ring locally closed rig-points are closed.) Properties: (3.2) B is finite over O_K and the integral closure of B in Frac(B) is the valuation ring O_{K'} of the finite extension K' = Frac(B) of K; (3.3) for an admissible blow-up X' → X the induced map on rig-points is bijective and preserves residue fields; (3.4) for X = Spf A the rig-points correspond bijectively to primes p ⊂ A with ϖ ∉ p and dim A/p = 1, and to closed points of Spec A[1/ϖ], i.e. to maximal ideals of the K-affinoid algebra A_K; (3.5) every closed point of X_s is the special point of some rig-point. Consequently rig-points of X correspond bijectively to the classical points of d(X) (residue field finite over K); the compatibility with the specialisation map λ (the special point of u is λ of the corresponding classical point) is proved in node R2.12, which depends on this target. Conrad's Lemma A.1.5: for Y flat and locally tfp over Spf O_K, every point y: Sp(K') → Y_rig is y_rig for a unique closed immersion Spf B → Y with B a finite flat O_K-domain, and every closed point of Y_s underlies such a map. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `RigPoint, RigPoint.residueField, RigPoint.integralClosure_eq_valuationRing, RigPoint.map, RigPoint.equivOfBlowUp, RigPoint.equivMaxSpec, RigPoint.exists_of_isClosedPoint, RigPoint.equivClassicalPoints, RigPoint.specialPoint`. *Tests:* `rigPoint_test_disc` (computation): For c ∈ O_K, the O_K-algebra map O_K⟨T⟩ → O_K, T ↦ c, defines a rig-point of Spf O_K⟨T⟩ with residue field K whose special point is T = c̄ and whose classical point is T = c. `rigPoint_test_notDim1` (non-example): The closed immersion Spec k → Spf O_K⟨T⟩ at T = 0 is not a rig-point: k is a field (dimension 0) and Spec k is not admissible (it is ϖ-torsion). `rigPoint_test_blowUp` (compatibility): For the admissible blow-up X' of (T, ϖ) on Spf O_K⟨T⟩, the rig-point T = 0 lifts uniquely to X', into the chart Spf O_K⟨T, U⟩/(T − ϖU) at U = 0, with the same residue field K. `rigPoint_test_base` (degenerate): The only rig-point of Spf O_K is the identity, with B = O_K and residue field K. *Source:* BL I §3, Def. 3.1(b), p. 303. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.1, R2.10, R2.15, R1.31, R0.50.

### Admissible blow-ups and Raynaud's theorem

**R2.15 Admissible formal blow-ups and strict transforms** (construction `FormalScheme.admissibleBlowUp`). Let X be an admissible formal O_K-scheme and 𝒜 ⊆ O_X a coherent open ideal (coherent, and locally ϖ^N O_X ⊆ 𝒜 for some N; a single N when X is quasi-compact). The admissible blow-up of 𝒜 is X' := lim_m Proj(⊕_{n≥0} 𝒜ⁿ ⊗_{O_X} O_X/ϖ^{m+1}) with its projection φ: X' → X. (a) Over a formal affine open Spf A with 𝒜 ↔ a = (f_0, …, f_r) ∋ ϖ^N, φ⁻¹(Spf A) is the ϖ-adic completion of the scheme-theoretic blow-up of a on Spec A, covered by the charts Spf A'_i with A'_i = A⟨ζ_j : j ≠ i⟩/(f_iζ_j − f_j)_{j≠i} modulo its ϖ-torsion (which equals its f_i-torsion), Spf A'_i being the locus where f_i generates 𝒜O_{X'}. (b) X' is admissible and 𝒜O_{X'} is invertible. (c) Universal property: every morphism ψ: Z → X of formal O_K-schemes (Z not necessarily admissible) with 𝒜O_Z invertible factors uniquely through φ. Further: φ is an isomorphism over X ∖ V(𝒜); formation commutes with flat base change and, in the admissible category, with arbitrary base change (strict transform); φ is proper and surjective on special fibres; blowing up 𝒜 and then ℬO_{X'} is blowing up 𝒜ℬ; for X quasi-compact a composite of admissible blow-ups is an admissible blow-up (Bosch–Lütkebohmert I 2.5), blow-ups of finitely many opens extend to X and are dominated by one admissible blow-up of X (2.6), and any two admissible blow-ups of X are dominated by a third, so admissible blow-ups of X form a cofiltered system. The strict transform of a coherent O_X-module ℳ is ℳ' = φ*ℳ/𝒩, where 𝒩 is the 𝒜O_{X'}-power torsion of φ*ℳ (sections killed locally by a power of 𝒜O_{X'}; the scheme-theoretic 'sections supported in φ⁻¹(V(𝒜))' of Raynaud–Gruson 5.1.1, V(𝒜) being read scheme-theoretically since the underlying space of X' is its special fibre); 𝒩 lies in the ϖ-power torsion, and if ℳ' has no ϖ-torsion, 𝒩 is the ϖ-power torsion of φ*ℳ, which is coherent (Lemma 1.4(c)). The scheme-theoretic charts of the blow-up are Tau Ceti's `affineBlowupι` and `iSup_opensRange_affineBlowupι`; this target forms the ϖ-adic completion and removes ϖ-torsion. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `admissibleBlowUp, admissibleBlowUp.π, admissibleBlowUp.isAdmissible, admissibleBlowUp.isInvertible, admissibleBlowUp.lift, admissibleBlowUp.lift_comp_π, admissibleBlowUp.chart, admissibleBlowUp.isIso_of_disjoint, admissibleBlowUp.baseChange, admissibleBlowUp.isProper_surjective_specialFibre, admissibleBlowUp.mul, admissibleBlowUp.comp, admissibleBlowUp.extend, admissibleBlowUp.dominate, strictTransform, strictTransformBaseChange, strictTransformBaseChange_comp, admissibleBlowUp.comp_of_disjoint`. *Tests:* `admissibleBlowUp_test_disc_origin` (computation): For X = Spf O_K⟨T⟩ and 𝒜 = (T, ϖ): X' is covered by Spf O_K⟨U⟩ (T = ϖU) and Spf O_K⟨T,V⟩/(TV − ϖ), and X'_s is the union of P¹_k (exceptional) and A¹_k (strict transform of X_s) meeting in one point. `admissibleBlowUp_test_unit` (degenerate): For 𝒜 = O_X or 𝒜 = ϖ^n O_X, φ: X' → X is an isomorphism. `admissibleBlowUp_test_nonOpenCentre` (non-example): Blowing up the non-open ideal (S, T) of Spf O_K⟨S,T⟩ (formal completion of the scheme blow-up of the origin) changes the generic fibre: it produces the blow-up of the origin in the closed bidisc, which is not isomorphic to the bidisc; so the openness condition in the definition is necessary for node R2.16. `admissibleBlowUp_test_universal` (characterisation): For 𝒜 = (T, ϖ) on Spf O_K⟨T⟩ and ψ: Spf O_K → Spf O_K⟨T⟩, T ↦ ϖ², the ideal ψ*𝒜 = (ϖ) is invertible and ψ lifts uniquely to X', into the chart U = T/ϖ with U ↦ ϖ. `admissibleBlowUp_test_scheme` (compatibility): For X = Spf A and a = (ϖ, f), φ⁻¹(X) is the ϖ-adic completion of the Proj of the Rees algebra reesAlgebra a over Spec A, modulo ϖ-torsion. `strictTransform_test_annulus` (computation): For X = Spf O_K⟨ζ, ξ⟩/(ζξ − ϖ) → T = Spf O_K⟨ζ⟩ and 𝒜 = (ζ, ϖ): on the chart ζ = ϖu of T' the pull-back of O_X is O_K⟨u, ξ⟩/(ϖ(uξ − 1)), and its strict transform is O_K⟨u, ξ⟩/(uξ − 1) ≅ O_K⟨u, u⁻¹⟩. *Source:* BL I §2, p. 298. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.4, R2.3, R2.2, R2.5, F0.19, F0.17, F0.14.

- **R2.16** The generic fibre of an admissible blow-up is an isomorphism. *Source:* BL I §4, proof of Thm 4.1(a).

- **R2.17** Adjoining power-bounded functions to an admissible algebra is an admissible blow-up. *Source:* BL I §4, Lem. 4.5.

- **R2.18** Quasi-compact opens of the generic fibre come from open formal subschemes of an admissible blow-up. *Source:* BL I §4, Lem. 4.4.

- **R2.19** Every affinoid has an admissible formal model. *Source:* BL I §4, after Thm 4.1.

**R2.20 Raynaud's theorem: quasi-compact admissible formal schemes localised at admissible blow-ups are quasi-compact quasi-separated rigid spaces** (theorem). Let K be complete with nontrivial rank-one valuation (not necessarily discrete). The functor X ↦ X_rig from quasi-compact admissible formal O_K-schemes to rigid K-spaces sends admissible blow-ups to isomorphisms and induces an equivalence between (1) the category of quasi-compact admissible formal O_K-schemes localised by admissible blow-ups and (2) the category of quasi-compact quasi-separated rigid K-spaces. Composing with node R2.10 and Huber's equivalence r between quasi-separated rigid K-spaces and quasi-separated adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, O_K) (R1.31), X ↦ d(X) induces an equivalence between (1) and the category of quasi-compact quasi-separated adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, O_K). Concretely: (a) admissible blow-ups become isomorphisms; (b) morphisms φ, ψ: Y → X with φ_rig = ψ_rig are equal; (c) for quasi-compact X, Y and any morphism g: Y_rig → X_rig there are an admissible blow-up Y' → Y and a morphism φ: Y' → X inducing g; (d) if g is an isomorphism, φ can be chosen to be an admissible blow-up of X as well; (e) every quasi-compact quasi-separated rigid K-space (resp. adic space as above) has a quasi-compact admissible formal model. Morphisms in the localised category are Hom(X, Y) = colim over admissible blow-ups X' → X of Hom(X', Y) (calculus of fractions, Bosch–Lütkebohmert I §5). *Hypotheses:* K complete, nontrivial rank one; O_K may be non-noetherian. Quasi-compactness of formal models and quasi-compactness plus quasi-separatedness of rigid spaces are necessary: X ↦ X_rig produces only quasi-separated spaces, and the open unit disc has no quasi-compact formal model. *Source:* BL I §4, Thm 4.1, p. 306. *Needs:* R2.16, R2.14, R2.18, R2.17, R2.19, R2.15, R2.10, R1.31, R1.7, R1.32.

### Separatedness, flattening, quasi-finite models and finiteness criteria

- **R2.21** A morphism of admissible formal schemes is separated iff its generic fibre is. *Source:* BL I §4, Prop. 4.7.

**R2.22 Separatedness, properness and partial properness of a formal model versus its generic fibre (Huber 1996 Remark 1.3.18)** (comparison). Let K be complete of rank one and f: X → Y a morphism of formal schemes that are flat and locally of finite type over Spf O_K (admissible formal O_K-schemes), with generic fibre d(f) ≅ r_K(f_rig) (node R2.10). Then (i) d(f) is separated iff f is separated (node R2.21, and R1's comparison of rigid and adic separatedness); (ii) d(f) is proper (R0.63) iff f is proper; (iii) d(f) is partially proper (R0.65) iff f|_Z: Z → Y_s is proper for every irreducible closed subset Z of the special fibre X_s (with its reduced structure). *Hypotheses:* X, Y flat and locally of finite type over Spf O_K, K complete of rank one, discreteness not assumed. Huber states the remark quoting [Hu4, 3.11, 3.13] (not public); the rigid-analytic 'f proper ⇒ f_rig proper' without discreteness is Temkin [Te, Cor. 4.4, 4.5], and the discretely valued case is Lütkebohmert [L1] — none read. *Source:* Huber96 §1.3, Rem. 1.3.18, p. 59. *Needs:* R2.21, R2.10, R2.12, R2.6, R1.35, R1.7, R0.63, R0.65, R0.69.

**R2.23 Flattening by admissible blow-up (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Theorem 5.2)** (theorem). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of quasi-compact admissible formal O_K-schemes (K complete of rank one, not necessarily discretely valued; Bosch–Lütkebohmert work over an admissible quasi-compact base S, and an S-morphism is in particular a morphism over Spf O_K) and n ∈ ℕ. If f_rig is flat, i.e. O_X is rig-flat over Y at every rig-point (resp. f_rig is flat in dimension ≥ n, i.e. O_X is rig-flat over Y in dimension ≥ n; node R2.58), there is an admissible blow-up Y' → Y such that the induced morphism f': X' → Y' is flat (resp. flat in dimension ≥ n; node R2.55), where X' is the strict transform of X: the closed formal subscheme of X ×_Y Y' defined by the ideal of sections supported over the centre, i.e. the strict transform of the module O_X (node R2.15). This is the case T = Y, ℳ = O_X of the module flattening theorem (node R2.69, Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Theorem 4.1). *Hypotheses:* X, Y quasi-compact admissible over Spf O_K; f_rig flat (resp. flat in dimension ≥ n) in the sense of rig-flatness at rig-points. The blow-up is of Y (the target), and X' is the strict transform, not the total transform. Classical rigid case: the base is Spf O_K; Bosch–Lütkebohmert's noetherian base case (II) is proved by parallel noetherian arguments that are not decomposed here. *Source:* BL II §5, Thm 5.2, p. 426. *Needs:* R2.69, R2.58, R2.55, R2.56, R2.15, R2.6.

- **R2.24** Quasi-finite and bounded-fibre-dimension formal models (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Corollary 5.3). *Source:* BL II §5, Cor. 5.3(b).

- **R2.25** Conrad's scheme lemma: a flat, locally finitely presented, separated morphism with finite fibres of locally constant rank is finite. *Source:* Conrad-MR Appendix A.1, Lem. A.1.4.

**R2.26 Conrad's fibral finiteness criterion for flat rigid (adic) morphisms** (theorem). Theorem A.1.2: a flat map f: X → Y of rigid spaces over k (k complete nonarchimedean, not necessarily discretely valued) is finite if and only if it is quasi-compact and separated with finite fibres and locally constant fibre rank on Y. Through R1.31 the same holds for flat morphisms of quasi-separated adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(k, k°), with fibres and ranks taken at classical points. *Hypotheses:* f flat; quasi-compact; separated (quasi-separated is insufficient, cf. Remark A.1.3); finite fibres; fibre rank locally constant on Y. k arbitrary complete nonarchimedean; formal models over Spf R with R possibly non-noetherian. Quasi-compactness is necessary: {|t| = 1} ⊔ {|t| < 1} → {|t| ≤ 1} is a bijective local isomorphism, flat with fibres of rank 1, not finite. *Source:* Conrad-MR Appendix A.1, Thm A.1.2, p. 37. *Needs:* R2.25, R2.21, R2.24, R2.23, R2.14, R2.20, R0.79, R0.61, R1.31, R1.7, R1.32.

**R2.27 Topological invariance of affinoidness and finiteness (Conrad A.1.1)** (theorem). A rigid space X over k is affinoid iff X_red is affinoid; hence a morphism f: Y → Z of rigid spaces is finite iff Y_red → Z_red is finite. Through R1.31 (affinoids correspond to affinoids, 1.1.11) the same holds for quasi-separated adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(k, k°). No Serre-type cohomological criterion is available: over every nonarchimedean k there is a quasi-compact separated surface with vanishing higher coherent cohomology that is not affinoid [Liu]. *Hypotheses:* Rigid spaces over a complete nonarchimedean field k; no discreteness. Inputs [BGR 6.1.1/4, 7.3.5/3, 9.5.3/5] are unread (recorded gap). *Source:* Conrad-MR Appendix A.1, Thm A.1.1, p. 36. *Needs:* R1.7, R1.31, R1.32, R0.79, R0.45, R1.4.

### The proof of flattening (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II §§1–4)

- **R2.28** Generators modulo ϖ generate topologically (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 1.3(a)). *Source:* BL II §1, Lem. 1.3(a) and its proof.

- **R2.29** A basis modulo ϖ of a flat module lifts to a topological basis (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 1.3(b)). *Source:* BL II §1, Lem. 1.3(b).

- **R2.30** Smooth morphisms of the reduction lift to smooth formal morphisms (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 1.4(a)). *Source:* BL II §1, Lem. 1.4(a).

- **R2.31** Lifting factorisations through smooth and étale morphisms (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 1.4(b)). *Source:* BL II §1, Lem. 1.4(b).

- **R2.32** Generisation maps of stalks of formal schemes are flat (Bosch–Lütkebohmert I Corollary 1.8(b)). *Source:* BL I §1, Cor. 1.8(b).

- **R2.33** Connected elementary étale neighbourhoods at points with geometrically irreducible closure are geometrically irreducible (Raynaud–Gruson I, Lemme 1.1.2). *Source:* RG Première partie, §1.1, Lemme 1.1.2.

- **R2.34** After an elementary étale base change, a smooth morphism has geometrically integral fibres near a geometrically integral fibre (Raynaud–Gruson I, Lemme 1.1.3). *Source:* RG Première partie, §1.1, Lemme 1.1.3.

**R2.35 Étale-local structure of a morphism locally of finite type: finite over smooth with geometrically integral fibres (Raynaud–Gruson I, Théorème 1.1.1)** (theorem). Let f: (X, x) → (S, s) be a pointed morphism of schemes with X locally of finite type over S, and n = dim_x(X ⊗ k(s)). There is a commutative diagram of pointed schemes (Y, y) → (T, t) → (S', s') over (X, x) → (S, s) such that (a) Y, T and S' are affine and (Y, y) → (X, x), (S', s') → (S, s) are elementary étale neighbourhoods; (b) T → S' is smooth with geometrically integral fibres of dimension n; (c) Y → T is finite and y is the only point of Y above t. *Hypotheses:* X locally of finite type over S (no noetherian hypothesis). Elementary étale: trivial residue field extension at the marked points. *Source:* RG Première partie, §1.1, Théorème 1.1.1, p. 3. *Needs:* R2.33, R2.34.

**R2.36 Local structure of formal morphisms: finite over smooth with geometrically irreducible fibres (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Theorem 1.5)** (theorem). Conventions: K is complete for a nontrivial rank-one valuation, O_K its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudouniformiser; formal schemes are formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation (node R2.6), X_λ = X ⊗ O_K/ϖ^{λ+1}, X_0 has the underlying space of X, and for t ∈ T_0 the fibre X ⊗ k(t) is X_0 ×_{T_0} Spec k(t). Let f: (X, x) → (T, t) be a pointed morphism of formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation (x ∈ X_0, t = f(x)) and d = dim_x(X ⊗ k(t)). There is a commutative diagram (X, x) ←u (X', x') →g (Z', z') →h (T', t') →v (T, t) of pointed formal O_K-schemes with f ∘ u = v ∘ h ∘ g such that (a) T', Z', X' are affine and u, v are elementary étale neighbourhoods (étale, with trivial residue field extensions at x' and t'); (b) h is smooth with geometrically irreducible fibres of dimension d; (c) g is finite and x' is the only point of X'_0 above z'. *Hypotheses:* f locally of tf presentation; no flatness assumption. The dimension is that of the fibre of the reduction at x. *Source:* BL II §1, before Thm 1.5, p. 406. *Needs:* R2.35, R2.30, R2.31, R2.76, R2.75, R2.6, R2.28.

- **R2.37** A smooth algebra with geometrically integral fibres is a projective module (Raynaud–Gruson I, Proposition 3.3.1). *Source:* RG Première partie, §3.3, Prop. 3.3.1.

- **R2.38** Smooth formal morphisms with geometrically irreducible fibres admit local topological bases (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 1.8(b), smooth case). *Source:* BL II §1, proof of Prop. 1.8(b).

**R2.39 Ideals of coefficients (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Definition 2.1, Remark 2.2)** (definition `FormalScheme.IsIdealOfCoefficients`). Let h: Z → T be a morphism of formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation and 𝒥 ⊆ O_Z a coherent ideal, with closed formal subscheme V(𝒥) (locally Spf(B/b), b finitely generated). A coherent ideal 𝒞 ⊆ O_T is an ideal of coefficients of 𝒥 if (i) the projection Z ×_T V(𝒞) → Z factors through V(𝒥), and (ii) for every morphism T' → T of formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation such that Z ×_T T' → Z factors through V(𝒥), the morphism T' → T factors through V(𝒞). Equivalently: 𝒥 ⊆ 𝒞O_Z, 𝒞 is the smallest coherent ideal of O_T with this property, and this remains true after every base change T' → T. An ideal of coefficients is unique when it exists; its formation commutes with base change (𝒞O_{T'} is the ideal of coefficients of 𝒥O_{Z×_T T'}) and with restriction to opens of T, so local ideals of coefficients glue. Set-theoretically, V(𝒞) ∩ T_0 is the set of t ∈ T_0 whose fibre Z ⊗ k(t) is contained in V(𝒥). If 𝒥 is open (ϖ^N O_Z ⊆ 𝒥) and h is faithfully flat, then 𝒞 is open (Remark 2.2, which Bosch–Lütkebohmert state without the flatness hypothesis). Affine formula: if Z = Spf B → T = Spf A and B has a topological basis (e_i)_{i∈I} over A (node R2.29), and b = (f_1, …, f_r) with f_j = Σ_i a_{ij} e_i, then the ideal a = (a_{ij}) is contained in every ideal c ⊆ A with b ⊆ Bc, and is the ideal of coefficients of b whenever it is finitely generated. For f ∈ B, the content c(f) is the ideal of coefficients of fB. The same definitions apply on generic fibres, to ideals of B_K = B ⊗_{O_K} K and A_K (coherent ideals of O_{Z_rig}, O_{T_rig}), with rig-points in place of points. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `IsIdealOfCoefficients, IsIdealOfCoefficients.unique, IsIdealOfCoefficients.le_map, IsIdealOfCoefficients.baseChange, IsIdealOfCoefficients.restrict, IsIdealOfCoefficients.glue, IsIdealOfCoefficients.mem_zeroLocus_iff, IsIdealOfCoefficients.isOpen, isIdealOfCoefficients_of_topologicalBasis, content, content_polynomial`. *Tests:* `idealOfCoefficients_test_polynomial` (computation): Over A = O_K and B = O_K⟨ξ⟩, the content of f = ϖ² + ϖξ + ϖ³ξ² is (ϖ). `idealOfCoefficients_test_unit` (degenerate): For h surjective, the ideal of coefficients of 𝒥 = O_Z is O_T; for h faithfully flat and 𝒥 = 𝒜O_Z with 𝒜 ⊆ O_T coherent, it is 𝒜. `idealOfCoefficients_test_baseChange` (compatibility): For Z = Spf O_K⟨a, ζ⟩ → T = Spf O_K⟨a⟩ and 𝒥 = (aζ, ϖ): 𝒞 = (a, ϖ); after the base change a ↦ 0 to T' = Spf O_K, 𝒥' = (ϖ) and 𝒞' = (ϖ) = 𝒞O_{T'}. `idealOfCoefficients_test_notZeroLocus` (non-example): For A = O_K⟨a⟩, B = A⟨ζ⟩ and 𝒥 = (a²ζ, ϖ), (a², ϖ) is the ideal of coefficients and (a, ϖ) is not, although both have the same zero locus: a definition by the set of t with Z ⊗ k(t) ⊆ V(𝒥) fails. *Source:* BL II §2, Def. 2.1, p. 407. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.4, R2.29, R2.3, R2.14, R2.10, F0.22.

- **R2.40** Existence of ideals of coefficients for open ideals along smooth morphisms with geometrically irreducible fibres (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 2.3(b)). *Source:* BL II §2, Prop. 2.3(b).

- **R2.41** Ideals of coefficients on generic fibres, and openness when the rigid ideal of coefficients is the unit ideal (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 2.3(b), rigid parts). *Source:* BL II §2, proof of Prop. 2.3(b).

- **R2.42** A function vanishing on no fibre is a non-zero-divisor at every level (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 2.5(a)). *Source:* BL II §2, Lem. 2.5(a).

- **R2.43** A function vanishing on no fibre is a non-zero-divisor (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 2.5(b)). *Source:* BL II §2, proof of Lem. 2.5(b).

- **R2.44** Hartogs-type extension across subsets of the special fibre of smaller relative dimension (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 2.5(c), classical rigid case). *Source:* BL II §2, proof of Lem. 2.5(c).

- **R2.45** Contents are homogeneous: c(af) = a·c(f) (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 2.6(a)). *Source:* BL II §2, Lem. 2.6.

- **R2.46** Multiplying by a function vanishing on no fibre does not change contents (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 2.6(b)). *Source:* BL II §2, proof of Lem. 2.6(b).

- **R2.47** Contents are multiplicative when one factor has principal content (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 2.6(c)). *Source:* BL II §2, proof of Lem. 2.6(c).

- **R2.48** Fibres over the generic fibre of the base are integral (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 2.4(a), classical rigid case). *Source:* BL II §2, Prop. 2.4(a).

- **R2.49** Associated primes of B are generic points of fibres over the generic fibre (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 2.4(b)). *Source:* BL II §2, Prop. 2.4(b).

**R2.50 T-dévissages of a coherent module in one dimension (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Definition 3.1)** (definition `FormalScheme.DevissageStep`). Conventions: K is complete for a nontrivial rank-one valuation, O_K its valuation ring, ϖ a pseudouniformiser; formal schemes are formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation (node R2.6), X_λ = X ⊗ O_K/ϖ^{λ+1}, X_0 has the underlying space of X, and for t ∈ T_0 the fibre X ⊗ k(t) is X_0 ×_{T_0} Spec k(t). Let f: X → T be a morphism of quasi-compact formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation, ℳ a coherent O_X-module (node R2.6, coherent modules), x ∈ X_0, t = f(x) and n ∈ ℕ. A T-dévissage of ℳ in dimension n at x is a tuple D = (g: (Y, y) → (Z, z), ℳ, α: ℒ → 𝒩, 𝒫) consisting of (a) a pointed closed immersion (Y, y) → (X, x) of finite presentation whose ideal annihilates ℳ, so that ℳ is the direct image of a coherent O_Y-module, again written ℳ; (b) a pointed factorisation (Y, y) →g (Z, z) →h (T, t) of Y → T with g finite, y the only point of Y_0 above z, and h affine and smooth with geometrically irreducible fibres of dimension n; (c) a homomorphism α: ℒ = O_Z^ℓ → 𝒩 := g_*ℳ of coherent O_Z-modules such that α ⊗ k(ζ) is bijective, ζ the generic point of the fibre Z ⊗ k(t); 𝒫 := coker α. Then dim_z(𝒫 ⊗ k(t)) ≤ n − 1, and 𝒫 is a module over a closed formal subscheme Y' ⊆ Z of finite presentation with dim(Y' ⊗ k(t)) ≤ n − 1. The fibre dimension of ℳ at x is at most n (Definition 3.1 does not require equality). Dévissages pull back along pointed base changes (T', t') → (T, t). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `DevissageStep, DevissageStep.pushforward, DevissageStep.cokernel, DevissageStep.surjective_generic, DevissageStep.dim_cokernel_le, DevissageStep.exists_closedSubscheme_cokernel, DevissageStep.dim_le, DevissageStep.baseChange, DevissageStep.reduction`. *Tests:* `devissageStep_test_free` (computation): For X = Z = D^n_T, ℳ = O_X^ℓ, g = id, α = id: a T-dévissage in dimension n at every point, with 𝒫 = 0. `devissageStep_test_dimZero` (degenerate): In dimension n = 0, h is étale with fibres Spec k(t), so after shrinking Z = T; α: O_T^ℓ → 𝒩 with ℓ = dim_{k(t)} 𝒩 ⊗ k(t), and 𝒫 = 0 near t. `devissageStep_test_notFlat` (non-example): For X = Spf O_K⟨ζ, ξ⟩/(ζξ − ϖ) → T = Spf O_K⟨ζ⟩ at x = (ζ, ξ), ℳ = O_X: Z = D¹_T (coordinate ξ), g the closed immersion, ℓ = 1 and α: O_Z → g_*O_X the quotient map give a dévissage in dimension 1 with 𝒫 = 0, although ℳ is not T-flat at x: a dévissage with 𝒫 = 0 need not have α injective. `devissageStep_test_reduction` (compatibility): Reduction modulo ϖ^{λ+1} turns a T-dévissage of ℳ into an S-dévissage of ℳ_λ over T_λ in the sense of Raynaud–Gruson I 1.2.1 (with X' = Y_λ and T = Z_λ). *Source:* BL II §3, Def. 3.1, p. 411. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.75, R2.4, F0.22.

**R2.51 T-dévissages in several dimensions and complete dévissages (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Definition 3.2)** (definition `FormalScheme.Devissage`). In the setting of node R2.50, a T-dévissage of ℳ at x in dimensions N = n¹ > n² > … > n^r = n ≥ 0 (between N and n) is a sequence (D¹, …, D^r) of T-dévissages D^i = (g^i: (Y^i, y^i) → (Z^i, z^i), ℳ^i, α^i: ℒ^i → 𝒩^i = g^i_*ℳ^i, 𝒫^i) such that D¹ is a T-dévissage of ℳ at x in dimension n¹ and, for i = 2, …, r, D^i is a T-dévissage of the coherent O_{Z^{i−1}}-module 𝒫^{i−1} at z^{i−1} in dimension n^i; thus ℳ¹ = ℳ|_{Y¹} and ℳ^i = 𝒫^{i−1}|_{Y^i}. Its length is r; it is complete if 𝒫^r = 0. Dévissages, complete or not, are stable under base change (T', t') → (T, t), and truncations (D¹, …, D^k) are dévissages in dimensions between N and n^k. The support of 𝒩^i has relative dimension ≤ n^i and that of 𝒫^i relative dimension < n^i, which is what makes dévissages the vehicle for induction on relative dimension. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `Devissage, Devissage.IsComplete, Devissage.length, Devissage.dim, Devissage.truncate, Devissage.baseChange, Devissage.cons, Devissage.reduction`. *Tests:* `devissage_test_complete_free` (computation): For ℳ = O_X^ℓ on X = D^N_T the one-step dévissage with α = id is complete of length 1. `devissage_test_lengthTwo` (computation): X = D²_T = Spf A⟨ξ_1, ξ_2⟩, ℳ = O_X/(ξ_1) ⊕ O_X: D¹ in dimension 2 with α¹: O_X → ℳ, 1 ↦ (0, 1), 𝒫¹ = O_X/(ξ_1), then D² in dimension 1 on Z² = Spf A⟨ξ_2⟩ with α² = id: complete of length 2. `devissage_test_single` (degenerate): A dévissage in dimensions between N and N is a single step D¹, complete iff 𝒫¹ = 0. `devissage_test_truncation` (non-example): The truncation (D¹) of the length-two example is a dévissage in dimensions between 2 and 2 but is not complete (𝒫¹ = O_X/(ξ_1) ≠ 0). *Source:* BL II §3, Def. 3.2, p. 411. *Needs:* R2.50.

- **R2.52** Existence of complete dévissages after elementary étale localisation (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 3.3). *Source:* BL II §3, before Prop. 3.3.

- **R2.53** Universal injectivity of α at z is bijectivity at the generic point of the fibre (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 3.6). *Source:* BL II §3, proof of Lem. 3.6.

- **R2.54** Flatness in terms of a dévissage (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 3.5). *Source:* BL II §3, before Prop. 3.5.

**R2.55 Flat locus and flatness in dimension ≥ n (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II §3, after Corollary 3.8)** (definition `FormalScheme.flatLocus`). Let f: X → T be a morphism of formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation and ℳ a coherent O_X-module. The flat locus of ℳ over T is F(ℳ/T) = {x ∈ X_0 : ℳ_x is a flat O_{T,f(x)}-module} ⊆ X_0 (points of X_0 are the points of X). For n ∈ ℕ and x ∈ X_0 with t = f(x), ℳ is T-flat in dimension ≥ n at x if dim_x((X_0 ∖ F(ℳ/T)) ∩ (X ⊗ k(t))) < n — the dimension at x of the non-flat locus inside the fibre, −∞ when x is not in its closure; ℳ is T-flat in dimension ≥ n if this holds at every x ∈ X_0, and T-flat in dimension ≥ n over an open U_0 ⊆ T_0 if it holds at every x over U_0. A morphism f is flat in dimension ≥ n if O_X is. Flatness in dimension ≥ n implies flatness in dimension ≥ n + 1, and if dim(X/T) ≤ n then every ℳ is T-flat in dimension ≥ n + 1. (Raynaud–Gruson 5.2.1 give the scheme-theoretic notion through a retrocompact open of flatness with small complement.) *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `flatLocus, mem_flatLocus_iff, IsFlatInDimAt, IsFlatInDim, IsFlatInDim.mono, isFlatInDim_of_relDim_le, IsFlatInDim.of_flat, flatLocus_etale, flatLocus_pushforward_finite`. *Tests:* `flatInDim_test_annulusModel` (computation): For X = Spf O_K⟨ζ, ξ⟩/(ζξ − ϖ) → T = Spf O_K⟨ζ⟩: F(O_X/T) = X_0 ∖ V(ζ); O_X is T-flat in dimension ≥ 2 and not in dimension ≥ 1 (the non-flat line ζ = 0 has dimension 1 in the fibre over ζ = 0). `flatInDim_test_zero` (degenerate): ℳ = 0: F(0/T) = X_0 and ℳ is T-flat in every dimension. `flatInDim_test_relDim` (characterisation): For X = D^n_T and any coherent ℳ, ℳ is T-flat in dimension ≥ n + 1. `flatInDim_test_fibreNotTotal` (non-example): For X = T = Spf O_K⟨s_1, s_2⟩, f = id and ℳ = O_T/(s_1): the non-flat locus V(s_1) ⊆ T_0 has dimension 1 but meets each fibre (a point) in dimension ≤ 0, so ℳ is T-flat in dimension ≥ 1; a definition using dim(X_0 ∖ F(ℳ/T)) instead of fibre dimensions would deny this. *Source:* BL II §3, before Cor. 3.7, p. 415. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.5.

- **R2.56** The flat locus of a coherent module is open (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Corollary 3.7). *Source:* BL II §3, Cor. 3.7.

- **R2.57** Flatness in dimension ≥ n in terms of a dévissage (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Corollary 3.8). *Source:* BL II §3, before Cor. 3.8.

**R2.58 Rig-flatness, relative rig-dimension and rig-flatness in dimension ≥ n (Bosch–Lütkebohmert I §5; II Definition 3.9 and §4)** (definition `FormalScheme.IsRigFlatAt`). Let f: X → T be a morphism of quasi-compact formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation with T admissible, and ℳ a coherent O_X-module. (i) Rig-flatness: for formal affine opens Spf C ⊆ X and Spf A ⊆ T with f(Spf C) ⊆ Spf A, ℳ ↔ the finite C-module M, and a rig-point x of Spf C (a maximal ideal of C_K = C ⊗_{O_K} K, node R2.14), ℳ is rig-flat over T at x if the localisation M_x is a flat A-module (equivalently (M_K)_x is flat over (A_K)_y, y the image maximal ideal); this is independent of the charts and invariant under admissible blow-ups of X and of T (Bosch–Lütkebohmert I Proposition 5.3). (ii) Relative rig-dimension (II Definition 3.9): for a rig-point x of X over the rig-point t of T, dim_x(X/T) is the Krull dimension of the local ring at x of the affinoid algebra C_K ⊗_{A_K} K(t), K(t) = A_K/t — the dimension at x of the rigid fibre X_rig ×_{T_rig} t; for a closed formal subscheme Y ⊆ X, dim(Y/T)_rig is the supremum of dim_y(Y/T) over the rig-points y of Y (−∞ if there are none). (iii) ℳ is rig-flat over T in dimension ≥ n if there is a closed formal subscheme Y ⊆ X of finite presentation with dim(Y/T)_rig ≤ n − 1 such that ℳ is rig-flat at every rig-point of X not factoring through Y; above a rig-point t of T if this holds for the rig-points of X and Y over t. Rig-flat in dimension ≥ 0 means rig-flat at every rig-point ('f_rig flat' for ℳ = O_X); if dim(X/T)_rig ≤ n − 1 then ℳ is rig-flat in dimension ≥ n. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `IsRigFlatAt, isRigFlatAt_iff_localization, IsRigFlatAt.admissibleBlowUp, rigRelDim, IsRigFlatInDim, IsRigFlatInDim.mono, isRigFlatInDim_of_rigRelDim_le, IsRigFlatInDim.pushforward_finite, IsRigFlatInDim.torsionQuotient, IsRigFlatInDim.baseChange, isRigFlatAt_iff_genericFibre_flat`. *Tests:* `rigFlat_test_annulusModel` (computation): X = Spf O_K⟨ζ, ξ⟩/(ζξ − ϖ) → T = Spf O_K⟨ζ⟩ is rig-flat at every rig-point, although not flat at x = (ζ, ξ). `rigFlat_test_torsion` (degenerate): ℳ = O_X/ϖO_X on any X: M_K = 0, so ℳ is rig-flat in every dimension. `rigFlat_test_closedPoint` (non-example): ℳ = O_T/(ζ) on X = T = Spf O_K⟨ζ⟩: not rig-flat at the rig-point ζ = 0, but rig-flat in dimension ≥ 1 (Y = V(ζ) has rig-dimension 0 over T); rig-flatness in dimension ≥ 1 does not imply rig-flatness. `rigFlat_test_affinoid` (compatibility): For a morphism Spf B → Spf A of admissible algebras, O is rig-flat at every rig-point iff A_K → B_K is flat at every maximal ideal of B_K, iff the morphism of adic spaces Spa(B_K, B_K°) → Spa(A_K, A_K°) is flat at all classical points. *Source:* BL I §5, p. 313. *Needs:* R2.14, R2.10, R2.6, R2.15, R2.16, R1.6, R0.97.

- **R2.59** Rig-flatness in top dimension is freeness at the generic point of the fibre (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 3.12). *Source:* BL II §3, before Prop. 3.12.

**R2.60 Fitting ideals of coherent modules on formal schemes (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Definition 3.13)** (construction `FormalScheme.fittingIdeal`). Let X be a formal O_K-scheme locally of tf presentation and ℳ a coherent O_X-module. For r ∈ ℕ the r-th Fitting ideal F_r(ℳ) ⊆ O_X is the coherent ideal given on a formal affine open U = Spf A by the ring-level Fitting ideal F_r(M) ⊆ A of the finitely presented A-module M = Γ(U, ℳ): for a presentation A^m →φ A^n → M → 0, F_r(M) is generated by the (n − r) × (n − r) minors of φ (F_r(M) = A if n − r ≤ 0), independently of the presentation and compatibly with base change, F_r(M ⊗_A A') = F_r(M)A' (Raynaud–Gruson I 5.4.1; the ring-level Fitting ideal of finitely presented modules is owned by the Tau Ceti roadmap StableReduction, Layer 0). These glue to a coherent ideal F_r(ℳ), compatible with restriction and with pull-back along morphisms of formal schemes; F_0(ℳ) ⊆ F_1(ℳ) ⊆ …; F_r(ℳ)_x = O_{X,x} iff ℳ_x is generated by r elements; if ℳ is locally free of rank r then F_{r−1}(ℳ) = 0 and F_r(ℳ) = O_X. The ring-level Fitting ideal and its scheme version are Tau Ceti's `fittingIdeal` and `Scheme.Modules.fittingIdeal` (with `fittingIdeal_Spec`, `fittingIdeal_monotone`); this target is the formal-scheme wrapper over those. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `fittingIdeal, fittingIdeal_spf, fittingIdeal_mono, fittingIdeal_pullback, fittingIdeal_restrict, fittingIdeal_eq_top_iff, fittingIdeal_of_locallyFree, fittingIdeal_isCoherent`. *Tests:* `fittingIdeal_test_cyclic` (computation): ℳ = O_X/𝒥: F_0(ℳ) = 𝒥 and F_1(ℳ) = O_X. `fittingIdeal_test_diagonal` (computation): For M = coker(diag(a_1, …, a_t) ⊕ 0: A^m → A^n) (t ≤ m, t ≤ n): F_{n−v}(M) is the ideal generated by the products a_{i_1} ⋯ a_{i_v} over the v-element subsets {i_1 < … < i_v} of {1, …, t} (the v × v minors of the diagonal matrix), and F_{n−v}(M) = 0 for v > t; when a_1 ∣ a_2 ∣ ⋯ ∣ a_t this is (a_1 ⋯ a_v). `fittingIdeal_test_zero` (degenerate): ℳ = 0: F_r(0) = O_X for every r ≥ 0. `fittingIdeal_test_baseChange` (compatibility): For ℳ = O_X/ϖO_X on X = Spf O_K⟨ζ⟩ and the open immersion Spf O_K⟨ζ, ζ⁻¹⟩ → X: F_0 = (ϖ) restricts to (ϖ). `fittingIdeal_test_notAnnihilator` (non-example): For ℳ = (O_X/ϖO_X)², Ann(ℳ) = (ϖ) but F_0(ℳ) = (ϖ²): the Fitting ideal is not the annihilator. *Source:* BL II §3, Def. 3.13, p. 418. *Needs:* R2.4, R2.5, R2.6, F0.17.

- **R2.61** A principal Fitting ideal makes M/Ann_M(F_r(M)) generated by r elements (Raynaud–Gruson I, Lemme 5.4.2). *Source:* RG Première partie, §5.4, Lemme 5.4.2.

- **R2.62** An invertible Fitting ideal and generic freeness give local freeness modulo the annihilator (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 3.14 = Raynaud–Gruson I, Lemme 5.4.3). *Source:* BL II §3, Lem. 3.14.

- **R2.63** An open and closed partition of the generic fibre extends to an admissible blow-up (formal form of Raynaud–Gruson I, Lemme 5.1.5). *Source:* RG Première partie, §5.1, Lemme 5.1.5.

- **R2.64** Flattening for modules on smooth formal schemes with geometrically irreducible fibres (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Special case 4.4). *Source:* BL II §4, before Special case 4.4.

- **R2.65** Torsion in extensions of a module of small support by a free module (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 4.5). *Source:* BL II §4, Lem. 4.5.

- **R2.66** In a dévissage of a module flat in dimension ≥ m + 1, the upper maps are universally injective and all 𝒩^i stay rig-flat (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 4.6). *Source:* BL II §4, proof of Lem. 4.6(a).

- **R2.67** One step of the flattening induction: enlarging the locus of flatness in dimension ≥ m (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Lemma 4.7). *Source:* BL II §4, proof of Lem. 4.7.

- **R2.68** Lowering the dimension of the non-flat locus by one admissible blow-up (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Proposition 4.2). *Source:* BL II §4, Prop. 4.2.

**R2.69 Flattening a coherent module by an admissible blow-up of the base (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Theorem 4.1)** (theorem). Let T be a quasi-compact admissible formal O_K-scheme (K complete of rank one, not necessarily discretely valued), f: X → T a morphism of quasi-compact formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation, ℳ a coherent O_X-module and n ∈ ℕ such that ℳ is rig-flat over T in dimension ≥ n (node R2.58). Then there is an admissible blow-up T' → T such that the strict transform ℳ' of ℳ on X' = X ×_T T' (node R2.15) is T'-flat in dimension ≥ n (node R2.55). *Hypotheses:* T quasi-compact admissible; X quasi-compact (Bosch–Lütkebohmert's standing assumption in §3) and locally of tf presentation, possibly with ϖ-torsion. The blow-up is of the base T, and ℳ' is the strict transform, not the pull-back. Classical rigid case only: Bosch–Lütkebohmert's noetherian base case uses parallel arguments that are not decomposed here. *Source:* BL II §4, Thm 4.1, p. 419. *Needs:* R2.68, R2.55, R2.58, R2.15, R2.6.

- **R2.70** The flattened strict transform is the pull-back modulo ϖ-torsion off a small closed set (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Theorem 4.1, additional claim). *Source:* BL II §4, Thm 4.1.

### Formal completions of schemes and the good-reduction locus

**R2.71 Formal completion of a scheme, the morphism σ: d(X̂) → X and the comparison φ: d(X̂) → X ×_Y d(Ŷ)** (comparison). (1.9.4) For a scheme X and its formal completion X̂ along a closed subscheme, assumed of type (S), there is a natural morphism of locally ringed spaces σ_X: d(X̂) → X, the unique one such that for every open U ⊆ X, with e = (X̂ → X) ∘ λ, one has e⁻¹(U) ⊆ σ⁻¹(U) and e* = σ* on O_X(U). (1.9.5) Let f: X → Y be locally of finite type between schemes, Y' ⊆ Y closed and defined by a quasi-coherent ideal of finite type, X' = X ×_Y Y', and X̂, Ŷ the completions along X' and Y'. If Ŷ is of type (S) then so is X̂, f̂ is adic, and the square formed by σ_X, σ_Y, d(f̂) and f induces a d(Ŷ)-morphism φ: d(X̂) → X ×_Y d(Ŷ) into the fibre product of a scheme locally of finite type with an adic space (R1.13). Proposition 1.9.6: (i) φ is a local isomorphism; (ii) if f is separated, φ is injective; (iii) if f is universally specializing (e.g. universally closed, e.g. proper), φ is surjective. Hence if f is separated and universally specializing, φ is an isomorphism. The source says 'One can easily prove' and gives no proof; the proofs below are supplied here. *Hypotheses:* X̂ (resp. Ŷ) of type (S); the ideal defining Y' of finite type; f locally of finite type. (ii) requires f separated; (iii) requires f universally specializing. σ and the fibre product X ×_Y d(Ŷ) come from Huber 1994 [Hu, 4.6.iv], [Hu4, 3.10.7] and [Hu, 3.8]; [Hu4] is not public. *Source:* Huber96 §1.9, Prop. 1.9.6, p. 98. *Needs:* R2.9, R2.7, R2.12, R1.13, F0.30, F0.19.

**R2.72 The generic fibre of the completion of an O_K-scheme is the integral (good-reduction) locus of the analytified generic fibre** (theorem). Let K be complete of rank one, X a separated O_K-scheme locally of finite type, X_K its generic fibre and X_K^ad = X ×_{Spec O_K} Spa(K, O_K) its adic analytification (R1.15). Let X̂ be the ϖ-adic completion of X (a formal O_K-scheme locally tft, of type (S)). Then the comparison map φ_X: d(X̂) → X_K^ad of node R2.71 (with Y = Spec O_K, Y' = V(ϖ)) is an open embedding, whose image is the integral locus X_K^{ad,int} := {x ∈ X_K^ad : the morphism Spec κ(x) → X extends to Spec κ(x)⁺ → X}, κ(x) the completed residue field and κ(x)⁺ its valuation ring. Under φ_X the specialisation λ(x) ∈ X_s of x ∈ d(X̂) is the image of the closed point of that extension. If X is proper over O_K, φ_X is an isomorphism d(X̂) ≅ X_K^ad. A classical point of X_K^ad (residue field K' finite over K) lies in the integral locus iff it extends to an O_{K'}-point of X. *Hypotheses:* X separated and locally of finite type over O_K; K complete, nontrivial rank one, discreteness not assumed. X_K^ad is Huber's fibre product X ×_{Spec O_K} Spa(K, O_K) (R1). The converse 'image = X_K^ad implies proper' needs flatness and is node R2.73. *Source:* Huber96 §1.9, Prop. 1.9.6, p. 98. *Needs:* R2.71, R2.7, R2.9, R2.12, F0.19, R1.15, R1.34.

- **R2.73** If the completion's generic fibre is all of the analytified generic fibre, a flat quasi-projective model is proper. *Source:* FK Ch. 0, §9.2(a), Cor. 9.2.3.

### Conrad's FS_C, smooth formal schemes, étale sites, Frobenius lifts and residue discs

**R2.74 Conrad's Berthelot-type functor (·)^rig on FS_C for arbitrary complete k** (construction `FormalScheme.FSC`).
