Fix an adic noetherian ring C with a continuous map C → R to the valuation ring R of a complete nonarchimedean field k (no discreteness assumption on k; examples: C = R if k is discretely valued; C = R_0 a complete discrete valuation ring with R_0 → R local; C = Z discrete). FS_C is the category of locally noetherian formal schemes X over Spf C whose underlying reduced scheme is locally of finite type over C_red; affine opens have algebras that are quotients of C[[q_1, …, q_n]]{{T_1, …, T_m}} with the (I, q)-adic topology. For X in FS_C and a rigid space Z over k, Hom^cont_C(Z, X) is the set of morphisms of locally ringed G-topologised spaces whose ring maps are C-algebra maps landing in locally power-bounded sections and carrying locally topologically nilpotent sections to locally topologically nilpotent sections. Theorem 3.1.5: Hom^cont_C(·, X) is represented by a quasi-separated rigid space X^rig; (·)^rig takes open/closed immersions to open/closed immersions and open covers to admissible covers, commutes with fibre products (a), with analytic extension of k (b) and with change of C (c), preserves separatedness (d), and sends topologically finite type maps to quasi-compact maps (e). Spf(C[[q]])^rig is the open unit disc Δ = {|t| < 1}. Through R1.31 this gives an adic space r(X^rig) over Spa(k, R). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `FSC, rigOfFSC, rigOfFSC.homEquiv, rigOfFSC_isOpenImmersion, rigOfFSC_pullback, rigOfFSC_baseChangeField, rigOfFSC_changeCoefficients, rigOfFSC_isSeparated, rigOfFSC_quasiCompact, rigOfFSC_powerSeries, rigOfFSC_eq_genericFibre`. *Tests:* `rigOfFSC_test_powerSeries` (computation): For C = Z (discrete) and any k, Spf(Z[[q]])^rig is the open unit disc Δ = {|t| < 1} over k, admissibly covered by the closed discs {|t| ≤ r}. `rigOfFSC_test_nondiscrete` (non-example): For k = C_p, Spf O_k⟨T⟩ is not an object of FS_C for any C (it is not locally noetherian); its generic fibre is given by Raynaud's functor instead. `rigOfFSC_test_restricted` (compatibility): For k discretely valued and C = R, Spf R{{T}}^rig is the closed unit disc, equal to Raynaud's generic fibre of Spf R⟨T⟩. `rigOfFSC_test_point` (degenerate): Spf(C)^rig = Sp(k), the final object of rigid spaces over k. *Source:* Conrad-MR §3.1, Thm 3.1.5, p. 16. *Needs:* F0.16, F0.10, R1.7, R1.31, R2.10, R2.8, R2.20, R0.55.

**R2.75 Smooth and étale morphisms of formal schemes locally of tf presentation; continuous differentials** (definition `FormalScheme.IsSmoothAt`). Let f: X → Y be a morphism of formal O_K-schemes locally of tf presentation (for P7: O_K = W(κ), κ perfect). f is smooth at x ∈ X of relative dimension d if there are an open neighbourhood U of x and a Y-immersion j: U → D^n_Y = Spf O_Y⟨ζ_1, …, ζ_n⟩ such that, locally at j(x), the ideal of j(U) is generated by n − d sections g_{d+1}, …, g_n whose differentials are linearly independent in Ω¹_{D^n_Y/Y} ⊗ κ(j(x)); f is smooth if smooth at all points, and étale if smooth of relative dimension 0 (Bosch–Lütkebohmert II Definition 1.1). Equivalently (II Lemma 1.2): f is flat at x and f_0: X_0 → Y_0 is smooth at x of relative dimension d; equivalently every reduction f_m: X_m → Y_m modulo ϖ^{m+1} is smooth at x of relative dimension d (Mathlib AlgebraicGeometry.Smooth for schemes). Equivalently f is formally smooth and locally topologically of finite presentation, and then locally f factors as an étale morphism to D^d_Y. The module of continuous differentials Ω¹_{X/Y} := lim_m Ω¹_{X_m/Y_m} is coherent; for f smooth of relative dimension d it is locally free of rank d, with basis dT_1, …, dT_d on an étale chart X → D^d_Y. A formal scheme smooth over Spf O_K is admissible. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `IsSmoothAt, IsSmooth, isSmoothAt_iff_flat_specialFibre, isSmooth_iff_forall_level, IsSmooth.exists_etale_affineSpace, kaehler, IsSmooth.kaehler_locallyFree, IsSmooth.isAdmissible, IsSmooth.comp, IsSmooth.of_isEtale, IsSmooth.genericFibre, IsEtale.lift_unique, IsElementaryEtaleNbhd`. *Tests:* `smoothFormal_test_affineSpace` (computation): Spf O_K⟨T_1, …, T_d⟩ → Spf O_K is smooth of relative dimension d and Ω¹ is free with basis dT_1, …, dT_d. `smoothFormal_test_torus` (computation): The formal torus Spf W(κ)⟨T_1^{±1}, …, T_d^{±1}⟩ is smooth of relative dimension d over Spf W(κ). `smoothFormal_test_semistable` (non-example): Spf O_K⟨S,T⟩/(ST − ϖ) → Spf O_K is flat with smooth generic fibre but is not smooth: its special fibre k[S,T]/(ST) is singular at the origin. `smoothFormal_test_levels` (compatibility): For a smooth O_K-scheme X, the ϖ-adic completion X̂ is smooth over Spf O_K, and each X̂_m = X ⊗ O_K/ϖ^{m+1} is smooth in the sense of mathlib AlgebraicGeometry.Smooth. `smoothFormal_test_identity` (degenerate): id: Spf O_K → Spf O_K is étale (smooth of relative dimension 0) and Ω¹ = 0. *Source:* BL II §1, Def. 1.1, p. 404. *Needs:* R2.6, R2.5, R2.76, R0.99, R0.100, R0.93.

**R2.76 The étale site of a formal scheme is the étale site of its special fibre; the generic-fibre functor on étale objects** (comparison). Let 𝔛 be a formal O_K-scheme locally of tf presentation, 𝔛_m = 𝔛 ⊗ O_K/ϖ^{m+1}. (a) The functor 𝔜 ↦ 𝔜_0 is an equivalence from the category of formal schemes étale over 𝔛 (adic, locally tfp, each 𝔜_m → 𝔛_m étale) to the category of schemes étale over 𝔛_0; composing with the topological invariance for 𝔛_{s,red} ⊆ 𝔛_0, both are equivalent to schemes étale over 𝔛_{s,red}. The equivalence preserves fibre products, open immersions and jointly surjective families, so it is an equivalence of small étale sites 𝔛_ét ≃ (𝔛_0)_ét ≃ (𝔛_{s,red})_ét. (b) For 𝔜 → 𝔛 étale, d(𝔜) → d(𝔛) is an étale morphism of adic spaces (R0.100); d preserves fibre products (node R2.11); and if (𝔜_i → 𝔛) is a covering of 𝔛_ét then (d(𝔜_i) → d(𝔛)) is jointly surjective. Hence 𝔜 ↦ d(𝔜) is a continuous functor 𝔛_ét → d(𝔛)_ét, and O_{𝔛_ét}(𝔜) := Γ(𝔜, O_𝔜) is a sheaf on 𝔛_ét. *Hypotheses:* 𝔛 locally tfp over O_K (the same holds for adic noetherian formal schemes with an ideal of definition). The étale site of the adic space d(𝔛) is AdicEtaleGeometry A1's; the morphism of topoi w: X_proét → 𝔛_ét is built by the consumer from the functor of (b). *Source:* Stacks Tag 039R (Étale Morphisms of Schemes, Thm 41.15.2). *Needs:* R2.6, R2.9, R2.11, R2.12, R0.100, R0.114, F0.19, F0.15.

- **R2.77** Unique Frobenius lift on a formal scheme étale over the formal torus. *Source:* TT §2.3, Rem. 2.20.

- **R2.78** The residue disc of a rational smooth point is an open unit polydisc. *Source:* GK02 §2, Lem. 2.6.

### Fractional bounds, section domains and Hasse domains

**R2.79 Fractional valuation bounds |f| ≤ |g|^q and |f| ≥ |g|^q for q ∈ ℚ_{≥0}** (definition `ValuationSpectrum.fracPowLE`). Let A be a commutative ring, f, g ∈ A and q ∈ ℚ_{≥0}. For v ∈ Spv A write q = a/b with a ∈ ℕ, b ∈ ℕ_{>0}, and define |f(v)| ≤ |g(v)|^q to mean v(f^b) ≤ v(g^a) (the valuative relation f^b ≤_v g^a), and |f(v)| ≥ |g(v)|^q to mean v(f^b) ≥ v(g^a); strict versions likewise. This does not depend on the representative (a, b) of q, because x ↦ x^n is strictly increasing on a totally ordered value monoid. Put fracPowLE f g q := {v ∈ Spv A : v(f^b) ≤ v(g^a)} and fracPowGE f g q := {v : v(f^b) ≥ v(g^a)}. No element 'g^q' of A (or of the base field) is used: when K = Q_p, |p|^{1/2} is not the absolute value of any element of K, yet {|f| ≤ |p|^{1/2}} is defined. Second convention, compatible with the first: if B is an A⁺-algebra containing c and a unit u of B⁺ with c^b = g^a u (e.g. c = ϖ^{a/b} ∈ L = K(ϖ^{1/b}), or c = p^ε ∈ Z_p^cycl of p-adic valuation ε), then for v ∈ Spa(B, B⁺) one has |f(v)| ≤ |g(v)|^q ⟺ v(f) ≤ v(c). When g is a unit (e.g. g = ϖ in a Tate ring), fracPowLE f g q ∩ Spa(A, A⁺) is the rational subset R({f^b, g^a}/g^a) and fracPowGE f g q ∩ Spa(A, A⁺) is R({g^a, f^b}/f^b). Tau Ceti's `IsRadiusLowerBound`/`IsRadiusUpperBound` on Spv of a Witt vector ring are the case f = [ϖ], g = p; this target is the general pair (f, g), and those predicates must be its specialisation. *API* (on `ValuationSpectrum`): `fracPowLE, fracPowGE, mem_fracPowLE_iff, fracPowLE_natCast, fracPowLE_inter_spa, fracPowGE_inter_spa, fracPowLE_mono, comap_fracPowLE, fracPowLE_iff_of_pow_eq, fracPowGE_mul_unit, fracPowLE_pow, fracPowLE_mul`. *Tests:* `fracPowLE_test_halfDisc` (computation): In Spa(Q_p⟨T⟩, Z_p⟨T⟩): fracPowLE T p (1/2) = {v : v(T²) ≤ v(p)}; the classical point T = p lies in it, the classical point T = 1 does not, and it contains no Q_p-rational point with |T| = p^{-1/2}. `fracPowLE_test_representative` (characterisation): fracPowLE f g (2/4) = fracPowLE f g (1/2) for all f, g: membership is v(f⁴) ≤ v(g²) iff v(f²) ≤ v(g). `fracPowLE_test_rounding` (non-example): Rounding the exponent down (|f| ≤ |g|^{⌊q⌋}) is a different set: for q = 1/2, f = T, g = p it gives the whole unit disc {|T| ≤ 1} ≠ {|T²| ≤ |p|}. `fracPowLE_test_root` (compatibility): For L = Q_p(√p) with c = √p (c² = p): the preimage of fracPowLE T p (1/2) in Spa(L⟨T⟩, O_L⟨T⟩) equals {v : v(T) ≤ v(c)}. `fracPowLE_test_zero` (degenerate): fracPowLE f g 0 = {v : v(f) ≤ 1} and fracPowGE f g 0 = {v : v(f) ≥ 1}. *Source:* BHW §2.1, p. 1721.

**R2.80 The domain {|s| ≥ |ϖ|^q} cut out by a section of an invertible sheaf on a formal model** (construction `FormalScheme.sectionDomainGE`). Let 𝔛 be an admissible formal O_K-scheme, ℒ an invertible O_𝔛-module, s ∈ Γ(𝔛, ℒ) and q ∈ ℚ_{≥0}. Choose an open cover 𝔛 = ⋃ U_j by formal affines U_j = Spf A_j with generators ℒ|_{U_j} = O e_j, and write s|_{U_j} = f_j e_j. Define 𝔛_η(|s| ≥ |ϖ|^q) := ⋃_j (fracPowGE f_j ϖ q ∩ d(U_j)) ⊆ d(𝔛), and likewise 𝔛_η(|s| ≤ |ϖ|^q) with fracPowLE. These sets do not depend on the cover or the generators: on U_j ∩ U_k one has e_k = u e_j with u ∈ O(U_j ∩ U_k)^×, so f_j = u f_k, and |u(x)| = 1 on d(U_j ∩ U_k) because u, u⁻¹ ∈ O ⊆ O⁺. They are open (finite unions of rational subsets when 𝔛 is quasi-compact) and functorial under pull-back. Independence of lifts: if s' ∈ Γ(𝔛, ℒ) with s − s' ∈ ϖ^c ℒ for some c ∈ ℚ with c > q (ϖ^c ℒ meaning sections whose local coordinates satisfy |·| ≤ |ϖ|^c), then 𝔛_η(|s| ≥ |ϖ|^q) = 𝔛_η(|s'| ≥ |ϖ|^q); hence a section s̄ of ℒ ⊗ O_K/ϖ^m (m > q) determines a well-defined domain 𝔛_η(|s̄| ≥ |ϖ|^q) through arbitrary local lifts. The domain depends on the integral structure ℒ, not only on the invertible sheaf ℒ_η on d(𝔛). *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `sectionDomainGE, sectionDomainLE, sectionDomainGE_of_trivialization, sectionDomainGE_indep_generator, isOpen_sectionDomainGE, sectionDomainGE_preimage, sectionDomainGE_congr, sectionDomainGE_ofMod, sectionDomainGE_mono, sectionDomainGE_zero, sectionDomainGE_pow, sectionDomainGE_tensor`. *Tests:* `sectionDomain_test_projectiveLine` (computation): For 𝔛 = P̂¹_{O_K}, ℒ = O(1), s = T_0 and q = 1: sectionDomainGE s 1 = {x : |T_0(x)| ≥ |ϖ| · max(|T_0(x)|, |T_1(x)|)}, the complement of the open disc {|T_0/T_1| < |ϖ|}. `sectionDomain_test_generatorIndependence` (characterisation): On 𝔛 = Spf O_K⟨T, T⁻¹⟩ with ℒ = O, the generators e and T·e give the same domain for s = T² e = T·(Te): {|T²| ≥ |ϖ|^q} = {|T| ≥ |ϖ|^q} because |T| = 1 on d(𝔛). `sectionDomain_test_liftIndependence` (characterisation): On Spf O_K⟨T⟩ with ℒ = O and q = 1/2: s = T and s' = T + ϖ give the same domain {|T²| ≥ |ϖ|}; for q = 1 they do not (the point T = −ϖ lies in the first but not the second). `sectionDomain_test_integralStructure` (non-example): Replacing ℒ by the lattice ϖ⁻¹ℒ ⊇ ℒ in the same ℒ_η multiplies the local coordinates of s by ϖ and changes the domain from {|s| ≥ |ϖ|^q} to {|s|_ℒ ≥ |ϖ|^{q−1}}; a definition using only ℒ_η is ill-defined. `sectionDomain_test_degenerate` (degenerate): If s̄ generates ℒ/ϖℒ everywhere then the domain is d(𝔛) for every q ≥ 0; if s = 0 it is empty. *Source:* BHW §2.1, p. 1721. *Needs:* R2.79, R2.6, R2.9, R2.12.

**R2.81 Formal model of a section domain: the open chart of the admissible blow-up of (s, c)** (construction `FormalScheme.sectionDomainModel`). Let 𝔛 be an admissible formal O_K-scheme, ℒ invertible, s ∈ Γ(𝔛, ℒ), and c ∈ O_K ∖ {0} (the threshold, e.g. c = ϖ^a, or c = p^ε when K contains such an element; for a fractional q = a/b without such an element use (s^{⊗b}, ϖ^a) in ℒ^{⊗b}). Let 𝒜 ⊆ O_𝔛 be the coherent open ideal locally generated by (f_j, c) where s = f_j e_j (independent of generators), and let 𝔛(|s| ≥ |c|) ⊆ Bl_𝒜 𝔛 be the open formal subscheme where s generates 𝒜O. Locally, over U = Spf A with ℒ|_U = Ae and s = fe, 𝔛(|s| ≥ |c|) ×_𝔛 U = Spf(A⟨u⟩/(fu − c) modulo ϖ-torsion), with u ∈ Γ(ℒ⁻¹) the section with u·s = c. Universal property: for a ϖ-torsion-free formal scheme Z over Spf O_K with g: Z → 𝔛, lifts of g to 𝔛(|s| ≥ |c|) correspond bijectively to sections u ∈ Γ(Z, g*ℒ⁻¹) with u·g*s = c (such u is unique if it exists). Generic fibre: d(𝔛(|s| ≥ |c|)) → d(𝔛) is an open immersion with image {x : |s(x)| ≥ |c(x)|} = sectionDomainGE s q when |c| = |ϖ|^q. Change of lift: if s' = s + c' t with c' ∈ c·m_K (so |c'| < |c|) and t ∈ Γ(𝔛, ℒ), the models for s and s' are canonically isomorphic over 𝔛 via u' = u(1 + (c'/c) u t)⁻¹, compatibly with the equality of generic-fibre domains; if c'' = wc with w ∈ O_K^×, the models for c and c'' are isomorphic via u'' = wu. Flatness: if f̄ is a non-zero-divisor on A/ϖ and c ∈ m_K (or c a unit), A⟨u⟩/(fu − c) is already ϖ-torsion-free. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `sectionDomainModel, sectionDomainModel.toBlowUp, sectionDomainModel_affine, sectionDomainModel.homEquiv, sectionDomainModel.u_mul_s, sectionDomainModel_genericFibre, sectionDomainModel_isoOfLift, sectionDomainModel_isoOfAssociated, sectionDomainModel_flat_of_isRegular, sectionDomainModel_mapOfDvd`. *Tests:* `sectionDomainModel_test_annulus` (computation): For 𝔛 = Spf O_K⟨T⟩, ℒ = O, s = T, c = ϖ: sectionDomainModel = Spf O_K⟨T,u⟩/(Tu − ϖ), already ϖ-torsion-free, with generic fibre {|ϖ| ≤ |T| ≤ 1}. `sectionDomainModel_test_torsion` (non-example): For s = ϖT on Spf O_K⟨T⟩ and c = ϖ, the ring O_K⟨T,u⟩/(ϖTu − ϖ) has nonzero ϖ-torsion (the class of Tu − 1); only after killing it does one get O_K⟨T, T⁻¹⟩, whose generic fibre {|T| = 1} = {|ϖT| ≥ |ϖ|} is correct. `sectionDomainModel_test_points` (characterisation): For s = T, c = ϖ on Spf O_K⟨T⟩: the O_K-point T ↦ ϖ lifts uniquely (u = 1), while T ↦ ϖ² does not lift (uϖ² = ϖ has no solution in O_K). `sectionDomainModel_test_unitThreshold` (degenerate): For c = 1 the model is the open formal subscheme D(s̄) = 𝔛 ∖ V(s̄), and no blow-up is needed; for s = 1 it is 𝔛. `sectionDomainModel_test_scholze` (compatibility): For 𝔛 = X* (p-adic completion of the minimal compactification), ℒ = ω^{⊗(p−1)}, s = H̃a and c = p^ε ∈ Z_p^cycl, the model is Scholze's X*(ε) and its generic fibre is {|Ha| ≥ |p|^ε}. *Source:* Scholze15 §III.2, Lem. III.2.13, p. 38. *Needs:* R2.15, R2.16, R2.80, R2.79, R2.6, R2.5.

**R2.82 Hasse domains X(ε) = {|Ha| ≥ |p|^ε} and their formal models** (construction `FormalScheme.hasseDomain`). Let p be a prime, K a complete extension of Q_p (|p| = p⁻¹), 𝔛 an admissible formal O_K-scheme, ω an invertible O_𝔛-module, and Ha ∈ Γ(𝔛 ⊗_{O_K} O_K/p, ω^{⊗(p−1)}) a section (the Hasse invariant; for modular curves and Shimura varieties it is supplied by the moduli roadmaps). For ε ∈ ℚ with 0 ≤ ε < 1 the Hasse domain is X(ε) := 𝔛_η(|Ha| ≥ |p|^ε) ⊆ d(𝔛), the section domain (node R2.80) of any local lift H̃a of Ha to ω^{⊗(p−1)}, with the fractional convention of node R2.79; it is well defined because two lifts differ by p·(section) and |p| < |p|^ε for ε < 1. If O_K contains an element p^ε of p-adic valuation ε (an explicitly chosen element; for instance the completion of Q_p(μ_{p^∞}) contains such elements for every ε ∈ (1/(p−1))·ℤ[1/p]), the formal model 𝔛(ε) := 𝔛(|H̃a| ≥ |p^ε|) (node R2.81) is independent of the local lift up to canonical isomorphism — equivalently it represents, on p-adically complete flat O_K-algebras S, pairs (g: Spf S → 𝔛, u ∈ Γ(Spf S, g*ω^{⊗(1−p)})) with u·Ha(ḡ) = p^ε in S/p, modulo u ~ u(1 + p^{1−ε}h) — and d(𝔛(ε)) = X(ε). X(0) = {|Ha| = 1} is the tube of the ordinary locus 𝔛_s ∖ V(Ha). Formation of X(ε) and 𝔛(ε) commutes with pull-back along morphisms 𝔜 → 𝔛 (with the pulled-back ω and Ha) and with base change along complete extensions of K. *API* (on `FormalScheme`): `hasseDomain, hasseDomain_eq_sectionDomainGE, hasseDomain_indep_lift, hasseDomainModel, hasseDomainModel.homEquiv, hasseDomainModel_genericFibre, hasseDomainModel_isoOfLift, hasseDomain_zero, hasseDomain_mono, hasseDomain_preimage, hasseDomainModel_flat`. *Tests:* `hasseDomain_test_toy` (computation): For 𝔛 = Spf Z_p⟨T⟩, ω = O, Ha = T mod p: hasseDomain (1/2) = {v : v(T²) ≥ v(p)}, and over O_K = Z_p[√p] the model is Spf O_K⟨T,u⟩/(Tu − √p). `hasseDomain_test_liftAtOne` (non-example): For ε = 1 the locus depends on the lift: with H̃a = T and H̃a' = T + p on Spf Z_p⟨T⟩, the classical point T = −p lies in {|T| ≥ |p|} but not in {|T + p| ≥ |p|}; hence the restriction ε < 1. `hasseDomain_test_ordinary` (degenerate): hasseDomain 0 = {|Ha| = 1} = tube of the ordinary locus 𝔛_s ∖ V(Ha); for Ha invertible (𝔛_s ordinary) every X(ε) is d(𝔛). `hasseDomain_test_scholze` (compatibility): For Scholze's X* over Z_p^cycl with p^ε ∈ Z_p^cycl, hasseDomainModel ε ≅ X*(ε) of Definition III.2.12 and its generic fibre is {|Ha| ≥ |p|^ε}. `hasseDomain_test_noRoot` (non-example): For K = Q_p and ε = 1/2 there is no element of Q_p of absolute value |p|^{1/2}: hasseDomain (1/2) is still defined by |H̃a²| ≥ |p|, while the formal model requires Z_p[√p] (or the blow-up of (H̃a², p) in ω^{⊗2(p−1)}). *Source:* Scholze15 §III.2, Def. III.2.12, p. 38. *Needs:* R2.80, R2.81, R2.79, R2.6, R2.13, R2.11.

- **R2.83** Transition maps between Hasse domains as the radius changes. *Source:* Conrad-MR §4.1, Thm 4.1.1(1).

**R2.84 Conrad's Hasse-invariant loci S^{>h}, S^{≥h} of a generalized elliptic curve are admissible opens** (construction `AdicSpace.hasseInvariantValue`). For a generalized elliptic curve E → S over a rigid space S over k (k complete over Q_p, |p| = p⁻¹), the Hasse invariant of a fibre E_s is h(E_s) = max(|a|, 1/p) ∈ [1/p, 1] (Definition B.3.3: a lifts the coefficient of X^p in [p]*(X) mod p on the formal group of a semistable model). The loci S^{>h} (h ∈ [1/p, 1) ∩ √|k^×|) and S^{≥h} (h ∈ (1/p, 1] ∩ √|k^×|) of points with h(E_s) > h (resp. ≥ h) are admissible opens; their formation commutes with base change on S and, for quasi-separated or pseudo-separated S (Conrad 4.1.1(2)), with analytic extension of k; S^{≥h} → S is quasi-compact; and {S^{≥h}}_{h ∈ (h_0,1)} is an admissible cover of S^{>h_0}. Local description: on a formal affine Spf A of the formal completion M̂ of the proper moduli scheme M over which e*Ω̂¹ is trivial, with Γ_A ≅ Spf A[[X]] the formal group and a_{Γ_A} the coefficient of X^p in [p]*(X), S^{>h} ∩ (Spf A)^rig = {|a_{Γ_A}| > h} and S^{≥h} ∩ (Spf A)^rig = {|a_{Γ_A}| ≥ h}, independent of the coordinate X. Thresholds h ∈ √|k^×| are read with the convention of node R2.79. Through R1's comparison r these are opens of the adic space, and for h = |p|^ε with 0 ≤ ε < 1 (so h ∈ (1/p, 1]) S^{≥h} is the Hasse domain X(ε) of node R2.82 for ω the Hodge bundle and Ha the Hasse invariant (a ≡ Ha in the trivialisation of ω by the dual of dX). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `hasseInvariantValue, hasseLocusGT, hasseLocusGE, isOpen_hasseLocusGE, hasseLocusGE_quasiCompact, hasseLocus_baseChange, hasseLocus_extendScalars, iUnion_hasseLocusGE, hasseLocusGE_local, hasseLocusGE_eq_hasseDomain`. *Tests:* `hasseLocus_test_ordinary` (computation): For an elliptic curve E over k with good ordinary reduction, h(E) = 1, so the point lies in S^{≥1}; for good supersingular reduction h(E) < 1. `hasseLocus_test_floor` (degenerate): h(E_s) ≥ 1/p always, so the locus {h(E_s) ≥ 1/p} is all of S; this is why S^{≥h} is only defined for h > 1/p. `hasseLocus_test_withoutMax` (non-example): For h < 1/p the locus {|a_{Γ_A}| ≥ h} can be strictly smaller than S (a can be divisible by p), whereas {h(E_s) ≥ h} = S: dropping the max(·, 1/p) in Definition B.3.3 gives a different, coordinate-dependent set. `hasseLocus_test_hasseDomain` (compatibility): For S = M^an with the universal curve and h = |p|^ε, 0 ≤ ε < 1: S^{≥h} = X(ε), the Hasse domain of M̂ with ω the Hodge bundle and Ha the Hasse invariant. `hasseLocus_test_fractionalThreshold` (characterisation): For h = |c|^{1/n} ∈ √|k^×| (c ∈ k^×), S^{≥h} ∩ (Spf A)^rig = {|a_{Γ_A}^n| ≥ |c|}, independent of the choice of (c, n) representing h. *Source:* Conrad-MR §4.1, proof of Thm 4.1.1, p. 27. *Needs:* R2.74, R2.72, R2.14, R2.79, R2.80, R2.82, R2.83, R2.11, R1.7, R1.31, R1.15, R2.88.

- **R2.85** Flatness from flatness modulo powers of an ideal (the adic flatness criterion behind Bosch–Lütkebohmert I Lemma 1.6). *Source:* BL I §1, proof of Lem. 1.6.

**R2.86 Conrad's Berthelot-type generic fibre preserves finiteness, smoothness, flatness, properness and fibre dimension (Conrad genpaper Theorem 3.1.6)** (theorem). Let C be an adic noetherian ring with a continuous map C → R to the valuation ring of a complete nonarchimedean field k (no discreteness assumption), and let f: X → Y be a morphism in FS_C that is locally topologically of finite type. Then: (a) if f is finite so is f^rig; (b) if f is formally smooth of pure relative dimension d then f^rig is smooth of pure relative dimension d (formally étale ⇒ étale); (c) if f is finite flat of constant rank d so is f^rig; (d) if f is proper so is f^rig; (e) if f is flat (resp. faithfully flat) so is f^rig; (f) if all fibres of f have dimension ≤ d so do those of f^rig; (g) if f is flat with geometrically reduced fibres of pure dimension d, so is f^rig. *Hypotheses:* f locally topologically of finite type in FS_C; k arbitrary complete nonarchimedean. (d) without discreteness uses Temkin [Te, Cor. 4.4, 4.5] and (f),(g) use Conrad Theorem A.2.1 (change of base field via Raynaud models); neither is a target of this roadmap: (d) is the gap recorded at R2.22, and Conrad A.2.1 is cited and not re-proved here. *Source:* Conrad-MR §3.1, Thm 3.1.6, p. 17. *Needs:* R2.74, R2.10, R2.20, R2.22, R2.75, R2.76, R1.7.

**R2.87 Coherent modules and coherent rings (the ring case of EGA 0 §5.3, as recalled in Bosch–Lütkebohmert I §1)** (definition `Module.IsCoherent`). Let A be a commutative ring. A finitely generated A-module M is coherent if every finitely generated submodule of M is finitely presented; A is a coherent ring if it is coherent as an A-module (every finitely generated ideal is finitely presented). If two of the modules in a short exact sequence 0 → M' → M → M'' → 0 are coherent, so is the third; hence finite direct sums, kernels, cokernels and images of maps between coherent modules, and sums and intersections of coherent submodules of a coherent module, are coherent. Over a coherent ring a module is coherent iff it is finitely presented, and M ⊗_A N and Hom_A(M, N) are coherent for coherent M, N. If A is coherent and a ⊆ A is a finitely generated ideal, A/a is a coherent ring and an A/a-module is coherent over A/a iff it is coherent over A. *API* (on `Module`): `IsCoherent, IsCoherentRing, IsCoherent.of_exact_left / of_exact_right / of_exact_middle, IsCoherent.ker / coker / range / sup / inf, IsCoherentRing.isCoherent_iff_finitePresentation, IsCoherent.tensorProduct / linearMap, IsCoherentRing.quotient, IsNoetherianRing.isCoherentRing`. *Tests:* `coherent_test_noetherian` (compatibility): A noetherian ring is IsCoherentRing, and over it Module.IsCoherent coincides with Module.Finite. `coherent_test_nonCoherentRing` (non-example): For a field k, A = k[x, y_1, y_2, …]/(x y_1, x y_2, …) is not a coherent ring: the principal ideal (x) is finitely generated but not finitely presented, since its annihilator (y_1, y_2, …) is not finitely generated. `coherent_test_notJustFinitelyPresented` (non-example): In the ring A of coherent_test_nonCoherentRing, the module A itself is finitely presented (free of rank one) but not coherent, since its finitely generated submodule (x) is not finitely presented; so over non-noetherian rings 'coherent' cannot be replaced by 'finitely presented' (or by 'finitely generated'). `coherent_test_infinitePolynomial` (computation): The polynomial ring Z[x_1, x_2, …] in countably many variables is a coherent ring that is not noetherian. `coherent_test_zero` (degenerate): The zero module is coherent over every ring, and the zero ring is a coherent ring. *Source:* BL I §1, before Prop. 1.3, p. 294.

### Notions this roadmap states for its own use

**R2.88 The compactified moduli of generalised elliptic curves with level structure, and the formal group along the identity section** (definition). The Deligne–Rapoport moduli scheme of generalised elliptic curves with Γ(N)-structure (N ≥ 3) or Γ₁(N)-structure (N ≥ 5), proper and finitely presented over ℤ[1/N], with its universal generalised elliptic curve; the formal group of the smooth locus of the universal curve along its identity section, as a one-parameter formal group over the moduli scheme. API: the moduli scheme and its properness; the identity section; the formal group and its coordinate. Tests: over ℂ the N = 1 moduli is the j-line; the formal group of the Tate curve is 𝔾̂_m; the smooth locus of a generalised elliptic curve with n-gon fibre is 𝔾_m × ℤ/n over the cusp. *Source:* Deligne–Rapoport Ch. IV §3 (representability and properness; the result numbers inside §3 are not verified against the book, which is not freely available); Conrad-GEC Thm 3.2.7, Thm 3.3.1, Thm 4.2.1(2) (M_Γ proper, flat, finitely presented; a scheme over ℤ[1/d] for d ≥ 5 dividing N, resp. d ≥ 3 for Γ(N), where Conrad's Γ(N) model lives over ℤ[ζ_N][1/N]); Conrad-MR §4.1, Ex. 4.1.2 (the universal curve as used there).


<a id="r3"></a>

## R3. Coherent sheaves and finite traces

**R3. Coherent sheaves and finite traces.** Depends on R0–R2. The sheaf `M̃` of a finite module on a noetherian affinoid and its acyclicity (Huber's Theorem 2.5 in both cases); Čech acyclicity of the structure sheaf on every sheafy Tate affinoid (Kedlaya–Liu), via the reduction of rational coverings to simple Laurent coverings — the library has this reduction for the structure presheaf in degree 0 only, and the targets here are for an arbitrary presheaf in all degrees; glueing squares and Kiehl's theorem; the abelian category of coherent sheaves, coherent ideals and finite algebras; quasi-Stein spaces and Theorems A and B; vector bundles on sheafy Tate affinoids and uniform rings (the library's `Huber.IsUniform` is uniformity as boundedness of `A°`; the target here is its Banach-ring form through the square bound); the sheaf of continuous differentials; Kiehl's proper mapping theorem with its base-change statements; proper GAGA; traces of finite projective modules and of finite locally free morphisms, and pull–identify–trace along correspondences.


### The associated sheaf and acyclicity for finite modules

**R3.1 The sheaf M̃ = M ⊗ 𝒪_X associated with a finite module on a noetherian affinoid** (construction `AdicSpace.tilde`). Let (A, A⁺) is a Huber pair with A complete and Hausdorff, and A is of one of Huber's two noetherian types: (I) A has a noetherian ring of definition, or (II) A is a strongly noetherian Tate ring (TauCeti.Huber.IsStronglyNoetherian: every A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩ is noetherian), let X = Spa(A, A⁺) and let M be a finitely generated A-module. The associated presheaf M̃ (Huber's M ⊗ 𝒪_A) is the presheaf of topological 𝒪_X-modules on X defined on the rational basis by M̃(U) = M ⊗_A 𝒪_X(U) for U = R(T/s), where 𝒪_X(U) = A⟨T/s⟩ is the complete rational localisation of anchor Layer 3.1 and the ordinary (uncompleted) tensor product carries its natural 𝒪_X(U)-module topology (the quotient topology along any surjection 𝒪_X(U)ⁿ ↠ M ⊗_A 𝒪_X(U)); the restriction for rational V ⊆ U is id_M ⊗ ρ_{V,U}. On an arbitrary open W ⊆ X, M̃(W) = lim M̃(V) over the rational V ⊆ W, with the inverse-limit topology. An A-linear map φ : M → N induces φ̃ : M̃ → Ñ, functorially. Convention: the tensor product is not completed; for finite M over these rings M ⊗_A 𝒪_X(U) is already complete and equals the completed tensor product (Huber Lemmas 2.3(iii), 2.4(ii)), and the construction is only made for finitely generated M. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `tilde, tilde_obj_rationalSubset, tilde_map, tildeRestrictIso, tildeSelfIso, tildeTensorIso, tilde_exact, completeSpace_tilde_obj, tilde_obj_top`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.tilde_test_quotient` (computation): For A = K⟨T⟩, M = A/(T) and U = {|T| ≥ 1}, M̃(U) = 0 (T is a unit on U), while for U = {|T| ≤ |ϖ|}, M̃(U) ≅ K. `AdicSpace.tilde_test_zero_and_self` (degenerate): 0̃ = 0, and for M = A the presheaf Ã is isomorphic, as a presheaf of topological 𝒪_X-modules, to 𝒪_X. `AdicSpace.tilde_test_not_algebraic_localisation` (non-example): For A = ℚ_p⟨T⟩, M = A and U = {|T| ≥ 1}: the algebraic localisation M ⊗_A A[1/T] = ℚ_p⟨T⟩[1/T] is not M̃(U) = ℚ_p⟨T, T⁻¹⟩, since Σ_{n≥0} pⁿT⁻ⁿ lies in the latter and not in the former; the definition must use the completed rational localisation. `AdicSpace.tilde_test_completed_tensor` (compatibility): For finite M the canonical map M ⊗_A 𝒪_X(U) → M ⊗̂_A 𝒪_X(U) is a topological isomorphism; for the non-finite module M = ⊕_{n∈ℕ} A it is not surjective (Σ ϖⁿ e_n ⊗ 1 is a limit not in the algebraic tensor product), which is why the construction is restricted to finite M. *Source:* Huber94 §2, definition preceding Thm 2.5, p. 525. *Needs:* R0.38, R0.18, R0.10.

**R3.2 Tate acyclicity for finite modules over a strongly noetherian Tate affinoid** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) is a Huber pair with A a complete Hausdorff strongly noetherian Tate ring and A⁺ a ring of integral elements, X = Spa(A, A⁺) and M a finite A-module. Then: (i) for every rational subset U ⊆ X and every finite covering 𝔘 of U by rational subsets, the augmented alternating Čech complex 0 → M̃(U) → ∏ M̃(U_i) → ∏ M̃(U_i ∩ U_j) → ⋯ is exact and all its differentials are strict; (ii) M̃ is a sheaf of complete Hausdorff topological 𝒪_X-modules; (iii) Hⁱ(U, M̃) = 0 for i > 0 and H⁰(U, M̃) = M ⊗_A 𝒪_X(U) for every rational U. In particular M̃(X) = M. *Hypotheses:* (A, A⁺) is a Huber pair with A a complete Hausdorff strongly noetherian Tate ring and A⁺ a ring of integral elements. M finitely generated. Hⁱ is sheaf cohomology of the underlying abelian sheaf on the topological space U. *Source:* Huber94 §2, Thm 2.5, p. 525. *Needs:* R3.1, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

**R3.3 Acyclicity of M̃ when A has a noetherian ring of definition (Huber Theorem 2.5, Case I)** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) be a complete affinoid ring such that A has a noetherian ring of definition, X = Spa(A, A⁺) and M a finite A-module. Then M̃ is a sheaf of complete topological groups on X, and Hⁱ(U, M̃) = 0 for every i > 0 and every rational subset U ⊆ X. The Čech input — exact strict Čech complexes of M ⊗ 𝒪 on standard rational coverings of rational subsets, which refine every open covering, and the case M = A (sheafiness of 𝒪_X) — is R0.18 (Huber (1.5)); this target adds the sheaf M̃ and sheaf-cohomology vanishing. *Hypotheses:* A complete with a noetherian ring of definition. M finitely generated. U rational. *Source:* Huber94 §2, proof of Thm 2.5, Case I, pp. 525–529. *Needs:* R3.1, R0.18, R0.10, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

- **R3.4** Reduction of local transitive properties of rational coverings to simple Laurent coverings. *Source:* KL15 §2.4, Prop. 2.4.20.

- **R3.5** The two edges of a double complex with exact augmented rows and columns have the same cohomology. *Source:* Stacks Tag 00M1, Lem. 10.75.3.

- **R3.6** Čech acyclicity is inherited from a refinement whose restrictions are acyclic. *Source:* KL15 §2.4, proof of Prop. 2.4.21.

- **R3.7** Čech acyclicity of a presheaf on all rational coverings follows from simple Laurent coverings (Kedlaya–Liu Proposition 2.4.21). *Source:* KL15 §2.4, Prop. 2.4.21.

- **R3.8** For a simple Laurent covering the difference map B₁ ⊕ B₂ → B₁₂ is surjective (Kedlaya–Liu Lemma 2.4.22). *Source:* KL15 §2.4, Lem. 2.4.22.

**R3.9 Čech acyclicity of the structure sheaf on every sheafy Tate affinoid (Kedlaya–Liu Theorem 2.4.23)** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) be a complete Hausdorff Tate Huber pair which is sheafy (`Huber.IsSheafyForEveryPresentation A A⁺`, anchor Layer 3.4: the structure presheaf 𝒪_X of X = Spa(A, A⁺) is a sheaf). Then (1) for every rational subset U ⊆ X and every finite covering 𝔘 = (U_i) of U by rational subsets, the augmented alternating Čech complex 0 → 𝒪_X(U) → ∏_i 𝒪_X(U_i) → ∏_{i<j} 𝒪_X(U_i ∩ U_j) → ⋯ is exact in every degree; (2) Hⁱ(U, 𝒪_X) = 0 for every i > 0 and every rational subset U, where Hⁱ is sheaf cohomology of the underlying sheaf of abelian groups on the topological space U. In particular H⁰(X, 𝒪_X) = A and Hⁱ(X, 𝒪_X) = Ȟⁱ(X, 𝒪_X; 𝔘) = 0 for i > 0 and every finite rational covering 𝔘 of X. No noetherian or uniformity hypothesis is made: the anchor's Layer 4.1 gives (1) for strongly noetherian A, and this theorem extends it to every sheafy Tate pair (for instance perfectoid and sousperfectoid ones). Pair-level sheafiness is Tau Ceti's `Huber.IsSheafyForEveryPresentation`; the Čech framework is `quasiIso_cechAugmentation_iff`; the target is exactness in all positive degrees. *Hypotheses:* (A, A⁺) complete Hausdorff Tate Huber pair. (A, A⁺) sheafy: 𝒪_X is a sheaf on X = Spa(A, A⁺). *Source:* KL15 §2.4, Thm 2.4.23, p. 46. *Needs:* R3.8, R3.7, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

### Glueing squares and Kiehl's theorem

**R3.10 Glueing squares of complete Tate rings** (definition `Huber.GlueingSquare`). A glueing square is a commutative square of complete Hausdorff Tate rings with continuous homomorphisms R → R₁, R → R₂, R₁ → R₁₂, R₂ → R₁₂ such that (a) the sequence 0 → R → R₁ × R₂ → R₁₂ → 0, (r₁, r₂) ↦ r₁ − r₂, is exact and strict (R carries the subspace topology of R₁ × R₂ and R₁ × R₂ → R₁₂ is open onto R₁₂); (b) R₂ → R₁₂ has dense image; (c) every maximal ideal of R is the contraction of a prime ideal of R₁ × R₂. A finite (resp. finite projective) glueing datum on it is a triple of finite (resp. finite projective) modules M₁, M₂, M₁₂ over R₁, R₂, R₁₂ with isomorphisms ψ_i : M_i ⊗_{R_i} R₁₂ ≅ M₁₂; its module of sections is M = ker(ψ₁ − ψ₂ : M₁ × M₂ → M₁₂). Convention: condition (c) replaces Kedlaya–Liu's surjectivity of Gel'fand spectra M(R₁ ⊕ R₂) → M(R), which they use only through (c); every topological ring here is metrised by the norm |x| = inf{cⁿ : ϖ⁻ⁿx ∈ R₀} of a pseudouniformiser ϖ and ring of definition R₀ (KL Remark 2.4.4). *API* (on `Huber`): `GlueingSquare, GlueingSquare.exact_sub, GlueingSquare.isStrictMap_sub, GlueingSquare.denseRange, GlueingSquare.exists_prime_comap_eq, GlueingDatum, GlueingDatum.sections, GlueingDatum.ofModule, GlueingSquare.symm`. *Tests:* `Huber.GlueingSquare.test_laurent` (computation): For A = ℚ_p⟨T⟩ and f = T/p (the simple Laurent covering {|T| ≤ |p|}, {|T| ≥ |p|}): R = ℚ_p⟨T⟩, R₁ = ℚ_p⟨T/p⟩, R₂ = ℚ_p⟨T, p/T⟩, R₁₂ = ℚ_p⟨T/p, p/T⟩ is a glueing square: every Σ_{n∈ℤ} a_n (T/p)ⁿ in R₁₂ splits as (Σ_{n≥0} a_n (T/p)ⁿ) − (−Σ_{n<0} a_n (p/T)^{−n}), the kernel of the difference map is ℚ_p⟨T⟩ (Tate acyclicity), and the truncations Σ_{n≤N} a_n (T/p)ⁿ ∈ R₂ show that R₂ → R₁₂ has dense image. `Huber.GlueingSquare.test_trivial` (degenerate): R₁ = R, R₂ = R₁₂ = 0 is a glueing square (R₂ → R₁₂ trivially dense); every glueing datum is (M, 0, 0) with M = M₁. `Huber.GlueingSquare.test_not_exact` (non-example): R = ℚ_p → R₁ = R₂ = R₁₂ = ℚ_p⟨T⟩ with identity maps R_i → R₁₂ satisfies (b) (identity) and (c) (R is a field), but ker(R₁ × R₂ → R₁₂) is the diagonal ≅ ℚ_p⟨T⟩ ≠ ℚ_p, so (a) fails; a definition omitting exactness of 0 → R → R₁ × R₂ would accept it, and then the section module of the datum (R₁, R₂, R₁₂) would be ℚ_p⟨T⟩ rather than R. `Huber.GlueingSquare.test_compat_KL` (compatibility): For complete Tate rings, conditions (a)–(b) are exactly KL Definition 2.7.3(a)–(b) for the norms of KL Remark 2.4.4, and KL's (c) implies condition (c) by Corollary 2.3.5. `Huber.GlueingSquare.test_not_dense` (non-example): The two-disc covering of the projective line, ℚ_p → ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℚ_p⟨T⁻¹⟩ → ℚ_p⟨T, T⁻¹⟩, satisfies (a) (strict exact: Laurent series split isometrically, kernel = constants) and (c), but not (b): ℚ_p⟨T⁻¹⟩ is a closed proper subspace of ℚ_p⟨T, T⁻¹⟩. Without (b) the glueing lemmas fail: the datum (ℚ_p⟨T⟩, ℚ_p⟨T⁻¹⟩, ℚ_p⟨T, T⁻¹⟩) with ψ₂ = multiplication by T⁻¹ (the line bundle 𝒪(−1)) has section module 0, so M ⊗ R_i → M_i is not surjective. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Def. 2.7.3, pp. 56-57. *Needs:* R0.26.

- **R3.11** Factorisation of matrices near the identity along a strict surjection. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Lem. 2.7.2.

- **R3.12** Sections of a finite glueing datum generate each piece. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Lem. 2.7.4(a).

- **R3.13** The simple Laurent covering of a sheafy Tate affinoid is a glueing square. *Source:* KL15 §2.4, Lem. 2.4.22.

- **R3.14** Finite glueing data on a simple Laurent covering of a strongly noetherian Tate affinoid are effective. *Source:* Kiehl-AB §1, proof of Satz 1.4.

**R3.15 Coherent sheaves on locally noetherian adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsCoherent`). Let X be a locally noetherian adic space (R0.38) with structure sheaf 𝒪_X (anchor Layer 5). A sheaf F of 𝒪_X-modules on the topological space X is coherent if there is an open covering of X by affinoid open subspaces U_i ≅ Spa(A_i, A_i⁺) with A_i complete of noetherian type (noetherian ring of definition, or strongly noetherian Tate) and finitely generated A_i-modules M_i with F|_{U_i} ≅ M̃_i (R3.1). Coh(X) is the full subcategory of 𝒪_X-modules on coherent objects. On strongly noetherian Tate affinoid opens U the condition then holds with M = F(U) (R3.16); on affinoid opens with a noetherian ring of definition the definition is used only through such coverings. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsCoherent, Coh, IsCoherent.of_iso, isCoherent_tilde, IsCoherent.restrict, isCoherent_structureSheaf, IsCoherent.isFiniteType, isCoherent_iff_isFinitePresentation, IsCoherent.obj_affinoid, IsCoherent.stalk_finite`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.IsCoherent.test_ideal` (computation): On X = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩) the ideal sheaf generated by T is coherent, its global sections are T·K⟨T⟩ (free of rank one) and its fibre at the origin is one-dimensional. `AdicSpace.IsCoherent.test_point` (degenerate): On X = Spa(K, K°) an 𝒪_X-module is coherent iff it is (V)~ for a finite-dimensional K-vector space V; the zero sheaf and 𝒪_X are coherent on every locally noetherian X. `AdicSpace.IsCoherent.test_extension_by_zero` (non-example): For U = {|T| ≤ |ϖ|} in the closed disc, j_!𝒪_U is a sheaf of 𝒪_X-modules that is finitely generated on U and zero off U, but it is not coherent: on any rational neighbourhood V of a boundary point of U a coherent sheaf is (F(V))~ with F(V) = 0, while j_!𝒪_U|_{U∩V} ≠ 0. A definition by 'F(V) finite over 𝒪(V) for all rational V' would wrongly accept it. `AdicSpace.IsCoherent.test_finitePresentation` (compatibility): If X is covered by strongly noetherian Tate affinoids, F is coherent iff F satisfies Mathlib's SheafOfModules.IsFinitePresentation on Opens(X). *Source:* Kiehl-E §0, Def. 0.5, p. 195. *Needs:* R0.38, R3.1, R3.2, R3.3.

**R3.16 Kiehl's theorem: coherent sheaves on a strongly noetherian Tate affinoid are the M̃** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) is a Huber pair with A a complete Hausdorff strongly noetherian Tate ring and A⁺ a ring of integral elements and X = Spa(A, A⁺). (i) A sheaf of 𝒪_X-modules F is coherent (R3.15) iff F ≅ M̃ for a finite A-module M, and then M ≅ F(X). (ii) M ↦ M̃ is an exact equivalence of categories from finite A-modules to Coh(X), with quasi-inverse F ↦ F(X), compatible with ⊗ and internal Hom (a tensor equivalence). (iii) Equivalently, for every finite rational covering {U_i = Spa(B_i, B_i⁺)} of X, A → ∏ B_i is an effective descent morphism for finite modules: every system of finite B_i-modules with compatible isomorphisms on the U_i ∩ U_j (cocycle condition) comes from a finite A-module. In particular (A affinoid algebra over a complete nonarchimedean field K) this is Kiehl's theorem in Kedlaya–Liu's adic form (KL Theorem 2.5.20(b)). *Hypotheses:* (A, A⁺) is a Huber pair with A a complete Hausdorff strongly noetherian Tate ring and A⁺ a ring of integral elements. *Source:* KL15 §2.5, Thm 2.5.20(b), p. 52. *Needs:* R3.15, R3.1, R3.2, R3.14, R3.4.

### Operations, coherent ideals and finite algebras

- **R3.17** Small perturbations of surjections of complete modules are surjective. *Source:* Kiehl-E §1, Lem. 1.3.

**R3.18 The abelian tensor category of coherent sheaves and pullback** (construction `AdicSpace.Coh.instAbelian`). Let X is a locally noetherian analytic adic space in the sense of R0.38 whose points all have affinoid neighbourhoods Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (for instance X locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), K a complete nonarchimedean field). Coh(X) is an abelian full subcategory of 𝒪_X-modules closed under kernels, cokernels, images, extensions, ⊗_{𝒪_X} and the internal Hom sheaf ℋom_{𝒪_X}(F, G); the inclusion is exact. For a morphism f : Y → X of such spaces, the pullback f*F = 𝒪_Y ⊗_{f⁻¹𝒪_X} f⁻¹F defines a right exact monoidal functor f* : Coh(X) → Coh(Y) with (id)* ≅ id and (g ∘ f)* ≅ f* ∘ g*; affinoid-locally, for Spa(B, B⁺) → Spa(A, A⁺) induced by a continuous A → B, f*M̃ ≅ (M ⊗_A B)~. Base change of coherent sheaves along a fibre product square (R0.42) is pullback along the projection. *API* (on `AdicSpace.Coh`): `instAbelian, tensorObj, internalHom, instMonoidal, pullback, pullback_tilde, pullbackTensorIso, pullback_rightExact, baseChange, support_isClosed`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.Coh.test_cokernel_skyscraper` (computation): On the closed disc the cokernel of multiplication by T on 𝒪_X is (K⟨T⟩/(T))~, whose stalk is K at the origin and 0 elsewhere. `AdicSpace.Coh.test_point` (degenerate): For X = Spa(K, K°), Coh(X) is equivalent to finite-dimensional K-vector spaces, ⊗ is ⊗_K, and pullback along Spa(K') → Spa(K) is − ⊗_K K'. `AdicSpace.Coh.test_pullback_not_left_exact` (non-example): For i : Spa(K) → disc the origin, i* applied to the injection T : 𝒪 → 𝒪 is the zero map K → K; pullback is right exact but not exact, so it must not be declared exact. `AdicSpace.Coh.test_rigid` (compatibility): For a quasi-separated rigid space X over K, Coh(r(X)) is equivalent to Kiehl's coherent sheaves on X, compatibly with ⊗, ℋom and pullback (R1.31). *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, Def. 2.1.1, p. 270. *Needs:* R3.15, R3.16, R3.1, R0.42, R1.31.

**R3.19 Closed adic subspaces correspond to coherent ideals** (theorem). Let X is a locally noetherian analytic adic space in the sense of R0.38 whose points all have affinoid neighbourhoods Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (for instance X locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), K a complete nonarchimedean field). The assignment I ↦ V(I) = (Supp(𝒪_X/I), (𝒪_X/I)|_{Supp}) is a bijection from coherent ideal sheaves I ⊆ 𝒪_X to closed adic subspaces of X (R0.45), with inverse Z ↦ ker(𝒪_X → i_*𝒪_Z); on a strongly noetherian Tate affinoid Spa(A, A⁺) the coherent ideals are the J̃ for ideals J ⊆ A (automatically closed) and V(J̃) = Spa(A/J, (A/J)⁺) with (A/J)⁺ the integral closure of the image of A⁺. The ringed-space structure is retained: V(I) and V(I') can have the same support with I ≠ I'. *Hypotheses:* X is a locally noetherian analytic adic space in the sense of R0.38 whose points all have affinoid neighbourhoods Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (for instance X locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), K a complete nonarchimedean field). *Source:* Kiehl-E §0, p. 197. *Needs:* R3.16, R3.15, R3.2, R0.45, Tau Ceti `IsOpenQuotientMap.isStronglyNoetherian`.

**R3.20 Finite morphisms correspond to coherent 𝒪_X-algebras** (theorem). Let X is a locally noetherian analytic adic space in the sense of R0.38 whose points all have affinoid neighbourhoods Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (for instance X locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), K a complete nonarchimedean field). The functor (f : Y → X) ↦ f_*𝒪_Y is an anti-equivalence from the category of finite morphisms of adic spaces to X (R0.79) to the category of sheaves of 𝒪_X-algebras that are coherent as 𝒪_X-modules. Its quasi-inverse 𝒜 ↦ Spa_X(𝒜) is glued from Spa(𝒜(U), 𝒜(U)⁺) over affinoid opens U = Spa(A, A⁺), with 𝒜(U) a finite A-algebra carrying its module topology and 𝒜(U)⁺ the integral closure of the image of A⁺. Formation of Spa_X commutes with base change along morphisms X' → X. *Hypotheses:* X is a locally noetherian analytic adic space in the sense of R0.38 whose points all have affinoid neighbourhoods Spa(A, A⁺) with A a complete strongly noetherian Tate ring (for instance X locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), K a complete nonarchimedean field). *Source:* Conrad-RA Cor. 2.2.8, p. 12. *Needs:* R0.79, R0.80, R0.25, R3.16, R3.15, R0.87.

### Quasi-Stein spaces

**R3.21 Quasi-Stein adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsQuasiStein`). Let X an adic space locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°), K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value. X is quasi-Stein if there is a sequence U₁ ⊆ U₂ ⊆ ⋯ of affinoid open subspaces with X = ⋃ₙ Uₙ such that each restriction 𝒪_X(U_{n+1}) → 𝒪_X(Uₙ) has dense image. It is Stein if moreover, for every n, Uₙ = {x ∈ U_{n+1} : |f₁(x)| ≤ 1, …, |f_r(x)| ≤ 1} is the Weierstrass domain of U_{n+1} defined by some f₁, …, f_r ∈ 𝒪_X(U_{n+1}) and there is aₙ ∈ K with 0 < |aₙ| < 1 such that f₁/aₙ, …, f_r/aₙ topologically generate the affinoid K-algebra 𝒪_X(U_{n+1}) (Kiehl's condition after Definition 2.3). The data of the exhausting sequence is not part of the predicate; theorems quantify over any such sequence. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsQuasiStein, IsQuasiStein.exhaustion, IsQuasiStein.of_isAffinoid, isQuasiStein_openPolydisc, isQuasiStein_analyticAffineSpace, IsQuasiStein.of_isClosedImmersion, IsQuasiStein.prod, IsQuasiStein.isStein_iff`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.IsQuasiStein.test_openDisc` (computation): For D = {|T| < 1} and Uₙ = {|T|ⁿ ≤ |ϖ|}, the restriction K⟨T, T^{n+1}/ϖ⟩ → K⟨T, Tⁿ/ϖ⟩ has dense image because K[T] is dense in the target. `AdicSpace.IsQuasiStein.test_affinoid` (degenerate): Every affinoid Spa(A, A°) with A an affinoid K-algebra, and Spa(K, K°) itself, is quasi-Stein with Uₙ = X. `AdicSpace.IsQuasiStein.test_projectiveLine` (non-example): The analytic projective line P^{1,an}_K is not quasi-Stein: being quasi-compact it would equal some Uₙ and hence be affinoid, but H¹(P^{1,an}, 𝒪(−2)) ≅ K ≠ 0 (R3.55) contradicts Tate acyclicity on affinoids. A definition allowing arbitrary quasi-compact opens Uₙ (not affinoid) would accept it with the constant sequence Uₙ = P^{1,an}_K. `AdicSpace.IsQuasiStein.test_affineLine` (compatibility): The analytification A^{1,ad}_K (R1.17) is quasi-Stein with Uₙ = {|T| ≤ |ϖ|⁻ⁿ}; its global functions are the entire series, and K[T] = 𝒪(A¹_K) is dense in each 𝒪(Uₙ). `AdicSpace.IsQuasiStein.test_exhaustion_density` (non-example): The density condition is a genuine condition on an exhaustion: in the closed disc, U₁ = {|T| = 1} ⊆ U₂ = U₃ = ⋯ = {|T| ≤ 1} is an increasing affinoid exhaustion, but K⟨T⟩ → K⟨T, T⁻¹⟩ has closed image ≠ K⟨T, T⁻¹⟩, so it is not an admissible `exhaustion` datum (the disc is nevertheless quasi-Stein via the constant sequence). *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, Def. 2.3, p. 270. *Needs:* R1.31, R1.17.

- **R3.22** Dense restriction of coherent sections on a quasi-Stein space. *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, Hilfssatz 2.5 and its consequence.

**R3.23 Kiehl's Theorem B on quasi-Stein spaces** (theorem). Let X be a quasi-Stein adic space lft over K and F a coherent 𝒪_X-module. Then Hⁱ(X, F) = 0 for all i > 0. *Hypotheses:* X quasi-Stein. F coherent. Hⁱ sheaf cohomology on the topological space X. *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, Satz 2.4, p. 271. *Needs:* R3.21, R3.22, R3.2, R3.16, R3.15, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

- **R3.24** Kiehl's Theorem A on quasi-Stein spaces. *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, Satz 2.4.3.

- **R3.25** Coherent cohomology of the open polydisc vanishes. *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, after Def. 2.3.

- **R3.26** Acyclicity of direct images along quasi-Stein inclusions. *Source:* Kiehl-AB §2, proof of Satz 2.4.

### Vector bundles on sheafy Tate affinoids and uniform rings

**R3.27 Vector bundles (locally free sheaves of finite rank) on adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.VectorBundle`). Let X be an adic space (anchor Layer 5; 𝒪_X is a sheaf). A vector bundle on X is a sheaf of 𝒪_X-modules E such that X has an open covering {U_i} with E|_{U_i} ≅ 𝒪_{U_i}^{n_i} for some n_i ∈ ℕ; equivalently E satisfies Mathlib's SheafOfModules.IsLocallyFree with local bases of finite type. The rank is the locally constant function x ↦ n_i on U_i. Vector bundles form the full subcategory VectorBundle(X) of 𝒪_X-modules. No noetherian hypothesis is imposed; on locally noetherian X every vector bundle is coherent, but not conversely. The predicate is Mathlib's `SheafOfModules.IsLocallyFree` with finite rank on the structure sheaf of the adic space, not a new one. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `VectorBundle, VectorBundle.rank, VectorBundle.tensor, VectorBundle.pullback, VectorBundle.isCoherent, VectorBundle.iff_isLocallyFree, VectorBundle.dualDualIso, VectorBundle.stalk_free, VectorBundle.ofFiniteProjective`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.VectorBundle.test_disconnected` (computation): For A = A₁ × A₂ (both nonzero complete Tate) and P = A₁ × 0, P̃ is a vector bundle on Spa(A, A⁺) whose rank is 1 on Spa(A₁) and 0 on Spa(A₂); P is finite projective but not free. `AdicSpace.VectorBundle.test_rank_zero` (degenerate): A vector bundle of rank 0 everywhere is the zero sheaf; 𝒪_Xⁿ is a vector bundle of constant rank n. `AdicSpace.VectorBundle.test_skyscraper` (non-example): On the closed disc, (K⟨T⟩/(T))~ is coherent but not a vector bundle: its fibre dimension is 1 at the origin and 0 elsewhere, which no locally constant rank allows. `AdicSpace.VectorBundle.test_mathlib` (compatibility): E is a vector bundle iff E satisfies SheafOfModules.IsLocallyFree and SheafOfModules.IsFiniteType on Opens(X); then E is of finite presentation (Tau Ceti IsLocallyFreeData.isFinitePresentation). *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Def. 2.7.6, p. 58. *Needs:* Tau Ceti `SheafOfModules.LocalGeneratorsData.IsLocallyFreeData.isFinitePresentation`.

- **R3.28** Finite projective glueing data on a glueing square are effective. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Prop. 2.7.5.

**R3.29 Kiehl glueing of finite projective modules over sheafy Tate affinoids** (theorem). Let (A, A⁺) be a complete Tate Huber pair which is sheafy (its structure presheaf is a sheaf on every rational subset). For every rational subset U = Spa(B, B⁺) of X = Spa(A, A⁺), the functor P ↦ P̃ (P̃(V) = P ⊗_B 𝒪_X(V) on rational V ⊆ U) is an equivalence from finite projective B-modules to vector bundles on U (R3.27), with quasi-inverse E ↦ E(U); it is compatible with ⊗, duals and pullback along rational localisations. The Tate sheaf property of 𝒪_X (Čech acyclicity on all rational coverings of rational subsets, KL Theorem 2.4.23) is R3.9. No noetherian hypothesis is made; since rational localisation need not be flat (e.g. for perfectoid A), only finite projective modules are glued. *Hypotheses:* (A, A⁺) complete Tate and sheafy. modules finite projective. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Thm 2.7.7, p. 58. *Needs:* R3.27, R3.30, R3.13, R3.28, R3.4, R3.9.

- **R3.30** Acyclicity of P̃ for finite projective P over a sheafy Tate affinoid. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, proof of Thm 2.7.7.

- **R3.31** Vector bundles on sheafy Tate affinoids are generated by global sections and acyclic. *Source:* KL15 §2.7, Def. 2.7.6.

- **R3.32** Descent of finite projective modules along finite étale covers of affinoids. *Source:* Scholze13 §7, proof of Lem. 7.3.

- **R3.33** Uniformity of a Banach ring via the square bound, and its relation to boundedness of A°. *Source:* KL15 §2.8, Def. 2.8.1(c).

- **R3.34** A split injection into a stably uniform ring gives stable uniformity (Kedlaya–Liu Remark 2.8.12). *Source:* KL15 §2.8, Rem. 2.8.12.

- **R3.35** Spectral norms of uniform rings and isometric maps. *Source:* KL15 §2.8, Rem. 2.8.3(a).

### The sheaf of continuous differentials

**R3.36 The coherent sheaf of continuous differentials Ω¹_{X/Y} and its exterior powers** (construction `AdicSpace.relativeDifferentials`). Let f: X → Y be a morphism locally of finite type (anchor Layer 5.2) between locally noetherian analytic adic spaces (R0.38). There is a coherent O_X-module Ω¹_{X/Y} (R3.15) with an f⁻¹O_Y-linear derivation d = d_{X/Y}: O_X → Ω¹_{X/Y}, characterised as follows. For every pair of affinoid opens U = Spa(B, B⁺) ⊆ X and V = Spa(A, A⁺) ⊆ Y with f(U) ⊆ V and A → B topologically of finite type (such U cover X), Ω¹_{X/Y}|_U ≅ (Ωᶜ_{B/A})~ (R3.1), with d = d_{B/A} on sections over U (R0.93). The identifications on a rational U' = Spa(B⟨T/s⟩) ⊆ U are the localisation isomorphisms Ωᶜ_{B⟨T/s⟩/A} ≅ B⟨T/s⟩ ⊗_B Ωᶜ_{B/A}. Shrinking V to a rational V' ⊇ f(U), A' = A⟨T'/s'⟩, does not change Ωᶜ_{B/A} = Ωᶜ_{B/A'}. Properties: (a) universal property: for every coherent O_X-module F, Hom_{O_X}(Ω¹_{X/Y}, F) is the set of f⁻¹O_Y-linear derivations D: O_X → F that are continuous on sections over affinoid opens, via u ↦ u ∘ d; (b) exact sequences: for X → Y → Z (both locally of finite type) the sequence f*Ω¹_{Y/Z} → Ω¹_{X/Z} → Ω¹_{X/Y} → 0 is exact, and for a closed embedding i: X → P over Y with coherent ideal 𝓘 (R3.19), 𝓘/𝓘² → i*Ω¹_{P/Y} → Ω¹_{X/Y} → 0 is exact; (c) f is unramified iff Ω¹_{X/Y} = 0; if f is smooth of relative dimension n at x, Ω¹_{X/Y} is a vector bundle of rank n near x (R3.27), and for X → Y → Z with f smooth the sequence of (b) is left exact and locally split; if f is étale, f*Ω¹_{Y/Z} ≅ Ω¹_{X/Z}, in particular u*Ω¹_{X/Y} ≅ Ω¹_{U/Y} for every étale u: U → X, which makes U ↦ Ω¹_{U/Y}(U) a sheaf on the small étale site of X; (d) base change: for a cartesian square X' = X ×_Y Y' (R0.42) with projection p: X' → X, p*Ω¹_{X/Y} ≅ Ω¹_{X'/Y'} compatibly with d; (e) analytification: for a morphism h of schemes locally of finite type over a complete nonarchimedean field K, Ω¹_{X^ad/Y^ad} ≅ (Ω¹_{X/Y})^ad compatibly with d (R1.24); (f) exterior powers: Ωⁱ_{X/Y} := ⋀ⁱ_{O_X} Ω¹_{X/Y}, the sheaf associated with U ↦ ⋀ⁱ_{O(U)} Ω¹_{X/Y}(U), is coherent, with sections (⋀ⁱ_B Ωᶜ_{B/A})~ over U as above; for f smooth of relative dimension n it is a vector bundle of rank C(n, i); d extends uniquely to f⁻¹O_Y-linear maps d: Ωⁱ_{X/Y} → Ωⁱ⁺¹_{X/Y} with d ∘ d = 0 and d(ω ∧ η) = dω ∧ η + (−1)ⁱ ω ∧ dη, the relative de Rham complex Ω•_{X/Y}. Conventions: this is the coherent sheaf of the continuous differentials of R0 (Huber 1996 (1.6.2)); Mathlib's algebraic KaehlerDifferential of the rings O_X(U) is not the object (it is not finitely generated). The Kähler differentials of rigid and dagger spaces (Grosse-Klönne) and the log differentials of Diao–Lan–Liu–Zhu with trivial log structures are the same sheaf. The sheaf on the étale site in (c) and the pro-étale sheaves built from it are AdicEtaleGeometry A2's and PadicHodgeTheory's. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `relativeDifferentials, relativeDifferentials.d, relativeDifferentials.isCoherent, relativeDifferentials.affinoidIso, relativeDifferentials.homEquiv, relativeDifferentials.map, relativeDifferentials.exact_firstFundamental, relativeDifferentials.exact_conormal, relativeDifferentials.eq_zero_iff_unramified, relativeDifferentials.isVectorBundle_of_smooth, relativeDifferentials.pullbackEtaleIso, relativeDifferentials.baseChangeIso, relativeDifferentials.analytificationIso, relativeDifferentials.exteriorPower, relativeDifferentials.deRhamComplex`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.relativeDifferentials.test_disc` (computation): Notation: K complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial rank-one valuation, K° its valuation ring, D = Spa(K⟨T⟩, K°⟨T⟩). Ω¹_{D/Spa K} ≅ O_D, generated by dT, with d(Σ aₙTⁿ) = (Σ n aₙTⁿ⁻¹) dT, and Ω²_{D/Spa K} = 0. `AdicSpace.relativeDifferentials.test_openEmbedding` (degenerate): For an open embedding j: U → X, Ω¹_{U/X} = 0 and j*Ω¹_{X/Y} ≅ Ω¹_{U/Y}; Ω¹_{Y/Y} = 0 for every Y. `AdicSpace.relativeDifferentials.test_notAlgebraic` (non-example): The presheaf U ↦ Ω[O_D(U)⁄K] of Mathlib's algebraic Kähler differentials on the disc D is not a coherent O_D-module (its value on D is not a finitely generated K⟨T⟩-module), whereas Ω¹_{D/K}(D) = K⟨T⟩ dT: a definition through algebraic Kähler differentials fails. `AdicSpace.relativeDifferentials.test_analytification` (compatibility): For X = A¹_K, (Ω¹_{A¹/K})^ad ≅ Ω¹_{A^{1,ad}/K} = O dT; for P¹_K, Ω¹_{P^{1,ad}/K} ≅ O(−2)^ad (R1.24). `AdicSpace.relativeDifferentials.test_derivations` (characterisation): On D, Hom_{O_D}(Ω¹_{D/K}, O_D) is the O_D(D)-module of continuous K-derivations of K⟨T⟩, free on d/dT; the node T₁T₂ = 0 has a nonzero torsion section T₁ dT₂ of Ω¹ whose support is the origin. *Source:* DLLZ-A §3.3, Construction 3.3.2, p. 34. *Needs:* R3.15, R3.1, R3.2, R3.16, R3.18, R3.19, R3.27, R3.32, R0.38, R0.93, R0.94, R0.95, R0.108, R0.109, R0.117, R0.111, R0.99, R0.100, R0.119, R0.42, R0.41, R0.48, R1.24, R1.23.

**R3.37 Kiehl's proper mapping theorem** (theorem). Let K be a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and f : X → Y a proper morphism (R0.63) of adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°). For every coherent 𝒪_X-module F and every i ≥ 0, Rⁱf_*F is a coherent 𝒪_Y-module; for every affinoid open V ⊆ Y, (Rⁱf_*F)(V) = Hⁱ(f⁻¹(V), F), a finite 𝒪_Y(V)-module, and (Rⁱf_*F)|_V ≅ (Hⁱ(f⁻¹V, F))~. Huber's adic form over locally noetherian bases with a noetherian ring of definition (Huber 1996, Remark 1.3.17, citing [Hu4, 3.12.13]) is not covered by this target. *Hypotheses:* K complete nonarchimedean, nontrivially valued. f proper, X and Y lft over Spa(K, K°). F coherent. *Source:* Kiehl-E §3, Thm 3.3, p. 206. *Needs:* R3.44, R3.46, R3.2, R3.15, R0.63, R0.44, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

**R3.38 Completely continuous maps of Banach modules over an affinoid algebra** (definition `Huber.IsCompletelyContinuous`). Let K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value; adic spaces are locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°); A denotes an affinoid K-algebra (a quotient of some K⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩, strongly noetherian Tate) with A⁺ = A°, and fix a ring of definition A₀ ⊆ A containing the image of K°. A Banach A-module is a complete topological A-module M whose topology is defined by an open A₀-submodule M₀ (so the ϖⁿM₀ form a basis of neighbourhoods of 0), with the norm |m| = inf{|a| : a ∈ K, m ∈ aM₀}. A continuous A-linear map f : M → N of Banach A-modules is completely continuous if it is the limit, in the operator norm, of continuous A-linear maps f_i : M → N with finitely generated image. It is strictly completely continuous if there are such f_i and a ∈ K° ∖ {0} such that for every i, a·f_i(M₀) lies in some finitely generated A₀-submodule of N₀, which may depend on i (Kiehl's 'streng vollständig stetig': 'in einem — von i abhängigen — endlichen B₀-Untermodul von N₀'); the scalar a, and hence the bound a·f_i(M₀) ⊆ N₀, is uniform in i. *API* (on `Huber`): `IsCompletelyContinuous, IsStrictlyCompletelyContinuous, IsStrictlyCompletelyContinuous.isCompletelyContinuous, IsCompletelyContinuous.of_finiteRank, IsCompletelyContinuous.comp_left, IsCompletelyContinuous.add, isClosed_setOf_isCompletelyContinuous, isStrictlyCompletelyContinuous_tsum`. *Tests:* `Huber.IsCompletelyContinuous.test_restriction` (computation): Over A = K: the map K⟨T⟩ → K⟨T/ϖ⟩, Σ aₙTⁿ ↦ Σ (aₙϖⁿ)(T/ϖ)ⁿ, is the limit of its truncations below degree N, at operator-norm distance ≤ |ϖ|^N, and each truncation maps K°⟨T⟩ into the finitely generated K°-module ⊕_{n<N} K°(T/ϖ)ⁿ ⊆ K°⟨T/ϖ⟩ (a module that grows with N); hence it is strictly completely continuous with a = 1. Under the misreading 'one finitely generated submodule for all i' it would not be, since its image is not contained in a finitely generated K-subspace. `Huber.IsCompletelyContinuous.test_finite` (degenerate): The zero map and every continuous A-linear map with finitely generated image are completely continuous; if N is finitely generated every continuous map M → N is. `Huber.IsCompletelyContinuous.test_identity` (non-example): Over A = K, the identity of K⟨T⟩ is not completely continuous: a finite-rank f with |id − f| < 1 would be invertible (Neumann series), forcing K⟨T⟩ to be finite-dimensional. (Over A = K⟨T⟩ the same identity has finitely generated image A and is completely continuous, so the base A must be recorded.) `Huber.IsCompletelyContinuous.test_compactOperator` (compatibility): For K a finite extension of ℚ_p (locally compact) and A = K, every completely continuous map of K-Banach spaces is a compact operator in Mathlib's sense (IsCompactOperator); for K = ℂ_p the identity of K is completely continuous (finite rank) but not IsCompactOperator, so the two notions must not be identified in general. *Source:* Kiehl-E §1, Def. 1.1, p. 198.

- **R3.39** The Schwartz finiteness theorem over affinoid algebras. *Source:* Kiehl-E §1, Satz 1.2.

- **R3.40** Strict complete continuity descends to a closed submodule for topologically free sources. *Source:* Kiehl-E §1, Satz 1.4.

**R3.41 Relatively compact affinoid subdomains over an affinoid base (Kiehl)** (definition `AdicSpace.RelativelyCompact`). Let K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value; adic spaces are locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°); A denotes an affinoid K-algebra (a quotient of some K⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩, strongly noetherian Tate) with A⁺ = A°. Let V = Spa(B, B°) be an affinoid adic space over Spa(A, A°) with B topologically of finite type over A, and U ⊆ V an affinoid open subspace. U is relatively compact in V over A, written U ⋐_A V, if there are a closed immersion V ↪ Dⁿ_A = Spa(A⟨X₁,…,Xₙ⟩) over A and ε ∈ √|K^×| with ε < 1 such that U ⊆ {|X_i| ≤ ε for all i}. (Kiehl's 'U liegt im Innern von V bezüglich A'.) *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `RelativelyCompact, RelativelyCompact.mono, RelativelyCompact.inter, RelativelyCompact.prod, relativelyCompact_self_of_finite, RelativelyCompact.baseChange, RelativelyCompact.of_isRational, RelativelyCompact.strictlyCompletelyContinuous`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.RelativelyCompact.test_disc` (computation): For V = Spa(K⟨X⟩) and U = {|X| ≤ |ϖ|}, U ⋐_K V via the identity embedding and ε = |ϖ|. `AdicSpace.RelativelyCompact.test_finite` (degenerate): If B is a finite A-algebra (in particular V = Spa(A)) then V ⋐_A V: for integral generators x₁,…,xₙ of B over A choose a ∈ K° ∖ {0} with |a xᵢ| < 1 on V; then Xᵢ ↦ a xᵢ gives A⟨X⟩ ↠ B with V ⊆ {|Xᵢ| ≤ ε} for some ε < 1 (Kiehl, after Definition 2.3). `AdicSpace.RelativelyCompact.test_unit_disc_not_self` (non-example): The closed unit disc is not ⋐_K itself: otherwise the identity would be Kiehl-proper and K⟨X⟩ = H⁰ would be finite over K by R3.37. `AdicSpace.RelativelyCompact.test_rigid` (compatibility): For rigid V = Sp(B) over Sp(A), Kiehl's U ⋐_A V holds iff r(U) ⋐_A r(V). *Source:* Kiehl-E §2, Def. 2.1, p. 202. *Needs:* R1.31.

- **R3.42** Proper morphisms to an affinoid admit relatively compact affinoid covers. *Source:* Kiehl-E §2, Def. 2.3.

- **R3.43** Restriction along a relatively compact inclusion is strictly completely continuous. *Source:* Kiehl-E §2, before Satz 2.5.

- **R3.44** Finiteness of coherent cohomology over an affinoid base (Kiehl Satz 2.6). *Source:* Kiehl-E §2, Satz 2.6.

- **R3.45** Completion of proper coherent cohomology (Kiehl Theorems 3.4 and 3.7). *Source:* Kiehl-E §3, Thm 3.4.

- **R3.46** Proper coherent cohomology commutes with affinoid subdomains (Kiehl Satz 3.5). *Source:* Kiehl-E §3, Satz 3.5.

**R3.37 Kiehl's proper mapping theorem** (theorem). Let K be a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and f : X → Y a proper morphism (R0.63) of adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, K°). For every coherent 𝒪_X-module F and every i ≥ 0, Rⁱf_*F is a coherent 𝒪_Y-module; for every affinoid open V ⊆ Y, (Rⁱf_*F)(V) = Hⁱ(f⁻¹(V), F), a finite 𝒪_Y(V)-module, and (Rⁱf_*F)|_V ≅ (Hⁱ(f⁻¹V, F))~. Huber's adic form over locally noetherian bases with a noetherian ring of definition (Huber 1996, Remark 1.3.17, citing [Hu4, 3.12.13]) is not covered by this target. *Hypotheses:* K complete nonarchimedean, nontrivially valued. f proper, X and Y lft over Spa(K, K°). F coherent. *Source:* Kiehl-E §3, Thm 3.3, p. 206. *Needs:* R3.44, R3.46, R3.2, R3.15, R0.63, R0.44, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

- **R3.47** Closed subspaces of Banach spaces of countable type have continuous linear sections. *Source:* Conrad-IC proof of Lem. 1.1.5.

- **R3.48** Completed extension of the base field for affinoid algebras is faithfully flat. *Source:* Conrad-IC Lem. 1.1.5.

- **R3.49** Proper coherent cohomology commutes with completed extension of the base field. *Source:* Conrad-RA Appendix A.1, Thm A.1.2.

- **R3.50** Proper coherent cohomology commutes with finite flat base change of affinoids. *Source:* Scholze13 §9, proof of Prop. 9.2.

**R3.51 Base change of proper coherent cohomology along étale maps of affinoids (Scholze Proposition 9.2(ii))** (theorem). Let g : T → S be an étale morphism (R0.100) of affinoid adic spaces lft over Spa(K, K°), f : X → S proper, F_X coherent on X, Y = X ×_S T and F_Y the pullback. Then for all i the natural map Hⁱ(X, F_X) ⊗_{𝒪_S(S)} 𝒪_T(T) → Hⁱ(Y, F_Y) is an isomorphism; equivalently g*(Rⁱf_*F_X) ≅ Rⁱf_{T*}F_Y. *Hypotheses:* g étale, S and T affinoid. f proper. F_X coherent. *Source:* Scholze13 §9, Prop. 9.2(ii), p. 54. *Needs:* R3.37, R3.46, R3.50, R0.121, R0.100, R0.44, R0.118.

### Proper GAGA

- **R3.52** Analytification carries coherent sheaves to coherent sheaves. *Source:* Conrad-RA Ex. 2.2.11.

**R3.53 The GAGA comparison maps on cohomology and higher direct images** (construction `AdicSpace.cohomologyComparison`). Let K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and A an affinoid K-algebra (A = K allowed); X is a scheme locally of finite type over Spec A, X^an = X ×_{Spec A} Spa(A, A°) its relative analytification (R1.13; for A = K R1.15) with the morphism of locally ringed spaces ι_X : X^an → X and F an 𝒪_X-module with analytification F^an (R1.23). The comparison map c_F : Hⁱ(X, F) → Hⁱ(X^an, F^an) is the composite of Hⁱ(X, F) → Hⁱ(X, ι_*ι*F) (the unit F → ι_*ι*F) with the edge map Hⁱ(X, ι_*F^an) → Hⁱ(X^an, F^an); it is A-linear and δ-functorial in F. For f : X → Y over Spec A, the relative comparison map (Rⁱf_*F)^an → Rⁱf^an_*(F^an) is the adjoint of the sheafified map V ↦ [Hⁱ(f⁻¹V, F) → Hⁱ((f^an)⁻¹V^an, F^an)], δ-functorial in F and compatible with composition in f. These maps are isomorphisms for proper f and coherent F (R3.55) and not in general. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `cohomologyComparison, cohomologyComparison_naturality, cohomologyComparison_δ, cohomologyComparison_zero, directImageComparison, directImageComparison_comp, directImageComparison_global, cohomologyComparison_baseChange`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.cohomologyComparison_test_affineLine` (non-example): For X = A¹_K and F = 𝒪 the degree-0 comparison K[T] → 𝒪(A^{1,ad}_K) is not surjective (the series Σ ϖ^{n²}Tⁿ is entire), so the comparison maps are not isomorphisms without properness. `AdicSpace.cohomologyComparison_test_point` (degenerate): For X = Spec K the comparison maps are the identity of H⁰ = F(X) and 0 in positive degrees. `AdicSpace.cohomologyComparison_test_skyscraper` (computation): For X = A¹_K and F = 𝒪_X/(T), the degree-0 comparison K → K is an isomorphism. `AdicSpace.cohomologyComparison_test_rigid` (compatibility): Under the rigid–adic comparison (R1.31) c_F agrees with Köpf's and Conrad's rigid-analytic comparison maps. *Source:* Conrad-RA Appendix A.1, p. 38. *Needs:* R1.23, R1.20, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

- **R3.54** Coherent cohomology of O(m) on analytic projective space. *Source:* Conrad-RA Appendix A.1.

**R3.55 Proper GAGA: comparison of coherent cohomology (Köpf)** (theorem). Let K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and A an affinoid K-algebra (A = K allowed); X is a scheme locally of finite type over Spec A, X^an = X ×_{Spec A} Spa(A, A°) its relative analytification (R1.13; for A = K R1.15) with the morphism of locally ringed spaces ι_X : X^an → X, f : X → Y a proper morphism of schemes locally of finite type over Spec A and F a coherent 𝒪_X-module. Then f^an : X^an → Y^an is proper and the δ-functorial comparison maps (Rⁱf_*F)^an → Rⁱf^an_*(F^an) are isomorphisms of coherent 𝒪_{Y^an}-modules for all i. In particular, for X proper over Spec A, Hⁱ(X, F) ≅ Hⁱ(X^an, F^an) as finite A-modules. *Hypotheses:* K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and A an affinoid K-algebra (A = K allowed); X is a scheme locally of finite type over Spec A, X^an = X ×_{Spec A} Spa(A, A°) its relative analytification (R1.13; for A = K R1.15) with the morphism of locally ringed spaces ι_X : X^an → X. f proper. F coherent. *Source:* Conrad-RA Appendix A.1, p. 38. *Needs:* R3.53, R3.52, R3.54, R3.37, R3.45, R1.28, R1.8, R1.23, F0.39, F0.65.

**R3.56 Proper GAGA: analytification is an equivalence on coherent sheaves** (theorem). Let K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and A an affinoid K-algebra (A = K allowed); X is a scheme locally of finite type over Spec A, X^an = X ×_{Spec A} Spa(A, A°) its relative analytification (R1.13; for A = K R1.15) with the morphism of locally ringed spaces ι_X : X^an → X with X proper over Spec A. Then F ↦ F^an is an exact equivalence of abelian tensor categories Coh(X) → Coh(X^an). In particular it is fully faithful on proper A-schemes' coherent sheaves, line bundles, vector bundles and coherent ideals correspond, and closed analytic subspaces of X^an are analytifications of closed subschemes of X. *Hypotheses:* K a complete nonarchimedean field with nontrivial absolute value and A an affinoid K-algebra (A = K allowed); X is a scheme locally of finite type over Spec A, X^an = X ×_{Spec A} Spa(A, A°) its relative analytification (R1.13; for A = K R1.15) with the morphism of locally ringed spaces ι_X : X^an → X. X proper over Spec A. *Source:* Scholze13 §9, Thm 9.1(i), p. 53. *Needs:* R3.55, R3.52, R3.53, R3.54, R3.18, R3.19, R1.23, R1.22, F0.65.

- **R3.57** Finite analytic covers of proper varieties are algebraic. *Source:* Conrad-RA proof of Thm 3.1.5.

### Finite locally free traces and pull–identify–trace

**R3.58 Trace of endomorphisms of finite projective modules** (construction `LinearMap.projectiveTrace`). Let R be a commutative ring and M a finitely generated projective R-module. The trace tr_M : End_R(M) → R is the R-linear map tr_M = contractLeft ∘ (dualTensorHomEquiv R M M)⁻¹, where dualTensorHomEquiv : M^∨ ⊗_R M ≅ End_R(M) (Mathlib, for M finite projective) and contractLeft(φ ⊗ m) = φ(m). Convention: this is the trace used throughout R3; Mathlib's LinearMap.trace R M agrees with it when M is free but is defined to be 0 when M has no finite basis, so it is not the trace of a finite projective non-free module. *API* (on `LinearMap`): `projectiveTrace, projectiveTrace_dualTensorHom, projectiveTrace_eq_trace, projectiveTrace_comp_comm, projectiveTrace_baseChange, projectiveTrace_prodMap, projectiveTrace_conj, projectiveTrace_one_localization, projectiveTrace_isNilpotent`. *Tests:* `LinearMap.projectiveTrace_test_nonfree` (non-example): For R = ℚ × ℚ and M = ℚ × 0, projectiveTrace R M id = (1, 0), while Mathlib's LinearMap.trace R M id = 0 because M has no finite basis; the Mathlib trace is not the trace of a finite projective module. `LinearMap.projectiveTrace_test_matrix` (computation): For M = R² and f with matrix [[a, b], [c, d]], projectiveTrace R M f = a + d. `LinearMap.projectiveTrace_test_zero_self` (degenerate): For M = 0 the trace is 0; for M = R, projectiveTrace R R f = f 1. `LinearMap.projectiveTrace_test_free` (compatibility): If M is free and finite, projectiveTrace R M = LinearMap.trace R M. *Source:* Stacks Tag 02DU, Exercise 111.22.6.

**R3.59 The trace of a finite locally free algebra** (construction `Algebra.projectiveTrace`). Let R be a commutative ring and B a commutative R-algebra which is finitely generated and projective as an R-module (a finite locally free R-algebra). The trace Tr_{B/R} : B → R is b ↦ tr_B(m_b), the projective-module trace (R3.58) of multiplication by b. It is R-linear, Tr_{B/R}(1) is the locally constant degree function (its image in R_𝔭 is rank_𝔭 B), and the trace form (x, y) ↦ Tr_{B/R}(xy) is symmetric. It is not defined as a sum over geometric points: for B = k[x]/(x²), Tr(1) = 2 although Spec B has one geometric point. *API* (on `Algebra`): `projectiveTrace, projectiveTrace_apply, projectiveTrace_eq_trace, projectiveTrace_algebraMap, projectiveTrace_baseChange, projectiveTrace_trans, projectiveTrace_prod, projectiveTraceForm, projectiveTrace_pi, projectiveTrace_localization`. *Tests:* `Algebra.projectiveTrace_test_ramified` (non-example): For B = k[x]/(x²) over a field k, projectiveTrace k B 1 = 2 while Spec B has a single geometric point; a definition as a sum over geometric points gives 1. `Algebra.projectiveTrace_test_nonconstant_rank` (non-example): For R = ℚ × ℚ and B = ℚ × (ℚ × ℚ) with R → B, (a, b) ↦ (a, (b, b)), B is projective of rank (1, 2) and projectiveTrace R B 1 = (1, 2), whereas Mathlib's Algebra.trace R B 1 = 0. `Algebra.projectiveTrace_test_quadratic` (computation): For B = R[x]/(x² − d) (free on 1, x), Tr(a + bx) = 2a and Tr(x²) = 2d. `Algebra.projectiveTrace_test_self` (degenerate): projectiveTrace R R = id and projectiveTrace R 0 = 0. `Algebra.projectiveTrace_test_free` (compatibility): For B free and finite over R, projectiveTrace R B = Algebra.trace R B. *Source:* Stacks Tag 02DV, Exercise 111.22.7. *Needs:* R3.58.

- **R3.60** Base change of the finite projective trace. *Source:* Stacks Tag 02DV, Exercise 111.22.7.

- **R3.61** Transitivity of the finite projective trace in towers. *Source:* Stacks Tag 02DU, Exercise 111.22.6.

- **R3.62** A finite locally free algebra is étale iff its trace pairing is perfect. *Source:* KL15 §1.2.

- **R3.63** The trace of a finite étale algebra over a field is the sum over geometric points. *Source:* Stacks Tag 03SH, §59.66.

**R3.64 The trace of a finite locally free morphism of schemes** (construction `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.finiteTrace`). Let π : X → Y be a finite locally free morphism of schemes (Mathlib: AlgebraicGeometry.IsFinite, Flat and LocallyOfFinitePresentation). The trace Tr_π : π_*𝒪_X → 𝒪_Y is the 𝒪_Y-linear map which on every affine open V = Spec A ⊆ Y with π⁻¹(V) = Spec B is Tr_{B/A} (R3.59); these glue because the affine formula is compatible with localisation (R3.60). Tr_π ∘ π^♯ is multiplication by the locally constant degree of π. The degree is Tau Ceti's `Scheme.Hom.finrank`. *API* (on `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom`): `finiteTrace, finiteTrace_app_affine, finiteTrace_comp_unit, finiteTrace_baseChange, finiteTrace_comp, finiteTrace_coprod, traceForm, etale_iff_traceForm_perfect`. *Tests:* `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.finiteTrace_test_double_cover` (computation): For π : Spec k[t][x]/(x² − t) → Spec k[t], Γ(Tr_π)(a + bx) = 2a. `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.finiteTrace_test_ramified` (non-example): For the same π and char k ≠ 2, the fibre over t = 0 has one geometric point but Tr_π(1)(0) = 2: the trace is not the sum over geometric points of fibres. `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.finiteTrace_test_id` (degenerate): Tr_{id_Y} = id and Tr for the empty scheme over Y is 0. `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.finiteTrace_test_affine_free` (compatibility): For Y = Spec A and X = Spec B with B free over A, Γ(Y, Tr_π) = Algebra.trace A B. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0BVH, §49.3. *Needs:* R3.59, R3.60, R3.61.

**R3.65 Finite locally free morphisms of adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsFiniteLocallyFree`). A morphism of adic spaces f : Y → X is finite locally free if it is finite (R0.79) and f_*𝒪_Y is a vector bundle on X (R3.27). Its degree is the rank of f_*𝒪_Y, a locally constant function on X. Over an open affinoid Spa(A, A⁺) ⊆ X with A a complete Tate ring such that f⁻¹(Spa A) is affinoid — such affinoids cover X by the definition of finiteness, and every open affinoid with A strongly noetherian qualifies (R0.79, Zavyalov Theorem B.2.18) — f⁻¹(Spa A) = Spa(B, B⁺) with B a finite projective A-algebra (R3.29) and B⁺ the integral closure of A⁺, and for every rational V ⊆ Spa(A, A⁺), f⁻¹(V) = Spa(B ⊗_A 𝒪_X(V)) with the uncompleted tensor product (R0.87 (iii)). It is not asserted that f⁻¹ of every sheafy Tate affinoid is affinoid (Zavyalov Remark B.2.19; Kedlaya–Liu Remark 8.2.18). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsFiniteLocallyFree, IsFiniteLocallyFree.degree, IsFiniteLocallyFree.comp, IsFiniteLocallyFree.baseChange, IsFiniteLocallyFree.of_finite_etale, isFiniteLocallyFree_affinoid_iff, IsFiniteLocallyFree.analytification, IsFiniteLocallyFree.isProper`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.IsFiniteLocallyFree.test_power_map` (computation): f : Spa(K⟨z⟩) → Spa(K⟨w⟩), w ↦ zⁿ, is finite locally free of degree n (basis 1, z, …, z^{n−1}); for p ∤ n it is étale exactly away from w = 0. `AdicSpace.IsFiniteLocallyFree.test_closed_point` (non-example): The closed immersion Spa(K) → Spa(K⟨w⟩) of the origin is finite but f_*𝒪 = (K⟨w⟩/(w))~ is not locally free. `AdicSpace.IsFiniteLocallyFree.test_id_empty` (degenerate): id_X is finite locally free of degree 1; ∅ → X is finite locally free of degree 0. `AdicSpace.IsFiniteLocallyFree.test_analytification` (compatibility): For a finite locally free morphism π of K-schemes lft, π^an is finite locally free of the same degree. *Source:* BHW §10.1, Def. 10.2, p. 1789. *Needs:* R0.79, R3.27, R3.29, R0.100, R0.42, R1.21, R0.87.

- **R3.66** Projection formula for finite morphisms. *Source:* BHW §10.1, Def. 10.2.

**R3.67 The trace of a finite locally free morphism of adic spaces** (construction `AdicSpace.finiteTrace`). Let f : Y → X be a finite locally free morphism of adic spaces (R3.65). The trace Tr_f : f_*𝒪_Y → 𝒪_X is the composite of the multiplication map f_*𝒪_Y → ℋom_{𝒪_X}(f_*𝒪_Y, f_*𝒪_Y) with the trace ℋom(E, E) → 𝒪_X of the vector bundle E = f_*𝒪_Y, defined on every open U over which E is free as the matrix trace (independent of the basis) and glued. On a sheafy Tate affinoid U = Spa(A, A⁺) it is Tr_{B/A} for B = 𝒪_Y(f⁻¹U) (R3.59). Tr_f is 𝒪_X-linear, Tr_f ∘ f^♯ is multiplication by the degree, and Tr_f is not a sum over geometric points of fibres unless f is étale. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `finiteTrace, VectorBundle.trace, finiteTrace_app_affinoid, finiteTrace_comp_unit, finiteTrace_linear, finiteTrace_baseChange, finiteTrace_comp, finiteTraceForm, finiteTrace_coprod`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.finiteTrace_test_power_map` (computation): For f : z ↦ zⁿ = w on the closed disc, Tr_f(zᵏ) = n·w^{k/n} if n divides k and 0 otherwise. `AdicSpace.finiteTrace_test_ramified` (non-example): For f : z ↦ z² at the point w = 0, the fibre has one geometric point but Tr_f(1)(0) = 2; defining Tr_f(s)(x) = Σ_{y ↦ x} s(y) is wrong at ramified points. `AdicSpace.finiteTrace_test_id` (degenerate): Tr_{id_X} = id and for ∅ → X the trace is 0. `AdicSpace.finiteTrace_test_split` (characterisation): For Y = ⊔_{i=1}^d X → X, Tr_f(s₁, …, s_d) = Σ s_i. `AdicSpace.finiteTrace_test_algebraic` (compatibility): For π finite locally free between K-schemes lft, Tr_{π^an} = (Tr_π)^an (R3.70). *Source:* BHW §10.1, Def. 10.2, p. 1789. *Needs:* R3.65, R3.59, R3.60, R3.58, R3.27.

- **R3.68** Base change and composition laws for the analytic trace. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0BVH, proof of Lem. 49.3.1.

- **R3.69** Perfect trace pairing and point-count formula for finite étale morphisms. *Source:* KL15 §1.2.

- **R3.70** The analytic trace of an analytified finite locally free morphism is the analytified algebraic trace. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0BVH, §49.3.

**R3.71 Pull–identify–trace operators along correspondences** (construction `AdicSpace.Correspondence`). A correspondence from Y to X is an adic space C with morphisms q : C → Y and p : C → X, p finite locally free.
