Given vector bundles (or, on locally noetherian analytic spaces, coherent sheaves) E_Y on Y and E_X on X and an 𝒪_C-linear identification φ : q*E_Y → p*E_X, the pull–identify–trace operator T = T(C, φ) : Hⁱ(Y, E_Y) → Hⁱ(X, E_X) is the composite Hⁱ(Y, E_Y) →(q*) Hⁱ(C, q*E_Y) →(φ) Hⁱ(C, p*E_X) = Hⁱ(X, p_*p*E_X) = Hⁱ(X, p_*𝒪_C ⊗ E_X) →(Tr_p ⊗ id) Hⁱ(X, E_X), using that p is finite (p_* exact on coherent / vector bundles) and the projection formula. No normalising factor is included; consumers multiply by their own constants (e.g. 1/q_a in BHW). *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `Correspondence, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace_id, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace_comp, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace_add, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace_one, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace_analytification, Correspondence.pullIdentifyTrace_restrict`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.Correspondence.test_split` (computation): For C = X ⊔ X, p = id ⊔ id, q = id ⊔ σ and E = 𝒪 with φ canonical, T(s) = s + σ*s on H⁰. `AdicSpace.Correspondence.test_identity` (degenerate): For C = X = Y, p = q = id and φ = id, T is the identity on every Hⁱ. `AdicSpace.Correspondence.test_ramified_count` (non-example): For X = Y = disc, C = disc, p : z ↦ z², q : z ↦ z² and trivial coefficients, T(1) = Tr_p(1) = 2 everywhere, including at 0 where the p-fibre has one geometric point; 'sum over geometric points of the fibre' gives 1 at 0. `AdicSpace.Correspondence.test_algebraic` (compatibility): For an algebraic correspondence of K-schemes lft with p finite locally free, T(C^an, φ^an) on Hⁱ(Y^an, E_Y^an) is the analytification of the algebraic operator (R3.70 and the GAGA comparison maps). *Source:* BHW §10.1, Def. 10.2, p. 1789. *Needs:* R3.67, R3.66, R3.18, R3.68, R3.2, R3.30.

- **R3.72** Almost noetherianity of modules over integral affinoid rings (Kiehl, Anhang Satz 5.1). *Source:* Kiehl-E §5 (Anhang), Satz 5.1.

### Notions this roadmap states for its own use

**R3.73 The Gel'fand spectrum of a Banach ring and Berkovich's formula for the spectral seminorm** (construction). For a complete normed commutative ring A (a Banach ring, not assumed an algebra over a field) the set M(A) of bounded multiplicative seminorms, with the topology of pointwise convergence. M(A) is nonempty when A ≠ 0, compact Hausdorff, and functorial in bounded ring homomorphisms; the spectral seminorm of x ∈ A is lim ‖xⁿ‖^{1/n} = max_{α ∈ M(A)} α(x). API: `GelfandSpectrum`, `compactSpace`, `nonempty_of_nontrivial`, `comap`, `spectralSeminorm_eq_sup`. Tests: M(ℚ_p) is a single point; for a complete nonarchimedean field K the Gauss norm is a point of M(K⟨X⟩) at which the maximum is attained for every f; the spectral seminorm of a nilpotent element is 0 while its norm need not be. *Source:* Berkovich Thm 1.2.1 (nonempty compact spectrum) and Thm 1.3.1 (the spectral-seminorm formula); KL15 Def. 2.3.2, Thm 2.3.4, Thm 2.3.10, Rem. 2.3.11(b).


<a id="r4"></a>

## R4. Étale and pro-étale sites

**R4. Étale and pro-étale sites.** Depends on R0, R1 and AdicEtaleGeometry A1. The layer transports A1's sites to the carriers of R0 and R1 and proves what the consumers need on them: functoriality and slices, the pro-étale site and the morphism ν, the comparison with the rigid étale site, analytification, geometric points and pullback, smooth pairs, and restriction to the complement of a boundary. No site is constructed here and no derived cohomology is defined.


### The étale site on the analytic carriers

**R4.1 The étale site of AdicEtaleGeometry A1 on the analytic carriers of R0 and R1** (comparison). Let X be a locally noetherian analytic adic space (R0.38): every point has an open affinoid neighbourhood Spa(A, A⁺) with A a strongly noetherian complete Tate ring or with a noetherian ring of definition, and every point is analytic. This covers r(X_rig) for a rigid analytic variety X_rig over a complete nonarchimedean field k (R1.31) and X^ad for a scheme X locally of finite type over k (R1.15). The small étale site X_ét of AdicEtaleGeometry A1 on X is identified with the following structure, and no other étale site is constructed: (i) its underlying category is the full subcategory Et/X of adic spaces over X whose structure morphism is étale in the sense of R0 (Huber Definition 1.6.5: locally of finite presentation, and Hom_X(Spa B, -) -> Hom_X(Spa B/I, -) bijective for every affinoid ring B and square-zero ideal I); on such X this class coincides with A1's class of morphisms that are locally on the source an open immersion followed by a finite étale morphism followed by an open immersion (Huber Lemma 2.2.8; Kedlaya-Liu Definition 8.2.16), so the identity on objects is an isomorphism between the two presentations of the category; (ii) every X-morphism between objects of Et/X is étale, Et/X has a final object X and fibre products, computed as fibre products of adic spaces (R0, Huber 1.2.2(a)); (iii) a family (f_i: U_i -> U)_i in Et/X is a covering exactly when U = ∪_i f_i(U_i) as sets, these families form a pretopology, and the Grothendieck topology it generates is A1's étale topology. Open subspaces of X are objects of Et/X and open covers are étale coverings, so the analytic (open-subset) topology of X is coarser than the étale topology. *Hypotheses:* X locally noetherian and analytic; for non-noetherian carriers (sousperfectoid, perfectoid) the étale site is A1's local-description site and Huber's infinitesimal definition is not used (see R5.13). Étale is taken in the sense of R0 (Huber 1.6.5), which includes 'locally of finite presentation'. The identification (i) is Huber's Lemma 2.2.8 as imported by A1 (requests entry); only the coverings by jointly surjective families are used, not Huber's strongly surjective rigid coverings. *Source:* Huber96 §2.1, p. 109. *Needs:* R0.38, R0.100, R0.42, R0.44, R0.119, R0.48, R0.105, R0.104, R0.101, R0.43, R0.33, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`.

### Functoriality and slices

- **R4.2** Base change defines a morphism of étale sites f_ét: X_ét -> Y_ét. *Source:* Huber96 §2.1.

- **R4.3** The étale site of an étale X-space is the slice site: U_ét ≃ X_ét/U. *Source:* Huber96 §2.1.

### Pro-étale site and ν

**R4.4 The corrected pro-étale site of AdicEtaleGeometry A1 on the analytic carriers, and ν: X_proét -> X_ét** (comparison). Let X be a locally noetherian analytic adic space as in R4.1 (Scholze's standing hypothesis: locally Spa(A, A⁺) with A strongly noetherian or with a noetherian ring of definition; étale objects are then locally connected). The pro-étale site X_proét of AdicEtaleGeometry A1 on X is built on pro-(X_ét) for the étale site X_ét of that node: (i) objects are the pro-objects U = lim U_i of X_ét (formal cofiltered inverse systems; SGA 4 I 8) that are pro-étale over X, with underlying space |U| = lim |U_i|; morphisms are morphisms of pro-objects; (ii) a morphism U -> V is pro-étale if U = lim U_i with U_i -> V étale and U_i -> U_j finite étale surjective for large i > j; (iii) coverings are families (f_i: U_i -> U) of pro-étale morphisms with |U| = ∪ f_i(|U_i|) and, following the erratum, each f_i is a composite U_i -> W_i -> U of a transfinite composition U_i = lim_{μ<λ} U_μ -> W_i (U_0 = W_i) in which every U_μ -> U_{<μ} := lim_{μ'<μ} U_{μ'} is the pullback of a finite étale surjective morphism, and an étale morphism W_i -> U; families indexed by countable presentations are coverings exactly as in the uncorrected definition, and the underlying category is unchanged by the correction; (iv) the functor c: X_ét -> X_proét sending U to the constant pro-object is fully faithful, preserves finite limits and coverings, and defines the morphism of sites ν_X: X_proét -> X_ét (Scholze's ν). No arbitrary open surjection of profinite sets is assumed to split, and the deleted Proposition 3.8 and last sentence of Proposition 3.13 are not used. *Hypotheses:* X locally noetherian (Scholze 2013 §3); the pro-étale site of a perfectoid or general analytic adic space in the sense of ECD §8 is a different site owned elsewhere (DiamondsAndVStacks). The form of the corrected covering condition for Definition 3.9 (étale map followed by a transfinite composition of pullbacks of finite étale surjections) is pinned here and requested from A1 (the erratum states that 'a similar condition' applies without displaying it). *Source:* Scholze13 §3, before Def. 3.3, p. 13. *Needs:* R4.1, R0.38, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`.

- **R4.5** Functoriality of the corrected pro-étale site and compatibility with ν. *Source:* Scholze13 §3, after Prop. 3.12.

- **R4.6** The pro-étale site of an open subspace is the slice site: U_proét ≃ X_proét/U. *Source:* Scholze13 §3, Lem. 3.10 (iii).

### Rigid comparison

**R4.7 Huber 2.1.4: the étale topos of a rigid analytic variety is the étale topos of its adic space** (comparison). Let k be a complete nonarchimedean field and X a rigid analytic variety over k, with adic space r(X) (R1.31). Let X_ét^rig be the rigid étale site: the category of rigid analytic varieties étale over X, with the Grothendieck topology in which a family is a covering exactly when its image under r is a covering of r(X)_ét (equivalently, by R4.8, when it is strongly surjective in the sense of Huber Definition 2.1.1). Then the functor r: X_ét^rig -> r(X)_ét into the étale site of R4.1 induces an equivalence of topoi ρ_X: Sh(r(X)_ét) ≃ Sh(X_ét^rig) (Huber Proposition 2.1.4), for sheaves of sets and hence for sheaves with values in any category of algebraic structures. *Hypotheses:* X any rigid analytic variety (not necessarily quasi-separated): the proof passes through the quasi-separated étale objects. The rigid category and its étale morphisms are supplied by R1 (rigid-adic comparison, étale comparison 1.7.11, R1.36); r is fully faithful and commutes with fibre products. *Source:* Huber96 §2.1, Prop. 2.1.4, p. 111. *Needs:* R4.1, R4.8, R4.2, R1.31, R1.36, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`.

- **R4.8** Strongly surjective rigid étale families are exactly those with jointly surjective adic image (Huber 2.1.2). *Source:* Huber96 §2.1, Def. 2.1.1 and Lem. 2.1.2 with proof.

### Analytification

**R4.9 The morphism of sites X^ad_ét -> X_ét for a scheme X locally of finite type over k** (comparison). Let k be a complete nonarchimedean field and X a scheme locally of finite type over k, with analytification X^ad = X ×_{Spec k} Spa(k, k°) (R1.15). The functor (-)^ad: X_ét -> X^ad_ét, from the small étale site of X (Mathlib `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.smallEtaleTopology` on the category Mathlib `AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Etale` of schemes étale over X) to the étale site of R4.1, U ↦ U^ad = U ×_X X^ad, is well defined, preserves the final object and fibre products, and sends jointly surjective étale families to jointly surjective families. It therefore defines a morphism of sites λ_X: X^ad_ét -> X_ét, natural in X for morphisms of schemes locally of finite type over k (λ_Y ∘ f^ad_ét = f_ét ∘ λ_X). Only the site morphism is constructed here; comparison theorems for cohomology along λ_X belong to ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology. *Hypotheses:* X locally of finite type over the complete nonarchimedean field k (so X^ad is locally noetherian: Spa(k, k°) is strongly noetherian). Schemes are mathlib schemes; étale in mathlib's sense (Mathlib `AlgebraicGeometry.Etale`). *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Cor. 1.7.3, pp. 85-86. *Needs:* R1.15, R1.25, R1.13, R1.20, R1.14, R4.1.

### Geometric points and pullback

**R4.10 Geometric points of A1 as points of the étale site, and their compatibility with pullback** (comparison). Let X be a locally noetherian analytic adic space. A geometric point of X (AdicEtaleGeometry A1) is a morphism ξ: Spa(C, C⁺) -> X with C an algebraically closed complete nonarchimedean field and C⁺ ⊆ C an open valuation subring (higher-rank C⁺ allowed). (a) The fibre functor ε_ξ: X_ét -> Set, U ↦ Hom_X(Spa(C, C⁺), U), is a point of the site X_ét in mathlib's sense (Mathlib `CategoryTheory.GrothendieckTopology.Point`): its category of elements (étale neighbourhoods of ξ) is cofiltered and initially small, and coverings are sent to jointly surjective families; the stalk F_ξ = colim over étale neighbourhoods of F(U). The covering property rests on the splitting of étale covers of a geometric point: every jointly surjective family of étale morphisms to Spa(C, C⁺) has a member that admits a section (Scholze, Étale cohomology of diamonds, Proposition 14.3). An étale space over Spa(C, C⁺) is only locally on the source an open subspace of Spa(C, C⁺): when C⁺ ≠ O_C, Spa(C, C⁺) is a chain of at least two specializations and an étale space over it need not be a disjoint union of open subspaces of copies of Spa(C, C⁺). (b) For a morphism f: X -> Y, f∘ξ is a geometric point of Y and ε_{f∘ξ} ≅ ε_ξ ∘ f* naturally, where f* is the base change of R4.2; hence (f^{-1}G)_ξ ≅ G_{f∘ξ} for every sheaf G on Y_ét, naturally in G. (c) On the pro-étale site of R4.4, for a point x ∈ X with Y_x = Spa(K, K⁺) -> X, Scholze's morphism of topoi i_x: Sh(Y_x,profét) -> Sh(X_proét) is compatible with pullback: for f: X -> Y, f_proét ∘ i_x ≅ i_{f(x)} ∘ (Y_x -> Y_{f(x)})_profét. Stalks at geometric points alone are not conservative on X_proét, and the deleted final sentence of Scholze Proposition 3.13 (enough points given by profinite covers of geometric points) is not used; both topoi are coherent and have enough points abstractly. *Hypotheses:* X locally noetherian analytic; geometric points in Huber's sense with arbitrary valuation subring C⁺. The conservativity of the family (ε_ξ) and the description of strict localizations are A1's (Huber §2.5, via the A1 node). *Source:* Huber96 §2.1, p. 109. *Needs:* R4.1, R4.2, R4.4, R4.5, R0.42, R0.108, R0.48, R0.119, R0.43, R0.112, R0.114, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`.

### Smooth pairs

**R4.11 Smooth pairs: a smooth adic space with a strict normal crossings divisor** (definition `AdicSpace.SmoothPair`). Let K be a complete nonarchimedean field and B^n_K := Spa(K⟨T_1, ..., T_n⟩, K°⟨T_1, ..., T_n⟩) the closed unit polydisc (Tau Ceti `ValuationSpectrum.closedPolydisc`). A smooth pair over K is a pair (X, D) where X is an adic space smooth over Spa(K, K°) (R0.99) and D ⊆ X is a closed adic subspace with ideal sheaf I_D ⊆ O_X (R0.45) such that every x ∈ X has a strict normal crossings chart: an open neighbourhood V of x, integers 0 ≤ r ≤ n and an étale morphism h: V -> B^n_K with I_D|_V = h^*(T_1 ⋯ T_r)·O_V (so D ∩ V = h^{-1}(V(T_1 ⋯ T_r)); r = 0 means D ∩ V = ∅). The boundary complement is the open subspace U := X ∖ D with its open immersion j: U -> X. Conventions: 'strict' means that charts exist on an open cover of X over K itself; the normal crossings divisors of Diao-Lan-Liu-Zhu (charts étale-locally, equivalently analytic-locally after a finite separable extension of K) form the weaker class and are not smooth pairs in this sense unless charts exist over K. D is reduced and an effective Cartier divisor. No condition is imposed on irreducible components, and r may vary with x. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `SmoothPair, SmoothPair.Chart, SmoothPair.complement, SmoothPair.j, SmoothPair.isOpenImmersion_j, SmoothPair.mem_complement_iff, SmoothPair.trivial, SmoothPair.model, SmoothPair.restrict, SmoothPair.pullbackEtale, SmoothPair.isCartier_D, SmoothPair.analytification, SmoothPair.isNormalCrossings_DLLZ`. *Tests:* `SmoothPair.test_punctured_disc` (computation): For (X, D) = (B^1_K, V(T)) the complement U = {0 < |T| ≤ 1} equals the increasing union of the rational subsets R(ϖ^n/T) = {|ϖ^n| ≤ |T|}, n ≥ 1, no finite subfamily covers U, and so U is not quasi-compact and j: U -> X is not a quasi-compact morphism. `SmoothPair.test_empty_boundary` (degenerate): For X smooth over K, (X, ∅) is a smooth pair (charts with r = 0 from R0.117), its complement is X and j is the identity. `SmoothPair.test_three_lines_not_pair` (non-example): (B^2_K, V(T_1 T_2 (T_1 - T_2))) is not a smooth pair: at the origin every chart would present D as V(h_1 ⋯ h_r) with r ≤ 2 and h_i part of étale coordinates, whose tangent cone has at most 2 lines, while that of T_1 T_2 (T_1 - T_2) has 3. `SmoothPair.test_nonstrict_conjugate_lines` (non-example): Over K = Q_p, D = V(T_1^2 - p T_2^2) ⊂ B^2 is a normal crossings divisor in the sense of Diao-Lan-Liu-Zhu (after adjoining √p it is V(xy) in coordinates x, y) but (B^2, D) is not a smooth pair: the germ of D at 0 is irreducible over Q_p, whereas a chart over Q_p would split it into two branches. `SmoothPair.test_log_structure_trivial_on_complement` (compatibility): For a smooth pair (X, D), the Diao-Lan-Liu-Zhu log structure M_X = {f ∈ O_{X_ét} : f invertible on X - D} restricted to U = complement is O_{U_ét}^×, i.e. U is the maximal open subspace on which M_X is trivial. *Source:* DLLZ-RH §2.1, Ex. 2.1.2, p. 10. *Needs:* R0.99, R0.45, R0.100, R0.101, R0.104, R0.121, R0.117.

- **R4.12** Analytification of an algebraic strict normal crossings pair is a smooth pair. *Source:* Huber96 §1.7, Cor. 1.7.3.

### Restriction to the boundary complement

**R4.13 Restriction of the étale and pro-étale sites of a smooth pair to its boundary complement** (construction `AdicSpace.SmoothPair.complementEtale`). Let (X, D) be a smooth pair over K (R4.11) with boundary complement j: U = X ∖ D -> X. Construct: (a) U as an object of X_ét and the equivalence of sites U_ét ≃ X_ét/U (R4.3), under which the morphism of sites j_ét: U_ét -> X_ét of R4.2 is the slice morphism; (b) the functors on sheaves of sets and of abelian groups j^{-1}F = F|_U with (j^{-1}F)(V) = F(V) for V ∈ U_ét, j_*G with (j_*G)(W) = G(W ×_X U), and j_! (extension by zero for abelian sheaves), the sheafification of the presheaf W ↦ G(W) if W -> X factors through U and W ↦ 0 otherwise; with adjunctions j_! ⊣ j^{-1} ⊣ j_* and canonical isomorphisms j^{-1} j_* ≅ id ≅ j^{-1} j_! (j is a monomorphism); (c) the same on the pro-étale sites, U_proét ≃ X_proét/U (R4.6), with j_proét compatible with ν: ν_X ∘ j_proét corresponds to j_ét ∘ ν_U. The inclusion j is in general not quasi-compact (for (B^1, V(T)) the complement is not quasi-compact), so no base-change or continuity statement requiring a qcqs morphism (such as Scholze Corollary 3.17(ii) for j) is part of this construction; exactness properties and derived functors of j_!, j_* belong to ClassicalAdicEtaleCohomology. *API* (on `AdicSpace.SmoothPair`): `complementEtale, etaleSiteComplementEquiv, jEtale, jInv, jInv_obj_apply, jPush, jShriek, jAdjunction, jShriekAdjunction, jInv_jPush_iso, jInv_jShriek_iso, proetaleSiteComplementEquiv, nu_comp_jProet`. *Tests:* `SmoothPair.test_restriction_empty_boundary` (degenerate): If D = ∅ then j^{-1}, j_* and j_! are naturally isomorphic to the identity functor of Sh(X_ét). `SmoothPair.test_jShriek_global_sections_disc` (computation): For (B^1, V(T)) and the constant sheaf Z/n on U_ét, H^0(X, j_! Z/n) = 0 and H^0(X, j_* Z/n) = Z/n. `SmoothPair.test_j_not_quasicompact` (non-example): For (B^1, V(T)), j is not quasi-compact, so the qcqs hypothesis of the étale/pro-étale base change (Scholze Corollary 3.17(ii)) fails for j; no isomorphism ν^* R j_{ét,*} ≅ R j_{proét,*} ν^* is part of the restriction package. `SmoothPair.test_slice_topology_is_mathlib_over` (compatibility): Under U_ét ≃ X_ét/U, the étale topology of U is (etaleTopology X).over U (Mathlib `CategoryTheory.GrothendieckTopology.over`) and j^{-1} is GrothendieckTopology.overPullback. `SmoothPair.test_jInv_jPush` (characterisation): For every sheaf G on U_ét, the counit j^{-1} j_* G -> G is an isomorphism, and the unit G -> j^{-1} j_! G is an isomorphism. *Source:* DLLZ-A §2.3, Ex. 2.3.16, p. 16. *Needs:* R4.11, R4.1, R4.2, R4.3, R4.6, R4.5, R0.101.


<a id="r5"></a>

## R5. Families and sousperfectoid spaces

**R5. Families and sousperfectoid spaces.** Depends on R0, R3, AdicEtaleGeometry A1 and PerfectoidSpaces P1–P3. Sousperfectoid rings, their stability under rational localisation and finite étale extension, uniformity (the library's `IsUniform.isReduced` gives the reducedness clause) and sheafiness; sousperfectoid spaces; the product of a perfectoid space with a smooth rigid space, its sheafiness and its tilde-limit presentation; completed coefficient algebras and coefficient sheaves with their base-change and dense-restriction properties.


### Sousperfectoid rings

**R5.1 Sousperfectoid Tate rings and perfectoid frames** (definition `Huber.PerfectoidFrame`). Fix a prime p. Let R be a complete Hausdorff Tate ring (Tau Ceti `Huber.IsTateRing`) in which p is topologically nilpotent (equivalently, a Tate ring with a continuous ring map Z_p -> R: a complete Tate Z_p-algebra). A perfectoid frame for R is a triple (R̃, ι, σ) consisting of a perfectoid Tate ring R̃ (PerfectoidSpaces P1: complete, uniform, with a pseudo-uniformizer ϖ such that ϖ^p divides p in R̃° and Frobenius R̃°/ϖ -> R̃°/ϖ^p is bijective), a continuous ring homomorphism ι: R -> R̃, and a continuous R-linear map σ: R̃ -> R (R̃ an R-module through ι) with σ ∘ ι = id_R. R is sousperfectoid if it admits a perfectoid frame; a Huber pair (R, R⁺) is sousperfectoid if R is. Conventions: ι is then injective and a homeomorphism onto the closed subring ι(R) = ker(id - ι∘σ); σ need not be multiplicative and need not map any ring of integral elements into R⁺; no perfectoid base field is fixed, and R̃ is not required to contain one; characteristic p is allowed (then R̃ is a perfect complete Tate ring). *API* (on `Huber`): `PerfectoidFrame, IsSousperfectoid, IsSousperfectoid.of_frame, IsSousperfectoid.of_isPerfectoidTateRing, PerfectoidFrame.isClosedEmbedding_ι, PerfectoidFrame.isPowerBounded_iff, IsSousperfectoid.isUniform, IsSousperfectoid.isReduced, IsSousperfectoid.of_ringEquiv, PerfectoidFrame.baseChange, IsSousperfectoid.rationalLocalization, IsSousperfectoid.finiteEtale, IsSousperfectoid.restrictedMvPowerSeries, IsSousperfectoid.isStablyUniform, IsSousperfectoid.isSheafyPair`. *Tests:* `IsSousperfectoid.test_padic_tateAlgebra` (computation): Q_p⟨T⟩ is sousperfectoid: ι: Q_p⟨T⟩ -> Q_p^cycl⟨T^{1/p^∞}⟩ with σ = (retraction Q_p^cycl -> Q_p) applied to coefficients followed by deleting non-integral exponents is a perfectoid frame. `IsSousperfectoid.test_padic_tateAlgebra_not_perfectoid` (non-example): Q_p⟨T⟩ is not a perfectoid Tate ring: Frobenius on Z_p⟨T⟩/p = F_p[T] is not surjective (T is not a p-th power), so 'sousperfectoid' is strictly weaker than 'perfectoid'. `IsSousperfectoid.test_dualNumbers` (non-example): A = Q_p⟨X, Q⟩/(Q²) is strongly noetherian and sheafy (anchor Layer 4 example) but not sousperfectoid: a sousperfectoid ring is uniform, hence reduced, and Q ≠ 0 is nilpotent. A definition by 'sheafy' or by a splitting of abstract modules into a non-perfectoid ring fails this test. `IsSousperfectoid.test_padic` (degenerate): Q_p (the Tate algebra in zero variables) is sousperfectoid, with frame Q_p -> Q_p^cycl split by a continuous Q_p-linear retraction (R5.9). `IsSousperfectoid.test_of_perfectoid` (compatibility): Every perfectoid Tate ring in the sense of PerfectoidSpaces P1 (e.g. Q_p^cycl, or F_p((t^{1/p^∞}))^∧) is sousperfectoid via the identity frame. `IsSousperfectoid.test_charP_Fsplit` (characterisation): For a uniform complete Tate ring A of characteristic p whose Frobenius is finite: A is sousperfectoid iff Frobenius A -> A splits as A-modules (Hansen-Kedlaya Lemma 7.10); e.g. F_p((t))⟨T⟩ is sousperfectoid. *Source:* Berkeley Lecture 6, §6.3, Def. 6.3.1, p. 47. *Needs:* `PerfectoidSpaces P1`.

### Stability of the class

- **R5.2** Topologically split injections are stable under completed base change (Hansen-Kedlaya Remark 7.2). *Source:* HK §7, Rem. 7.2.

- **R5.3** Sousperfectoid rings are uniform. *Source:* HK §7, Def. 7.1.

- **R5.4** Rational localisations of sousperfectoid rings are sousperfectoid (Berkeley 6.3.3(1)). *Source:* Berkeley Lecture 6, §6.3, Prop. 6.3.3(1).

- **R5.5** Finite étale algebras over sousperfectoid rings are sousperfectoid (Berkeley 6.3.3(2), HK 7.5(a)). *Source:* Berkeley Lecture 6, §6.3, Prop. 6.3.3(2).

- **R5.6** Descent of the sousperfectoid property along faithfully flat finite étale maps (HK 7.5(b)). *Source:* HK §7, Lem. 7.5.

**R5.7 Tate algebras over sousperfectoid rings are sousperfectoid (Berkeley 6.3.3(3), HK Lemma 7.3)** (theorem). Let R be sousperfectoid and n ≥ 0. Then R⟨T_1, ..., T_n⟩ (Tau Ceti `Huber.restrictedMvPowerSeriesCompletion`) is sousperfectoid: for a frame (R̃, ι, σ) of R, the perfectoid Tate ring R̃⟨T_1^{1/p^∞}, ..., T_n^{1/p^∞}⟩ (the completion of R̃[T_i^{1/p^∞}] for the topology in which R̃°[T_i^{1/p^∞}] is open with its ϖ-adic topology) with ι⟨T⟩(Σ a_α T^α) = Σ ι(a_α) T^α and σ'(Σ_{α ∈ (Z[1/p]_{≥0})^n} x_α T^α) = Σ_{α ∈ N^n} σ(x_α) T^α is a perfectoid frame. This node refines the decomposition node of the same id (which bundled Propositions 6.3.3 and 6.3.4) to part (3); parts (1), (2) and 6.3.4 are the nodes R5.4, R5.5 and R5.10. *Hypotheses:* R sousperfectoid; the case n = 0 is trivial. *Source:* Berkeley Lecture 6, §6.3, Prop. 6.3.3(3), p. 47. *Needs:* R5.1, R0.23, `PerfectoidSpaces P1`.

- **R5.8** Laurent algebras and annuli over sousperfectoid rings are sousperfectoid. *Source:* HK §7, Lem. 7.3.

- **R5.9** Finite extensions of Q_p are sousperfectoid. *Source:* HK §7, Rem. 7.8.

### Stable uniformity and sheafiness

**R5.10 Sousperfectoid pairs are stably uniform (Berkeley 6.3.4)** (theorem). Let (R, R⁺) be a Tate Huber pair with R sousperfectoid. Then (R, R⁺) is stably uniform: for every rational subset U ⊆ Spa(R, R⁺), O(U) is uniform (Hansen-Kedlaya Definition 3.13; anchor Layer 4.2). *Hypotheses:* R complete Tate sousperfectoid; R⁺ any ring of integral elements. *Source:* Berkeley Lecture 6, §6.3, Prop. 6.3.4, p. 48. *Needs:* R5.4, R5.3, R3.34, `PerfectoidSpaces P2`.

- **R5.11** Sousperfectoid pairs are sheafy, strongly sheafy and O-acyclic. *Source:* BHW §3.1, Prop. 3.3.

### Sousperfectoid spaces

**R5.12 Sousperfectoid adic spaces** (definition `AdicSpace.IsSousperfectoid`). An adic space X (anchor Layer 5: an object of Huber's category 𝒱 covered by affinoid adic spaces) is sousperfectoid if it admits an open cover by affinoid adic spaces Spa(R, R⁺) with R sousperfectoid (R5.1); such Spa(R, R⁺) are adic spaces by R5.11. Equivalently, the open affinoid subspaces Spa(R, R⁺) ⊆ X with R sousperfectoid form a basis of the topology of X. A sousperfectoid space is analytic (its charts are Tate). A morphism of sousperfectoid spaces is a morphism of adic spaces. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsSousperfectoid, IsSousperfectoid.spa, IsSousperfectoid.of_perfectoidSpace, IsSousperfectoid.restrict, IsSousperfectoid.exists_basis, IsSousperfectoid.isAnalytic, IsSousperfectoid.isReduced_sections, IsSousperfectoid.of_etale, IsSousperfectoid.relativePolydisc, IsSousperfectoid.isStablyAdic, IsSousperfectoid.fibreProduct_etale`. *Tests:* `AdicSpace.IsSousperfectoid.test_closedDisc_Qp` (computation): The closed unit disc Spa(Q_p⟨T⟩, Z_p⟨T⟩) is a sousperfectoid adic space (Berkeley Example 6.3.2(2)). `AdicSpace.IsSousperfectoid.test_perfectoid` (compatibility): Every perfectoid space (PerfectoidSpaces P2) is a sousperfectoid adic space, with charts the affinoid perfectoid opens. `AdicSpace.IsSousperfectoid.test_dualNumbers` (non-example): Spa(Q_p⟨X,Q⟩/(Q²)) is an adic space (strongly noetherian) but not sousperfectoid: sections of O over any open of a sousperfectoid space are reduced (products and equalizers of reduced rings), while Q ≠ 0 is nilpotent in the global sections. `AdicSpace.IsSousperfectoid.test_empty_and_point` (degenerate): The empty adic space and Spa(Q_p, Z_p) are sousperfectoid. `AdicSpace.IsSousperfectoid.test_smooth_over_perfectoid_field` (characterisation): A rigid space smooth over a perfectoid field L is sousperfectoid (R5.17 with X = Spa L); e.g. B^1_L is sousperfectoid and not perfectoid. *Source:* BHW §3.1, Def. 3.2(3), p. 1724. *Needs:* R5.1, R5.11, R5.4, `PerfectoidSpaces P2`.

- **R5.13** Étale spaces over a sousperfectoid space are sousperfectoid adic spaces. *Source:* Berkeley Appendix to Lecture 19, §19.5.

- **R5.14** Sousperfectoid spaces are stably adic. *Source:* KL15 §8.2, Def. 8.2.19.

- **R5.15** Base change of étale maps to locally noetherian spaces along sousperfectoid spaces. *Source:* Berkeley Lecture 7, §7.5, after Def. 7.5.1.

### Perfectoid times smooth

- **R5.16** Perfectoid space times a polydisc: sousperfectoid, not perfectoid. *Source:* BHW §3.1, proof of Cor. 3.4.

**R5.17 The product X ×_L Y of a perfectoid space and a smooth rigid space (BHW Corollary 3.4)** (construction `AdicSpace.perfectoidTimesSmooth`). Let L be a perfectoid field, L' a perfectoid field extension of L (complete, with L -> L' continuous), X a perfectoid space over L and Y a rigid space over L' (an adic space locally of topologically finite type over Spa(L', O_L')) that is smooth over L' (R0.99). Then the fibre product X ×_L Y := X ×_{Spa(L, O_L)} Y exists in the category of adic spaces and is a sousperfectoid adic space. Construction: X ×_L Y = X' ×_{L'} Y with X' = X ×_L Spa(L', O_L') perfectoid; for affinoid perfectoid X' = Spa(S, S⁺) and an open V ⊆ Y with an étale map h: V -> B^n_{L'} (Huber 1.6.10), X' ×_{L'} V = (X' ×_{L'} B^n_{L'}) ×_{B^n_{L'}} V with X' ×_{L'} B^n = Spa(S⟨T_1, ..., T_n⟩, S⁺⟨T⟩); these pieces glue. It is not asserted, and is false in general, that X ×_L Y is perfectoid. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `perfectoidTimesSmooth, perfectoidTimesSmooth.fst, perfectoidTimesSmooth.snd, perfectoidTimesSmooth.isPullback, perfectoidTimesSmooth.lift, perfectoidTimesSmooth.isSousperfectoid, perfectoidTimesSmooth.baseChangeIso, perfectoidTimesSmooth.polydiscChart, perfectoidTimesSmooth.map, perfectoidTimesSmooth.restrictIso, perfectoidTimesSmooth.sections_affinoid`. *Tests:* `perfectoidTimesSmooth_test_point` (degenerate): For Y = Spa(L', O_L'), X ×_L Y is the perfectoid fibre product X ×_L Spa L' of PerfectoidSpaces P2, and for L' = L it is X. `perfectoidTimesSmooth_test_polydisc` (computation): For X = Spa(S, S⁺) affinoid perfectoid over L and Y = B^n_L, X ×_L Y = Spa(S⟨T_1, ..., T_n⟩, S⁺⟨T⟩) with O(X ×_L Y) = S⟨T⟩. `perfectoidTimesSmooth_test_not_perfectoid` (non-example): For X = Spa(L, O_L) and Y = B^1_L, X ×_L Y = B^1_L = Spa(L⟨T⟩) is not a perfectoid space (R5.16): a definition of the product inside the category of perfectoid spaces fails. `perfectoidTimesSmooth_test_points` (characterisation): For every affinoid field (C, C⁺) over L', (X ×_L Y)(C, C⁺) = X(C, C⁺) ×_{Spa(L)(C, C⁺)} Y(C, C⁺): the universal property tested on points. `perfectoidTimesSmooth_test_affinoid_sections` (compatibility): For affinoid perfectoid V = Spa(S, S⁺) ⊆ X and affinoid U' = Spa(B, B⁺) ⊆ Y, V ×_L U' is affinoid with O(V ×_L U') = S ⊗̂_L B, the completed tensor product of R0.21. *Source:* BHW §3.1, Cor. 3.4, p. 1724. *Needs:* R5.16, R5.15, R5.12, R0.117, R0.99, R0.40, `PerfectoidSpaces P2`, `PerfectoidSpaces P2`.

- **R5.18** Products with a smooth space preserve tilde-limits (BHW Corollary 3.5). *Source:* BHW §3.1, Cor. 3.5.

### Coefficient algebras

**R5.19 Profinite flat modules over the ring of integers of a p-adic field** (definition `Huber.IsProfiniteFlat`). Let E be a finite extension of Q_p, O = O_E with uniformizer ϖ. A topological O-module M is linear-topological if 0 has a basis of neighbourhoods consisting of O-submodules (mathlib IsLinearTopology O M), and M is a profinite flat O-module if it is flat over O (equivalently ϖ-torsion-free), linear-topological, compact and Hausdorff (CHJ Definition 6.1; CHJ's "compact" includes Hausdorff). Then the topology of M is profinite, every open submodule has finite index, and M is ϖ-adically complete and separated. The profinite part is Tau Ceti's `IsCompactModule` with Mathlib's `IsLinearTopology`; this target adds Hausdorffness and flatness over O_K. *API* (on `Huber`): `IsProfiniteFlat, IsProfiniteFlat.pi, IsProfiniteFlat.pseudobasis, IsProfiniteFlat.finite_quotient, IsProfiniteFlat.isAdicComplete, IsProfiniteFlat.closedSubmodule, IsProfiniteFlat.ofSmall`. *Tests:* `IsProfiniteFlat.test_powerSeries` (computation): Z_p[[T_1, ..., T_d]] with its (p, T_1, ..., T_d)-adic topology is a profinite flat Z_p-module. `IsProfiniteFlat.test_zero_and_O` (degenerate): 0 and O (with the ϖ-adic topology) are profinite flat O-modules. `IsProfiniteFlat.test_torsion` (non-example): O/ϖ (finite, discrete) is compact and linear-topological but not flat, so not profinite flat; O with the discrete topology is flat but not compact. `IsProfiniteFlat.test_E_not` (non-example): E itself is flat and linear-topological but not compact, so not profinite flat. `IsProfiniteFlat.test_not_hausdorff` (non-example): O with the indiscrete topology is flat, compact and linear-topological (the only neighbourhood of 0 is O) but not Hausdorff, so it is not profinite flat; it is not isomorphic to prod_I O for any I, so a definition without T2 would falsify the pseudobasis theorem. *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Def. 6.1(2), p. 46.

- **R5.20** Pseudobases of profinite flat modules (CHJ Proposition 6.2). *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Prop. 6.2.

**R5.21 Small Z_p-algebras** (definition `Huber.IsSmallZpAlgebra`). A small Z_p-algebra (CHJ §1.4) is a ring R that is reduced, p-torsion-free and finite as an algebra over Z_p[[X_1, ..., X_d]] for some d ≥ 0. It carries its canonical adic profinite topology (the a-adic topology for an ideal of definition a, e.g. the Jacobson radical, independent of choices), for which it is a profinite flat Z_p-module (R5.19) and a noetherian semilocal ring, complete for its p-adic topology. *API* (on `Huber`): `IsSmallZpAlgebra, IsSmallZpAlgebra.topology, IsSmallZpAlgebra.isNoetherianRing, IsSmallZpAlgebra.isProfiniteFlat, IsSmallZpAlgebra.quotient, IsSmallZpAlgebra.isAdicComplete_p`. *Tests:* `IsSmallZpAlgebra.test_iwasawa` (computation): Z_p[[Z_p^×]] (the completed group ring) is a small Z_p-algebra (finite over Z_p[[T]] via 1 + p ↦ 1 + T for p odd). `IsSmallZpAlgebra.test_Zp` (degenerate): Z_p is small (d = 0). `IsSmallZpAlgebra.test_nonreduced` (non-example): Z_p[[T]]/(T²) is p-torsion-free and finite over Z_p[[T]] but not reduced, so not small. `IsSmallZpAlgebra.test_Fp` (non-example): F_p[[T]] is reduced and finite over Z_p[[T]] but has p-torsion, so not small. `IsSmallZpAlgebra.test_not_finite` (non-example): Q_p is reduced and p-torsion-free but not small: a nonzero finite module M over the local ring Z_p[[X_1..X_d]] has pM ≠ M by Nakayama, while pQ_p = Q_p. *Source:* CHJ §1.4, p. 7. *Needs:* R5.19.

**R5.22 The mixed completed tensor product V ⊗̂ M (CHJ §6.1)** (construction `Huber.mixedTensorInt`). Let E be a finite extension of Q_p with O = O_E, ϖ a uniformizer, and M a profinite flat O-module. For an O-module X define X ⊗̂ M := lim_i (X ⊗_O M/I_i), the limit over any cofinal system (I_i) of open O-submodules of M (CHJ Definition 6.3; independent of the system). For an E-vector space V with an O-lattice V° ⊆ V (V°[1/ϖ] = V) define V ⊗̂ M := (V° ⊗̂ M)[1/ϖ] (CHJ Definition 6.6). When V is an E-Banach space, V° is always taken open and bounded, and V ⊗̂ M is then canonically independent of V°. Suppose V is a Banach E-algebra and M carries an O-algebra structure for which 0 has a basis of open ideals. (CHJ call this a "small O-algebra" without defining it. Examples: a small Z_p-algebra when E = Q_p, or an O-algebra that is reduced, ϖ-torsion-free and finite over O[[X_1, ..., X_d]].) Then choosing V° an open bounded subring and the I_i ideals makes V ⊗̂ M a ring. It is a Banach E-algebra with unit ball V° ⊗̂ M, and with a pseudobasis (e_i) of M it is, as a Banach space, the space of bounded I-indexed families in V. *API* (on `Huber`): `mixedTensorInt, mixedTensorOfLattice, mixedTensor, mixedTensor.latticeIndep, mixedTensor.instCommRing, mixedTensor.banach, mixedTensor.piEquiv, mixedTensor.map, mixedTensor.tmul, mixedTensor.oneEquiv, mixedTensor.baseChange`. *Tests:* `mixedTensor_test_tateAlgebra_powerSeries` (computation): Q_p⟨X⟩ ⊗̂ Z_p[[T]] ≅ (Z_p⟨X⟩[[T]])[1/p]: power series Σ f_n T^n with f_n ∈ Q_p⟨X⟩ uniformly bounded. `mixedTensor_test_not_algebraic` (non-example): The algebraic tensor product Q_p⟨X⟩ ⊗_{Z_p} Z_p[[T]] -> Q_p⟨X⟩ ⊗̂ Z_p[[T]] is not surjective: Σ_n X^n T^n is not in the image (the T^n-coefficients of an element of the image lie in a finite-dimensional subspace of Q_p⟨X⟩). `mixedTensor_test_unbounded_lattice` (non-example): For V = Q_p⟨X, Q⟩/(Q²) and the unbounded lattice V° = power-bounded elements = Z_p⟨X⟩ + Q_p⟨X⟩Q, (V° ⊗̂ Z_p)[1/p] = Q_p⟨X⟩ ≠ V: the ϖ-adic completion kills the Q-part, so the lattice must be bounded. `mixedTensor_test_one` (degenerate): V ⊗̂ O = V canonically (pseudobasis with one element). `mixedTensor_test_finite_free` (compatibility): If M ≅ O^n then V ⊗̂ M ≅ V ⊗_O M (mathlib TensorProduct) ≅ V^n. *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Def. 6.3, p. 46. *Needs:* R5.19, R5.21.

- **R5.23** The product formula X ⊗̂ M ≅ ∏_I X̂ (CHJ Proposition 6.4). *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Prop. 6.4.

- **R5.24** Exactness of - ⊗̂ M (CHJ Corollary 6.5). *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Cor. 6.5(1).

- **R5.25** Rational and integral coefficient sheaves agree: O_X ⊗̂ M = (O_X⁺ ⊗̂ M)[1/ϖ] on reduced affinoids. *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Cor. 6.5(2).

- **R5.26** Finite projective base change of mixed tensors (CHJ Lemma 6.7). *Source:* CHJ §6.1, Lem. 6.7.

### Coefficient sheaves

**R5.27 The coefficient sheaf R = O_X ⊗̂ R on an affinoid rigid space (CHJ §6.3)** (construction `AdicSpace.coefficientSheaf`). Let K be a complete nonarchimedean field extension of Q_p, X = Spa(A, A°) an affinoid rigid space over K and R a small Z_p-algebra (R5.21). On the site of rational subsets of X with finite rational covers, define R(U) := O_X(U) ⊗̂ R. This is the mixed completed tensor over Z_p of R5.22, computed with an open bounded subring of the Banach Q_p-algebra O_X(U); by lattice independence the result does not depend on that choice. Restriction maps are induced by those of O_X, which are bounded. Each R(U) is a Banach K-algebra and the restriction maps are continuous K-algebra maps, so R is a presheaf of Banach K-algebras on the rational site. That R is a sheaf, with vanishing higher Čech cohomology, is the separate theorem R5.33; it then extends to all quasi-compact opens by finite covers. When X is reduced the unit ball of R(U) can be taken to be O_X°(U) ⊗̂ R (R5.25); for non-reduced X it cannot: for O_X(U) = ℚ_p⟨X, Q⟩/(Q²) and R = ℤ_p the power-bounded elements bQ form a p-divisible submodule, so (O_X°(U) ⊗̂ R)[1/p] = ℚ_p⟨X⟩ ≠ O_X(U) ⊗̂ R. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `coefficientSheaf, coefficientSheaf.isSheaf, coefficientSheaf.obj_eq, coefficientSheaf.banach, coefficientSheaf.unitBall, coefficientSheaf.map, coefficientSheaf.ofStructureSheaf, coefficientSheaf.piEquiv, coefficientSheaf.isNoetherian, coefficientSheaf.flat_restriction`. *Tests:* `coefficientSheaf_test_Zp` (degenerate): For R = Z_p, R ≅ O_X as presheaves of Banach K-algebras. `coefficientSheaf_test_disc_powerSeries` (computation): For K discretely valued, X the closed unit disc and R = Z_p[[T]]: R(X) = O_K⟨Y⟩[[T]][1/p] and, on the rational subset {|Y| ≤ |p|}, R = O_K⟨Y/p⟩[[T]][1/p]. `coefficientSheaf_test_not_structure_sheaf_of_product` (non-example): R(X) is not O(X) ⊗_{Z_p} R: for X the disc and R = Z_p[[T]], Σ Y^n T^n ∈ R(X) is not a finite sum of tensors (R5.22, test mixedTensor_test_not_algebraic). `coefficientSheaf_test_affinoid_weight` (compatibility): For an affinoid coefficient algebra S (reduced affinoid over Q_p) in place of R, O_X ⊗̂_{Q_p} S on rational U is O(U ×_{Q_p} Spa S), the structure sheaf of the product rigid space (R5.38). `coefficientSheaf_test_nonreduced` (non-example): For X = Spa(K⟨Y, Q⟩/(Q²)) and R = Z_p, R(X) = O(X) (computed with the bounded lattice O_K⟨Y, Q⟩/(Q²)), whereas (O_X°(X) ⊗̂ Z_p)[1/p] = K⟨Y⟩: a definition using power-bounded elements as unit ball would give the wrong ring. *Source:* CHJ §6.3, p. 50. *Needs:* R5.22, R5.24, R5.21, R5.25.

**R5.28 The localisation functor Loc for R-modules (CHJ Definition 6.14, Proposition 6.16)** (construction `AdicSpace.Loc`). Let X be affinoid rigid over a complete nonarchimedean extension K of Q_p, R small, U ⊆ X rational and M a finitely generated R(U)-module. Loc(M) is the presheaf of R|_U-modules V ↦ R(V) ⊗_{R(U)} M on rational V ⊆ U. If K is discretely valued: Loc is a fully faithful exact functor from finitely generated R(U)-modules to R-modules on U, Loc(M) is a sheaf, and Ȟ^i(𝔘, Loc(M)) = 0 = H^i(U, Loc(M)) for i ≥ 1 and every finite rational cover 𝔘. If K is not discretely valued, Loc(M) is still a sheaf with vanishing higher cohomology when M is finitely generated projective. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `Loc, Loc.obj_apply, Loc.isSheaf, Loc.functor, Loc.full_faithful, Loc.exact, Loc.globalSections, Loc.restrict`. *Tests:* `Loc_test_free` (degenerate): Loc(R(U)^n) ≅ (R|_U)^n. `Loc_test_quotient_T` (computation): For R = Z_p[[T]], K discretely valued and M = R(X)/(T) = O_X(X): Loc(M) = O_X|_U (structure sheaf), with Loc(M)(V) = O_X(V). `Loc_test_fully_faithful` (characterisation): For K discretely valued, Hom(Loc M, Loc N) = Hom_{R(U)}(M, N) for finitely generated M, N. `Loc_test_Zp_is_associated_sheaf` (compatibility): For R = Z_p, R = O_X and Loc(M) is the associated module sheaf M̃ of R3.1, M̃(V) = M ⊗_{O(U)} O(V). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Def. 6.14, p. 51. *Needs:* R5.33, R5.31, R5.30, R5.27.

**R5.29 Coherent R-modules (CHJ Definition 6.18)** (definition `AdicSpace.IsCoherentCoefficient`). Let K be a discretely valued complete extension of Q_p, X affinoid rigid over K, R small. For a rational U ⊆ X and a finite rational cover 𝔘 of U, a sheaf F of R|_U-modules is 𝔘-coherent if for every U_i ∈ 𝔘 there is a finitely generated R(U_i)-module M_i with F|_{U_i} ≅ Loc(M_i). F is coherent if it is 𝔘-coherent for some finite rational cover 𝔘. F is locally projective if the M_i can be chosen projective. *API* (on `AdicSpace`): `IsCoherentCoefficient, IsCoherentCoefficient.of_Loc, IsCoherentCoefficient.kernel, IsCoherentCoefficient.restrict, IsLocallyProjectiveCoefficient, IsCoherentCoefficient.eq_Loc`. *Tests:* `IsCoherentCoefficient.test_Loc` (degenerate): Loc(M) is coherent (trivial cover). `IsCoherentCoefficient.test_glued_line_bundle` (computation): On the disc with the Laurent cover {|Y| ≤ |p|}, {|p| ≤ |Y|}, gluing R|_{U_1} and R|_{U_2} along multiplication by a unit u ∈ R(U_12)^× gives a sheaf F that is 𝔘-coherent and locally projective of rank one (without using Kiehl, whose general case is open; see R5.36). `IsCoherentCoefficient.test_not_coherent` (non-example): The sheaf ⊕_{n ∈ N} R (countable direct sum, sheafified) is not coherent: its sections over any U are not finitely generated. *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Def. 6.18, p. 52. *Needs:* R5.28.

- **R5.30** R(U) is noetherian over a discretely valued base (CHJ Lemma 6.13(1)). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Lem. 6.13.

- **R5.31** Rational restriction maps of R are flat (CHJ Lemma 6.13(2)). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Lem. 6.13.

- **R5.32** Finite rational covers give faithfully flat maps (CHJ Lemma 6.13(3)). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Lem. 6.13(3).

**R5.33 Tate acyclicity for the coefficient sheaf (CHJ Proposition 6.15)** (theorem). Let K be a complete nonarchimedean extension of Q_p (not necessarily discretely valued), X affinoid rigid over K and R small. For every rational U ⊆ X and every finite rational cover 𝔘 of U, the augmented Čech complex 0 -> R(U) -> ∏ R(U_i) -> ∏ R(U_i ∩ U_j) -> ... is exact. Consequently R is a sheaf on the rational site and H^i(U, R) = 0 for i ≥ 1. *Hypotheses:* X affinoid, K ⊇ Q_p. The unit balls are open bounded lattices compatible with the restriction maps on the finite Čech nerve: for reduced X, O_X°(U); in general, lattices chosen inductively on the nerve (O_X°(U) is not bounded when X is not reduced, see R5.25). The étale-site analogue (CHJ Remark 6.17) is NOT part of this target: it needs Tate acyclicity of O_{X_ét} on affinoid étale objects (Huber 1996 / KL Theorem 8.2.22(c)), which no target of this roadmap supplies. *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Prop. 6.15, p. 52. *Needs:* R5.27, R5.23, R3.2, R3.16, `DiamondsAndVStacks D0`.

- **R5.34** Local equalizers for the integral and rational coefficient sheaves. *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Prop. 6.15.

- **R5.35** Density of restriction for Laurent covers (CHJ Lemma 6.19). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Lem. 6.19(2).

**R5.36 Kiehl's theorem for coherent R-modules (CHJ Theorem 6.20)** (theorem). Let K be a discretely valued complete extension of Q_p, X affinoid rigid over K, R small, and F a coherent R-module on X (R5.29). Claim (CHJ Theorem 6.20): there is a finitely generated R(X)-module M with F ≅ Loc(M), namely M = F(X); if F is locally projective, M is projective. Status. (a) Proved when R is finite free as a Z_p-module. (b) The projectivity clause holds whenever F ≅ Loc(M). (c) The existence of M for R with an infinite pseudobasis (e.g. R = Z_p[[T]]) is currently unproven: CHJ's proof uses the false density Lemma 6.19(2). *Hypotheses:* K discretely valued, K ⊇ Q_p; F coherent for some finite rational cover. CHJ's proof follows Ardakov-Wadsley §§5.3-5.5 (not read here; gap) and needs Banach-dense restriction on Laurent covers, which fails for infinite pseudobases (R5.35 (3)). A new argument is needed for (c). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Thm 6.20, p. 53. *Needs:* R5.29, R5.28, R5.33, R5.35, R5.32, R5.30, R3.16.

- **R5.37** Base change of coefficient sheaves to C (CHJ Lemma 6.21). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Lem. 6.21.

- **R5.38** Affinoid coefficient algebras: O_X ⊗̂_{Q_p} S is the structure sheaf of a product (CHJ Remark 6.22). *Source:* CHJ §6.3, Rem. 6.22.

**R5.39 Reduction of étale coverings to rational and finite étale coverings on a stable basis (Kedlaya-Liu 8.2.20-8.2.21)** (theorem). Let X be an analytic adic space with its étale site X_ét (AdicEtaleGeometry A1's local-description sense), and let B be a class of affinoid objects Spa(C, C⁺) of X_ét with C complete Tate and sheafy that covers every object of X_ét and is closed under fibre products, finite étale extensions and rational subdomain embeddings. (i) Let P be a property of coverings in X_ét of and by elements of B such that (a) a covering with a refinement having P has P, (b) composites of coverings having P have P, (c) every rational covering of an element of B has P, (d) every surjective finite étale morphism Y' -> Y of elements of B, viewed as a covering, has P. Then every covering of and by elements of B has P. (ii) Let F be a presheaf of abelian groups on X_ét such that, for every Y ∈ B and every covering V of Y that is a simple Laurent covering or a surjective finite étale morphism, Ȟ^0(Y, F; V) = F(Y) (resp. Ȟ^i(Y, F; V) = 0 for all i > 0 and Ȟ^0 = F(Y)). Then for every covering V of Y ∈ B by elements of B, H^0(Y, F) = Ȟ^0(Y, F; V) = F(Y) (resp. also H^i(Y, F) = Ȟ^i(Y, F; V) = 0 for i > 0). *Hypotheses:* Kedlaya-Liu state this for preadic spaces over an analytic field; the argument does not use the field. B consists of sheafy affinoids so that the Čech complexes are those of adic affinoid spaces. *Source:* KL15 §8.2, Prop. 8.2.20, p. 163. *Needs:* R3.7, R3.4, R0.25, `AdicEtaleGeometry A1`.

**R5.40 Vector bundles on the étale site of an affinoid with a stable basis (Kedlaya-Liu 8.2.22(c),(d))** (theorem). Let X = Spa(A, A⁺) with (A, A⁺) a complete Tate sheafy pair, and let B be a stable basis of X_ét as in R5.39. (c) For every finite projective A-module M, the presheaf M̃, Y = Spa(C, C⁺) ↦ M ⊗_A C, is an acyclic sheaf on B: H^0(Y, M̃) = M ⊗_A C and H^i(Y, M̃) = 0 for i > 0, for every Y ∈ B. (d) M ↦ M̃ is an equivalence from finite projective A-modules to finite locally free O_{X_ét}-modules, with quasi-inverse E ↦ E(X). *Hypotheses:* (A, A⁺) sheafy; B a stable basis (closed under fibre products, finite étale extensions and rational subdomains). Only finite projective modules: rational localisation is not flat in general. *Source:* KL15 §8.2, Thm 8.2.22(c), p. 165. *Needs:* R5.39, R3.30, R3.9, R3.32, R3.29.

- **R5.41** Tilde-limits are stable under base change along morphisms locally of weakly finite type (Huber Remark 2.4.3(ii)). *Source:* Huber96 §2.4, Rem. 2.4.3 (ii).

- **R5.42** Rational localisations of reduced affinoid algebras are reduced, and preserve A⁺ = A° (KL Lemma 2.5.9(c),(d)). *Source:* KL15 §2.5, Lem. 2.5.9.

### Notions this roadmap states for its own use

**R5.43 Tilde-limits of adic spaces and the affinoid criterion** (theorem). Let (X_i) be a filtered inverse system of adic spaces with quasi-compact quasi-separated transition maps and X an adic space with compatible maps f_i : X → X_i. One writes X ∼ lim X_i when (a) |X| → lim |X_i| is a homeomorphism and (b) X has a covering by open affinoids Spa(A, A⁺) such that, for the colimit A_∞ of the rings A_i over the open affinoids Spa(A_i, A_i⁺) ⊆ X_i through which Spa(A, A⁺) → X_i factors, the map A_∞ → A has dense image. Affinoid criterion: let (A_i, A_i⁺) be a direct system of complete affinoid rings with compatible rings of definition A_{i,0} ⊆ A_i and finitely generated ideals of definition I_i ⊆ A_{i,0} satisfying I_j = I_i·A_{j,0} for j ≥ i, and let (A, A⁺) be the direct limit, topologised so that A₀ = colim A_{i,0} is an open adic subring with ideal of definition colim I_i; then Spa(A, A⁺) ∼ lim Spa(A_i, A_i⁺), and the same holds after passing to the completion of (A, A⁺). The relation is stable under pullback along open immersions and fibre products with a fixed adic space over the system. *Hypotheses:* transition maps quasi-compact and quasi-separated; in the criterion, every (A_i, A_i⁺) complete, with compatible rings and finitely generated ideals of definition as stated; no sheafiness is assumed for (A, A⁺), whose Spa is taken as a pre-adic space when it is not sheafy. *Source:* Scholze–Weinstein 2013 Def. 2.4.1, Prop. 2.4.2, Prop. 2.4.3 (pullbacks; the latter through Huber96 Rem. 2.4.3, which is cited and not verified here, the book not being freely available).


<a id="f1"></a>

## F1. Dagger geometry and overconvergent de Rham complexes

**F1. Dagger geometry and overconvergent de Rham complexes.** Depends on R0–R4. Overconvergent Tate algebras and their Weierstrass theory; dagger algebras with the comparison to their completions, approximation and finiteness statements; fringe presentations and the fringe topology; dagger tensor products and rational localisation; weak completions and algebras of Monsky–Washnitzer type; affinoid dagger spaces, Tate acyclicity and the G-topology; dagger spaces, coherent modules and strict neighbourhoods; the dagger–rigid comparison; differentials and the overconvergent de Rham complex; supports, compact support and duality; finiteness (Grosse-Klönne's Theorems A and C) and Künneth; logarithmic and compact-support complexes for smooth pairs.


### Overconvergent power series

- **F1.1** Tate algebras of polydiscs of radius rho are affinoid, and shrinking the radius is injective with dense image. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.1.

**F1.2 The Monsky–Washnitzer algebra W_n = K⟨X_1, …, X_n⟩† of overconvergent power series** (construction `Dagger.washnitzerAlgebra`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). For n ∈ ℕ define W_n = K⟨X_1, …, X_n⟩† := ⋃_{ρ>1} T_n(ρ) ⊆ K[[X_1, …, X_n]], the set of power series Σ_ν a_ν X^ν for which there is a real ρ > 1 with |a_ν| ρ^{|ν|} → 0 (equivalently: there are ρ > 1 and C ≥ 0 with |a_ν| ≤ C ρ^{-|ν|} for all ν; equivalently there is ρ ∈ Γ*, ρ > 1, such that the series converges at every point x of K_a^n (K_a an algebraic closure) with max_i |x_i| ≤ ρ; convergence only at the K-rational points of such a polydisc does not suffice when K is discretely valued and ρ < |π|^{-1}, since those points already lie in the unit polydisc). Because ρ ↦ T_n(ρ) is antitone, the union is directed and W_n is a K-subalgebra of K[[X]]; the union may be taken over ρ ∈ Γ* ∩ (1, ∞) (Γ* = √|K^×|) but NOT only over ρ ∈ |K^×|. W_n ⊆ T_n = T_n(1), and W_n carries the Gauss norm |f| = max_ν |a_ν| restricted from T_n. Convention: the variables are X_1, …, X_n, |ν| = ν_1+…+ν_n, and ‘overconvergent’ always means ‘restricted at some radius strictly bigger than 1’. The radius-ρ pieces T_n(ρ) are Mathlib's `MvPowerSeries.IsRestricted.subring` for the constant radius; the union over ρ > 1 is new. *API* (on `Dagger`): `washnitzerAlgebra, mem_washnitzerAlgebra_iff, mem_washnitzerAlgebra_iff_exists_bound, mem_washnitzerAlgebra_iff_exists_mem_gamma, restrictedSubring_antitone, washnitzerAlgebra_le_restrictedMvPowerSeriesSubring, washnitzerAlgebra.X, dense_washnitzerAlgebra, gaussNorm_mul_washnitzerAlgebra, washnitzerAlgebra_zero, isUnit_washnitzerAlgebra_iff, pderiv_mem_washnitzerAlgebra, primitive_mem_washnitzerAlgebra, washnitzerAlgebra_succ`. *Tests:* `washnitzerAlgebra_test_geometric` (computation): For K = ℚ_p, f = Σ_{m≥0} p^m X^m ∈ W_1: it is restricted at every ρ < p (|p^m|ρ^m = (ρ/p)^m → 0). `washnitzerAlgebra_test_not_mem_lacunary` (non-example): For K = ℚ_p, g = Σ_{m≥0} p^m X^{p^m} ∈ T_1 but g ∉ W_1: for every ρ > 1, |p^m| ρ^{p^m} = p^{-m}ρ^{p^m} → ∞. A definition W_n := T_n fails this test. `washnitzerAlgebra_test_radii_in_gamma` (characterisation): For K = ℚ_p, h = Σ_m p^m X^{2m} ∈ W_1 (restricted at every ρ < p^{1/2}) but h ∉ T_1(p) (|p^m| p^{2m} = p^m → ∞); so the union over ρ ∈ |ℚ_p^×| ∩ (1,∞) = {p, p^2, …} is strictly smaller than W_1. `washnitzerAlgebra_test_zero` (degenerate): W_0 = K and W_n contains MvPolynomial (Fin n) K. `washnitzerAlgebra_test_tate` (compatibility): W_n ⊆ TauCeti.Huber.restrictedMvPowerSeriesSubring n K, with equality iff n = 0. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.2, p. 3. *Needs:* F1.1.

- **F1.3** Weierstrass division and preparation in W_n, and distinguishedness after an automorphism. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.3 (i).

- **F1.4** W_n is a noetherian factorial Jacobson ring and every ideal of W_n is strictly closed. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.4 (1).

- **F1.5** W_n → T_n is faithfully flat, bijective on maximal ideals with equal residue fields, and W_n is regular of dimension n. *Source:* GK00 §1, Prop. 1.5.

### Dagger algebras

**F1.6 Dagger algebras over a complete nonarchimedean field** (definition `Dagger.IsDaggerAlgebra`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). A K-dagger algebra is a commutative K-algebra A for which there exist n ∈ ℕ and a surjective K-algebra homomorphism W_n → A (equivalently A ≅ W_n/I for an ideal I). A morphism of dagger algebras is a K-algebra homomorphism (no continuity is imposed; it is automatic, F1.8). Sp(A) denotes the set of maximal ideals of A. Attached data, each independent of the presentation up to canonical isomorphism: the residue norm class of a presentation (F1.8), the completion A → Â (written A' by Grosse-Klönne), Â = T_n/IT_n, a K-affinoid algebra (F1.9), the supremum seminorm |a|_sup = max_{m ∈ Sp(A)} |a mod m|, and the integral subring A^int = {a ∈ A : |a|_sup ≤ 1} = A ∩ (Â)°, which is the ring of power-bounded elements of A. *API* (on `Dagger`): `IsDaggerAlgebra, IsDaggerAlgebra.washnitzerAlgebra, IsDaggerAlgebra.quotient, IsDaggerAlgebra.of_finite, IsDaggerAlgebra.isNoetherianRing, IsDaggerAlgebra.isJacobsonRing, IsDaggerAlgebra.finite_residueField, completion, intSubring, mem_intSubring_iff_isPowerBounded, isUnit_iff_isUnit_completion, toHuberPair, supNorm_le, completion_washnitzerAlgebra`. *Tests:* `IsDaggerAlgebra_test_laurent` (computation): A = W_2/(X_1X_2 − 1) = K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩† is a dagger algebra; its completion is T_2/(X_1X_2 − 1) = K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩, and 1/(X − a) ∈ A for every a ∈ K with |a| < 1. `IsDaggerAlgebra_test_trivial` (degenerate): K = W_0 and the zero ring W_0/(1) are dagger algebras; Sp(K) is a point. `IsDaggerAlgebra_test_polynomial` (non-example): K[X] is not a dagger algebra: the maximal ideals (X − c), c ∈ K, give |X|_sup = ∞, whereas every dagger algebra has finite supremum seminorm. (A definition 'finitely generated K-subalgebra of T_n' would admit K[X].) `IsDaggerAlgebra_test_not_complete` (non-example): W_1 is a dagger algebra that is not complete for its norm: Σ_{m≤N} p^m X^{p^m} is Cauchy in W_1 (K = ℚ_p) with limit in T_1 ∖ W_1; so 'dagger algebra = affinoid algebra' is false. `IsDaggerAlgebra_test_int` (characterisation): W_n^int = W_n ∩ O_K[[X]]: a series in W_n is power-bounded iff all its coefficients lie in O_K. `IsDaggerAlgebra_test_completion` (compatibility): The completion of W_n is (isomorphic to) TauCeti.Huber.restrictedMvPowerSeriesCompletion n K, compatibly with the inclusions of MvPolynomial (Fin n) K. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.2, p. 3. *Needs:* F1.2, F1.4, R0.53, R0.55.

- **F1.7** Noether normalisation for dagger algebras and finiteness of residue fields. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.4 (2).

- **F1.8** Residue norms on dagger algebras and automatic continuity of morphisms. *Source:* GK00 §1, Prop. 1.6 (1).

**F1.9 A dagger algebra and its completion: faithful flatness, points, completed local rings, reducedness and regularity, supremum norm** (theorem). Let A be a K-dagger algebra with completion τ : A → Â (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)). Then: (1) τ is faithfully flat and m ↦ τ^{-1}(m) is a bijection Max(Â) → Max(A) with isomorphic residue fields; (2) for corresponding maximal ideals τ induces isomorphisms of completed local rings A^_{τ^{-1}m} ≅ Â^_m; (3) A is reduced (resp. normal, resp. regular) if and only if Â is; (4) if A is reduced, the supremum seminorm |·|_sup on A is a norm lying in the canonical norm class of F1.8. Moreover the completion is functorial: every morphism φ : A → B of dagger algebras extends uniquely to a continuous K-algebra map φ̂ : Â → B̂, and φ ↦ φ̂ is a functor. *Hypotheses:* A a K-dagger algebra. K complete, nontrivially and nonarchimedeanly valued. *Source:* GK00 §1, Thm 1.7 (1), p. 5. *Needs:* F1.5, F1.8, F1.6, R0.56, F1.71.

- **F1.10** Dagger algebras are weakly complete; elements of A^int with reduction 1 are units. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.9.

- **F1.11** Finite algebras over a dagger algebra are dagger algebras. *Source:* GK00 §1, Lem. 1.10.

**F1.12 Finiteness, surjectivity, injectivity and strictness of morphisms of dagger algebras are detected on completions** (theorem). Let φ : A → B be a morphism of K-dagger algebras with completion φ̂ : Â → B̂ (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)). Then: (a) φ is surjective (resp. bijective) iff φ̂ is; (b) the following are equivalent: (i) φ is finite, (ii) φ̂ is finite, (iii) the map of reductions φ̃ : Ã → B̃ (R̃ = R°/R°° for the sup norm) is finite; (c) if φ is finite, φ is injective iff φ̂ is; (d) if φ is finite, the norm class on B from F1.8 coincides with the class of B as a finite A-module, and Â ⊗_A B → B̂ is an isomorphism; (e) if φ is finite, φ and φ̂ are strict. *Hypotheses:* φ : A → B a morphism of K-dagger algebras. *Source:* GK00 §1, Thm 1.12 (b), p. 7. *Needs:* F1.11, F1.9, F1.8, F1.10, F1.3, R0.59, Tau Ceti `LinearMap.isStrictMap_of_module_finite`.

**F1.13 Approximation of solutions and of morphisms from the completion (Bosch's rigid Artin approximation)** (theorem). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). Assume moreover char K = 0 or [K : K^p] < ∞ (the hypothesis under which Vezzani, Proposition 2.8, states the morphism form; Grosse-Klönne 1.13/1.15 state none, and Bosch's paper, which would settle it, was not read). (1) (Bosch) Let I ⊆ W_n, A = W_n/I ⊆ Â = T_n/IT_n with a Banach norm |·|_Â, and let (f_i)_{i∈Λ} be elements of A⟨Y_1..Y_m⟩† = W_{n+m}/IW_{n+m}. If there are ȳ_1..ȳ_m ∈ Â with |ȳ_j|_sup ≤ 1 and f_i(ȳ) = 0 for all i, then for every δ > 0 there are y_1..y_m ∈ A with |y_j|_sup ≤ 1, f_i(y) = 0 for all i, and |y − ȳ|_Â < δ. (2) Let p_1 : A_1 → B_1 and p_2 : A_2 → B_2 be surjective morphisms of dagger algebras, and φ_A : Â_1 → Â_2, φ_B : B̂_1 → B̂_2 morphisms with φ_B ∘ p̂_1 = p̂_2 ∘ φ_A. Then there are morphisms γ_A : A_1 → A_2 and γ_B : B_1 → B_2 with γ_B ∘ p_1 = p_2 ∘ γ_A, and for given ε > 0 and a Banach norm on Â_2, γ_A can be chosen with |γ̂_A − φ_A| < ε; if φ_A is an isomorphism, γ_A can be chosen an isomorphism. In particular two dagger algebras with isomorphic completions are isomorphic (non-canonically). *Hypotheses:* K complete, nontrivially and nonarchimedeanly valued, with char K = 0 or [K : K^p] < ∞ (Vezzani's hypothesis in Proposition 2.8; GK states 1.13/1.15 without it). *Source:* GK00 §1, Prop. 1.13, p. 8. *Needs:* F1.12, F1.9, F1.6, F1.10.

- **F1.14** Reduced dagger algebras are integrally closed in their completions; A is a domain iff Â is. *Source:* GK00 §1, Prop. 1.14 (1).

### Fringe presentations and the fringe topology

**F1.15 Fringe (radius-indexed) presentations of a dagger algebra** (construction `Dagger.FringePresentation`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K) and A a K-dagger algebra. A fringe presentation of A consists of n ∈ ℕ, a radius ρ_0 ∈ Γ* with ρ_0 > 1, elements f_1, …, f_r ∈ T_n(ρ_0), and a K-algebra isomorphism W_n/(f_1, …, f_r)W_n ≅ A. For ρ ∈ Γ* ∩ (1, ρ_0] put A_ρ := T_n(ρ)/(f)T_n(ρ), a K-affinoid algebra (F1.1), with transition maps A_{ρ'} → A_ρ for ρ ≤ ρ' induced by T_n(ρ') ⊆ T_n(ρ), and put A_1 := T_n/(f)T_n. Then A = colim_{ρ → 1+} A_ρ as K-algebras, and A_1 = Â is the completion. The fringe algebra of the presentation at radius ρ is the image B_ρ ⊆ A of A_ρ (equivalently of T_n(ρ)); A_ρ → A need not be injective. Geometrically, X_ρ = Sp(A_ρ) is a decreasing family of affinoids with ⋂_ρ X_ρ = Sp(Â) and Sp(Â) ⊆ X_ρ the Weierstrass domain {|X_1|, …, |X_n| ≤ 1}; this situation is written Sp(Â) ⊂† X_ρ. *API* (on `Dagger`): `FringePresentation, FringePresentation.exists, FringePresentation.stage, FringePresentation.transition, FringePresentation.toDagger, FringePresentation.isColimit, FringePresentation.fringeAlgebra, FringePresentation.stage_one_eq_completion, FringePresentation.isWeierstrassDomain, FringePresentation.exists_le_map, FringePresentation.plusColimit, FringePresentation.cofinal`. *Tests:* `FringePresentation_test_washnitzer` (computation): For A = W_n with the identity presentation, A_ρ = T_n(ρ), fringe algebras are the T_n(ρ), and colim_ρ A_ρ = W_n. `FringePresentation_test_field` (degenerate): For A = K (n = 0, no relations) every stage A_ρ is K and the only fringe algebra is K. `FringePresentation_test_dependence` (non-example): Fringe algebras depend on the presentation: for A = W_1 and the fringe presentation W_2 → W_1, X_1 ↦ X, X_2 ↦ X^2 (relation X_2 − X_1^2 ∈ T_2(ρ_0) for every ρ_0), the fringe algebra at radius ρ ∈ (1, ρ_0] is the image of T_2(ρ), namely T_1(ρ^{1/2}) (write X^m = X^{m mod 2}(X^2)^{⌊m/2⌋}), which strictly contains the fringe algebra T_1(ρ) of the identity presentation at the same radius (e.g. for K = ℚ_p and ρ = p^2 ≤ ρ_0: Σ_m p^{3m} X^{2m} lies in T_1(p) = T_1(ρ^{1/2}), since p^{-3m}p^{2m} → 0, but not in T_1(p^2), since p^{-3m}p^{4m} → ∞). `FringePresentation_test_completion` (compatibility): The stage at radius 1 of any fringe presentation of A is the completion Â, i.e. T_n/(f) ≅ Dagger.completion A under A. `FringePresentation_test_annulus` (computation): For A = W_2/(X_1X_2 − 1), X_ρ = Sp(A_ρ) is the annulus ρ^{-1} ≤ |X| ≤ ρ and ⋂_ρ X_ρ is the circle |X| = 1 = Sp(Â). *Source:* GK00 §1, Lem. 1.8, p. 6. *Needs:* F1.6, F1.1, F1.4, F1.2, F1.8.

- **F1.16** Morphisms of dagger algebras respect fringe systems (independence of fringe presentations). *Source:* GK00 §1, Lem. 1.8.

**F1.17 The fringe (direct limit) topology on dagger algebras and their finite modules** (construction `Dagger.fringeTopology`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K) and A a K-dagger algebra. The fringe topology on A is the finest locally K-convex topology for which every map A_ρ → A of a fringe presentation is continuous, A_ρ carrying its Banach topology; equivalently the direct limit of the intrinsic Banach topologies of the fringe algebras of A. It does not depend on the fringe presentation, is Hausdorff, is finer than the norm (affinoid) topology of F1.8, and every K-algebra homomorphism of dagger algebras is continuous for it. A finite A-module M carries the fringe topology obtained as the quotient topology from A^r ↠ M (equivalently by base extension from a module over a fringe algebra), independent of choices; A-linear maps of finite modules are continuous. If K is spherically complete (in particular discretely valued), A with its fringe topology is a complete reflexive Hausdorff locally convex K-space. *API* (on `Dagger`): `fringeTopology, fringeTopology_independent, continuous_of_fringe, fringeTopology_le_affinoid, fringeTopology.isTopologicalRing, moduleFringeTopology, fringeTopology.completeSpace, fringeTopology.continuous_stage`. *Tests:* `fringeTopology_test_field` (degenerate): For A = K the fringe topology is the valuation topology. `fringeTopology_test_not_affinoid` (non-example): The fringe topology on W_1 is strictly finer than the Gauss-norm topology: over ℚ_p the partial sums g_N = Σ_{m≤N} p^m X^{p^m} form a Gauss-norm Cauchy sequence which is not Cauchy for the fringe topology, as witnessed by the fringe-continuous seminorm q(f) = sup_k |a_k| p^{√k} (continuous on each T_1(ρ), ρ > 1, since p^{√k} = O(ρ^k)), for which q(g_N − g_M) → ∞. Likewise h_m = p^{⌊√m⌋}X^m → 0 in the Gauss norm but q(h_m) ≥ 1. `fringeTopology_test_restriction` (computation): For A = W_1 and f ∈ T_1(ρ), the sequence of truncations of f converges to f in the fringe topology (it converges in T_1(ρ') for ρ' < ρ). `fringeTopology_test_compat` (compatibility): On each fringe algebra T_n(ρ) of W_n the fringe topology restricts to a topology coarser than the ρ'-Gauss norm topology for every ρ' ∈ (1, ρ] (for ρ' = 1 the comparison goes the other way) and finer than the Gauss norm topology of T_n. *Source:* GK00 §4, 4.2, p. 19. *Needs:* F1.15, F1.16, F1.18, F1.8.

- **F1.18** Finite modules and their morphisms descend to a fringe stage. *Source:* GK00 §2, Lem. 2.15 (1).

### Tensor products and rational localisation

**F1.19 The dagger (overconvergent completed) tensor product A_1 ⊗†_B A_2** (construction `Dagger.tensor`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K) and let α_1 : B → A_1, α_2 : B → A_2 be morphisms of K-dagger algebras with surjections f_1 : W_{n_1} → A_1, f_2 : W_{n_2} → A_2. Define A_1 ⊗†_B A_2 as the image of W_{n_1+n_2} = K⟨X, Y⟩† under the continuous map T_{n_1+n_2} → Â_1 ⊗̂_{B̂} Â_2 into the completed tensor product of the K-affinoid algebras Â_1, Â_2 over B̂ that sends X_i ↦ f̂_1(X_i) ⊗ 1 and Y_j ↦ 1 ⊗ f̂_2(Y_j). It is a K-dagger algebra with maps ι_i : A_i → A_1 ⊗†_B A_2, independent of the surjections up to unique isomorphism, and it is the pushout of α_1, α_2 in the category of dagger algebras: for morphisms u_i : A_i → C with u_1α_1 = u_2α_2 there is a unique u : A_1 ⊗†_B A_2 → C with u ι_i = u_i. Its completion is Â_1 ⊗̂_{B̂} Â_2. Equivalently (Kedlaya) A_1 ⊗†_K A_2 is the direct limit of the completed tensor products B_1 ⊗̂_K B_2 of fringe algebras B_i of A_i. Notation: for λ ∈ Γ*, K⟨λ^{-1}Z⟩† := ⋃_{ρ>λ} K⟨ρ^{-1}Z⟩ and A⟨λ^{-1}Z⟩† := A ⊗†_K K⟨λ^{-1}Z⟩†, A⟨Z⟩† := A⟨1^{-1}Z⟩†. *API* (on `Dagger`): `tensor, tensor.lift, tensor.lift_comp_inl, tensor.hom_ext, tensor.completion, tensor.eq_tensorProduct_of_finite, tensor.eq_colim_fringe, tensor.washnitzer, relTate, tensor.map, tensor.flat_inl`. *Tests:* `tensor_test_washnitzer` (computation): K⟨X⟩† ⊗†_K K⟨Y⟩† ≅ K⟨X, Y⟩† compatibly with X ↦ X ⊗ 1, Y ↦ 1 ⊗ Y. `tensor_test_base` (degenerate): A ⊗†_A A = A and A ⊗†_K K = A. `tensor_test_not_naive` (non-example): For A = W_1 = K⟨X⟩†, the series f = Σ_ν π^ν X^{ν^2} Z^ν has coefficients a_ν = π^ν X^{ν^2} ∈ A with |a_ν| ρ^ν → 0 for every ρ < |π|^{-1}, but f ∉ A⟨Z⟩† = K⟨X, Z⟩† (for every ρ_1 > 1, |π|^ν ρ_1^{ν^2} → ∞). A definition A⟨Z⟩† := {Σ a_ν Z^ν : |a_ν|ρ^ν → 0} fails this test. `tensor_test_completion` (compatibility): The completion of K⟨X⟩† ⊗†_K K⟨Y⟩† is K⟨X⟩ ⊗̂_K K⟨Y⟩ = K⟨X, Y⟩, the completed tensor product of Tate algebras. `tensor_test_finite` (characterisation): For a finite extension L/K, W_n ⊗†_K L = W_n ⊗_K L = L⟨X⟩†. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.16, p. 9. *Needs:* F1.6, F1.10, F1.9, F1.16, F1.11, R0.21.

**F1.20 Rational localisation A⟨f/g⟩† of a dagger algebra and its universal property** (construction `Dagger.rationalLocalization`). Let A be a K-dagger algebra (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)) and g, f_1, …, f_m ∈ A without common zero on Sp(A) (equivalently generating the unit ideal). Define A⟨f/g⟩† := A⟨X_1, …, X_m⟩†/(gX_i − f_i)_{i≤m} (A⟨X⟩† from F1.19). The map A → A⟨f/g⟩† is universal among morphisms of dagger algebras φ : A → C such that Sp(φ) lands in R(f/g) := {x ∈ Sp(A) : |f_i(x)| ≤ |g(x)| for all i}: φ(g) is then a unit and φ factors uniquely through A⟨f/g⟩†. Special cases: Weierstrass localisations A⟨f⟩† (g = 1) and Laurent localisations A⟨f^{-1}⟩† = A⟨X⟩†/(fX − 1). Sp(A⟨f/g⟩†) = R(f/g), the completion of A⟨f/g⟩† is the affinoid rational localisation Â⟨f/g⟩, and A⟨f/g⟩† depends only on the subset R(f/g), not on (f, g). *API* (on `Dagger`): `rationalLocalization, rationalLocalization.algebraMap, rationalLocalization.lift, rationalLocalization.lift_comp_algebraMap, rationalLocalization.completion, rationalLocalization.maximalSpectrum, rationalLocalization.congr, rationalLocalization.flat, rationalLocalization.trans, laurentLocalization`. *Tests:* `rationalLocalization_test_annulus` (computation): W_1⟨X^{-1}⟩† = K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩† (series Σ_{m∈ℤ} a_m X^m with |a_m| ρ^{|m|} → 0 for some ρ > 1), the dagger annulus |X| = 1. `rationalLocalization_test_trivial` (degenerate): For g = f_1 = 1, A⟨f/g⟩† = A. `rationalLocalization_test_not_algebraic` (non-example): W_1[1/X] ⊊ W_1⟨X^{-1}⟩†: for a ∈ K with 0 < |a| < 1, 1/(X − a) = Σ_k a^k X^{-k-1} lies in K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩† but not in W_1[1/X] (its pole at X = a). A definition by algebraic localisation fails. `rationalLocalization_test_completion` (compatibility): The completion of W_1⟨X^{-1}⟩† is K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩, the Tau Ceti rational localisation of (K⟨X⟩, K°⟨X⟩) at T = {1}, s = X. `rationalLocalization_test_points` (characterisation): Max(W_1⟨X/π⟩†) is the set of Galois orbits of points x with |x| ≤ |π| of the closed unit disc. *Source:* GK00 §1, 1.18, p. 9. *Needs:* F1.19, F1.6, F1.10, F1.9.

### Weak completions and algebras of Monsky–Washnitzer type

**F1.21 The integral Monsky–Washnitzer algebra O_K⟨X_1, …, X_n⟩† = W_n ∩ O_K[[X]]** (construction `Dagger.intWashnitzerAlgebra`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K) and O_K = {a : |a| ≤ 1}. Define O_K⟨X_1..X_n⟩† := W_n ∩ O_K[[X_1..X_n]], the overconvergent power series with coefficients in O_K; it is W_n^int (F1.6). If O_K is a discrete valuation ring with uniformiser π, a series f ∈ O_K[[X]] lies in O_K⟨X⟩† iff there is c > 0 with f = Σ_{j≥0} π^j P_j(X), P_j ∈ O_K[X], deg P_j ≤ c(j+1) (the Monsky–Washnitzer degree growth condition), and then O_K⟨X⟩† is noetherian, O_K⟨X⟩†/π^m = O_K[X]/π^m for all m, its π-adic completion is O_K⟨X⟩ = T_n ∩ O_K[[X]], and O_K⟨X⟩† ⊗_{O_K} K = W_n. *API* (on `Dagger`): `intWashnitzerAlgebra, mem_intWashnitzerAlgebra_iff, mem_intWashnitzerAlgebra_iff_degree, intWashnitzerAlgebra_eq_intSubring, intWashnitzerAlgebra.quotient_pow, intWashnitzerAlgebra.adicCompletion, intWashnitzerAlgebra.isNoetherianRing, intWashnitzerAlgebra.isUnit_one_add, intWashnitzerAlgebra.tensor_fraction`. *Tests:* `intWashnitzerAlgebra_test_geometric` (computation): Σ_{j≥0} π^j X^j ∈ O_K⟨X⟩† with P_j = X^j of degree j ≤ 1·(j+1). `intWashnitzerAlgebra_test_zero` (degenerate): For n = 0, O_K⟨⟩† = O_K. `intWashnitzerAlgebra_test_not_mem` (non-example): Σ_j π^j X^{j^2} lies in the π-adic completion O_K⟨X⟩ but not in O_K⟨X⟩† (degree j^2 is not O(j)); so 'π-adic completion' is the wrong definition. `intWashnitzerAlgebra_test_fraction` (compatibility): O_K⟨X⟩†[1/π] = W_n as subrings of K[[X]]. *Source:* vdP §2, p. 35. *Needs:* F1.2, F1.6, F1.9, F1.3, F1.5, F1.72.

**F1.22 The Monsky–Washnitzer weak completion S† of a finitely generated O_K-algebra** (construction `Dagger.weakCompletion`). Let O_K be a complete discrete valuation ring with uniformiser π, residue field k = O_K/π and fraction field K (any characteristic; K carries the π-adic absolute value). Let S be a finitely generated O_K-algebra and Ŝ = lim_m S/π^mS its π-adic completion. Choose generators x_1, …, x_n of S. The weak completion S† ⊆ Ŝ is the set of z ∈ Ŝ that can be written z = Σ_{j≥0} π^j P_j(x_1, …, x_n) (convergent in Ŝ) with P_j ∈ O_K[X_1..X_n] of total degree ≤ c(j+1) for some real c; equivalently, S† is the image of O_K⟨X_1..X_n⟩† → Ŝ, X_i ↦ x_i. S† is an O_K-subalgebra of Ŝ containing the image of S, and it does not depend on the choice of generators. The dagger algebra attached to S is A† := S† ⊗_{O_K} K (F1.25). *API* (on `Dagger`): `weakCompletion, mem_weakCompletion_iff, weakCompletion_eq_range, weakCompletion_independent, weakCompletion.of, weakCompletion.quotient_pow, weakCompletion.isNoetherianRing, weakCompletion.lift, weakCompletion.map, weakCompletion.genericFibre, weakCompletion.polynomial, weakCompletion_polynomialRing`. *Tests:* `weakCompletion_test_laurent` (computation): For S = O_K[z, z^{-1}], S† = {Σ_{m∈ℤ} a_m z^m : a_m ∈ O_K, ∃ ε > 0, C, v_π(a_m) ≥ ε|m| − C} = O_K⟨z, w⟩†/(zw − 1). `weakCompletion_test_finite` (degenerate): If S is finite over O_K (e.g. S = O_K or the ring of integers of a finite extension), S† = Ŝ = S. `weakCompletion_test_not_completion` (non-example): For S = O_K[X], Σ_j π^j X^{j^2} ∈ Ŝ ∖ S†; and, when O_K has mixed characteristic (0, p), (Kedlaya) the 1-form Σ_n p^n X^{p^n − 1} dX on Ŝ is a limit of exact forms but not exact, whereas on S† every 1-form of O_K⟨X⟩† ⊗ K is exact (termwise integration preserves overconvergence because char K = 0; in characteristic p, X^{p−1}dX is not exact) (F1.49). `weakCompletion_test_generic_fibre` (compatibility): S† ⊗ K is a dagger algebra whose completion is Ŝ ⊗ K; for S = O_K[X] this is W_n ⊆ T_n = Tau Ceti's restrictedMvPowerSeriesCompletion n K. `weakCompletion_test_mod_pi` (characterisation): S†/πS† = S/πS; for S = O_K[z, z^{-1}, (z^N − 1)^{-1}] (p ∤ N) this is k[z, z^{-1}, (z^N − 1)^{-1}]. *Source:* Kedlaya01 §2, p. 2. *Needs:* F1.21.

- **F1.23** Presentation, noetherianity and reduction of a weak completion. *Source:* vdP §2, (2.2).

- **F1.24** Weak completeness and the universal property of the weak completion. *Source:* vdP §2, (2.1).

- **F1.25** The generic fibre S† ⊗ K of a weak completion is a dagger algebra with completion Ŝ ⊗ K. *Source:* GK02 Introduction.

**F1.26 Dagger algebras of Monsky–Washnitzer type** (definition `Dagger.IsMWType`). Let O_K be a complete discrete valuation ring with uniformiser π, residue field k = O_K/π and fraction field K (any characteristic; K carries the π-adic absolute value). A K-dagger algebra A is of Monsky–Washnitzer type (MW-type) if A is a regular ring and the ring A^int/πA^int is a smooth k-algebra (A^int = elements of supremum norm ≤ 1, F1.6). Its reduction (special fibre) is A^int/πA^int. The regularity clause is part of the definition: it does not follow from smoothness of A^int/πA^int. *API* (on `Dagger`): `IsMWType, IsMWType.reduction, IsMWType.reduction_eq, IsMWType.of_smooth, isMWType_iff, IsMWType.isReduced, IsMWType.projective_differentials, IsMWType.laurent, IsMWType.washnitzer`. *Tests:* `IsMWType_test_washnitzer` (computation): W_n = O_K⟨X⟩† ⊗ K is of MW-type with reduction k[X_1..X_n]. `IsMWType_test_field` (degenerate): K = W_0 is of MW-type with reduction k. `IsMWType_test_not_regular` (non-example): A = W_1/(X^2): X is nilpotent so X/π^m ∈ A^int for all m, hence A^int/πA^int = k is smooth, but A is not regular; the regularity clause (Kedlaya's errata to Definition 3.2.1) excludes it. `IsMWType_test_ramified` (non-example): A = W_1[Y]/(Y^2 − π) = K(√π)⟨X⟩† is regular and finite étale over W_1 (char K ≠ 2, so Y^2 − π is separable), but A^int/πA^int = k[ε, X]/(ε^2) is not reduced, so A is not of MW-type over K. `IsMWType_test_projective` (compatibility): For A of MW-type, Ω^1_{A/K} is a finite projective A-module (Mathlib Module.Projective). *Source:* Kedlaya06 §3.2, Def. 3.2.1. *Needs:* F1.6, F1.25.

**F1.27 Lifting morphisms of smooth reductions to weak completions (van der Put 2.4.4 (ii))** (theorem). Let O_K be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic (0, p) with uniformiser π, residue field k = O_K/π and fraction field K (K carries the π-adic absolute value; this is van der Put's standing hypothesis 'K = Qt(R) of characteristic 0' — the Artin approximation property of O_K⟨t⟩† used in the proof is not available in the sources for equal characteristic). Let A_0, C_0 be smooth finitely generated k-algebras and A, C lifts of them: weakly complete finitely generated O_K-algebras, flat over O_K, with A/πA ≅ A_0 and C/πC ≅ C_0. Then every k-algebra homomorphism f : A_0 → C_0 lifts to an O_K-algebra homomorphism F : A → C (F mod π = f). More generally, every O_K-algebra homomorphism F̂ : A → Ĉ into the π-adic completion can be approximated: for every m there is F : A → C with F ≡ F̂ mod π^m. *Hypotheses:* O_K a complete DVR of mixed characteristic (0, p). A, C lifts of smooth finitely generated k-algebras. *Source:* vdP §2, (2.4.4) (ii), p. 37. *Needs:* F1.24, F1.23, F1.21.

**F1.28 Existence and uniqueness of dagger algebras of MW-type with a given smooth reduction** (theorem). Let O_K be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic (0, p) with uniformiser π, residue field k = O_K/π and fraction field K (K carries the π-adic absolute value) and A_0 a smooth finitely generated k-algebra. Then (a) there is a smooth finitely generated O_K-algebra B with B/πB ≅ A_0 (Elkik); (b) B† is a lift of A_0 (weakly complete finitely generated, flat over O_K, B†/πB† ≅ A_0) and B† ⊗ K is a dagger algebra of MW-type with reduction A_0; (c) any two lifts of A_0 are isomorphic as O_K-algebras (non-canonically). (That two dagger algebras of MW-type with isomorphic reductions are isomorphic additionally needs that their integral subrings are lifts: F1.73.) *Hypotheses:* O_K a complete DVR of mixed characteristic (0, p). A_0 smooth and finitely generated over k. *Source:* vdP §2, p. 35. *Needs:* F1.27, F1.23, F1.25, F1.26.

### Affinoid dagger spaces

**F1.29 Affinoid subdomains of the maximal spectrum of a dagger algebra** (definition `Dagger.IsAffinoidSubdomain`). Let A be a K-dagger algebra (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)) and X = Sp(A) its set of maximal ideals. A subset U ⊆ X is an affinoid subdomain if there are a dagger algebra B = O_X(U) and a morphism π : A → B with Sp(π)(Sp B) ⊆ U such that for every dagger algebra D the map Hom_K(B, D) → {f ∈ Hom_K(A, D) : Sp(f)(Sp D) ⊆ U}, g ↦ g ∘ π, is bijective. Then B is unique up to unique isomorphism and Sp(π) : Sp(B) → U is a bijection.
