Rational subdomains R(f/g) with O_X(R(f/g)) = A⟨f/g⟩† (F1.20) and Weierstrass domains (g = 1) are affinoid subdomains; the notion is transitive and stable under base change A → D. *API* (on `Dagger`): `IsAffinoidSubdomain, IsAffinoidSubdomain.algebra, IsAffinoidSubdomain.lift, IsAffinoidSubdomain.maximalSpectrum_equiv, isAffinoidSubdomain_rational, IsAffinoidSubdomain.trans, IsAffinoidSubdomain.inter, IsAffinoidSubdomain.preimage, IsAffinoidSubdomain.completion, IsAffinoidSubdomain.flat`. *Tests:* `IsAffinoidSubdomain_test_weierstrass` (computation): U = {|X| ≤ |π|} ⊆ Sp(W_1) is an affinoid subdomain with O(U) = W_1⟨X/π⟩† ≅ K⟨Y⟩† (Y = X/π). `IsAffinoidSubdomain_test_whole` (degenerate): U = Sp(A) is an affinoid subdomain with O(U) = A and π = id; U = ∅ with O(U) = 0. `IsAffinoidSubdomain_test_open_disc` (non-example): The open disc {x : |X(x)| < 1} ⊆ Sp(W_1) is not an affinoid subdomain: a representing B would have |X|_sup < 1 on Sp(B), hence |X|_sup ≤ |ε| < 1 for some ε by the maximum modulus principle, so U would be contained in a disc of radius < 1. `IsAffinoidSubdomain_test_rational` (compatibility): For f, g ∈ A, the affinoid subdomain R(f/g) ⊆ Sp(A) is the set of classical points of the Tau Ceti rational subset TauCeti.ValuationSpectrum.rationalSubset Â° {f} g of Spa(Â, Â°). `IsAffinoidSubdomain_test_points` (characterisation): U ⊆ Sp(A) is an affinoid subdomain with algebra B iff A → B is a morphism inducing a bijection Sp(B) ≅ U that is universal for D = A/m^k, all maximal m and k ≥ 1 (the dagger analogue of BGR 7.2.2/1). *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.1, p. 9. *Needs:* F1.20, F1.19, F1.7, F1.6.

- **F1.30** Affinoid subdomains, stalks, open immersions and Runge immersions agree with their completions. *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.2.

**F1.31 Tate acyclicity for dagger algebras** (theorem). Let A be a K-dagger algebra (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)), X = Sp(A) and M an A-module. Every finite covering X = U_1 ∪ … ∪ U_n by affinoid subdomains is acyclic for the presheaf U ↦ O_X(U) ⊗_A M: the augmented Čech complex 0 → M → ∏_i O_X(U_i) ⊗_A M → ∏_{i<j} O_X(U_i ∩ U_j) ⊗_A M → … is exact. *Hypotheses:* A a K-dagger algebra. a finite covering of Sp(A) by affinoid subdomains. M any A-module. *Source:* GK00 §2, Prop. 2.6, p. 11. *Needs:* F1.29, F1.30, F1.20, F1.3.

- **F1.32** The G-topology of an affinoid dagger space is that of its completion; affinoid subdomains form a basis. *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.7.

### Dagger spaces, coherent modules and strict neighbourhoods

**F1.33 Dagger spaces (rigid spaces with overconvergent structure sheaf)** (definition `DaggerSpace`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). Rigid spaces are, throughout, the quasi-separated Tate rigid-analytic spaces over K of R1.7 (G-topological spaces locally isomorphic to Sp(B) for K-affinoid algebras B), compared with adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, O_K) by R1.31, under which admissible opens and admissible coverings correspond to opens and open coverings. A K-dagger space is a pair (X, O†_X) where X is a rigid space over K and O†_X ⊆ O_X is a subsheaf of K-algebras for the G-topology of X such that X has an admissible covering by affinoids U = Sp(Â_U) together with dagger algebras A_U with completion Â_U (a dagger structure on U) for which O†_X(V) = A_U⟨f/g⟩† for every rational subdomain V = R(f/g) ⊆ U with f, g ∈ A_U. A morphism (X, O†_X) → (Y, O†_Y) is a morphism of rigid spaces φ : X → Y over K with φ^♯(O†_Y) ⊆ φ_*O†_X. The affinoid dagger space of a dagger algebra A is Sp(A) := (Sp(Â), O†_A) with O†_A(R(f/g)) = A⟨f/g⟩†. This is Grosse-Klönne's definition 2.12 (locally G-ringed spaces with saturated G-topology admitting an admissible covering by affinoid dagger spaces, F1.32) written, as in Vezzani 2.22, as a rigid space with a subsheaf; through R1.31 it is equivalently a quasi-separated adic space locally of finite type over Spa(K, O_K) with a subsheaf O† ⊆ O that is locally of the form R(f/g) ↦ A⟨f/g⟩† on rational subsets of Spa(Â, Â°). Dagger spaces are assumed quasi-separated. *API*: `DaggerSpace, DaggerSpace.Hom, DaggerSpace.Sp, DaggerSpace.Sp_fullyFaithful, DaggerSpace.sections_rational, DaggerSpace.toRigid, DaggerSpace.restrict, DaggerSpace.fibreProduct, DaggerSpace.baseChange, DaggerSpace.equivAdic, DaggerSpace.disc, DaggerSpace.stalk_iso`. *Tests:* `DaggerSpace_test_disc` (computation): Γ(D, O†) = K⟨X⟩† for the closed dagger disc D = Sp(K⟨X⟩†), while Γ(D', O) = K⟨X⟩ for the underlying rigid disc. `DaggerSpace_test_point` (degenerate): Sp(K) is a point with O† = O = K; Sp(0) is empty. `DaggerSpace_test_not_full_sheaf` (non-example): O_X itself is not a dagger structure on the closed unit disc X = Spa(K⟨X⟩): that would need a dagger algebra A with A → Â bijective and dim A = 1, but Noether normalisation W_1 ↪ A finite would then force W_1 → T_1 to be bijective, which is false. So 'dagger space = rigid space' is a wrong definition (and it would also change de Rham cohomology, F1.48). `DaggerSpace_test_underlying` (compatibility): The underlying rigid space of Sp(A) is the affinoid rigid space Sp(Â) of R1.7, whose adic space is Spa(Â, Â°) (anchor Layer 5). `DaggerSpace_test_morphisms` (characterisation): Morphisms Sp(W_1) → Sp(W_1) over K correspond to power-bounded elements of W_1, i.e. to W_1^int = O_K⟨X⟩† (Vezzani Proposition 2.14). *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.12, p. 12. *Needs:* F1.20, F1.31, F1.32, F1.9, F1.8, F1.19, R1.7, R1.31, R0.42.

**F1.34 Coherent modules on dagger spaces** (definition `DaggerSpace.tilde`). Let X be a K-dagger space. For an affinoid X = Sp(A) and an A-module M, M ⊗ O_X denotes the sheaf extending U ↦ M ⊗_A O†_X(U) on affinoid subdomains (a sheaf by F1.31). An O†_X-module F is coherent if there are an admissible (open) affinoid covering X = ⋃ U_i, U_i = Sp(A_i), finite A_i-modules M_i and isomorphisms F|_{U_i} ≅ M_i ⊗ O_{U_i}. Coherent ideals, closed immersions of dagger spaces (defined by coherent ideals), separated and finite morphisms are defined as for rigid spaces. *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `tilde, IsCoherent, IsCoherent.tilde, IsCoherent.kernel, IsCoherent.pullback, coherentIdeal_closedImmersion, IsCoherent.toRigid, IsLocallyFree`. *Tests:* `IsCoherent_test_skyscraper` (computation): On X = Sp(W_1), (W_1/(X)) ⊗ O_X has stalk K at the origin and 0 elsewhere. `IsCoherent_test_zero` (degenerate): The zero module and O_X are coherent. `IsCoherent_test_not_coherent` (non-example): For the inclusion j of the open unit disc D^0 into the closed dagger disc D, j_*O_{D^0} is not coherent: its global sections (functions on the open disc) are not a finite W_1-module. `IsCoherent_test_rigid` (compatibility): For M finite over A, (M ⊗ O_{Sp A})' = (M ⊗_A Â) ⊗ O_{Spa Â}, the coherent sheaf of R3.15 attached to the finite Â-module M ⊗_A Â. *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.14, p. 12. *Needs:* F1.33, F1.31, F1.32, F1.29, R3.15.

**F1.35 Coherent modules on affinoid dagger spaces come from finite modules (dagger Kiehl theorem)** (theorem). Let X = Sp(A) be an affinoid K-dagger space and F a coherent O_X-module. Then there are a finite A-module M and an isomorphism F ≅ M ⊗ O_X; the functor M ↦ M ⊗ O_X is an equivalence between finite A-modules and coherent O_X-modules, with quasi-inverse F ↦ Γ(X, F). *Hypotheses:* X = Sp(A) affinoid. F coherent. *Source:* GK00 §2, Thm 2.16, p. 13. *Needs:* F1.34, F1.18, F1.30, F1.31, R3.16.

**F1.36 Theorems A and B for affinoid dagger spaces** (theorem). Let X = Sp(A) be an affinoid K-dagger space and F a coherent O_X-module. Then F is generated by its global sections and H^n(X, F) = 0 for all n ≥ 1. *Hypotheses:* X = Sp(A) affinoid. F coherent. *Source:* GK00 §3, Prop. 3.1, p. 17. *Needs:* F1.35, F1.31, F1.32.

**F1.37 Strict neighbourhoods of affinoids and the cofinal fringe system** (definition `DaggerSpace.IsStrictNeighbourhood`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). Rigid spaces are, throughout, the quasi-separated Tate rigid-analytic spaces over K of R1.7 (G-topological spaces locally isomorphic to Sp(B) for K-affinoid algebras B), compared with adic spaces locally of finite type over Spa(K, O_K) by R1.31, under which admissible opens and admissible coverings correspond to opens and open coverings. Let W be a rigid space and U ⊆ W an admissible open for which the complement W − U is admissible open (e.g. U an affinoid subdomain or a finite union of such). An admissible open V ⊆ W is a strict neighbourhood of U in W if {W − U, V} is an admissible covering of W; equivalently, in the adic space of W (R1.31), V contains the closure Ū of U (Vezzani's U ⋐_W V). For a fringe presentation, the affinoid Sp(T_n/IT_n) is written Sp(C) ⊂† Sp(D) inside D = T_n(ρ_0)/I (Grosse-Klönne 2.21), and for dagger spaces Sp(A) ⊂† Sp(B) means Sp(Â) ⊂† Sp(B̂). Cofinality: given ρ_0 ∈ Γ*, ρ_0 > 1, I ⊆ T_n(ρ_0) and an open immersion Sp(T_n(ρ_0)/I) → W, the affinoids V_ρ = Sp(T_n(ρ)/IT_n(ρ)), 1 < ρ ≤ ρ_0, form a cofinal system of strict neighbourhoods of V_1 = Sp(T_n/IT_n) in W. (Strict neighbourhoods of tubes in frames are PadicDifferentialEquationsAndRigidCohomology RD.3's notion, not this one.) *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `IsStrictNeighbourhood, isStrictNeighbourhood_iff_closure, IsStrictNeighbourhood.inter, IsStrictNeighbourhood.mono, IsStrictNeighbourhood.trans, IsStrictNeighbourhood.preimage, isStrictNeighbourhood_fringe, IsStrictNeighbourhood.sections, daggerIncl`. *Tests:* `IsStrictNeighbourhood_test_discs` (computation): In W = {|X| ≤ ρ_0}, the disc {|X| ≤ ρ} (1 < ρ ≤ ρ_0) is a strict neighbourhood of the closed unit disc U. `IsStrictNeighbourhood_test_whole` (degenerate): W is a strict neighbourhood of every quasi-compact open U ⊆ W; if U is also closed (a union of connected components), U is a strict neighbourhood of itself. `IsStrictNeighbourhood_test_self` (non-example): The closed unit disc U is not a strict neighbourhood of itself in {|X| ≤ ρ_0}, ρ_0 > 1: the closure of U contains the rank-two point with |X| = 1^+, and {W − U, U} is not an admissible covering; likewise the open disc {|X| < 1} is not a strict neighbourhood of the closed disc. `IsStrictNeighbourhood_test_closure` (compatibility): V is a strict neighbourhood of U iff U ⋐_W V in Vezzani's notation (the closure of U lies in V). `IsStrictNeighbourhood_test_cofinal` (characterisation): For W = {|X| ≤ ρ_0}, every strict neighbourhood of the closed unit disc contains {|X| ≤ ρ} for some ρ > 1. *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.22, p. 15. *Needs:* F1.15, F1.33, R1.7, R1.31.

- **F1.38** Overconvergent sections are sections extending to a strict neighbourhood. *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.23.

**F1.39 Relative compactness, partially proper and proper morphisms, and Stein spaces for dagger spaces** (definition `DaggerSpace.RelCompact`). Let f : X = Sp(A) → Y = Sp(B) be a morphism of affinoid K-dagger spaces and U ⊆ X an affinoid subdomain. Write U ⊂⊂_Y X if there are a surjection τ : B⟨X_1..X_r⟩† → A and ε ∈ Γ*, ε < 1, with U ⊆ Sp(A⟨ε^{-1}τ(X_1), …, ε^{-1}τ(X_r)⟩†). A morphism f : X → Y of dagger spaces is partially proper if it is separated and there are admissible affinoid coverings Y = ⋃ Y_i and f^{-1}(Y_i) = ⋃ X_{ij} such that each X_{ij} satisfies X_{ij} ⊂⊂_{Y_i} X̃_{ij} for some admissible open affinoid X̃_{ij} ⊆ f^{-1}(Y_i); f is proper if it is quasi-compact and partially proper. A dagger space X is a Stein space if it has an admissible affinoid covering X = ⋃_{i∈ℕ} U_i with U_i ⊂† U_{i+1} for all i (a Stein covering). The same definitions apply to rigid spaces, where they agree with Huber's partial properness (R0.65). *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `RelCompact, IsPartiallyProper, IsProper, IsStein, isPartiallyProper_iff_toRigid, IsPartiallyProper.comp, IsStein.isPartiallyProper, IsStein.interior`. *Tests:* `IsStein_test_open_disc` (computation): The open unit disc D^0 = ⋃_{ε ∈ Γ*, ε<1} Sp(K⟨ε^{-1}X⟩†) is a Stein space: for radii ε_n ↑ 1 in Γ*, the dagger discs U_n = Sp(K⟨ε_n^{-1}X⟩†) satisfy U_n ⊂† U_{n+1}. `IsPartiallyProper_test_point` (degenerate): The identity of any dagger space and Sp(K) → Sp(K) are proper. `IsPartiallyProper_test_closed_disc` (non-example): The closed dagger disc D = Sp(K⟨X⟩†) is not partially proper over Sp(K): D ⊂⊂ D fails (no ε < 1 with D ⊆ {|X| ≤ ε}); correspondingly the rigid closed disc is not partially proper (Vezzani A.2). `IsPartiallyProper_test_analytification` (compatibility): The dagger analytification of a finite type K-scheme is partially proper, and its underlying rigid space is partially proper in Huber's sense. *Source:* GK00 §2, 2.24, p. 15. *Needs:* F1.33, F1.29, F1.37, F1.9, F1.34, R0.65, R0.61, R1.7, R1.31.

### The dagger–rigid comparison

**F1.40 The comparison functor from dagger spaces to rigid spaces** (construction `DaggerSpace.toRigidFunctor`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). The functor (·)' : (X, O†_X) ↦ X from K-dagger spaces to the quasi-separated rigid-analytic spaces of R1.7, with the natural inclusion ν : O†_X ⊆ O_{X'} of structure sheaves, is faithful and satisfies: (1) Sp(A)' = Sp(Â) for every dagger algebra A (whose adic space is Spa(Â, Â°)); (2) X is connected (resp. normal, reduced, regular) iff X' is; (3) X and X' have the same opens and coverings (the same underlying G-topological space) and ν is an isomorphism on local rings at classical points; (4) a morphism ρ : X → Y is a closed immersion (resp. open immersion, locally closed immersion, isomorphism, quasi-compact, separated) iff ρ' is; (5) if ρ is finite, so is ρ'; (6) if Y is a quasialgebraic rigid space (admissibly covered by opens of analytifications of affine finite type K-schemes; e.g. Y smooth), there are an admissible affinoid covering Y = ⋃ V_i and affinoid dagger spaces U_i with U_i' ≅ V_i, and U_i is unique up to (non-canonical) isomorphism. On coherent modules, F ↦ F' (with F' = O_{X'} ⊗ (F(X) ⊗_A Â) on X = Sp(A), glued) is a faithful exact functor coh(O†_X) → coh(O_{X'}) with a natural injection of underlying abelian sheaves F → F'. *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `toRigidFunctor, toRigidFunctor.faithful, toRigid_Sp, toRigid.structureInclusion, toRigid_isClosedImmersion_iff, toRigid_isConnected_iff, toRigid_isFinite, exists_daggerModel_of_quasialgebraic, coherentToRigid, toRigid_rigidToAdic_Sp`. *Tests:* `toRigid_test_disc` (computation): For the closed dagger disc D = Sp(K⟨X⟩†), D' is the rigid closed disc Sp(K⟨X⟩) and Γ(D, O†) = K⟨X⟩† ⊊ K⟨X⟩ = Γ(D', O). `toRigid_test_not_full` (non-example): (·)' is not full on affinoids: for a ∈ K⟨X⟩ ∖ K⟨X⟩† with |a| < 1, X ↦ X + a is an automorphism of D' that does not preserve O† (it sends X to an element of T_1 ∖ W_1); a definition making (·)' an equivalence on affinoid dagger spaces fails this test. `toRigid_test_point` (degenerate): (Sp K)' = Sp K and ν : O† → O is the identity on the one-point space. `toRigid_test_rigidToAdic` (compatibility): For A = K⟨X⟩†, r_K((Sp A)') = Spa(K⟨X⟩, K°⟨X⟩), the adic closed unit disc of R1.31. `toRigid_test_stalk` (characterisation): For a classical point x of D, ν_x : O†_{D,x} → O_{D',x} is an isomorphism although Γ(D, O†) → Γ(D', O) is not surjective. *Source:* GK00 §2, Thm 2.19, p. 14. *Needs:* F1.33, F1.34, F1.9, F1.12, F1.14, F1.30, F1.13, F1.20, R1.7, R1.31, R1.9, R3.15, R3.16, R1.4.

**F1.41 On partially proper dagger spaces coherent modules are those of the associated rigid space** (theorem). Let X be a partially proper K-dagger space. Then the functor (·)' : coh(O†_X) → coh(O_{X'}) of F1.40 is an equivalence of categories, and Γ(X, M) = Γ(X', M') for every coherent O†_X-module M. *Hypotheses:* X partially proper (F1.39), quasi-separated. *Source:* GK00 §2, Thm 2.26, p. 15. *Needs:* F1.40, F1.38, F1.39, F1.35, F1.37, R3.15.

**F1.42 Partially proper dagger spaces are equivalent to partially proper rigid spaces** (theorem). The functor (·)' of F1.40 restricts to an equivalence between the category of partially proper K-dagger spaces and the category of partially proper rigid spaces over K. In particular every partially proper rigid space (e.g. every rigid space without boundary, every Stein space, the analytification of every finite type K-scheme) carries a dagger structure, unique up to unique isomorphism. *Hypotheses:* partial properness over Sp(K) (F1.39). *Source:* GK00 §2, Thm 2.27, p. 16. *Needs:* F1.41, F1.39, F1.40, F1.38, R0.65, R1.7.

**F1.43 Coherent cohomology of partially proper dagger spaces equals that of the associated rigid space** (theorem). Let X be a partially proper K-dagger space and F a coherent O†_X-module. Then H^i(X, F) = H^i(X', F') for all i ≥ 0. If X is a Stein space, these groups vanish for i > 0. *Hypotheses:* X partially proper, quasi-separated. F coherent. *Source:* GK00 §3, Thm 3.2, p. 17. *Needs:* F1.41, F1.36, F1.38, F1.39, R3.23.

**F1.44 Dagger analytification of schemes of finite type** (construction `DaggerSpace.analytification`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K) and X a K-scheme of finite type. Its dagger analytification X^† is the partially proper K-dagger space with (X^†)' = X^an, the rigid analytification (R1.9), which exists and is unique up to unique isomorphism by F1.42 because X^an is partially proper. X ↦ X^† is a functor, compatible with open and closed immersions and fibre products; for X affine with presentation K[X_1..X_n]/(f), X^† = ⋃_{ρ ∈ Γ*} Sp(K⟨ρ^{-1}X⟩†/(f)). For X proper, coherent cohomology satisfies GAGA: H^i(X, F) ≅ H^i(X^†, F^†) for coherent F. *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `analytification, analytification_toRigid, analytification.map, analytification.isPartiallyProper, analytification_affineSpace, analytification.fibreProduct, analytification.openImmersion, analytification.gaga`. *Tests:* `analytification_test_line` (computation): Γ((A^1)^†, O†) is the ring of series Σ a_m X^m with |a_m|ρ^m → 0 for all ρ > 0 (entire functions). `analytification_test_point` (degenerate): (Spec K)^† = Sp(K) and (Spec L)^† = Sp(L) for a finite extension L/K. `analytification_test_not_affinoid` (non-example): (A^1)^† is not an affinoid dagger space (it is not quasi-compact), although A^1 is affine. `analytification_test_rigid` (compatibility): The underlying rigid space of (A^1)^† is the rigid analytic affine line A^{1,an} of R1.9. `analytification_test_gaga` (characterisation): H^1((P^1)^†, O(−2)) is one-dimensional, as for the scheme P^1 (GAGA). *Source:* GK00 §3, 3.3, p. 18. *Needs:* F1.42, F1.43, F1.39, R1.9, R1.7, R3.55.

- **F1.45** Identity principle on connected smooth dagger spaces. *Source:* Conrad-IC §2.1, Lem. 2.1.4.

### Differentials and the overconvergent de Rham complex

**F1.46 The module of (universally finite) differentials Ω^1_A of a dagger algebra and the exterior derivative** (construction `Dagger.Omega`). Let A be a K-dagger algebra (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)). The module of differentials of A is a finite A-module Ω^1_A = Ω^1_{A/K} with a K-derivation d : A → Ω^1_A that is universal among K-derivations of A into finite A-modules: every K-derivation D : A → M, M finite, is u ∘ d for a unique A-linear u. Explicitly, for a presentation A = W_n/(f_1, …, f_r): Ω^1_A = (⊕_i A dX_i) / (Σ_j (∂f_k/∂X_j) dX_j)_k with d g = Σ_i (∂g/∂X_i) dX_i. Equivalently Ω^1_A = J/J^2 for J the kernel of the multiplication A ⊗†_K A → A (Kedlaya's module of continuous differentials). Put Ω^i_A = ⋀^i_A Ω^1_A with d extended by d(a db_1 ∧ … ∧ db_i) = da ∧ db_1 ∧ … ∧ db_i. These sheafify to coherent O†_X-modules Ω^i_X on every dagger space X, with (Ω^i_X)' = Ω^i_{X'}. For a finitely generated algebra S over a complete DVR O_K the same construction gives Ω^1_{S†/O_K} (van der Put's D^1), with Ω^1_{S†/O_K} ⊗ K = Ω^1_{S†⊗K}. *API* (on `Dagger`): `Omega, Omega.d, Omega.lift, Omega.lift_comp_d, Omega.hom_ext, Omega.finite, Omega.washnitzerBasis, Omega.quotient, Omega.projective_of_isRegularRing, Omega.ofKaehler, Omega.completion, Omega.map, Omega.rationalLocalization, Omega.relTate, Omega.dComp`. *Tests:* `Omega_test_annulus` (computation): For A = K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩†, Ω^1_A is free of rank 1 on dX and d(X^m) = m X^{m−1} dX for all m ∈ ℤ. `Omega_test_field` (degenerate): Ω^1_K = 0, and Ω^1_L = 0 for a finite separable extension L/K. `Omega_test_not_kaehler` (non-example): If char K = 0 with residue characteristic p, the series exp(λ_1X), …, exp(λ_NX) with |λ_i| < |p|^{1/(p−1)} and λ_i linearly independent over ℚ lie in W_1 and are algebraically independent over K(X), so Mathlib's KaehlerDifferential Ω[W_1⁄K] has rank ≥ N + 1 over Frac(W_1) for every N and is not finite; the correct Ω^1_{W_1} is W_1 dX. `Omega_test_completion` (compatibility): Ω^1_{W_n} ⊗_{W_n} T_n ≅ ⊕ T_n dX_i, the continuous differentials of the Tate algebra. `Omega_test_derivation` (characterisation): For A = W_2/(Y^2 − X^3 − 1), Ω^1_A = (A dX ⊕ A dY)/(2Y dY − 3X^2 dX), projective of rank 1 because 2Y and 3X^2 generate the unit ideal of A (char K ≠ 2, 3). *Source:* GK00 §4, 4.1, p. 19. *Needs:* F1.2, F1.4, F1.6, F1.19, F1.20, F1.34, F1.23, R0.93, R3.36.

**F1.47 Smooth dagger spaces** (definition `DaggerSpace.IsSmooth`). A K-dagger space X (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)) is smooth if its associated rigid space X' (R1.7) is smooth over K, i.e. its adic space (R1.31) is smooth over Spa(K, O_K) in the sense of R0.99. Equivalently: for every affinoid Sp(A) ⊆ X, A is regular and Ω^1_A is projective of rank equal to the local dimension; equivalently X is locally étale over a dagger polydisc. X has pure dimension n if dim O_{X,x} = n at every point; then ω_X := Ω^n_X. *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `IsSmooth, isSmooth_iff_affinoid, IsSmooth.locallyEtale, IsSmooth.omega_locallyFree, IsSmooth.open, IsSmooth.analytification, IsSmooth.weakCompletion, pureDimension`. *Tests:* `IsSmooth_test_polydisc` (computation): The dagger polydisc Sp(W_n) is smooth of pure dimension n, with Ω^1 free on dX_1..dX_n. `IsSmooth_test_point` (degenerate): Sp(K) is smooth of dimension 0; Sp(L) is smooth for L/K finite separable. `IsSmooth_test_node` (non-example): Sp(K⟨X, Y⟩†/(XY)) is reduced but not smooth (Ω^1 has rank 2 at the origin); Sp(W_1/(X^2)) is not smooth (not reduced). `IsSmooth_test_rigid` (compatibility): X is smooth iff X' is smooth over K in the sense of R0.99. *Source:* GK00 §4, 4.1, p. 19. *Needs:* F1.33, F1.46, F1.9, F1.40, F1.13, R0.99, R0.117, R1.31.

**F1.48 The overconvergent de Rham complex of a smooth dagger space, with coefficients in a module with integrable connection** (construction `DaggerSpace.deRhamComplex`). Let X be a smooth K-dagger space (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)). The de Rham complex Ω^•_X = (Ω^0_X → Ω^1_X → Ω^2_X → …) is the complex of coherent locally free O†_X-modules Ω^i_X = ⋀^i Ω^1_X with the K-linear exterior derivative d (F1.46). More generally, for a coherent O†_X-module E with an integrable connection ∇ : E → E ⊗ Ω^1_X (K-linear, ∇(fe) = f∇e + e ⊗ df, ∇_1 ∘ ∇ = 0), DR(E) = E ⊗ Ω^•_X with differentials ∇_i. The de Rham cohomology is the hypercohomology H^i_dR(X, E) := H^i(X, DR(E)) on the site of X, and H^i_dR(X) := H^i_dR(X, O†_X). For X = Sp(A) affinoid, H^i_dR(X, E) = H^i(Γ(X, E) ⊗_A Ω^•_A). For X' the associated rigid space, (Ω^i_X)' = Ω^i_{X'} and DR(E)' = DR(E'); but in general H^i_dR(X) ≠ H^i_dR(X') (see the tests). *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `deRhamComplex, IntegrableConnection, IntegrableConnection.deRhamComplex, deRham, deRham_affinoid, deRhamComplex.d_comp_d, deRham.pullback, deRham.toRigid, deRham_zero, IntegrableConnection.tensor`. *Tests:* `deRham_test_disc` (computation): For the closed dagger disc D = Sp(K⟨X⟩†) (char K = 0): H^0_dR(D) = K and H^1_dR(D) = 0, since every f dX with f ∈ K⟨X⟩† has the overconvergent primitive Σ a_m X^{m+1}/(m+1) (F1.49). `deRham_test_point` (degenerate): H^0_dR(Sp K) = K and H^i_dR(Sp K) = 0 for i > 0. `deRham_test_rigid_disc` (non-example): For the rigid closed disc D' = Spa(K⟨X⟩) with K ⊇ ℚ_p, the form ω = Σ_n p^n X^{p^n − 1} dX is not exact (its formal primitive Σ X^{p^n} is not in K⟨X⟩), and H^1_dR(D') is infinite-dimensional; D and D' have the same points, so de Rham cohomology is not determined by the underlying point set. A definition H_dR(X) := H_dR(X') fails. `deRham_test_annulus` (computation): For the dagger annulus A = Sp(K⟨X, X^{-1}⟩†) with char K = 0: H^1_dR(A) = K·[dX/X]. `deRham_test_mw` (compatibility): For S a smooth finitely generated O_K-algebra (O_K a complete DVR of mixed characteristic), H^i_dR(Sp(S† ⊗ K)) = H^i(Ω^•_{S†⊗K}), the Monsky–Washnitzer cohomology of S/πS (Kedlaya, van der Put). *Source:* GK00 §4, 4.1, p. 19. *Needs:* F1.46, F1.47, F1.34, F1.36, F1.40, F1.33.

- **F1.49** De Rham cohomology of dagger polyannuli and the relative Poincaré lemma. *Source:* GK02 §2, Lem. 2.1 (a).

**F1.50 De Rham cohomology of a smooth partially proper dagger space equals that of its rigid space (and fails to for affinoids)** (theorem). Let X be a smooth partially proper K-dagger space. Then the natural map H^i_dR(X) → H^i_dR(X') is an isomorphism for all i. The partial properness hypothesis cannot be dropped: for the closed unit disc, H^1_dR(D) = 0 (char K = 0) while H^1_dR(D') is infinite-dimensional, although D and D' have the same underlying points and G-topology. *Hypotheses:* X smooth and partially proper. for the non-example: char K = 0, residue characteristic p. *Source:* GK00 §3, 3.3, p. 18. *Needs:* F1.48, F1.43, F1.49, F1.39, F1.47.

**F1.51 De Rham cohomology of a smooth dagger space depends only on its rigid space** (theorem). Let K be a field of characteristic 0 complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value. Let X_1, X_2 be smooth K-dagger spaces and φ : X_1' → X_2' an isomorphism of the associated rigid spaces. Then φ induces an isomorphism φ† : H^*_dR(X_2) → H^*_dR(X_1). (Grosse-Klönne states without proof in the source that φ ↦ φ† is compatible with composition; that compatibility is not part of this target.) *Hypotheses:* char K = 0. X_1, X_2 smooth. φ an isomorphism of rigid spaces (not assumed to preserve O†). *Source:* GK02 §1, Lem. 1.18, p. 14. *Needs:* F1.49, F1.13, F1.48, F1.19, F1.40, F1.12, F1.37.

- **F1.52** Strictness and closed image of de Rham differentials on smooth affinoid and Stein dagger spaces. *Source:* GK00 §4, Lem. 4.7.

### Supports, compact support and duality

**F1.53 De Rham cohomology with supports in the complement of an admissible open** (construction `DaggerSpace.deRhamSupport`). Let X be a smooth K-dagger (or rigid) space and j : U → X the inclusion of an admissible open with complement Y = X − U (a closed subset, not given a space structure). For a sheaf F on X let Γ_Y(F) = ker(F → j_*j^{-1}F) and RΓ_Y its right derived functor; for a complex such as Ω^•_X define H^i_Y(X, Ω^•_X) := H^i(X, RΓ_Y Ω^•_X) (and likewise H^i_Y(X, F) for coherent F). There is a distinguished triangle RΓ_Y(X, Ω^•) → RΓ(X, Ω^•) → RΓ(U, Ω^•) →, a Mayer–Vietoris triangle RΓ_{Y∩Y'} → RΓ_Y ⊕ RΓ_{Y'} → RΓ_{Y∪Y'} → when U ∪ U' is an admissible covering of an admissible open, and for Z ⊆ Y closed a triangle RΓ_Z → RΓ_Y → Rj'_*RΓ_{Y−Z}j'^{-1} → with j' : X − Z → X. *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `deRhamSupport, deRhamSupport.triangle, deRhamSupport.excision, deRhamSupport.mayerVietoris, deRhamSupport.nested, deRhamSupport.pullback, deRhamSupport_empty`. *Tests:* `deRhamSupport_test_disc` (computation): For char K = 0, X_ρ = {|x| ≤ ρ} and Y the closed unit disc: H^0_Y = H^1_Y = 0 and H^2_Y ≅ H^1_dR({1 < |x| ≤ ρ}) = K·dx/x (from the triangle, H^*_dR(X_ρ) = K in degree 0, and F1.49 for the half-open annulus). `deRhamSupport_test_empty` (degenerate): H^i_∅(X, Ω^•) = 0 and H^i_X(X, Ω^•) = H^i_dR(X). `deRhamSupport_test_not_cohomology_of_Y` (non-example): H^i_Y(X) is not the de Rham cohomology of Y: for char K = 0 and Y the closed unit disc in X_ρ, H^0_dR(Y) = K but H^0_Y(X_ρ) = 0. `deRhamSupport_test_coherent` (compatibility): For coherent F, H^i_Y(X, F) is the local cohomology used by Grosse-Klönne 4.3 for H^i_c(X, F), and for i = 0 it is the space of sections of F supported in Y. *Source:* GK02 §1, 1.15, p. 11. *Needs:* F1.48, F1.33, F1.47.

- **F1.54** Local cohomology of the de Rham complex along closed subspaces: algebraic versus topological supports, Gysin isomorphism and independence of the embedding. *Source:* GK02 §1, Prop. 1.16 (a).

**F1.55 Compactly supported coherent and de Rham cohomology of affinoid and Stein dagger spaces** (construction `DaggerSpace.compactSupport`). Let K be a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K). Let X = Sp(A) be an affinoid K-dagger space with a fringe presentation A = W_m/IW_m, I ⊆ T_m(ρ_0), fringe stages X_ρ = Sp(T_m(ρ)/IT_m(ρ)), so X' ⊂† X_ρ. For a coherent O†_X-module F choose (after shrinking ρ_0) a coherent O_{X_{ρ_0}}-module F_{ρ_0} with F(X) = colim_ρ F_{ρ_0}(X_ρ) (F1.18) and put H^i_c(X, F) := H^i_{X}(X_{ρ_0}, F_{ρ_0}), the cohomology of F_{ρ_0} with supports in the complement of the admissible open X_{ρ_0} − X' (F1.53). For X smooth, H^i_{dR,c}(X) := H^i_{X}(X_ρ, Ω^•_{X_ρ}). Both are independent of ρ and of the fringe presentation. For a smooth Stein dagger space X with Stein covering (U_j)_{j∈ℕ}, H^i_c(X, F) := colim_j H^i_{U_j}(U_{j+1}, F) and H^i_{dR,c}(X) := colim_j H^i_{U_j}(U_{j+1}, Ω^•), along the extension-by-zero maps. H^i_c(X, F) carries the finest locally convex topology making the boundary maps H^{i−1}(X_{ρ_0} − X', F_{ρ_0}) → H^i_c(X, F) continuous, and a topological A-module structure. *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `compactSupport, compactSupportDeRham, compactSupport_independent, compactSupport.longExact, compactSupport.extensionByZero, compactSupport.vanishing, compactSupport.module, compactSupport_top_polydisc`. *Tests:* `compactSupport_test_disc` (computation): For char K = 0 and the closed dagger disc D: H^0_{dR,c}(D) = H^1_{dR,c}(D) = 0 and H^2_{dR,c}(D) = K (from the support triangle on X_ρ = {|x| ≤ ρ}, H^*_dR(X_ρ) = K in degree 0, and H^1_dR({1 < |x| ≤ ρ}) = K·dx/x). `compactSupport_test_point` (degenerate): For X = Sp(K): H^0_{dR,c}(X) = K and all other groups vanish. `compactSupport_test_not_ordinary` (non-example): H^i_{dR,c} is not H^i_dR: for char K = 0 and the closed dagger disc, H^0_{dR,c}(D) = 0 ≠ K = H^0_dR(D); nor is it the cohomology of D with supports in D inside D itself (that is H_dR(D)). `compactSupport_test_rd4` (compatibility): For X = Sp(T_n)-type affinoids inside V_ρ = Sp(T_{n,ρ}), H^i_c(X, F) is the cohomology of the complex [F → ι_*ι^*F] of PadicDifferentialEquationsAndRigidCohomology RD.4/compact-support-complex-of-affine-space, concentrated in degree n. `compactSupport_test_serre` (characterisation): For K spherically complete (Grosse-Klönne's standing hypothesis from 4.2 on) and A = K⟨T⟩†, the residue pairing A × H^1_c(X, ω) → K, (Σ b_α T^α, Σ a_μ T^μ dT) ↦ Σ_{α≥0} b_α a_{−α−1}, is perfect for the topologies of 4.2/4.3 (Grosse-Klönne 4.4). *Source:* GK00 §4, 4.3, p. 19. *Needs:* F1.53, F1.15, F1.18, F1.16, F1.37, F1.48, F1.34, F1.39.

**F1.56 Serre duality for smooth affinoid dagger spaces** (theorem). Let K be spherically complete (complete, nonarchimedean; e.g. discretely valued). Let X be a smooth affinoid K-dagger space of pure dimension d and F a coherent O†_X-module; give Ext^i_{O_X}(F, ω_X) the fringe topology and H^{d−i}_c(X, F) the topology of F1.55. Then for 0 ≤ i ≤ d there is a pairing Ext^i_{O_X}(F, ω_X) × H^{d−i}_c(X, F) → K, functorial in F, inducing topological isomorphisms Ext^i_{O_X}(F, ω_X) ≅ Hom_{K,cont}(H^{d−i}_c(X, F), K) and H^{d−i}_c(X, F) ≅ Hom_{K,cont}(Ext^i_{O_X}(F, ω_X), K). *Hypotheses:* K spherically complete. X smooth affinoid of pure dimension d. F coherent. *Source:* GK00 §4, Thm 4.4, p. 20. *Needs:* F1.55, F1.17, F1.47, F1.35, F1.34.

**F1.57 Poincaré duality for de Rham cohomology of smooth affinoid and smooth Stein dagger spaces** (theorem). Let K be discretely valued of characteristic 0, and (for the strictness and finiteness inputs, see below) the fraction field of a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic (0, p). (a) If X is a smooth affinoid K-dagger space of pure dimension n, there are canonical isomorphisms H^i_dR(X) ≅ Hom_{K,cont}(H^{2n−i}_{dR,c}(X), K) and H^{2n−i}_{dR,c}(X) ≅ Hom_{K,cont}(H^i_dR(X), K) for 0 ≤ i ≤ 2n. (b) The same holds for a smooth Stein K-dagger space X of pure dimension n whose de Rham cohomology is finite-dimensional (needed for the strictness of its de Rham differentials, F1.52: finite codimension of the image of d in the closed subspace ker d makes that image closed, and the open mapping theorem then gives strictness; the reduction to the affine space alone does not). Since these groups are finite-dimensional (F1.65) and separated (F1.52), (a) is a perfect duality of finite-dimensional K-vector spaces. *Hypotheses:* K the fraction field of a complete DVR of mixed characteristic (0, p) (discretely valued of characteristic 0, hence spherically complete; mixed characteristic is what the finiteness theorem behind Lemma 4.7 provides). X smooth of pure dimension n, affinoid in (a), Stein with finite-dimensional de Rham cohomology in (b). *Source:* GK00 §4, Thm 4.9, p. 22. *Needs:* F1.56, F1.55, F1.52, F1.65, F1.48, F1.39.

### Finiteness

- **F1.58** Resolution of a closed subspace of a smooth affinoid dagger space to a normal crossings divisor. *Source:* GK02 §0, Lem. 0.1.

- **F1.59** Trace on de Rham complexes for finite étale morphisms of smooth dagger spaces. *Source:* GK02 §1, 1.14.

- **F1.60** Long exact sequence of de Rham cohomology with supports for a proper modification. *Source:* GK02 §1, Cor. 1.17.

**F1.61 De Rham cohomology of tubes in a strictly semistable formal scheme (Grosse-Klönne's Theorem C)** (theorem). Let R is a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic (0, p) with uniformiser π, K = Frac(R) and residue field k = R/π. Let 𝔛 be a strictly semistable formal R-scheme — a noetherian π-adic formal R-scheme with a Zariski covering by opens 𝔘_i admitting étale morphisms 𝔘_i → Spf(R⟨X_1..X_n⟩/(X_1⋯X_{r(i)} − π)) — with closed fibre 𝔛_k = ⋃_{i∈I} Y_i (irreducible components), Y_J = ⋂_{i∈J} Y_i. Let 𝔛^† be a K-dagger space whose rigid space is the generic fibre 𝔛_K, and for a subscheme T ⊆ 𝔛_k let ]T[^†_𝔛 be the open dagger subspace of 𝔛^† with rigid space the tube ]T[_𝔛 = sp^{-1}(T). Then for every nonempty J ⊆ I the restriction H^*_dR(]Y_J[^†) → H^*_dR(]Y_J − (Y_J ∩ ⋃_{i∈I−J} Y_i)[^†) is bijective. *Hypotheses:* R complete DVR of mixed characteristic. 𝔛 strictly semistable (locally étale over R⟨X⟩/(X_1⋯X_r − π)). J ≠ ∅. *Source:* GK02 §2, Thm 2.4, p. 18. *Needs:* F1.51, F1.49, F1.53, F1.33, F1.47, R2.6, R2.9, R2.12.

**F1.62 Finiteness of de Rham cohomology of tubes in a quasi-compact strictly semistable formal scheme** (theorem). In the situation of F1.61 assume moreover 𝔛 quasi-compact. Then for every J ⊆ I and q ∈ ℕ, dim_K H^q_dR(]Y_J[^†_𝔛) < ∞, and dim_K H^q_dR(𝔛^†) < ∞. *Hypotheses:* R complete DVR of mixed characteristic. 𝔛 quasi-compact strictly semistable. finiteness of Monsky–Washnitzer cohomology of smooth affine k-schemes (Berthelot 1997; Mebkhout 1997) — an external input. *Source:* GK02 §2, Thm 2.5, p. 20. *Needs:* F1.61, F1.49, F1.51, F1.25, F1.53.

- **F1.63** De Rham cohomology of dagger spaces commutes with finite extensions of the base field. *Source:* GK02 §1, 1.13.

- **F1.64** Finiteness of the de Rham invariants h^dR_q of quasi-algebraic affinoid dagger spaces. *Source:* GK02 §3, Thm 3.4.

**F1.65 Finiteness of de Rham cohomology of smooth quasi-compact dagger spaces (Grosse-Klönne's Theorem A), and base change to finite extensions** (theorem). Let R is a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic (0, p) with uniformiser π, K = Frac(R) and residue field k = R/π. Let X be a quasi-compact (quasi-separated) smooth K-dagger space, U ⊆ X a quasi-compact admissible open subset and Z → X a closed immersion. Then T = X − (U ∪ Z) has finite-dimensional de Rham cohomology: dim_K H^q_dR(T) < ∞ for all q. In particular (U = ∅) dim_K H^q_dR(X − Z) < ∞, and (Z = ∅) dim_K H^q_dR(X − U) < ∞; and for every finite extension K_1/K, H^q_dR(T_{K_1}/K_1) = H^q_dR(T/K) ⊗_K K_1. *Hypotheses:* R complete DVR of mixed characteristic (0, p) (K discretely valued of characteristic 0). X quasi-compact, quasi-separated, smooth. U quasi-compact admissible open; Z → X closed immersion. finiteness of Monsky–Washnitzer cohomology (external, through F1.62). *Source:* GK02 Introduction, Thm A, p. 2. *Needs:* F1.64, F1.58, F1.54, F1.53, F1.59, F1.60, F1.62, F1.13, F1.63, F1.44, R2.6, R2.15, F1.77.

**F1.66 Künneth formula for de Rham cohomology of smooth dagger spaces** (theorem). Let K be discretely valued of characteristic 0 and X, Y smooth K-dagger spaces for which the de Rham cohomology of all quasi-compact opens is finite-dimensional (true by F1.65 when K is the fraction field of a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic (0, p); no source proves it for residue characteristic 0). Then the cup-product maps ⊕_{p+q=n} H^p_dR(X) ⊗_K H^q_dR(Y) → H^n_dR(X × Y) are isomorphisms for all n. *Hypotheses:* K discretely valued of characteristic 0. X, Y smooth. *Source:* GK00 §4, Thm 4.12, p. 22. *Needs:* F1.65, F1.52, F1.19, F1.46, F1.48, F1.17, F1.53.

### Smooth pairs: logarithmic and compact-support complexes

**F1.67 Strict normal crossings divisors on smooth dagger spaces (smooth dagger pairs)** (definition `DaggerSpace.IsSNCDivisor`). Let X be a smooth K-dagger space (K is a field complete for a nontrivial nonarchimedean absolute value |.| of rank one (in Lean: NontriviallyNormedField K, IsUltrametricDist K, CompleteSpace K)) and D ⊆ X a closed dagger subspace (F1.34). D is a strict normal crossings divisor if its associated rigid pair (X', D') corresponds, under R1.31, to a smooth pair in the sense of R4.11 (X' smooth over K with the strict normal crossings divisor D'); only this dagger-specific transfer is defined here. Equivalently: every point has an affinoid neighbourhood Sp(A) with elements x_1, …, x_n ∈ A such that dx_1, …, dx_n is a basis of Ω^1_A and D ∩ Sp(A) = V(x_1⋯x_r) for some r ≤ n (as a closed subspace); equivalently, at every point x ∈ D the local ring O_{X,x} is regular with a regular system of parameters x_1..x_d such that D is cut out by x_1⋯x_r. The pair (X, D) is then a smooth dagger pair; D is reduced and an effective Cartier divisor, and locally on an admissible affinoid covering D = V(x_1) ∪ … ∪ V(x_r) with smooth pieces V(x_i) whose partial intersections are smooth of the expected codimension. As in R4.11 no condition is imposed on the global irreducible components: they need not be smooth (e.g. for residue characteristic ≠ 2 the analytified nodal cubic V(y^2 − x^2(1 + x)) ⊆ Sp(K⟨x, y⟩†) is a strict normal crossings divisor in this sense — at the origin it is V((y − x√(1+x))(y + x√(1+x))) with √(1+x) analytic on |x| < 1 — yet it is irreducible and singular). *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `IsSNCDivisor, isSNCDivisor_iff_toRigid, IsSNCDivisor.exists_coordinates, IsSNCDivisor.components, IsSNCDivisor.inter, IsSNCDivisor.ideal, IsSNCDivisor.complement, IsSNCDivisor.analytification, IsSNCDivisor.restrict`. *Tests:* `IsSNCDivisor_test_axes` (computation): V(x_1x_2) ⊆ Sp(K⟨x_1, x_2⟩†) is SNC with components V(x_1), V(x_2) meeting transversally in the origin. `IsSNCDivisor_test_empty` (degenerate): D = ∅ is an SNC divisor on every smooth X. `IsSNCDivisor_test_cusp` (non-example): V(x_2^2 − x_1^3) (a cusp), V(x_1x_2(x_1 − x_2)) (three concurrent lines) and V(x_1^2) (non-reduced) are not SNC divisors on the dagger bidisc. `IsSNCDivisor_test_rigid` (compatibility): D ⊆ X is SNC iff D' ⊆ X' is an SNC divisor of the smooth rigid space X' (R4.11). *Source:* Stacks Tag 0BI9, Def. 41.21.1. *Needs:* F1.47, F1.34, F1.40, F1.13, R4.11, R1.31.

**F1.68 The logarithmic de Rham complex Ω^•_X(log D) along a strict normal crossings divisor** (construction `DaggerSpace.logOmega`). Let (X, D) be a smooth K-dagger space with a strict normal crossings divisor (F1.67), j : X − D → X. The sheaf Ω^1_X(log D) ⊆ j_*Ω^1_{X−D} is the O†_X-submodule generated locally, in coordinates with D = V(x_1⋯x_r), by dx_1/x_1, …, dx_r/x_r, dx_{r+1}, …, dx_n; it is locally free of rank n and independent of the coordinates. Ω^p_X(log D) := ⋀^p Ω^1_X(log D), and the exterior derivative of j_*Ω^•_{X−D} preserves these subsheaves, giving the logarithmic de Rham complex Ω^•_X(log D) with Ω^•_X ⊆ Ω^•_X(log D) ⊆ j_*Ω^•_{X−D}. For a smooth component D_1 of D (a smooth closed dagger subspace which is a union of irreducible components of D) with D = D_1 + D' — so that D'|_{D_1} is a strict normal crossings divisor on D_1 — there is a residue map Res_{D_1} and an exact sequence of complexes 0 → Ω^•_X(log D') → Ω^•_X(log D) → ι_*Ω^•_{D_1}(log D'|_{D_1})[−1] → 0. Its hypercohomology is H^i_dR(X, log D) := H^i(X, Ω^•_X(log D)), and (Ω^•_X(log D))' = Ω^•_{X'}(log D'). *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `logOmega, logOmega_basis, logDeRhamComplex, logDeRhamComplex.incl, logDeRhamComplex.residue, logDeRhamComplex.residue_exact, logDeRhamComplex.wedge, logDeRhamComplex.pullback, logDeRhamComplex.toRigid, logDeRham, logDeRhamComplex_empty`. *Tests:* `logDeRham_test_polydisc` (computation): For the closed dagger polydisc V = Sp(K⟨x_1..x_n⟩†) and D = V(x_1⋯x_r) (char K = 0), H^q(V, Ω^•(log D)) has basis the classes dlog x_{i_1} ∧ … ∧ dlog x_{i_q}, i_1 < … < i_q ≤ r (the argument of F1.49 with nonnegative exponents). `logDeRham_test_empty` (degenerate): For D = ∅, Ω^•_X(log D) = Ω^•_X and H^i_dR(X, log ∅) = H^i_dR(X). `logDeRham_test_not_meromorphic` (non-example): dx/x^2 is a section of j_*Ω^1_{X−D} (and of Ω^1_X(∗D)) but not of Ω^1_X(log D) for D = {x = 0}: the log complex is not the complex of forms with arbitrary poles. `logDeRham_test_gk_monodromy` (compatibility): For the open unit bidisc U with D = V(x_1x_2) and Ω^•_U the quotient of Ω^•_U(log D) by the subalgebra generated by dlog(x_1x_2), the restriction to U^0 = V(x_1x_2) has H^1 one-dimensional, generated by dlog(x_1) (Grosse-Klönne, Frobenius and monodromy, Introduction). `logDeRham_test_residue` (characterisation): For char K = 0 and D = {0} in the dagger disc, Res(dx/x) = 1 and the residue sequence 0 → Ω^• → Ω^•(log 0) → ι_*K[−1] → 0 gives H^1(D, log 0) ≅ K via the residue (using H^1_dR(D) = 0). *Source:* Stacks Tag 0FMU, Def. 50.15.3. *Needs:* F1.67, F1.48, F1.46, F1.40.

**F1.69 The boundary-vanishing (compact-support) logarithmic de Rham complex Ω^•_X(log D)(−D)** (construction `DaggerSpace.compactLogDeRhamComplex`). Let (X, D) be a smooth K-dagger space with a strict normal crossings divisor and I_D ⊆ O†_X its (invertible) ideal. The compact-support logarithmic de Rham complex is the subcomplex Ω^•_X(log D)(−D) := I_D · Ω^•_X(log D) of Ω^•_X(log D); in coordinates with D = V(x_1⋯x_r) it is x_1⋯x_r·Ω^•_X(log D) ⊆ Ω^•_X. Its hypercohomology H^i_{c}(X, D) := H^i(X, Ω^•_X(log D)(−D)) is the de Rham cohomology of the pair with vanishing along the boundary. There are natural maps Ω^•_X(log D)(−D) ⊆ Ω^•_X ⊆ Ω^•_X(log D), inducing H^i_c(X, D) → H^i_dR(X) → H^i_dR(X, log D), and on X − D the complex restricts to Ω^•_{X−D}. With coefficients: for a locally free E with integrable connection having logarithmic poles along D, DR_c(E) := I_D·E ⊗ Ω^•_X(log D). *API* (on `DaggerSpace`): `compactLogDeRhamComplex, compactLogDeRhamComplex_le, compactLogDeRham, compactLogDeRhamComplex_restrict, compactLogDeRhamComplex_coordinates, compactLogDeRhamComplex.pullback, compactLogDeRhamComplex.connection, compactLogDeRhamComplex_empty`. *Tests:* `compactLogDeRham_test_P1` (computation): For X = (P^1)^† and D = {0, ∞}: H^0_c(X, D) = 0, H^1_c(X, D) = K and H^2_c(X, D) = K (Čech computation on P^1 = U_0 ∪ U_∞ via GAGA, F1.44). `compactLogDeRham_test_empty` (degenerate): For D = ∅ the complex is Ω^•_X and H^i_c(X, ∅) = H^i_dR(X). `compactLogDeRham_test_naive_twist` (non-example): The naive twist I_D · Ω^•_X is not a complex: for D = {x = 0}, d(x) = dx ∉ x·Ω^1_X. The logarithmic forms are needed to get a subcomplex. `compactLogDeRham_test_restrict` (compatibility): The restriction of Ω^•_X(log D)(−D) to X − D equals Ω^•_{X−D} (F1.48). *Source:* Stacks Tag 0FMU, proof of Lem. 50.15.2. *Needs:* F1.68, F1.67, F1.48, F1.34.

- **F1.70** Localisation sequences for boundary-vanishing log de Rham complexes and for compactly supported cohomology. *Source:* Stacks Tag 0FMU, proof of Lem. 50.15.2.

- **F1.71** Dagger algebras (and affinoid algebras) are excellent in characteristic 0. *Source:* GK00 §1, proof of Thm 1.7 (3).

- **F1.72** The integral Monsky–Washnitzer algebra O_K⟨X_1, …, X_n⟩† is noetherian (O_K a DVR). *Source:* Kedlaya06 §2.4, after Lem. 2.4.1.

- **F1.73** The integral subring of a dagger algebra of MW-type is a Monsky–Washnitzer lift. *Source:* Kedlaya06 §3.2, after Def. 3.2.1.

- **F1.74** A locally closed immersion of affinoid rigid spaces is Runge over a finite rational covering (BGR 7.3.5/1). *Source:* GK00 §2, proof of Prop. 2.5.

- **F1.75** Trace maps for the projection X × Y → Y with X smooth and proper (Grosse-Klönne, Finiteness, Proposition 1.11). *Source:* GK02 §1, Prop. 1.11.

- **F1.76** Independence of the embedding for de Rham cohomology with supports, and the invariants h^dR_q (Grosse-Klönne, Finiteness, 1.12–1.13). *Source:* GK02 §1, Cor. 1.12.

### Notions this roadmap states for its own use

**F1.77 De Jong's strictly semistable alteration theorem** (theorem). Let O_K be a complete discrete valuation ring with fraction field K (no hypothesis on the residue field) and X an integral scheme which is separated, flat and of finite type over O_K, and let Z ⊊ X be a proper closed subset containing the special fibre. There exist a finite extension of complete discrete valuation rings O_K ⊆ O_{K′}, a scheme X̄′ projective and strictly semistable over O_{K′} (regular, with reduced special fibre whose irreducible components are smooth divisors meeting transversally) with geometrically irreducible generic fibre, an open subscheme X′ ⊆ X̄′ and an alteration X′ → X over O_K (proper, surjective, finite over a dense open of X) such that the preimage of Z together with X̄′ ∖ X′ is a divisor with strict normal crossings making (X̄′, that divisor) a strictly semistable pair. If X is proper over O_K then X′ = X̄′, and the alteration factors through X ×_{O_K} O_{K′}. *Hypotheses:* O_K complete discretely valued; X integral, separated, flat and of finite type over O_K; Z a proper closed subset containing the special fibre. *Source:* de Jong Thm 6.5, with the definitions 2.12 (trait), 2.15 (S-variety), 2.16 (strictly semistable), 2.20 (alteration) and 6.3 (strictly semistable pair).
